# Changelog

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/2.0.0.html).

## [Unreleased]

### Changed

- **The embed-batch metric carries its unit in the name — `loopctl.embed.batch` is now `loopctl.embed.batch.duration_ms`** — the released 0.4.0 name sold a duration histogram without saying so, while the registry's grammar (`loopctl.<subsystem>.<name>[.<unit>]`) reserves the bare name for the signal itself; the metric observes one batch's wall-clock duration, so the name now spells it. **Migration:** a dashboard keying the 0.4.0 stream moves its filter from `metric == "loopctl.embed.batch"` to `metric == "loopctl.embed.batch.duration_ms"` — field set and semantics are unchanged (`provider`, `model`, `dim`, `inputs`, `outcome`, token counts ride exactly as before), and the emission site's pin now matches the full name with the old spelling asserted absent, so a revert fails the suite.

- **The compacted history opens the way providers expect** — the coalescing pass removed the adjacent assistant pair but folded the summary into the tail's opening tool turn, so the compacted machine opened with an assistant carrying a tool use — a shape zai's Anthropic-compatible endpoint rejects with `400 [1214]` (verified against the live endpoint: `[user, …]` always legal, `[assistant(text), user(text), …]` legal, an assistant with `tool_use` as the first message rejected, adjacent assistants rejected). Every LLM compactor now emits its summary as a **user-role message** at the head of the assembled output — `structured`, `qa`, and `rolling` alike — so a compacted history opens `[system?, user(summary), …]` whatever the tail starts with; the QA accumulator's prior-summary recognition and the rolling ledger detection follow the carrier's new role. A per-request net backs the seam: the Anthropic request build sends a history that would open with a tool-carrying assistant through `user_head_before_tool_use_opening`, which rides a minimal user message ahead of it — stateless by design, never persisted, so a compactor that kept producing the shape could not lock the engine into an inject-and-drop loop. The strict-double provider models the provider's actual rules — adjacent assistants *and* a tool-use opening both draw the recorded `400 [1214]` — and the request-build pin (`the_request_build_injects_a_user_head_before_a_tool_use_opening`) proves the repair on the wire. The QA accumulator's carrier lookup followed the role change too slowly: it took **the first user message** as the coalesced summary carrier, so an injected head riding ahead of the carrier pointed the lookup at itself, the prefix check never fired, and the prior summary reached the summarizer prompt **twice** — in `<prior-summary>` and again in `<conversation>`. The carrier is now found by **its leading summary part**, wherever it sits, with the injected head preserved ahead of it and the carrier's own remaining parts intact (pinned by `a_pinned_head_does_not_hide_the_summary_carrier`, which reads the rendered line head — a `!contains("SUMMARY\n")` substring guard cannot see the doubling, because the renderer joins text parts with no separator).

- **The wire-failure variants carry their forensics — `ApiError::Api`/`Auth`/`Http` gained a second (meta) tuple element, and `RateLimit` is now `#[non_exhaustive]`** — the shape change that lets *Provider forensics* (the Added entry below) ride the typed errors instead of a side channel: `Api(String)` became `Api(String, Option<Box<ProviderMeta>>)` (likewise `Auth`, `Http`), and the `RateLimit` struct variant gained a `meta` field behind the marker, following the `MessagePart::ToolResult` precedent — in-crate patterns bind it with `..`, external construction goes through `ApiError::rate_limited(message, retry_after)`. **Migration, mechanical:** external patterns `ApiError::Api(m)` / `Auth(m)` / `Http(m)` become `Api(m, _)` / `Auth(..)`; struct literals of `RateLimit { .. }` become the constructor; constructors (`api()`/`auth()`/`http()`/`http_with_status()`/`rate_limited()`) keep their signatures, so construction sites that already go through them see nothing, and the census of the shipped consumers matched zero variant patterns. The choice over a wrapper variant is deliberate: a wrapper silently re-types every wire error at runtime (variant drift through a new arm), while an arity change fails the compile once at each pattern and says exactly what to fix. Classification, retry, and message text are byte-unchanged for a provider that sends no forensics headers (pinned by the `Display` goldens); `Display` appends ` [req <id>]` only when an id was captured.

- **Edit results stop echoing files — the edit tools become the cheapest way to edit** — hosts routing file edits through `sed` and inline python instead of `Edit` were answering properties of the tools, not preferences: an edit result could echo the whole touched file (the `Before:`/`After:` fallback printed up to 1 000 lines per side, on `Retention::Durable` output that survives compaction), the read-before-write guard refused on **session bookkeeping rather than on bytes** — a file last read before a resume was uneditable until a fresh `Read`, with the text the edit named sitting intact on disk the whole time — and `dry_run` reported success without showing anything it would have done. All three are closed. **Receipts, everywhere:** `format_large_diff` and `LARGE_DIFF_PREVIEW_LINES` are gone, and a change that cannot be hunked — no line unique to either side to align on, or hunks that would print past `MAX_DIFF_LINES` — renders two lines instead of a file: `Changed: … (modified, change not shown)` and a `│` note giving both exact line counts and *which* of the two failures it was, the shape this module already used for a change it cannot diff line by line (`previous content not UTF-8`, `previous content not read`). No edit-family result echoes a file now, and `Created:` additions are bounded by the same `MAX_DIFF_LINES` as everything else. **Anchors, not bookkeeping:** a resume-armed baseline no longer refuses an `Edit` or `MultiEdit` — the edit is located in bytes read from disk moments earlier, every write still re-checks its own file before its rename, and the anchor matching *is* the verification, so the round trip it used to demand is gone. What a target this session never read loses is **tolerance**: the whitespace-normalized rescue — the tool deciding where the model's recollection lands — applies only to files actually read here, so a near-match on bytes nobody here has seen still refuses, and every refusal names the mismatch (`conflict::unverified_anchor_note`). `Write` keeps its refusal; it has no anchor to verify, only content to replace. **A preview that previews:** `dry_run` returns exactly the hunks the call would have applied plus one line, `Nothing was written (dry run).`, as the whole difference — the pin asserts the two runs are byte-identical — and the `Multi-File Edit Summary` / `Dry Run Preview` banners, the `File N:` labels, the two-space indentation the receipts sat under, and the `━━━ Applied: N file(s)` footer are gone, because a receipt is a contract and anything wrapped around it has to be parsed away before it can be read. **Migration:** a host matching `(modified, large file)`, `Before:`/`After:`, `Multi-File Edit Summary`, `File N:`, `Applied: N file(s)` or a per-file `(N edits)` count now sees none of them; `… (modified, change not shown)` replaces the first. The `Changed:` / `│` / `@@` contract is unchanged — a receipt parser that opens a section on a trimmed `Changed: `/`Created: ` header and ignores prose that is not diff content reads the de-indented and label-only forms with no change on its side (the `fs_golden` corpus re-pegged here for the two MultiEdit scenarios). Pinned by `an_edit_result_costs_hunks_not_files` (a one-line edit to a 2 000-line file costs a dozen lines), `a_fresh_anchor_edits_without_a_re_read`, `a_drifted_anchor_still_refuses_with_the_mismatch_named`, `an_unverified_target_takes_no_whitespace_guess`, `dry_run_returns_the_hunks_without_writing`, `a_resumed_batch_whose_anchors_all_match_applies`, `a_resumed_batch_with_one_drifted_anchor_refuses_the_batch`, `an_unalignable_large_rewrite_shows_nothing_and_says_why`, `a_wholesale_overwrite_of_a_large_file_shows_no_echo`, `a_rewritten_large_file_that_still_aligns_is_refused_whole`, `scattered_one_line_changes_past_the_print_bound_are_refused`.

- **Edit receipts keep their hunks for large files — a diff is bounded by the change, not by the file** — the line matcher was sized against the whole file: past `MAX_LCS_PRODUCT` (1 000 000 line pairs — the *product* of the two sides' line counts, so ~1 000 lines against ~1 000) an edit abandoned the diff for a hunkless `Changed: … (modified, large file)` header plus a Before/After preview of up to 1 000 lines per side. An edit's two sides are a file against nearly itself, so in practice that meant **any change to a file over ~1 000 lines rendered no diff at all** — a 2 000-line file truncated to 20 lines stayed under the cap and diffed normally — while a Markdown edit in the same session rendered a proper one, which is the shape a consumer that draws these receipts has nothing to work with — and the same fallback echoed up to **2 × 1 000 lines of file the edit never touched** into the model's context per edit. The bound now sits on the matcher, where the quadratic cost actually is: the unchanged bulk around a change is *measured* by head and tail scans and rendered as counted elisions, a differing middle still too wide for the budget is cut at lines occurring exactly once on each side (no coincidence, so sound alignment; the run is kept increasing so the anchors cannot cross), and every region then prints its change with three lines of context and its own `@@ -old,count +new,count @@` header. The summary, the gutter, and the numbers mean exactly what they meant for a small file, and a one-line edit to a 2 000-line file now costs eleven receipt lines. The bound has two halves, because a limit that only ever applies to one region limits no receipt: `MAX_TOTAL_LCS_CELLS` caps the matcher work a receipt spends **across** its regions and `MAX_DIFF_LINES` caps what those regions print, the second at 2 000 lines — priced as the reader sees it, because a region costs the receipt its own lines *plus* the context and `@@` header drawn around it: 700 scattered one-line edits in a 20 000-line file cost 1 400 changed lines and would print about 6 300 (`scattered_one_line_changes_past_the_print_bound_are_refused`), while an ordinary spread of them stays hunked (`scattered_changes_under_the_print_bound_keep_their_hunks`). Without them a wholesale-but-alignable change beats the bound while obeying every per-region check — re-indent a file and every top-level signature stays unique and untouched, so each signature is an anchor and each re-indented line a change, region by region affordable, the sum a diff of the entire file: 6 000 printed lines in `a_rewritten_large_file_that_still_aligns_is_refused_whole`, on receipts stamped `Retention::Durable` that survive compaction. Such a change is refused whole rather than trimmed — `DiffPlan::TooWide`, pinned by `a_budget_a_region_exhausts_refuses_the_change_whole`, each bound asserted at compile time against the constant it has to clear (`MAX_TOTAL_LCS_CELLS` above one region's cap, `MAX_DIFF_LINES` above a receipt's own scaffold), so a bound configured below what it must hold fails every build, not only the run that happens to exercise it. `Created:` blocks widened past the 10-line thumbnail to **every** addition, bounded at `MAX_DIFF_LINES` with the `... +N more lines` tail kept, so a 30-line new file shows thirty lines while a generated one still cannot double itself into context. What this change left standing — the Before/After preview for a change with nothing to align on — is retired by *Edit results stop echoing files* below, where the reasoning for its replacement lives; its two pins became `an_unalignable_large_rewrite_shows_nothing_and_says_why` and `a_wholesale_overwrite_of_a_large_file_shows_no_echo`. The emitter's shape, which consumers already parse and nothing here pinned before, is pinned at the source for the first time: `large_file_edit_emits_hunks` (the receipt says `(modified)`, carries `@@`, never the words `large file`, and is eleven lines for a 2 000-line file), `hunk_line_numbers_match_the_file` (a change at line 1 001 of a 2 000-line file addresses lines 998–1004 exactly — the synthesis counts the file, not the span, so an elision that lost track of what it skipped cannot pass), `two_far_apart_edits_in_a_large_file_report_their_own_hunks` beside its merge twin `two_edits_within_the_context_margin_share_one_hunk`, `a_line_deleted_from_a_large_file_reports_both_sides_counts`, `a_line_appended_to_a_large_file_reports_the_end_of_the_file`, `created_file_renders_full_additions` with its bounded twin `a_new_file_past_the_receipt_bound_keeps_its_tail_note`, and `multi_edit_hunks_merge_cumulatively` (one receipt, two hunks, nothing echoed from the 1 400 lines between them). `the_disclosure_survives_the_large_diff_path` becomes `the_disclosure_rides_the_large_file_receipt`, because the normalized-match note now lands in the `│` channel ahead of the first hunk instead of after a header that had none, and the two pins of the retired behaviour (`large_files_fall_back_to_a_preview`, `new_file_truncates_long_preview`) are replaced rather than deleted. **Migration:** a host that asserted `… (modified, large file)` on a large *edit* now sees `(modified)` plus hunks — that label is reserved for changes with nothing to align. A host that compares receipts **byte-for-byte** must be told more: the matcher no longer sees the file but the middle left after the shared head and tail are trimmed, and where repeated lines make the alignment ambiguous the two forms break the tie differently — old `a`, `b`, `a` against `a` keeps the first `a` as context where the whole-file matcher kept the last, moving a line between `-` and ` `. The counts do not move (`repeated_lines_move_between_context_and_deletion_where_counts_cannot`), so compare `-`/`+` counts rather than bytes; content that does not turn on an ambiguous alignment is unchanged.

- **The Anthropic off rung is stated, not implied — `ThinkingEffort::None` now sends `thinking: {"type": "disabled"}` instead of omitting the `thinking` block** — omitting the block was written when thinking was opt-in per request, and it no longer means off: on a Messages model that thinks by default (the Claude 5 family and later) an omitted block selects the model's own adaptive thinking, so the rung loopctl sells as "off" was silently served as "thinking, at the provider's discretion" — a caller who set `None` to cut latency or reasoning cost got neither, and could not tell from the request. The off is now sent in the provider's own spelling, which every thinking-capable Messages model has an opinion about: where `disabled` is accepted the answer is genuinely un-thought, and where a model accepts no off at all the request is refused rather than run on a setting nobody chose — the capability probe ([*Anthropic thinking control follows the model's own disclosure*](#added)) names the model client-side where the disclosure is known, and an endpoint that discloses nothing answers with the provider's own error — the same honest-400 doctrine the widened ladder applies to an OpenAI label an endpoint tops out below, and the reason the omission could not stand as "off by another name". The rung's other legs are unchanged: no budget, no `max_tokens` headroom raise, the plain-text `<thinking>` replay for text-carrying history reasoning (a redacted part still dropped), and a request that sets **no** effort still sending no `thinking` field at all — byte-identical to before. **Migration:** a host pinned to a Messages model that rejects `thinking: {"type": "disabled"}` and discloses no lower off setting (an adaptive-only tier without `between_tools`) cannot express off on that model; leave the effort unset and accept the model's own thinking default, or move to a model with an off level. Pinned by `the_off_rung_says_disabled_rather_than_omitting_the_block` (both the stated off and the untouched unset shape), with the per-request-override pin extended to read the stated `disabled`.

- **The source-hygiene ratchet scans where the code actually lives and fails loud when it cannot** — the ratchet walked `src/ tests/ examples/ benches/` plus an `other` bucket rooted at a nonexistent `crates/`, so `derive/` — a workspace member whose tests *implement the patterns the ratchet documents* — was never scanned, companion-crate build output under `tests/` was collected as source, and the traversal silently skipped directories it could not open (the failure mode that hides forbidden text instead of reporting it) or treated symlinks as trees. The roots are a named table (`src/ derive/ sqlite/ migrations/ tests/ examples/ benches/`, plus the `.cargo/config.toml` and any real `crates/**` shapes); an absent root scans empty — the documented optional case — while a present-but-unopenable root or an unreadable entry panics naming its path, symlinked directories and files are never followed, and a `target` directory is pruned. The planning-id allowlist narrowed from whole files to what its documented purpose needs: **comment lines in allowlisted files are scanned again** (an id smuggled into a comment of the redaction middleware is metadata on the ship line — exactly what the ratchet catches), while id-shaped *data* lines stay exempt (they are the input the middleware's own tests exercise). Scan probes take their predicate by reference. Pinned by `missing_optional_dir_scans_empty` (the optional case the loud version must preserve), `traversal_errors_are_not_silently_skipped`, `symlinked_dirs_and_files_are_not_followed`, `target_directories_are_not_scanned`, and `allowlisted_files_still_scan_comment_lines` — each of the latter four red against the previous scan, which reported no problem at all. The divider probe strips the full `///`/`//!` marker before measuring the decoration run: a probe that stripped only `//` left the marker's third character ahead of the decoration, so a rule drawn inside a doc comment (`/// ----`, `//! ====`) walked past the check as ordinary text. Pinned by `doc_comment_dividers_are_detected` (red against the marker-blind probe; its prose arms hold that a doc bullet, a short run, and plain doc text stay unflagged). The ratchet now covers the release log itself: `CHANGELOG.md` is scanned for planning ids, development-process provenance, planning-doc inventories, and internal consumer references (`changelog_ships_changes_not_planning_metadata`) — an entry records changes between versions, never the task, review, or planning artifact behind one.

- **The doctest census — an ignored example states its reason or fails the build, and 36 more doc examples run in CI** — the doctest suite carried 158 `rust,ignore` blocks: examples that never compile, rot silently, and — for the crate-level Quick Start, the most-read block in the docs — were the first thing a reader copied. The flagship now runs, mock-backed behind the `testing` feature; the engine config's builder blocks gained the same hidden scaffold (a `# #[cfg(feature = "testing")]` gate and `# fn` wrapper around hidden `use` lines — the visible example text is what the reader always saw); the detection manager's examples proved the cheapest rung of the ladder — stripped of their ignores they needed only imports, no fixture at all; and a block that had been ignored as a "fragment" turned out to have no blocker at all (the `mcp` module example already carried its own hidden scaffold — converting it also compiled a never-before-compiled `rmcp` path for the first time; its body defines an `async fn run`, so the compiler type-checks it end to end even though nothing executes it — the one compile-only conversion, and it says so). The suite moved from 411 running / 158 ignored to **448 running / 122 ignored** under `cargo test --doc --all-features`. Every survivor is an honest survivor — a provider-client constructor that reads its credential from the environment, a schematic fragment naming a binding the reader defines, an API-shape illustration where running is meaningless — and each opens with `// ignore — <why>` as the block's first body line, because the reason must live inside the block: rustdoc extracts nothing from a fence whose info string carries any non-ASCII character — an em dash typed before a reason deletes the doctest from the suite entirely, with no build error to trace. [`tests/doctest_census.rs`](tests/doctest_census.rs) fails the build on any unreasoned `rust,ignore` (`every_ignored_doctest_states_its_reason`, seen red against a planted unreasoned fence; `make doc-census` prints the live totals — counted by the census parser itself, so plain fences count as the Rust doctests rustdoc treats them as, and the parse agrees with the suite fence-for-fence — shows the failing assertion's full output, every violating fence's file:line included, when a gate trips, and propagates the gate's exit status). The same test sweeps the other direction too: an **executable** fence that defines a hidden `fn example` must also drive it — a line of real code that actually calls `example()` (a string literal or comment merely naming the call drives nothing), bare or inside `block_on`; a definition-shaped empty `fn main` and an unrelated `block_on` prove nothing — because rustdoc wraps a doctest body in its own `fn main`, so an uncalled example is dead code: the block compiles, "passes", and asserts nothing while looking runnable (`no_executable_doctest_defines_an_uncalled_example`, seen red against a planted uncalled wrapper; the flagship Quick Start now drives a full mock-backed run through `block_on`, and each `BareLoop` setter example ends with a wired caller). Three more sharpnesses ride the same file: a reason must *start* its line — a marker inside a string literal or a trailing comment is code, not a stated reason; fence attributes are read the way rustdoc reads them — words split on commas and whitespace alike, so `rust,ignore,edition2021` and `rust ignore` must state their reason alike — and a fence rustdoc silently drops, any non-ASCII character anywhere in its info string, is reported as malformed, the shape that deletes the doctest without a compile error; fence boundaries follow the CommonMark rules rustdoc's markdown follows — a closer matches its opener's length and carries no trailing text, and an unterminated fence ends at its doc block's edge instead of swallowing the next item's examples (`the_census_reads_each_reason_shape_correctly` pins the reading, wrapper shapes by `the_census_reads_each_wrapper_shape_correctly`, boundaries by `fence_boundaries_follow_the_rules_rustdoc_extracts_by`) — and the walk fails loud on an unreadable path naming it, never silently skipping the subtree it could not read (`traversal_errors_are_not_silently_skipped`, `missing_optional_root_scans_empty`). The docs canon states all of it going forward — `ignore` is the escape hatch for what cannot run in-process, a feature requirement is a hidden `cfg` line inside the block rather than an ignore, and every example either runs or says why it cannot — recorded in a new `TESTING.md` §14.

### Added

- **Run metrics as typed data — `metrics::AgentMetrics` snapshots and the metric registry catalog, behind the new `metrics` feature** — telemetry so far has been wire events; a host that wanted the *shape of a run* (turns, latency, tool attempts, token detail) had to build and maintain one from observer callbacks. [`AgentMetrics`](src/metrics.rs) is the typed snapshot: run identity and outcome, settled-turn counts, and fixed-bucket [`LatencyHistogram`](src/metrics/session.rs)s whose p50/p90/p99 reads are bucket-upper-bound estimates — conservative, the right claim for an SLO — over a default 5 ms-to-60 s scheme with an overflow slot; per-tool [`ToolStats`](src/metrics/session.rs) with attempt-counted calls (a retry is an attempt), an error numerator, and per-attempt latency; [`TokenTotals`](src/metrics/session.rs) carrying the cache-write and reasoning detail the usage classes report; and counters for streams, compactions, fallbacks, transport downgrades, model switches, budget warnings, and the two guardrail detectors. Everything saturates, serde round-trips with forward-compatible defaults (partial JSON reads as defaults; a missing run uuid reads nil), and the tool map is a `BTreeMap` so export diffs stay stable. The [`registry`](src/metrics/registry.rs) is the matching contract: one `MetricDescriptor` per signal declared under the telemetry contract — emitted signals plus declared-not-yet-shipped ones — name, kind, closed label set — behind `all`/`lookup`, with `validate` accepting only emissions whose label keys match the declared set *exactly*, so an undeclared name or shape is a counted gap, never data stored under an invented one. The value types and catalog are fillable today from `LoopObserver`; the official consumers — the layer, the observer adapter, and the store — arrive with the collectors entry below.

- **The provider answers *which models* and *is it alive* before the turn — model discovery, a liveness probe, and a metadata funnel on `ApiClient`** — three new trait methods, each with a working default so every client is immediately probeable: `list_models()` (what the endpoint actually serves), `ping()` (liveness evidence — the four shipped clients answer on a metadata route; the trait default sends one minimal completion, so a host's own client on a metered endpoint pays tokens to be probed, exactly as the method's docs disclose), and `model_metadata(model)` (window, modalities, capabilities, and **who said so**). Failures land in one four-way-plus-leftovers vocabulary, `ProbeClass::from_error` (`Auth` / `RateLimited` / `Server` / `Answered` / `Network` / `Unsupported` / `Other`), derived mechanically from the variant and status the wire already chose — a 429/503/529 riding a plain `Http` failure reads `RateLimited` too, whoever carries it, because "retry later" is what those statuses spell — an endpoint that never answers can never read as a credential problem, and the probe vocabulary is pinned to agree with the provider classification and the wire-failure telemetry on every status from 400 to 529, swept exhaustively over both the classified and the unclassified `Http` shape. The value types live in `loopctl::api::probe` (`#[non_exhaustive]`, constructor-built): `ModelInfo` (usable id, display name, verbatim creation stamps — the providers disagree on units and the crate does not guess them, `owned_by`, weight size), `ModelMetadata` (`context_window`, `input_modalities`/`output_modalities`, `ModelCapabilities` of `structured_output`/`tool_calling`, and a `MetadataSource` provenance marker — `Unreported` / `Curated` / `Disclosed` / `DisclosedAndCurated` — because a number the provider served and a published opinion are different kinds of fact, a marker every construction path keeps honest: the builders and deserialization both promote a record that states a fact without labeling its source), and `PingReport` (measured latency plus the answer's rate-limit snapshot and request id). `model_metadata` is the single funnel: **provider disclosures beat curation field by field** — Gemini's listing `inputTokenLimit`, a llama.cpp/Ollama deployment's own disclosed window folded in for the client's current model through the same cached probe behind `model_context_window` (and no probe for any other id) — and an id nobody documented answers fully unreported, never a plausible zero; the curated table is deliberately small (published, stable families only) and holds back wherever the record is thin (`None`, not `false`, for unverified claims; `Some(false)` only where the refusal is documented — the Messages family's lack of a native JSON-schema mode, the DeepSeek reasoning endpoint's lack of tool support). Per-provider wire truth: the OpenAI-compatible client tries `GET {base}/models` and, on a genuine `404` — no OpenAI-style route — falls back to Ollama's `GET /api/tags` (names, modification stamps, weight sizes), surfacing the **original** refusal when the fallback fails too — a route set the `ping` verb shares, so a tags-only server pings as live rather than reading as a wrong URL; Anthropic reads `GET /v1/models` with the credential pair; Gemini reads `/models` and folds each entry's disclosed input-token limit over curation; Bedrock `SigV4`-signs a GET to the control-plane `/foundation-models` (catalog truth, not account entitlement — documented as such) and accepts both array spellings the endpoint has shipped, resolving provider-prefixed ids (`anthropic.claude-…`, `meta.llama…`) to their curated family under both dot and dash spellings. Listings answer the endpoint's **first page**: Anthropic's `has_more`, Gemini's `nextPageToken`, and Bedrock's `nextToken` continuation are documented page-one-only (no result flag — the bound is the contract), so a completed listing is complete within that bound, and a failure is never a short list — and neither is a body that answered without speaking of models at all: a 200 carrying no entries array in any accepted spelling is a failure (`ProbeClass::Other`), never a catalog dressed up empty, because "no models" is an answer only an empty array is entitled to give. Probe GETs ride a shared checked-transport helper that records the rate-limit slot and runs the same `Retry-After`-reading, forensics-capturing failure classification as completions — so a probe rejection carries the request id in `provider_meta()` and the ` [req <id>]` suffix exactly like a completion failure — under a shared 5-second total deadline that keeps a dead metadata route from stalling on the conversational timeout — and every listing and probe body is read through the completion path's shared `read_bounded_body` ceiling, so an endpoint answering with an oversized metadata document fails as an `Http` error instead of being materialized into memory. Supporting public surface: `ApiError::http_status()` (the answered-status-or-never-answered distinction, typed), `Modality::from_wire_token`, `curated_model_metadata`, and `MockApiClient::with_models` for fixtures — unscripted, the mock refuses a listing with the trait default's message: one shared constant, both refusal sites pinned to the same rendered text, so probe error paths exercise identically against mock and reality. All three methods have defaults, so **no break**: existing external `ApiClient` impls compile unchanged and simply answer "this client cannot list" / probe through `create_message`; pinned by the trait-object integration suite, the per-provider listing-parser pins (both Ollama and OpenAI shapes, verbatim stamps, skipped malformed entries, disclosed-beats-curated provenance), a cross-classifier agreement sweep, an independently computed `SigV4` GET signature vector, page-bound pins that fail if a continuation marker ever silently buys a second request, and constructor sweeps of every new type.

- **`api::error::parse_retry_after` is public — the `Retry-After` parser the clients themselves use** — the provider clients read an RFC 9110 `Retry-After` (delta-seconds or HTTP-date, past dates and unparseable values reading as no delay) with one parser; hosts classifying a provider failure of their own, and conformance harnesses asserting a recorded delay reached the typed error, previously could only guess at the rules with a second implementation. Exposing the crate's own parser keeps reader and checker reading the field the same way (the conformance oracle now reads through it — an HTTP-date recording no longer parses for one side only).

- **Provider conformance suite — one behavioral contract, five families, run per provider over the recorded cassettes** — each provider's tests used to assert their own subset of the behaviors every client must uphold, and there was no single contract a new client could run to prove itself. `tests/common/conformance.rs` is that contract, written once: **stream_lifecycle** (exactly one terminal; nothing after it; **no synthetic stop on a truncated stream** — the 0.3.0 rule; balanced lanes), **usage_reporting** (recorded numbers reach the terminal `MessageDelta`, repeated reports merge field-wise by max — the 0.3.x latch; an unreported stream never gets a fabricated number; a fixture cut below its terminal gaps instead of blaming a client that stayed silent), **stop_reason_routing** (tool vs text route identically; tool lanes carry the requested identity), **error_taxonomy** (401/403 → `Auth` one-attempt; 429/503/529 → `RateLimit` carrying **exactly** the recorded `Retry-After` — equality both ways, so inventing a delay the endpoint never advised fails as surely as dropping one it did; else typed `Http`), **options_honoring** (the model override on the wire bytes, `include_usage` agreeing with the scenario, the tool-constraint capability probe telling the truth). The **fixture is the oracle** — expected numbers, reasons, and retry values are parsed from the recorded bytes, never hardcoded — and per-provider instantiation is configuration: a profile names a client builder and dialect; the battery never changes. All seven recorded vendors run green (OpenAI, Anthropic, Gemini, DeepSeek, Grok, zAI, and the Ollama/`openai-compat` dialect), and three new locally-recorded Ollama fixtures complete that dialect across every family: a genuinely usageless stream (`minimal_text_no_usage` — unreported ≠ zero, arm one), a per-request model override that outranks the client default on the wire (`model_override`, recorded through `stream_messages_with_options`, and a reasoning-delta P8 rider), and a **real 404** from asking for an unpulled model (`missing_model_error` — the corpus's first genuine error fixture, gated in like the 429 attempt: `finish_errored` writes only when the provider really answered ≥ 400). Cloud error cassettes stay `n/r` until a human recording meets them — the matrix says so out loud. The matrix (contract family × provider) is **generated, not hand-maintained**: `conformance_matrix_artifact` writes `target/provider-conformance/matrix.md` on every run and the CI test job publishes it as the `provider-conformance-matrix` artifact; `n/r` cells with reasons (Bedrock joins when its cassettes land) are visible gaps, never silently green. **Silence is not a verdict**: every family check reports a gap when the fixture's shape cannot decide its rules — a success fixture proves nothing about the taxonomy's status arms — so a family counts as exercised only where a check actually adjudicated a fixture, and the published table now reads `error_taxonomy | n/r` for every vendor with no recorded failure while the families its fixtures *could* decide stay green, with a per-provider, per-family coverage list naming each hole and what closes it (`the_matrix_counts_a_family_only_where_a_check_chose`, `a_family_no_recording_can_adjudicate_gaps_instead_of_passing`, `a_fabricated_retry_after_fails_the_taxonomy_equality`). The suite proves itself: `conformance_catches_a_planted_violation` edits a copy of a recorded fixture **in memory** (drops the provider terminal; renames the tool on the response side), drives the exact battery CI runs, and asserts the suite reports the right family with the right detail — with the unmutated fixture verified silent first. The truncation plant reads as a **fixture-integrity `n/r`, never a violation**: the pinned client contract answers a stream cut before its provider terminal with silence (the 0.3.0 rule — the engine, not the client, surfaces incompleteness), so a suite that failed a conformant client for silence would be lying in the other direction; only a fabricated terminal is decidable on such a fixture (`truncation_gaps_unless_the_client_fabricates_a_terminal`). The rule covers **usage** with the same reasoning: a Messages-family stream reports input usage on its opening event, so a recording cut below its `message_delta` *always* shows usage the client never surfaced — blaming that silence would make every re-record cut (a routine release act) unshippable-green, so the carrying rule reports a named `usage_reporting` fixture gap instead (`a_truncated_usage_fixture_gaps_instead_of_blaming_the_client`, asserted through the meta-pin itself, whose truncation plant now keeps the usage chunk it used to filter out). Running the suite surfaced two wire realities the checks had to learn to read honestly: **Gemini reports `finishReason: STOP` even alongside `functionCall` parts** (so the routing contract accepts the decision riding a named, closed tool lane beside a parseable terminal — provider truth is not a violation, an unnamed lane still fails), and Gemini's pretty-printed JSON hid its terminal from a substring check (terminal detection is JSON-semantic now). `TESTING.md` states the rule going forward: **a new provider ships with a green conformance run** — a profile plus cassettes for the applicable families, or an explicit gap reason. No library code changed; `make conformance` runs the suite. The matrix-artifact check's openai-wire legs — cells only vendors in the `openai` battery can produce — carry the matching `cfg` now, so a feature-less run asserts what that build actually records (it used to fail on cells that cannot exist there while the every-build gap reasons went unasserted; Bedrock's names both gates its column waits on — the hermetic recording **and** its HTTP failures classifying through the shared status table — and the artifact asserts that too). And the taxonomy's `Retry-After` oracle stopped reading the wrong response: it took the first interaction carrying headers, so on a multi-interaction recording (a warm-up 200, then the 429) it compared the client's delay against the *warm-up's* headers — green on a client that dropped the failure's advice, red on a conformant client that correctly ignored a success's; the reader targets the last recorded failure now (`the_retry_after_oracle_reads_the_error_response` pins both mirrored shapes).

- **Provider forensics — request ids and rate-limit headers ride the typed errors, captured at HTTP time** — when a provider 429s or 500s in production, the two things a diagnosis needs are the **request id** and the **rate-limit state** ("what was the remaining quota at that moment?"), both riding the very headers the status line arrives with — and until now both were discarded with the response object, exactly like `Retry-After` was before 0.3.0 fixed it. `ApiError`'s wire-failure variants ([`Api`](src/api/error.rs), `RateLimit`, `Auth`, `Http`) now carry an internal [`ProviderMeta`](src/api/error.rs) — `request_id` (first present of `x-amzn-requestid`, `x-request-id`, `openai-request-id`, `apiproxy-id`, `cf-ray`, …) and the `x-ratelimit-*` / `rate-limit-*` family as a [`RateLimitSnapshot`](src/api/error.rs) of **verbatim strings**, never interpreted (units are per-provider — tokens vs requests, epoch vs HTTP date — and normalizing them here would be guesswork the consumer's job). Read it with `ApiError::provider_meta()` (an accessor, not fields on every variant — the 20+ construction sites outside the wire never see it); `Display` appends ` [req <id>]` **only when an id was captured**, so every pre-existing error-string golden is byte-unchanged (both shapes pinned). Capture happens once, at HTTP time, in the shared `post_json_checked` path — every OpenAI-compatible, Anthropic, and Gemini client inherits it, embeddings included — and Bedrock threads it through its own paths: response headers on both invoke shapes, and an exception frame carrying its own `x-amzn-requestid` **outranks** the response-level id, because that id names the frame that actually failed. Mid-stream failures name their request too: the id captured when the stream opened rides the error a mid-stream error frame, chunk-parse failure, dropped connection, **or provider `error` event surfaced at stream end** produces — on Bedrock all three now attach inside `BedrockStreamState`, the object that holds the response's forensics (`finish` included), so an Anthropic-path stream whose service delivers `overloaded_error` inside a chunk frame names its request exactly as the invoke response that opened the stream does. The **success path** was the other half of the capture — a 429's neighbor is a 200-with-3-remaining: the four chat clients gained `last_rate_limit() -> Option<RateLimitSnapshot>`, refreshed per response (failures included — a throttle's `remaining: 0` is exactly the state worth keeping), per-response not sticky (the next silent response clears it), behind a Category-1 advisory lock whose poison keeps the last snapshot — a slot cloned into each streaming task is multi-writer by design, so the rule is last-writer-wins: one acquisition swaps one response's snapshot whole, and no reader ever sees one response's `remaining` beside another's `limit`. `None` is **unreported, never zero** — local servers send none of these headers and change nothing. Forensics is pure observability: no classification, retry, or rate-limit *decision* consults it (deliberately — acting on `remaining` is a design fork held for consumer demand), the 8 KiB diagnostic-body bound still truncates with meta attached (the meta is not a size loophole), and header values are bounded at capture on `char` boundaries. Telemetry rides the same capture: wire failures narrate one event with `status`/`class` (`auth|rate_limit|server|network|other`) and the id, and a response reporting under 10% remaining warns **once per window** (latched, re-armed by recovery or silence — counted end to end through a capture subscriber by `low_water_event_fires_once_per_window_end_to_end`, not just latched in the unit). **Migration:** see the *wire-failure variants* Changed entry above. Pinned by `http_error_carries_request_id`, `no_headers_is_clean_none`, `rate_limit_snapshot_captured_verbatim`, `success_path_updates_last_rate_limit` (per response, same client), `body_bound_unchanged`, `mid_stream_error_carries_the_response_id`, `mid_stream_emitter_error_carries_the_response_meta` (the emitter's recorded mid-stream `error` carries the open-response id, quota, and ` [req <id>]` suffix), `bedrock_exception_frame_id_threaded` (frame id outranks response id; response quota survives the frame failure), `a_panicked_holder_keeps_the_last_snapshot`, `concurrent_writers_leave_one_whole_snapshot` (clones contending leave one response's snapshot, never a mixture), `the_status_table_types_a_failure_without_headers` / `the_pure_classifier_carries_no_forensics` (the status→variant table pinned with no headers captured, and meta shown to be the entry point's addition, never the table's invention), the `Display` byte-goldens `display_without_provider_id_is_byte_stable` / `display_appends_the_request_id_only_when_present`, and the capture/precedence/bounding/slot family in `src/provider/forensics.rs`. The low-water line now reads at `WARN` — a warning visible only under a filter the default host does not enable cannot keep its own *warns once per window* promise (the per-response state event stays at `DEBUG`; the latch and its per-window bound are unchanged, and the end-to-end pin asserts the level beside the count — `low_water_event_fires_once_per_window_end_to_end`). And two error paths threw forensics away on the non-streaming transport: when a 2xx response was too large for the bounded-body read, or its bytes did not parse, the error was built after the response had been consumed — every non-streaming client now captures the meta before the body is taken and attaches it on both arms, pinned across providers by `nonstream_body_read_failure_carries_the_response_id` and `nonstream_json_parse_failure_carries_the_response_id` (each client served raw oversized or malformed 2xx bytes; the typed error names the recorded request).

- **A length-cut completion compacts and retries the turn — salvage for the truncation the provider never reports** — a context with no room left for generation is not an error on the wire: the response simply ends at the output cap, on thinking models carrying a reasoning trace and nothing else. Taken as a completed turn, it ended the run silently on a truncated thought. The engine now reads that shape — a completion ending at `max_tokens` with no visible text and no tool calls — as the mirror of the provider-reported overflow and runs the salvage against it: the truncated message is never recorded, the history compacts toward the salvage target, and the turn is reissued once. Its turn events stay paired — the truncated attempt closes with a failed turn-end carrying its real usage (that request succeeded and its spend was real), while the turn's record and the run's totals keep the salvaged retry's figures, the two readings deliberately apart. A second length cut on the same turn, or one compaction cannot rescue (an unshrinkable history, a machine that declines the step), fails the run with `ContextExceeded` carrying the machine's figure floored just above the window rather than a measurement: the wire cannot distinguish an over-full context from an output cap too small for the answer, so the salvage always names the reading its remedy acts on — the same rule the overflow salvage has always applied, now shared code between them. A forced pass that can no longer make progress surfaces the genuine `CompactionStalled` the guard raises, never a salvage-invented overflow. Truncation and overflow are independent per-turn slots: either can rescue the same turn after the other, and a fresh turn is never denied by a prior turn's spent slot. Pinned at the engine: `a_length_cut_completion_compacts_and_retries_the_turn`, `a_second_length_cut_completion_ends_the_run`, `a_salvaged_length_cut_pairs_every_turn_start_with_a_turn_end` (fail-first on the silent-arm tree at `left: [0]` against `right: [0, 0]` start/end lists — the unpaired-start shape the pin exists to catch), `a_length_cut_salvage_reports_the_truncated_attempt_s_usage_at_its_failed_turn_end` (the two-reading split: failed end carries the attempt's real spend, the run's totals carry only the salvaged retry's), `a_third_length_cut_on_one_turn_ends_the_run`, `a_single_message_length_cut_fails_at_once_and_floors_the_reported_figure` (an unshrinkable history consults no compactor), `a_length_cut_then_an_overflow_rejection_salvages_both_and_completes`, `an_overflow_salvage_then_a_length_cut_still_completes_the_turn`, `length_cut_salvage_on_consecutive_turns_spends_one_slot_each`, `a_declined_salvage_compaction_ends_the_run`, and the trigger's boundaries — `a_tool_carrying_length_cut_is_an_ordinary_tool_turn_not_a_salvage`, `an_empty_completion_at_end_turn_still_completes_the_run`, and `a_length_cut_without_a_context_manager_ends_the_run` (every constructor seeds a manager; the managerless state is unreachable, and the honest termination rides the seed), and across transports by `a_length_cut_on_the_non_streaming_path_compacts_and_retries_the_turn` (the trigger reads the finish reason, not the transport — the empty non-streaming completion at the length stop runs the same arms on `create_message`).

- **Usage detail — cache-write and reasoning token classes on `Usage`, parsed by every shipped provider that reports them, threaded through the stream/turn/run-end observer contexts, the engine's `Turn` records, and the trajectory ledger** — prompt caching can be 90% of an agent loop's cost story and reasoning tokens are the silent budget eater on thinking models, and after 0.4.0's cached-read accounting the two remaining classes were still flattened away. `Usage` gains `cache_write_tokens` and `reasoning_tokens` (`#[serde(default)]`, `0` = not reported — the crate-wide convention, one shape with the shipped `cached_input_tokens`), with `with_cache_write`/`with_reasoning` builders mirroring `with_cached_input`, and each adapter fills them from its native wire keys on both response paths: OpenAI-compatible `completion_tokens_details.reasoning_tokens` (lenient like the cached count — a non-numeric placeholder from an OpenAI-compatible server reads as zero, never drops the report), Anthropic `cache_creation_input_tokens` (non-streaming reader and the streaming `message_start`→`message_delta` latch, max-merged per field like the read share), Gemini `cachedContentTokenCount` (**previously unparsed in both paths** — the cached share vanished on Gemini) and `thoughtsTokenCount` (already folded into the output count; now exposed as the reasoning subset), and Bedrock's matching cache classes on both wire shapes. **One latent fix rides the widening:** Bedrock Converse reports `cacheReadInputTokens`/`cacheWriteInputTokens` *outside* `inputTokens` — the same shape as the Anthropic wire — but `converse_usage` passed the raw input through un-normalized, so a cached Converse turn reported the uncached remainder as its prompt total (the same latent bug, on the Bedrock wire); the arm now normalizes input to raw + read + write like every Anthropic-shape conversion, proven red-first (`converse_usage_reports_cache_classes` failed at `left: 100, right: 1050` on the pre-fix tree). The per-family total rule is pinned on both sides of the oracle trap — `anthropic_totals_compose_without_double_counting` (raw 100 + read 900 + creation 50 → the conversation total *is* `input_tokens` at 1050) and `openai_totals_compose_without_double_counting` (prompt 1000 with cached 900 → the total *is* 1000) — so a consumer never sums the detail fields on top of the input. **Propagation:** the detail rides `StreamContext` (per request, alongside its now-documented cache-aware `input_tokens` total), `TurnEndContext` (per turn, through the engine's `TurnAccounting`/`TurnEnd` plumbing and the `Turn` record `handle_call_tools` reads back), and `RunEndContext` gains a full five-field run usage block (`input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, and the three classes as saturating sums over the run's turns) with a `with_usage_totals` builder for synthetic events. The trajectory ledger joins in: `TrajectoryTurn` carries the per-turn classes (old JSONL lines deserialize with zeroed detail) and `TokenSummary`'s reserved `Option` fields finally populate — `None` remains only as the pre-detail-ledger deserialize value. `MockApiClient::with_usage(Usage)` replaces the fixed `(50, 25)` pair at both serve paths so fixtures can stage any detail combination, still gated by `without_usage()`. Pinned per surface: the provider wire set (`parse_final_chunk_with_reasoning_tokens_detail`, `non_streaming_usage_reports_reasoning_detail`, `absent_completion_details_default_to_zero`, `a_non_numeric_reasoning_count_defaults_to_zero_without_dropping_usage`, `build_response_reports_cache_write_tokens`, `emitter_merges_cache_write_across_start_and_delta`, `emitter_keeps_latched_cache_write_when_delta_omits_it`, `extract_usage_reports_cached_and_reasoning_detail`, `streaming_latch_carries_cached_and_reasoning_detail`, `reasoning_is_inside_output_not_added` — the Gemini fold guard: output stays candidates + thoughts with the reasoning share a subset, `anthropic_shape_usage_reports_cache_write`), the serde set (`old_serialized_usage_deserializes` covering both the pre-detail and the 0.4.0-era blobs, `old_turn_json_deserializes_with_zero_detail`, `old_ledger_json_deserializes`), and the engine propagation set over the staged mock (`turn_end_carries_usage_detail`, `stream_context_carries_the_detail`, `run_end_reports_usage_totals`, `token_summary_populates_from_usage_detail`, `per_turn_records_carry_usage_detail`). **Migration (the three breaks this window absorbs):** `StreamContext` and `TrajectoryTurn` join the `#[non_exhaustive]` set with constructors — `StreamContext::new(turn, model, input, output)` + `with_usage_detail(cached, write, reasoning)` (external struct literals stop compiling; consumer-side test helpers migrate), and `TrajectoryTurn::new(turn, query, response_text)` + `with_tool_calls`/`with_duration_ms`/`with_usage`. The third break is a rename, not a widening: **`Turn.turn` is now `Turn.index`** — the field the detail fields sit beside read as a counter beside `input`/`output` and paid for the ambiguity; host code that referenced `turn.turn` must say `turn.index`. The wire does not move: the field keeps serializing as `turn` (`#[serde(rename = "turn")]`), so records written before the rename — `Turn` blobs, session records, anything storing the struct — deserialize into the renamed field unchanged, and the old shape pin (`old_turn_json_deserializes_with_zero_detail`) proves it. `TurnEndContext`/`RunEndContext`/`Usage` widen additively under their existing markers, `Turn` widens additively on top of the rename, and every old serialized shape — `Usage`, `Turn`, ledger lines, session records — deserializes unchanged. Unreported ≠ zero is documented at the type: cost displays must treat a `0` detail class as no data, never a measured zero. The Anthropic `message_delta` fold now adds the delta's detail tokens to the start usage instead of max-ing totals, so a cache-write share that arrives only mid-stream survives (`anthropic_stream_merge_folds_a_revised_share_into_the_input_total`), and the fold identity the `Usage` doc states is swept over generated counts rather than hoped for (`a_generated_counts_sweep_holds_the_anthropic_fold_identity`) — and it survives the defensive shape where a terminal delta revises the raw input while omitting the cache fields, the merged total carrying the latched shares on top of the revised raw (`anthropic_stream_delta_revising_input_carries_the_latched_shares_into_the_fold`, with the generated sweep drawing that shape too); the Gemini detail latch max-merges `cachedContentTokenCount`/`thoughtsTokenCount` so a cumulative final report that omits the detail keys can no longer zero a latched share (`a_final_gemini_report_omitting_detail_keeps_the_latched_share`), both Gemini paths saturate counts above `u32::MAX` identically (`oversized_gemini_counts_saturate_identically_on_both_paths`), and the subset invariants sweep too (`a_generated_counts_sweep_holds_the_gemini_subset_invariants`); the `with_usage` × `without_usage` precedence the mock documents is pinned (`with_usage_and_without_usage_together_serve_no_usage`); the detail reaches the turn record and both observer contexts over the `create_message` transport, pinned in a build with no streaming feature at all (`usage_detail_propagates_on_the_non_streaming_path`); cached-then-uncached turns sum exactly into the run-end totals, which equal the sum of the turn records (`run_end_totals_sum_heterogeneous_turn_usage`), and a failed run reports only its recorded turns there — the failed request closes its turn with nothing (`a_failed_run_reports_only_the_recorded_turns_in_its_totals`); the fold and subset identities ride generated-count sweeps on every family, not just the two that started with them (`a_generated_counts_sweep_holds_the_openai_subset_invariants`, `a_generated_counts_sweep_holds_the_bedrock_fold_identity`); and the budget gate charges the input total the real `converse_usage` transform produces for a cached Converse report — 1 090 against a 1 000 line, where any narrower reading of the report would sail past (`cached_converse_usage_charges_the_budget_gate_the_normalized_total`, on the Bedrock seam).

- **`provider::self_hosted_builder` — one preset for self-hosted OpenAI-compatible servers, behind the new `self_hosted` feature** — llama.cpp, vLLM, LM Studio, NVIDIA NIM, and every bring-your-own-server deployment speaks the OpenAI chat-completions wire, and until now each host hand-rolled the three facts that make one work: a base URL, a key the server ignores, and the `stream_options.include_usage` parameter that self-hosted servers reject more often than they honor. `self_hosted_builder(model)` seeds all three — `http://localhost:8080/v1` (llama.cpp's default port; `SELF_HOSTED_BASE_URL` overrides, as does `with_base_url`), an API key the server does not require — always the placeholder `"self-hosted"`: the preset is placeholder-only by design and reads no key environment at all, so an exported `OPENAI_API_KEY` (a credential for another host) never rides to an endpoint the user points it at, while the shipped `self_hosted()` constructor keeps its own `OPENAI_API_KEY`-or-placeholder rule (pass `with_api_key` when a gateway checks one), and `with_stream_usage(false)` (flip back on for a server that reports usage) — the exact shape of `ollama_builder`, its sibling in the per-family preset row (`self_hosted = ["providers", "openai"]`). Pinned by `self_hosted_builder_seeds_defaults_and_env_override` (default endpoint, model, and the `SELF_HOSTED_BASE_URL` override), `self_hosted_builder_overrides_beat_the_seeded_environment` (the explicit `with_base_url` outranks the ambient variable, knob layering leaves the seeding intact), and at the wire by header capture: `the_preset_sends_the_placeholder_key_on_the_wire` (the builder's call site carries the placeholder) and `the_preset_never_carries_the_ambient_openai_key_to_a_self_hosted_endpoint` (with `OPENAI_API_KEY` exported, the captured request still shows only the placeholder — the ambient credential never leaves for a self-hosted host).

- **Reasoning-effort ladder widened to the wire's seven rungs — `ThinkingEffort` gains `None`, `Minimal`, and `Xhigh`, the OpenAI family's labels ride verbatim, and a per-request `None` expresses off** — both provenance probes of the field (a local vLLM-style server and Z.AI's OpenAI-protocol endpoint) validate `reasoning_effort` as `none, minimal, low, medium, high, xhigh, max`, while the shipped ladder offered four rungs and **clamped `Max` → `"high"`** on the OpenAI family — a silent downgrade between the config a user writes and the request the wire carries. The clamp is gone: each level's label is its own name, verbatim, and a provider whose parameter tops out lower (stock OpenAI accepts neither `"xhigh"` nor `"max"`) answers with its own error, which surfaces the mismatch instead of quietly downgrading the run — the honest 400 beats the quiet substitution. **`None` is the explicit off** (a true per-request off was once said to need a future breaking window; none was needed — `#[non_exhaustive]` admitted the rung additively): OpenAI carries `"none"`, the Messages API is told `thinking: {"type": "disabled"}` — neither the budget nor its `max_tokens` headroom raise — and Gemini carries its own off sentinel `thinkingBudget: 0` (the one sentinel value on the ladder; `-1` stays off it) — **with the limit documented, not papered over: that sentinel switches thinking off only on a model that accepts it, and a model with no off level (Gemini 2.5 Pro lists `low`/`medium`/`high`) answers a `0` budget with its own error**, the client mapping the level and leaving the model's support to the provider, as it does for an OpenAI label an endpoint tops out below. The budget tables extend to match: Anthropic `Minimal` 1024 (the API's documented floor for an enabled block) and `Xhigh` 24576 beside `Low`/`Medium`/`High`/`Max` at 2048/8192/16384/32768; Gemini `Minimal` 512 and `Xhigh` 20480 beside 1024/8192/16384/24576 — numbers stay the crate's chosen table defaults, never invented per model. A per-request level still wins over a client default (`options.effort.or(client)` — `Some(None)` included), so the off rung disables a configured default for one call. Unset stays unset: no default-request byte changes. Pinned per provider — `the_ladder_s_outer_rungs_reach_the_openai_wire` rewritten to assert `"xhigh"`, `"none"`, and `"max"` verbatim with a negative probe against the old `"high"` clamp; `the_wider_rungs_reach_the_anthropic_wire` (new) pins `Minimal` 1024, `Xhigh` 24576, and a per-request `None` overriding a `High` client default by stating `disabled` in place of the budget; `the_ladder_s_outer_rungs_reach_the_gemini_wire` extended with `Minimal` 512 and `None` 0 and `the_off_sentinel_reaches_a_model_with_no_off_level` (new) pinning that the mapped sentinel rides the wire whatever model the request names — every pre-existing effort pin green unchanged, and effort-less clients (Bedrock) still reject a set level through the standing trait-default gate.

- **Anthropic thinking control follows the model's own disclosure — the Models API capability probe** — a request that resolves a thinking effort now reads `GET /v1/models/{model}` once per model and writes the thinking control *that model* accepts, instead of one spelling for every model on the endpoint: `thinking: {"type":"disabled"}` where `disabled` is accepted (unchanged), `{"type":"between_tools"}` on a model that refuses `disabled` and discloses that lower setting (Claude Sonnet 5.5), and `output_config: {"effort": <rung>}` on a model that refuses `budget_tokens` and takes thinking depth from effort — Anthropic's adaptive tier (Opus 4.7 and later, Sonnet 5 and later, Haiku 5.5, Fable, Mythos), with the caller's `max_tokens` restored when the headroom raise had been made for a budget the model cannot take. A model whose disclosure refuses `disabled` with no accepted alternative is **refused up front** with the model named rather than sent a known 400. `Minimal` keeps its own label on the effort ladder (`"minimal"`) and the API answers it — the verbatim-label rule [`ThinkingEffort`](src/structured.rs) already states, not a clamp. The read is soft and cached end to end, and it distinguishes what an endpoint *said* from what it merely failed to deliver: a **definitive** answer — a success, a non-JSON success (a gateway in front of the API), or a `404`/`405` saying there is no Models API at that base URL — is filed under the model asked about, while an **inconclusive** one (transport failure or timeout, `401`/`403`, `429`, any `5xx`, a body that could not be read within the response cap) is filed nowhere: that request takes the ladder's own mapping and the next request asks again — but the streak is counted, and a route that fails `CAPABILITY_PROBE_MISS_LIMIT` (3) probes in a row **runs on** *disclosed nothing*, so a scoped key that answers `401` on `/v1/models` while serving `/v1/messages`, or a metadata route that never responds, costs three probes rather than a probe — or its full 5-second timeout — ahead of every turn of the run. Runs, and is not filed: that reading is this client's inference rather than the endpoint's answer, so it expires — after `CAPABILITY_PROBE_REPROBE_AFTER` (32) turns served on it the route is asked again, which is one probe per 29 turns for a route that stays dead and an endpoint that came back believed within a turn or two of ordinary use, instead of an adaptive-tier model left being sent the budget spelling it refuses for the life of the client. An answer filed later outranks the streak: a blip, including one from a probe that lost a race to a request that got an answer, can never overwrite a disclosure. Filing a blip would have been the difference between a soft fallback and a stuck one — nothing clears a filed answer, so one lost round trip on the metadata route would have kept sending `budget_tokens` to an adaptive-tier model for the rest of the client's life, 400 on 400. The cache is keyed **per model** rather than one slot, so a `RequestOptions::model` override or a fallback chain gets its own disclosure for one probe each instead of a probe per request, entries are never flushed by `set_model` (an answer about a model is still true of that model), and a 32-model bound holds the map for a host handed a new model name per request. A request with **no effort never probes** — its wire stays byte-identical and it pays no extra call. The native-thinking gate widens from "the rung budgets" to "the rung is above the off rung", so signed thinking blocks ride back on the effort path as they already did under a budget. Pinned by `an_adaptive_model_gets_output_config_effort_instead_of_a_budget`, `the_minimal_rung_names_itself_on_the_effort_ladder`, `a_model_that_refuses_disabled_is_turned_off_at_its_lowest_setting`, `a_model_with_no_off_level_refuses_the_off_rung_before_sending`, `a_model_that_refuses_disabled_without_an_off_alternative_refuses`, `an_endpoint_without_a_models_api_keeps_the_budget_ladder`, `the_probe_asks_the_models_api_once_with_the_client_credential`, `a_model_change_rereads_the_disclosure`, `an_effort_less_request_never_probes_the_models_api`, and — for the caching split — `a_transient_probe_failure_is_asked_again_on_the_next_request`, `a_probe_that_fails_once_recovers_on_the_next_turn` (the 503 turn carries the budget ladder, the next turn carries `output_config.effort`), `a_definitive_404_is_filed_so_the_endpoint_is_asked_once`, `an_endpoint_that_never_answers_stops_being_probed`, and `a_conclusive_answer_clears_the_miss_streak`, with the verdict's bound and its renewal behind `the_capability_cache_renews_its_give_up_verdict_on_a_bound`, `a_recovered_route_takes_over_the_given_up_verdict`, and `a_route_that_recovers_after_the_give_up_verdict_reaches_the_wire`; every pre-existing ladder, replay, and conflict pin now runs behind a listener that answers the probe with `404` and stays green untouched. **Migration:** a host that pinned an adaptive-tier model and took a `budget_tokens` 400 gets a working `output_config.effort` request; a host that pinned the off rung on a model with no off level gets a client-side `config_validation` refusal naming the model instead of a provider 400. `thinking.types.enabled` and `thinking.types.adaptive` are not documented capability members: they are read opportunistically and treated as undisclosed when absent, which leaves the ladder's mapping in charge.

- **Price tables and cost stamping — a vendored, dated table of per-family rates, micro-dollar cost math over the usage superset, capture-time stamps on the event ledger, and a USD budget line that spends only what a table prices** — the cost plane had opinions (budgets, burn displays, `cost` rollups) but no price source: the budget gate's own docs said it "refuses to guess a cost", and a configured `budgets.cost_usd` mapped to nothing. [`pricing`](src/pricing.rs) is that source, always compiled with no new dependency (data + math, the curated-metadata precedent): a vendored `curated_prices.json` (`table_version` + `pricing_updated` + per-family `input`/`output`/optional `cache_read`/`cache_write` columns in USD per million tokens) parsed once behind `PriceTable::curated()` — seeded by converting **OpenRouter's public models listing** on the table's own date — 1 042 rows over 345 canonical models and 54 providers (the gpt-6 line, the Claude 5.x line, Gemini 3.8, DeepSeek v4.1, Grok 4.5, and the long tail), so the out-of-box table prices what people actually run on every provider, not two — and the refresh is no longer hand-maintenance: **`PriceTable::from_openrouter(listing, date)`** converts the listing offline and deterministically (per-token prices scaled once to per-million; every kept model under its bare id *and* its `vendor/model` spelling, so first-party and OpenRouter clients both resolve; dotted spellings carrying their dashed aliases — OpenRouter writes `anthropic/claude-opus-5.5`, Anthropic's own ids read `claude-opus-5-5-…`; `:free`/`:batch` variants and `~` aliases skipped as different products, not estimates; base-tier pricing with the long-context `overrides` caveat documented), **`to_json()`** renders the interchange format back, and the `import_openrouter_prices` example wires the two into the refresh ritual — `curl https://openrouter.ai/api/v1/models | cargo run --example import_openrouter_prices -- --date <date> > src/pricing/curated_prices.json`, diff reviewed like goldens; the fetch stays a deliberate act and the vendored file stays pinned, dated data, so replayed stamps remain comparable across refreshes — pinned by `openrouter_import_skips_variants_and_dual_keys_each_model` and `openrouter_round_trip_through_the_interchange_format`. **Runtime freshness for hosts** closes the consumer gap the baked table leaves — a long-lived binary wants day-fresh prices without a rebuild, and the fetch is the host's policy, never the library's: [`curated_with_cache(dir)`](src/pricing.rs) is the ladder's read half (the cache directory's files merge over the baked table in name order; a missing directory is the first-run norm; an unreadable or corrupt file is skipped with a warning naming it while the rest still applies — the ladder never fails a boot), [`today_iso()`](src/pricing.rs) stamps a runtime fetch with the day it actually happened (std offers no civil-date rendering; the inverse `epoch_days_to_iso` ships beside it), and the module docs state the whole consumer pattern — fetch through the host's HTTP stack, convert, install into gate and ledger, persist with `to_json` for the next offline boot, one effective table per session so a run's stamps name a single vintage. Pinned by `curated_with_cache_layers_a_cache_over_the_baked_table`, `curated_with_cache_never_fails_a_boot`, and `today_iso_round_trips_through_the_date_parser` (leap days included). **The nightly drift canary** keeps the vendored table honest between release cuts: [`same_prices`](src/pricing.rs) compares two tables by rows alone (vintage ignored, order-insensitive — a date-only difference is *unchanged*, so no PR without a real price or family move), the importer's `--check <file>` mode is the probe built on it (exit `0` no drift / `3` drift with the fresh table on stdout / `1` the fence — a conversion landing zero rows or dropping more than half the checked table's rows, counted through [`row_count`](src/pricing.rs) with every spelling counted, refuses to publish, so a broken listing fails the nightly instead of opening a PR that deletes prices; pinned by `row_count_counts_every_spelling`), and `.github/workflows/price-drift.yml` runs it nightly — fetch the listing, convert, compare, and on drift push `chore/price-drift` and open (or update) one standing refresh PR for human review; auto-detection, deliberate merge. The curated pins are data-robust: the seed's tests assert structure (a well-formed `openrouter-<date>` identity, no variant or alias ids, every prefixed key carrying its bare twin) rather than any particular family's numbers, a staleness ratchet fails master if the table goes `STALE_AFTER_DAYS` without any refresh, and the value-level doctest moved onto a fixture table so a routine price change cannot redden the canary's own PR. Pinned by `same_prices_ignores_vintage_and_detects_real_drift` and `the_curated_seed_holds_a_well_formed_openrouter_table`; override files in the same format load from a directory in filename order (`load_overrides_dir`, the `prices.d/` shape) and merge with `with_overrides` — exact-key replace, new keys add, version composes `base+override`, `pricing_updated` takes the freshest contributor so staleness tracks the data actually in use. Lookup is case-insensitive longest-prefix after stripping Bedrock's regional and provider spellings (`us.anthropic.claude-sonnet-4-5-…` inherits the family), so dated and regional ids price like the bare family. Cost math splits the reported usage superset — input, output, the cache-read share, the cache-write share — into its priced classes — uncached remainder at the base rate, cached reads at the read rate, cache writes at the write premium, output at the output rate — with a missing cache column pricing its class at the base input rate (the error bends toward overcharging, never a discount the table didn't state); money is **micro-dollars (`u64`) end to end**, the one decimal boundary being the parse-time scaling of published per-million rates, so no float drift ever lands in a stamp. **Unknown model → `None`, never a guess** — `price()` and `cost()` both — and every [`CostBreakdown`](src/pricing.rs) carries the priced family, the table version, and the pricing date, so a historical rollup reports the prices its runs actually ran under. Staleness is data, not silence: `days_stale`/`is_stale` measure ISO-date distance with `STALE_AFTER_DAYS = 90`, an unparseable as-of never guesses stale, and the vendored loader's failure mode is an error-logged empty epoch-dated table (everything unpriced, maximally stale) rather than a panic. **Capture stamping:** [`EventLedgerObserver`](src/memory/trajectory/events.rs) gains `with_price_table` — it remembers the served model from `on_stream_success` (observed, no line of its own) and stamps each `turn.ended` line's `data` with `cost: {per-class micro-dollars, model, table_version, pricing_updated, stale}` or `cost: null` plus a `cost_note` naming the unpriced model (or any turn whose model call never succeeded — the remembered model is cleared at every turn start, so a failed turn after a served one prices nothing rather than under the previous turn's model); without a table the line carries no cost field at all — the ledger's old shape byte-identical. **The USD budget line:** `BudgetDimension::Usd` joins tokens/turns/wall-clock — accrued by `BudgetGate::observe_cost(model, usage)`, which the engine now calls beside the token charge on every successful turn, pricing through the gate's installed table (`with_price_table`); a table-less gate never accrues and never refuses (the no-guess doctrine, now enforced by a table rather than by absence), the manifest's `budgets.cost_usd` finally maps somewhere (the `usd` limit), and the dimension's `u64` unit is micro-dollars — the same dimension-specific-unit pattern wall-clock uses with milliseconds, so `BudgetWarn`/`HardCrossing` keep their shape. Pinned by `the_curated_seed_holds_a_well_formed_openrouter_table` (structural invariants over the real seed — version identity, no variant or alias ids, every prefixed key carrying its bare twin — deliberately not any family's numbers), `unknown_model_costs_none_never_a_guess`, `cost_math_splits_the_usage_superset` (Anthropic's read-tenth/write-premium shape, OpenAI's halved-read shape, and the missing-column fallback each asserted to the micro-dollar — neutralization-proven: collapsing the cache split turns it red), `prefix_resolution_strips_bedrock_spellings`, `staleness_is_days_between_iso_dates` (the 89/90/91 boundary) and `invalid_calendar_dates_reject_instead_of_rolling_forward` (`2026-02-31` and friends reject, never roll into March), `overrides_replace_and_add_rows_and_redate`, `override_dir_loads_deterministically_and_names_bad_files`, `micro_usd_scales_exactly_and_saturates` (infinity is no ceiling, NaN is zero), `usd_line_charges_warns_and_refuses_in_micro_dollars`, `usd_line_without_a_table_never_accrues`, `unpriced_models_and_unreported_usage_accrue_nothing`, `manifest_cost_usd_maps_to_the_usd_line`, `a_negative_or_nan_cost_budget_maps_to_no_usd_line` (nonsense drops loudly, never arming the zero-refusal), `a_direct_negative_usd_limit_reads_as_zero_and_refuses_the_first_request` (the hand-set field's documented contract), `budget_gate_charges_cost_from_reported_usage` (the engine pin: three scripted `gpt-4o` turns at 375µ each against a $0.0011 line, the fourth request refused with `BudgetExhausted{usd, 1125 of 1100}` — removing the engine's `observe_cost` call turns it red), and the ledger pair `cost_stamped_at_capture_with_table_version` / `unknown_model_costs_null_with_warning_not_a_guess`, with `stale_table_flags_its_stamps`, `a_table_less_ledger_writes_no_cost_field_at_all`, `a_turn_without_a_served_model_says_so_instead_of_guessing`, and `a_failed_turn_after_a_served_one_costs_null_not_the_previous_model` beside them. **Migration (two additive-with-marker breaks, riding the window this release already opened):** `BudgetDimension` gains `Usd` and `BudgetLimits` gains `usd: Option<f64>`; both types are now `#[non_exhaustive]` so future widening is additive — exhaustive external matches add a `_` arm, and external `BudgetLimits` construction goes through `BudgetLimits::new()` (or `default()`) followed by setting the public fields, since struct literals — with or without `..` — compile only inside the crate. Refreshing the vendored table is a release-cut ritual: regenerate through the importer or hand-write this interchange shape (cost maps in other layouts do not parse — convert them first), and bump its `table_version`/`pricing_updated`.

- **Determinism test fixtures behind `testing` — `StepClock`, `CapturingObserver`, and the comparable `CapturedEvent` log it records** — the determinism seams made the engine's reported artifacts functions of the installed clock, id generator, and retry rng, but spending that property inside a test still meant hand-rolling a stepping clock per file and a bespoke recorder per callback. [`StepClock`](src/testing/determinism.rs) is the promoted engine-test private: a [`Clock`](src/determinism.rs) whose monotonic half advances a fixed step per read, so every duration the engine measures through the seam is an exact multiple of that step. [`CapturingObserver`](src/testing/determinism.rs) implements the whole `LoopObserver` surface by projecting each callback's engine-sourced fields — ids, durations, token counts, stop reasons, model names, error text — into a `CapturedEvent` log that implements equality, so two engines built from the same seeds can be asserted equal event for event; the projection deliberately leaves out the three fields that have no equality (a turn-end's history snapshot, a compaction's deep telemetry — its duration is carried — and a stream failure's typed error, carried as its rendered text), which the type's docs state. The fixtures are the on-ramp for a host replay suite: what pinned loopctl's own suites here is exactly what a cassette differencing consumer will want to build on. The engine's own scenario pins widen onto them: `same_seed_engines_emit_identical_engine_sourced_callback_streams` (two same-seed engines, one step clock, identical captured streams — session ids, durations, token pairs, stop reasons alike), `pinned_clock_zeroes_every_engine_reported_duration` (a `FixedClock` run spanning a model turn, a tool turn on the default-installed pipeline, and a compaction pass: every duration reaching observers is exactly zero), `stepping_clock_durations_are_exact_step_multiples` (turn-end — both phases — run-end, tool-post, and compaction durations are positive exact multiples of the 7 ms step), `session_and_run_id_sequences_match_a_fresh_oracle_per_scenario` (fallback-switch, cancellation-mid-tool, compaction-turn, and multi-run scripts each mint exactly the fresh same-seed oracle's sequence — session id first, then each run's id in order), `seeded_rng_pins_the_transport_retry_ladder` (handler transport retries under a `SeededRng` sleep the delays a fresh same-seed rng predicts, attempt for attempt, observed through tokio's paused clock and asserted up to its documented millisecond sleep rounding), and `gate_decision_timestamps_come_from_the_clock_seam` (the engine's one read of the clock's wall half — the timestamp stamp on permission-gate decision records — carries the installed clock's frozen wall, not the real system clock's). Each pin is neutralization-proven: reverting the pipeline re-stamp, a turn-start clock read, the session-id mint, the ladder's rng draw, or the gate stamp's wall read turns exactly the pin that watches it red.
