The seam docs match: the `Clock` trait, the determinism module doc, and `StepClock` name the engine's one wall read — the gate stamp — where they previously said the engine never reads the wall half. The `tokio` dev-dependency gains `test-util` for the paused-clock ladder pin — dev-dependencies never reach a consumer's build. No production behavior changed; the two engine pins that previously used the file-private `StepClock` now run under `cargo test --all-features` instead of a bare `cargo test`.

- **Metrics collectors behind `metrics` — `MetricsLayer` stores the crate's own telemetry events, `MetricsRegistry` keeps the validated series, and `MetricsObserver` fills the snapshot from callbacks** — the value types shipped with a contract and no official consumer: emissions went nowhere by default, and building `AgentMetrics` from `LoopObserver` was left as an exercise. [`MetricsLayer`](src/metrics/layer.rs) is a `tracing_subscriber` layer on the reserved metrics target that classifies every event by the contract: `metric` names the series (an unknown name is counted drift and stores nothing), string fields become labels **only from the descriptor's closed set, in both directions** (surplus keys are counted and dropped; an emission **missing** a declared key is refused outright and counted, so a partial label set can never fork a series beside the declared shape; `model` and the correlation-id fields are contract-exempt, read from the one `METRIC_EXEMPT_FIELDS` list the contract test shares, so classifier and test cannot diverge), and the numeric fields resolve to exactly one scalar — an explicit `value` field, else a field named like the metric's unit tail, else a lone number — with a multi-quantity ambiguity counted as drift, never guessed; wide integers store identically direct and `%`-rendered, past `u32::MAX` or not. [`MetricsRegistry`](src/metrics/store.rs) is where classified events land: counters add, gauges replace, histograms bucket (rendering to nearest, ties to even, negatives clamped), `snapshot` reads the stored series — every one of them, each reported with the kind its stored value holds (the descriptor's declared kind on the layer path), so a write the store accepted can never vanish from a scrape — `derived` answers the declared ratios (`compaction.never_failed`; `memory.demotion_roi` from the `memory.stored[origin=demoted]` numerator the label vocabulary actually supports), and the drift diagnostics — unknown names, surplus labels, missing declared labels, numerics on counters, ambiguous scalars — tell a conformant host from a drifting one; a write onto a foreign-kind series follows the documented rule (counter/histogram no-op, gauge replaces) and is pinned to it. [`MetricsObserver`](src/metrics/observer.rs) implements `LoopObserver` into `AgentMetrics` one run at a time: each `on_run_start` opens a fresh snapshot, so a reused observer reports per-run records without anyone resetting it between runs — turn-end carries the per-turn counts, run boundaries record only what no turn knew — a mid-run read is a live dashboard and a run-end read that run's completed record. Concurrent runs cannot share one observer: the callback surface carries no run identity (the session id is stable across a loop's runs by contract), so hosts running concurrent engines attach one observer each. The feature now depends on `tracing-subscriber` (`registry` + `std`, default-features off); `default = []` is unchanged. Pinned by `emission_catalog_contract` (real subsystems drive events; every captured emission validated field-by-field against the catalog), `metrics_layer_e2e` (`every_gauge_and_histogram_surface_accumulates`: every declared gauge/histogram surface stores, engine counters keep their declared labels, drift stays zero), `missing_declared_labels_are_refused_and_counted` (fail-first on the forked partial-label series; neutralization-proven — removing the refusal stores the fork and reddens the pin), `hand_rolled_descriptors_snapshot_with_their_written_shape` (fail-first on the silently dropped series), and the observer's multi-run pins (per-run isolation at each run start, exact reset boundary).

- **The span tree of a run — `tracing_spans` records runs, turns, and tool calls as `DEBUG` spans in `OTel` GenAI shape** — an engine run so far narrated itself as flat events; a host wanting a trace had to reconstruct parentage from ids. [`tracing_spans`](src/tracing_spans.rs) opens an `agent.run` root carrying run identity and outcome, an `agent.turn` per settled turn carrying duration, outcome, and the `gen_ai.usage.*` token figures — cache and reasoning detail recorded **by presence, not zero**, so "the provider said nothing" cannot read as "nothing was cached" — and an `agent.tool` per dispatched call; a tool's turn has already closed when dispatch runs, so tools sit beside turns under the run root, and exporters see both as top-level observations of the trace. Error classes ride a bounded `error_type` vocabulary (shapes the classifier does not name collapse to `"error"` — ten current variants among them, enumerated in the doc and pinned variant by variant). The tree is free when nobody listens: `tracing` never evaluates a disabled callsite's fields, and the turn's input text — the one field that can copy a whole tool result — is computed once for the turn-start observers and recorded onto the span from that same string, after steering, so no second walk of the history happens for the span's sake and the span and the observer can never disagree about what a steered turn's input was. Pinned by [`tests/tracing_spans.rs`](tests/tracing_spans.rs): names, targets, field spellings, and parentage — run root, turns under run, tools under run — a **failed run** (run-span `outcome=error` plus its bounded `error.type`), a **mid-run-steered turn** (the span's `query` equals the turn-start observer's query — the post-drain string; neutralization-proven: removing the run's `record_turn_query` call empties the span field and reddens the pin), the INFO-filter scenario (spans are DEBUG, so an INFO subscriber keeps only the events), and the no-subscriber completion scenario.

- **Two workspace companions export the telemetry — `loopctl-otel` and `loopctl-langfuse`** — the core emits, these deliver, and neither re-exports core (both depend on `loopctl` by `path = "../.."` pinned `=0.4.1`, so host and companion resolve one copy of the types). [`loopctl-otel`](crates/otel/) wraps the `tracing-opentelemetry` bridge with the pipeline loopctl's targets need: `install()` composes the layer, an `OTLP` exporter, and a `loopctl=DEBUG` target filter onto the global subscriber **all-or-nothing** — the only fallible step (claiming the global) runs before any `OTel` global moves, so a rejected second install leaves the first installation's providers and guard exactly as they were — and returns a `ShutdownGuard` whose `force_flush` is the checked flush; `otel_layer()` is the composable shape for hosts that own their subscriber, with `OtelTracingLayer::shutdown` tearing their providers down. Metrics travel two ways: `Pipeline` mode replays the registry snapshot into the `OTLP` pipeline with the delta ledger keyed per **label series** — every label series of a name tracks its own cumulative, so one series' growth can never swallow another's — and `SpanAttributes` mode stamps the run rollup onto the closing run span instead. The stamp is written at the **run-end record** — inside the run span's scope, after that run's observer callbacks — so in a multi-run process each run span carries its own run's numbers, never the flush-time session total; hosts composing manually add it with `OtelTracingLayer::with_stamper`. [`loopctl-langfuse`](crates/langfuse/) batches closed spans into ingestion `trace-create`/`span-create`/`score-create` envelopes for Langfuse's ingestion API (Basic auth, idempotent event ids so retries are safe, bounded exponential backoff, 4xx refused loudly, the 207 multi-status and 429 answers read and retried, queue bounded with counted evict-oldest); `install()`/`install_simple()` return the layer plus a guard whose drop waits for the drain — and because a `Drop` cannot `await`, `LangfuseGuard::flush(timeout)` is the cooperative drain for guards dropped from a thread the runtime drives. Pinned by `otel_export` (in-memory exporter: rename/kind/status mapping, metric-target events never mirrored as span events, a failing tool's `Status::error`, and per-run rollup stamps across two runs sharing one flush), the per-series delta ledger by `counter_deltas_track_each_label_series_apart` (three flush cycles, two label series, in the crate's unit tests), `install_globals` (a second install is rejected with the first install's globals untouched), and `langfuse_export` (mock HTTP collector: envelope contract, parenting, auto-scored runs, auth header, and a failed run's trace closing at level `ERROR`).

- **Hook follow-ups — `Hook::on_run_followup` and `PostRunAction::FollowUp`: one engine-injected turn after a clean run** — a run that completes without error may be extended by exactly one more turn, offered through the hook executor: each hook's `on_run_followup` sees the run-end context, the first `Some` wins (a `None` is a pass to the next hook, not a veto — the same short-circuit the pre-tool consult applies, with no decline-for-everyone power), and the winning `FollowUp { prompt, origin }` drives its prompt as the run's **next turn** — same run record, same session, one `on_run_start`/`on_run_end` pair, `origin` (`"hook:<name>"` by convention) recorded on `Run::followup_origin` for hosts and records to read. The injected turn is a continuation, not a restarted run: it indexes as the run's next turn (`LoopMachine::accept_followup` reopens the cleanly completed machine preserving the turn counter), spends from the same `max_turns` budget (a spent budget is offered nothing — the consult itself is gated on room remaining), and is prepared and recorded under the follow-up prompt — memory retrieval, contributors, and the turn's record see the hook's prompt, never the original run input. The prior answer is provisional from the moment a follow-up is accepted: it is cleared from `Run::output` before the injected turn drives, so a failed follow-up fails the run with its own error and no superseded text standing in as the output. The consult disarms for the injected turn's own end — a hook whose follow-up also completes cleanly is not asked again, so a hook that injects turns is a recursion primitive bounded by the engine, not by each policy's care. Pinned end to end by `a_followup_hook_extends_a_clean_run_by_one_turn`, `the_injected_turn_is_never_offered_its_own_followup`, `a_failing_run_is_not_offered_a_followup`, `the_first_hook_to_accept_wins_and_stops_the_consult`, `a_run_without_an_executor_behaves_as_before`, and — against the review findings this fix round closed — `the_injected_turn_continues_the_run_wide_turn_sequence` (indexes `[0, 1]`, turn 1 recorded under the follow-up prompt, both turns inside one shared budget), `an_exhausted_turn_budget_is_not_offered_a_followup`, and `a_failed_followup_leaves_no_output_on_the_run_record` (each proven red against the pre-fix `accept_input`-reset wiring); machine-level by `accept_followup_continues_the_turn_sequence_within_the_shared_budget`.

### Fixed

- **Telemetry ratios keep their magnitudes past `u32::MAX`** — every `u64 → f64` conversion on the metrics paths saturated at `u32::MAX` before dividing, so a ratio whose operands straddle that ceiling read wrong: 1e9 cached of 5e9 input reported `≈ 0.233` instead of `0.2`, and unequal wide totals both flattened onto one ceiling (`MetricsRegistry::derived` collapsing to `0.0`/`1.0`). The whole family — `TokenTotals::cache_hit_rate`/`reasoning_share`, `ToolStats::error_rate`, the registry's derived ratios, `loopctl-otel`'s run-gauge and replay/diagnostic conversions, and `loopctl-langfuse`'s score conversions — now composes the value from its two 32-bit limbs with one fused rounding: exact through 2^53, correctly rounded beyond. Pinned by `ratios_stay_true_when_one_operand_passes_u32_max`, `error_rate_keeps_its_ratio_past_u32_max`, `derived_ratios_keep_their_magnitudes_past_u32_max`, `wide_integers_keep_their_magnitude`, and the wide-turns leg of `pipeline_exports_registry_series_to_the_meter`.

- **Out-of-order histogram wire bounds keep their counts** — deserializing a `LatencyHistogram` sorted the bucket bounds without their counts, silently moving samples between ranges: `[10, 5]` with counts `[1, 0, 0]` landed its one sample in the `≤5` bucket. The conversion now pairs each written bound with its positional count through the sort, merges duplicate bounds by summing their counts, and keeps the count one past the last written bound in the overflow slot. Pinned by `out_of_order_wire_bounds_keep_their_counts_aligned`.

- **Overflowed percentile readings say so** — [`Percentile`](src/metrics/session.rs) gains `overflow: bool` (`#[serde(default)]`, so old blobs read `false`): a reading whose sample sat above the histogram's last bound is the bound standing in for an unbounded sample, and exporters stop publishing it as an ordinary finite latency — `loopctl-otel` omits the value attribute and emits a boolean `<key>.overflow` marker, `loopctl-langfuse` omits the `p90_turn_latency_ms` score. A dashboard can no longer read an exceeded 60-second objective as met. Pinned by `overflow_readings_flag_their_slot` and `overflowed_percentiles_omit_the_value_and_mark_the_overflow`.

- **A diagnostics counter add saturates instead of stalling** — `bump_by`'s compare-exchange loop exited without adding when `missing_labels + count` would overflow `u64::MAX`, leaving the counter one short of its ceiling. Pinned by `bump_by_saturates_at_the_u64_ceiling`.

- **Runtime label cardinality is bounded** — [`MetricsRegistry`](src/metrics/store.rs) keeps at most `MAX_SERIES_PER_METRIC` (256) distinct label-sets per metric name; a first-seen set past the bound routes to the name's shared `series=overflow` series, so a long-lived host churning runtime tool names accumulates a visible overflow total instead of growing the store — and every snapshot of it — without end. Existing series are never re-routed, and the search for the name's stored keys stops at the bound instead of counting every stored key on each first-seen label-set write (a capped metric is proven once the walk reaches the cap). **Migration:** a dashboard scraping more than 256 distinct label-sets under one name sees the excess collapse onto the `series=overflow` row. Pinned by `runtime_label_cardinality_routes_excess_series_to_overflow`.

- **A counter's unit-named magnitude is its delta** — `MetricsLayer` counted `+1` per event for every counter, so `loopctl.compaction.summarizer.tokens` counted passes and the derived `loopctl.compaction.summarize.ratio` was a ratio of event counts rather than tokens. A numeric field named as the metric's own last segment (`tokens` on `…summarizer.tokens`) is now that counter's delta — the same unit-in-name grammar the gauge/histogram arm already applies — and every other numeric on a counter stays counted drift. Pinned by `a_unit_named_counter_field_is_its_delta`.

- **Metric-less events on the metrics target are ignored** — the engine's own non-metric warnings on `loopctl::metrics` (the embedding dimension-mismatch WARN) were counted as `unknown_metric` drift; an event carrying no `metric` field is not a metric event and now counts nowhere. Pinned by `events_without_a_metric_field_are_ignored`.

- **Tool turns settle their span after the tool phase** — a tool-bearing turn's span recorded `outcome = ok` when the model phase committed, before any tool ran, and a later dispatch failure could never correct it (the span had already closed). The driver now holds the turn span open and the turn-end notification settles it with the final outcome and usage — tool-phase success, dispatch failure, LLM-phase failure, and tool-free turns all settle through one funnel. Pinned by the dispatch-failure and settlement scenarios in `the_engine_emits_the_documented_span_tree`.

- **A failed manager reset still closes the run span** — `reset_all()` failing before the first turn propagated straight out of `run()`, closing the run span with no `outcome`, `error.type`, or `duration_ms`; the early return now settles the span with the failure vocabulary first. The shared helper is the new public `tracing_spans::record_run_end`, and `record_turn_end`/`record_turn_end_on` now take one `TurnSettlement` struct instead of seven positional figures (**migration:** callers construct `TurnSettlement { success, duration_ms, … }`). Pinned by the helper scenarios in `the_engine_emits_the_documented_span_tree` — including the caller path end to end: a `reset_managers` run whose managers are poisoned drives a real `BareLoop` run, and its `AGENT_RUN` span is asserted to close with `outcome=error` and an `error.type`.

- **`loopctl-otel`: the metric exporter honors the config** — the OTLP metric exporter built with `.with_http()` and its own default endpoint, ignoring both `OtelConfig::endpoint` and `use_grpc`; it now mirrors the span exporter — endpoint on both transports, `with_tonic()` under gRPC, the same missing-`grpc`-feature error. Pinned by `a_pipeline_with_an_unroutable_endpoint_fails_to_build`.

- **`loopctl-otel`: registry-only stamping and a bounded stamp** — a `SpanAttributes` config with a registry but no observer built no stamper, so valid series never reached run spans; stamping no longer waits on an observer (`Stamper.observer` is now an `Option` — **migration:** stamper literals wrap the observer in `Some(..)`). The registry share of a run-span stamp is also bounded at 64 series attributes — the OTel SDK drops span attributes past 128 silently, and an unbounded stamp would push the run rollup and the drift diagnostics off the span; the five `loopctl.telemetry.*` diagnostics always ride. Pinned by `a_registry_only_config_still_builds_a_stamper`, `registry_stamping_is_bounded_and_keeps_diagnostics`, and the registry-only scenario in `the_collector_sees_genai_shaped_spans`.

- **`loopctl-otel`: series keys cannot collide and replays carry deltas** — the pipeline's delta ledger keyed series by a dot-joined string a label value containing `.` or `=` could alias, letting one series' growth silently swallow another's (keys are now length-prefixed, collision-free by construction); and histogram replays re-recorded every cumulative bucket count on each export, inflating cumulative-temporality counts and sums (the ledger now tracks per-bucket counts and replays only the new samples — an unchanged snapshot records nothing). Pinned by `colliding_label_spellings_keep_their_deltas_apart` and `unchanged_histograms_replay_only_new_samples`.

- **`loopctl-otel`: the install globals test observes the global** — after a rejected second install the test now also proves the global tracer still routes to the first installation (a span built through the global tracer reaches the first provider, whose dead-endpoint flush is the observable), not only that the guard's own provider flushes. Pinned by `a_refused_second_install_leaves_the_first_running`.

- **`loopctl-langfuse`: 207 and 429 answers get read** — a 207 settled the batch without reading its body, so entries the server reported in `errors` were lost; a 429 dropped the batch as an unrecoverable client error. A 207 whose body names failed entries now retries the same idempotent ids (a clean or unparseable body settles), and a 429 honors `Retry-After` (delta-seconds, capped at 5 s) for the same batch, falling back to the retry ladder when the header is absent or unparseable. Pinned by `a_207_with_failed_entries_retries_the_batch` and `a_429_honors_retry_after_and_retries_the_same_batch`.

- **`loopctl-langfuse`: the turn prompt never exports, and neither does a racing push** — the layer forwarded the turn span's `query` field (the assembled prompt, PII included) into observation metadata; it now maps to a suppressed slot and is dropped before the free-form fallback. A push racing shutdown can no longer append to a closed queue: the closed check runs under the queue lock, where shutdown cannot drain-and-exit between the check and the append. Pinned by the metadata assertion in `the_cloud_would_receive_traces_observations_and_scores` and `a_push_racing_shutdown_cannot_append_to_a_closed_queue`.

- **`loopctl-langfuse`: the guard drops safely and the credentials must be real** — `Drop` on a current-thread runtime parked the only worker for the full five seconds, which guaranteed the pending events were lost (the flusher cannot run); `Drop` now detects the runtime flavor, closes the queue, warns, and returns at once — ordinary threads keep the bounded blocking drain. `from_env` rejects empty `LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY`/`LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY` values through the existing `MissingEnv` error (its own documented rule: empty counts as missing) instead of sending blank Basic auth every batch is rejected on. Pinned by `guard_drop_on_a_current_thread_runtime_does_not_park` and `empty_credentials_are_missing`.

- **`loopctl-langfuse`: token scores stop double-counting** — `token_total` summed all five token classes, double-counting the cache and reasoning detail that rides *inside* input and output (it is now `input + output`, matching `TokenTotals::total`), and `cache_hit_rate`'s denominator re-added `cache_write` that the input total already contains (now `cached_input / input`, matching `TokenTotals::cache_hit_rate`). Pinned by `token_total_and_cache_hit_rate_do_not_double_count`.

- **The rustdoc gate covers the exporter crates** — `make docs` now runs `cargo doc --no-deps` under `RUSTDOCFLAGS="-D warnings"` for `loopctl-otel` and `loopctl-langfuse` as well (the root invocation documents only the root package), which surfaced and fixed three latent doc errors in `loopctl-otel`: an unresolved intra-doc link and two redundant explicit targets.

- **The embeddings failure assertion matches the whole field** — the Ollama-failure test's metric checks matched `metric=loopctl.embed.batch.duration_ms` as a substring, so an unregistered extension of that name would have passed; both sites now compare the complete whitespace-delimited field.

- **`loopctl-langfuse`: the flusher's exit check reads both facts at once** — the drain loop tested queue emptiness and closedness in two steps, so a push accepted between them could report a successful hand-off while the worker exited for good, stranding the event; the exit check now decides emptiness and closedness together under the queue lock. Pinned by `closed_and_empty_reads_both_facts_under_one_lock` and `a_push_accepted_before_close_keeps_the_flusher_from_exiting`.

- **`loopctl-langfuse`: an unreadable 207 answer retries instead of settling** — the count of entries the collector reported failed came back `0` for a body that was not JSON, not an object, or carried an `errors` field that is not an array, and `0` settled the batch — a partially processed batch could therefore lose entries on an answer the client never read. The reader now answers three ways (settle, retry with the counted failures, retry as unreadable), while a well-formed 207 that names no errors still settles on the first try. Pinned by `an_unreadable_207_answer_retries_the_batch` and `a_207_naming_no_errors_settles_on_the_first_try`.

- **`loopctl-langfuse`: `Retry-After` accepts an HTTP-date, and an absurd delta-seconds no longer panics** — a collector throttling with a date saw its advice fall through to the short retry ladder, so the client could exhaust its attempts and drop a batch the server had only postponed; an HTTP-date is converted to the delay it advertises (a date already past retries at once), the client's own 5-second cap still applies, and a delta-seconds value beyond `u32::MAX` clamps to the cap instead of overflowing the `Duration` constructor in release-insecure builds. Pinned by `retry_after_accepts_delta_seconds_and_http_dates`.

- **`loopctl-langfuse`: a guard dropped on a one-worker multi-thread runtime no longer parks it** — the starvation the current-thread arm already refuses is the same shape when a multi-thread runtime is configured with a single worker — the parking thread is the flusher's only executor; `Drop` now checks the worker count and closes the queue without waiting, while runtimes with workers to spare keep the bounded blocking drain. Pinned by `guard_drop_on_a_single_worker_multi_thread_runtime_does_not_park`.

- **`loopctl-langfuse`: `query` suppression is scoped to the turn span** — the suppression the turn span's assembled prompt needs was applied to every span, so a host recording a `query` field on its own span (a search tool's argument, say) had it dropped from exported metadata; non-turn spans keep it, free-form as ever. Pinned by `a_query_recorded_on_the_turn_span_is_dropped`, `a_query_recorded_on_another_span_stays_in_metadata`, and the positive `gen_ai.tool.name` assertion in `the_cloud_would_receive_traces_observations_and_scores`.

- **`loopctl-otel`: the delta ledger belongs to the registry that filled it** — the pipeline's counter and histogram ledgers outlived the `MetricsRegistry` they were built from, so replacing the registry (a fresh subsystem, a rebuilt pipeline) made `saturating_sub` compare the newcomer's cumulative counts against its predecessor's and silently drop samples until the new totals overtook the old; the ledger now records which registry produced it and resets when that changes, so the first export of a replacement replays its own counts whole. Pinned by `a_replacement_registry_replays_its_own_counts`.

- **`loopctl-otel`: a run-span stamp keeps its diagnostics whatever the tool count** — the span-attribute bound capped the registry share of a stamp but not the per-tool rollup, so a host with enough tools pushed the `loopctl.telemetry.*` diagnostics off the run span — the SDK drops attributes past its 128-attribute cap in silence — and the cardinally-limiting signals were the ones a reader needed to see the limit had been hit; the rollup share is now bounded at 48 attributes, tool groups ride whole (a tool is never split from its error count or latency), and the base run figures always ride. Pinned by `a_rolled_up_run_stays_inside_its_attribute_budget` beside the strengthened `registry_stamping_is_bounded_and_keeps_diagnostics` (which compares the retained attributes in order, not only their count).

- **The registry's `series` label is the store's own** — a write carrying a `series` label of its own could land on, or become, the reserved `series=overflow` series that excess label-sets fold into, merging a caller's population into the store's overflow total; caller-written `series` labels are renamed to `caller.series` before routing, and the gauge write path now shares the same bound and the same renaming. Pinned by `a_caller_written_series_label_cannot_merge_with_the_overflow_row` and the gauge arm of `runtime_label_cardinality_routes_excess_series_to_overflow` (an overflow gauge keeps the last-written value, as the contract states).

- **The newest-observation pins hold in whatever order the suite runs** — the file-baseline pins built their older observation by stepping the observation stamp down with `saturating_sub`, and that stamp is a process-wide counter starting at zero: run before any other observation, the "older" reading tied the newer one, newest-observation-wins settled the tie for the newcomer, and the pin failed when it was selected alone while passing inside the full suite. Both pins now move the stamp before taking the newer observation and assert the premise, so the out-of-order landing is genuinely older in any selection.

- **A run refused at the manager-reset boundary reports its own rollup** — an exporter that reads a `MetricsObserver` when a run span closes (the `loopctl-otel` run stamp, the `loopctl-langfuse` score source) took the figures the observer held at that instant, and a run the manager reset refuses was the one run that settled its span without ever announcing its start — the observer's only reset point. A host sustaining one observer across a session's runs therefore saw the previous run's numbers attributed to the failure: `loopctl.run.turns` carrying the last completed run's count and `loopctl.run.success` reading `true` on a span that recorded an `error.type`, and a failed Langfuse trace scored `outcome = "success"` beside that run's turn and token totals. The engine now announces the run start ahead of that settlement, so every run span is stamped from a ledger its own run opened: the refused run carries zeroes and `run_success = false` — the honest reading of a run that never reached a turn — while the completed run keeps its own figures untouched. The run-start ordering is stated where the callback is defined, including the asymmetry that this path never reaches the run-end notification, because no run record exists yet to report. Pinned by `a_run_failing_at_manager_reset_stamps_its_own_rollup` (otel spans) and `a_run_failing_at_manager_reset_scores_its_own_run` (Langfuse scores), each asserting the completed run's stamp unchanged so a change that blanked every run could not pass.

## [0.4.1] - 2026-10-21

### Added

- **Compaction seam coalesces the summary with the tail's assistant** — the `f8e96a6a` class is dead: every LLM compactor emits its summary as an assistant-role message at the head of the preserved tail, and when the tail opens with an assistant turn (the common case mid-run) the assembled machine carried **two adjacent assistant messages** — a shape zai's GLM API rejects with `HTTP 400 [1214]`, killing the run within two seconds of every successful compaction (the observed session compacted twice and 400'd twice; the transcript rebuild — which coalesces consecutive assistant records — resumed and ran for hours, diverging from the live engine in shape alone). **The seam coalesces:** `LoopMachine::compaction_result` adopts compacted history through `coalesce_adjacent_assistants` (message.rs) — runs of adjacent assistant messages fold into one message carrying the parts of every member in order — the summary's parts first, a `\n\n` separator parting two adjacent texts at the merge so the wire never glues statements, then the tail's — the same shape the rebuild produces — so the assembled machine never carries two adjacent assistant messages whatever the compactors emit, while a tail opening with a user or system message keeps today's standalone summary assistant message. **The provider seam is the safety net:** the Anthropic request build (which zai rides) merges any adjacent assistant messages it is handed after system-folding, so a future producer of the shape cannot kill a run even if a compactor changes. The summary-role design stands — assistant-role summary ahead of the tail — now in the coalesced spelling, and the QA accumulate mode's prior-summary recognition moved from exact text equality to a two-arm rule — exact equality anywhere, and on the first assistant message alone (pins riding ahead of it included) a strip of exactly the leading summary part, so the tail turn's own text, tool calls, and thinking ride on as the next pass's transcript (the coalesced head) — so a later message that merely echoes the summary and continues keeps its continuation in the next pass's input (`an_echoed_summary_with_continuation_survives_the_transcript`). Pinned fail-first at three levels: the machine (`compaction_result_coalesces_an_adjacent_assistant_pair`, red at the surviving pair), the wire end-to-end (`a_post_compaction_request_is_legal_on_a_strict_provider` — a hostile-shape compactor inside a live loop against a strict double that rejects adjacent assistants exactly the way GLM does; red at the reproduced `400 [1214]` rejection, with a compactor-consulted guard so the pin can never pass vacuously), and the provider net (`the_request_build_merges_adjacent_assistant_messages`, red at the passthrough). Existing summary-role and compaction pins green untouched; the machine and rebuilt machine now agree in shape at the seam.

- **Edit/MultiEdit miss repair — errors that echo the repair, whitespace-tolerant unique matches, and a shared `match_repair` module** — session-history mining (70 failed Edit/MultiEdit calls across 22 sessions: 63 `Old text not found` + 7 `changed on disk`) shows the misses cluster into three root causes the tools can now distinguish. **Errors that echo the repair:** on a miss the tool classifies the cause — *stale* (the file's closest passage differs in content; the first differing line cited with both sides quoted), *whitespace-only* (the text matches modulo indentation width, trailing whitespace, or line endings), or *no similar passage* (nothing resembles the text) — and echoes the file's actual lines around the best candidate window with 1-based line numbers, bounded (16 lines / 2 `KiB`, the everything-the-model-reads rule): the error becomes the re-read the old hints demanded. **Whitespace-tolerant matching:** a *unique* normalized match applies outright, spliced through a core range that preserves the file's own leading indentation and trailing whitespace (the file's actual bytes on both edges, never the model's spelling), disclosed in the receipt's `│`-note channel — `matched with normalized indentation` — and in MultiEdit's per-file block; an ambiguous normalized match never applies and rejects with the echo (`WhitespaceAmbiguous` carries the count). The overlap detector sees the same normalized ranges the merge applies, so two edits overlapping only under normalization still abort before anything writes. Staleness resemblance is scored by word-overlap at the half mark per aligned line (`fn two() { todo!() }` resembles `fn two() {}`; a paraphrase shares nothing), threshold at half the old text's lines. Pinned fail-first (`a_whitespace_mismatched_old_text_applies_and_discloses`, `an_ambiguous_normalized_match_rejects_with_the_echo`, `a_stale_miss_cites_the_first_differing_line`, `a_miss_with_no_similar_passage_says_so`, `a_miss_on_a_huge_file_echoes_bounded`, the MultiEdit twins, and the classifier unit suite); the `edit_not_found` golden regenerated deliberately from the tool. The tolerant apply **trims the replacement's own edge whitespace symmetric to the core's shrink** (`edge_trimmed_replacement`) — the model that mismatched the indent spells *both* texts with it, and the splice had concatenated file-leading with model-leading into a ten-space first line; the corpus-shape pins (`the_tolerant_apply_gives_the_file_s_indentation_the_first_line` + the batch twin) own that fixture; and the stale classification is **anchored** — a one-line old text anchors on its similar line, a longer one needs a matching non-generic first or last line, so one generic `}` in an unrelated region can no longer masquerade as the neighborhood (`an_unanchored_single_generic_line_is_no_passage`); `classify_escape` gained its unit home in resolve.rs beside the machinery. The tolerant apply **re-indents the whole replacement uniformly** by the window's indentation delta (`normalized_splice` — the model's spelling stays relative, the file's absolute indent wins; the Python-IndentationError geometry and its negative-delta twin pinned red-first, `a_tolerant_apply_re_indents_the_whole_replacement` / `a_tolerant_apply_dedents_when_the_model_over_indented`); the scratch root **validates before creating** (lexical normalization plus the resolver's symlink-aware walk — `../` escapes and roots through outbound symlinks refused, both pinned); the normalized-match disclosure **survives the large-diff receipt path** (no `@@` marker — the note rides at the end, `the_disclosure_survives_the_large_diff_path`); `classify_miss` **prepares each line once** (normalized form + token set precomputed before the window loop — refactor-neutral, every pin green unchanged); the symlink escape arm **classifies as a link pointing out** with a real-target remedy; and a root-level cwd parent **no longer turns every absolute escape into a sibling workspace** (`/etc/hosts` under `/work` is a system path). The tolerant apply takes **the file's indent characters wholesale** — each replacement line aligned to a window line inherits that file line's leading whitespace verbatim, characters and width (`file_indents`/`aligned_replacement`; the tab-Python shape, the delta-zero Makefile recipe, and the tab-dedent twin pinned on full output bytes) — joins multi-line replacements with **the file's own line endings** (`window_eol`, interior breaks first, the terminator after a single-line window — a CRLF file never receives LF-only interior joins, pinned on full bytes), and the scratch root **creates and stores the path it validated** — the normalized destination, never the original spelling whose `..` segments would re-walk an outbound symlink at creation time (`a_scratch_root_through_a_link_and_dotdot_creates_inside`). The indent map goes **through `old_text` by content** — a surviving line keeps its own block membership however the edit re-arranges it (the removed-line silent move dies), and a genuinely new line inherits the nearest mapped neighbor's file indent shifted by the model's relative depth through a level scale derived from the aligned pairs themselves (the added-block, removed-line, and Makefile triggers pinned red-first on full bytes; every prior pin green untouched). The content-keyed lookup gives way to a **model-width → file-indent map**: each non-blank replacement line takes the greatest mapped base not exceeding its own width plus its relative depth through the file's unit — a surviving line the model re-indented carries its new depth, a shallower line rises, and an unmappable repair (a width bound to two indents, or a line shallower than every mapped width) **refuses** with the classified miss rather than guess (`a_surviving_line_the_model_moved_deeper_takes_the_model_depth`, `a_shallower_line_after_a_nested_line_rises`, `a_line_shallower_than_every_old_line_refuses_the_apply`; the headline fixtures moved to the model-mirroring indent, the width-0-against-width-6 shape being exactly the baseless one that must refuse). A **single mapped width with a tab base refuses a deeper line** rather than pad one tab per model space — the one-line-in, block-out trigger (`a_rate_less_tab_base_refuses_a_deeper_replacement`) — while the spaces fallback scales one-for-one (`a_rate_less_space_base_scales_one_for_one`). **Layer 3 (self-attributed staleness refresh) moves to the next breaking window:** the newest-observation-wins lineage already covers the model's own writes (pinned by `the_models_own_edit_never_conflicts` and the conflict-stress suite), and the remaining within-call TOCTOU rejection is load-bearing against racing self-edits — softening it would only weaken a guard protecting the model from itself; external changes are byte-identical either way (nothing in the guards changed).

- **Resolve-policy escape errors that teach (`FileSession::with_scratch_root` / `with_unrestricted_available`)** — containment rejections keep their byte-identical first line and gain the repair: the working directory the policy holds to, the target's class, and the remedies that actually exist for the session. The classes come from the corpus: **temp scratch** (a script dropped directly in a temp root — `/tmp/probe.py`), **sibling workspace** (a neighbor repo's context under the working directory's parent), **system or dependency path**, and traversal spellings; each with its own remedy line. The **scratch root ships behind the additive gate**: `FileSession::with_scratch_root` is opt-in-from-the-host (off unless passed; only a root at or under the working directory is honored — anything else is ignored with a warning, since the policy would refuse the directory it advertised), created best-effort, named in temp-shaped errors only when configured, and resolved into without ceremony — it sits under the working directory, so containment already admits it (a Write into it lands: `a_scratch_root_target_writes_without_ceremony`; a relative spelling joins the working directory first, `a_relative_scratch_root_joins_the_working_directory`). The **unrestricted remedy appears only when the host declares the mode exists** (`with_unrestricted_available` — a session under `Unrestricted` never rejects, so the flag marks the *host's* opt-out as available, the user's to enable); the message never advertises an escape hatch the session does not have. Every escape flows through the session-aware resolve (`FileSession::resolve`, threaded through Write/Edit/MultiEdit/Read/FileViewer; the resume re-arm keeps the silent path); behavior is unchanged — every escaping path still rejects, and the existing containment pins stay green untouched. Pinned fail-first through the model-facing path (`a_sibling_workspace_escape_names_the_boundary_and_class`, `a_temp_shaped_escape_names_the_scratch_root_when_configured` and its unconfigured twin, `the_unrestricted_remedy_appears_only_when_declared` both arms, `a_scratch_root_outside_the_working_directory_is_ignored`), neutralization-proven at the teaching seam (the append removed → the class pins red; restored green).

- **Steering seam — `BareLoop::steering()` and the `Steering` slot** — the shared way to say something to the turn that is running: a host with a live transcript offers guidance through a cloneable handle (the way `cancel_signal()` is shared, not called — the task driving a session lives inside `run()`'s borrow), and the loop drains the slot at each turn boundary through the machine's own `inject`, so guidance enters the pending set the machine commits and the conversation the user reads is the one the model continues — pinned into committed history (`a_push_mid_run_reaches_the_next_request_and_commits`), the property the contributor path deliberately cannot offer. **Boundary, not interruption**: a push during dispatch is absent from the request whose tools are running and present in the next one, after the tool results that opened the boundary; three pushes land in push order as separate user messages in history (`three_pushes_land_in_order_as_separate_user_messages`, with the provider's adjacent-user coalescing joined on the wire, order pinned both levels). **The idle edge is atomic**: `push` returns `false` when no run is in flight — acceptance and the in-flight check share one lock — and the message stays the caller's, never swallowed by a terminal machine's no-op inject (`a_push_while_idle_is_refused_and_leaves_nothing_waiting`). **Leftovers belong to the host**: guidance the run never reached survives cancellation in the slot, in order, collectable via `take_leftovers` (`a_cancelled_run_leaves_the_slot_intact_and_leftovers_survive`), and the next run *seals* it rather than delivering it — advice about a turn that no longer stands is not advice (`leftovers_never_ride_turn_zero_of_the_next_run`). **The invalidation is load-bearing**: a drain that takes anything drops the stashed prepared turn and the usage anchor exactly as a rewind does — a gated-selector pin drives a push into the turn-0 preparation window and proves the stash is rebuilt with the guidance in view (the selector consulted twice, the first request carrying it: `a_prepared_turn_plus_steering_sends_the_guidance`). **The estimate rides along**: the drain sits ahead of the pre-request re-measure, so a large push moves the machine's context figure before the next threshold decision (`a_steering_message_refreshes_the_context_estimate`). Semver-additive: `machine()`, `history()`, `inject`, and the contributor path are untouched, and the untouched-host suite is green. The collection API splits along the same line: `take_leftovers` reads the **sealed pocket alone** (a mid-run peek cannot steal live guidance: `take_leftovers_never_steals_the_live_run_s_guidance`), and the new `take_all` is the documented destructive shutdown collect (`take_all_collects_sealed_and_waiting_at_shutdown`).

- **History rewind seam — `LoopMachine::rewind_to` and `BareLoop::rewind_history_to`** — the host-side path for correcting a committed prompt: a host that lets its user replace a prompt rewinds to the history length it observed before the superseded turn, and the conversation reads as though that turn never ran — the same machine, the same client, the same observers, and the same `cancel_signal()` the host already holds (nothing is rebuilt; `Arc::ptr_eq` pins it). The cut is **length-shaped** (no message identity exposed), **snaps to a turn boundary** — a `len` landing between a tool call and its result snaps down to that turn's start, so the pairing property survives (`rewind_to_a_len_inside_a_turn_snaps_to_the_turn_start`, with the orphan scan as its assert); a turn **in flight refuses** with the state named (`rewind_to_refuses_while_a_turn_is_in_flight`), and cancel-then-resolve-then-rewind is the supported path out (`cancel_then_rewind_succeeds_once_the_run_resolves`); `len` **at or beyond the current length is `Ok(0)`**, not an error (`rewind_to_beyond_the_length_removes_nothing`). Bookkeeping is settled on record: the deferred-compaction latch clears so a stale latch from the dropped tail cannot suppress a trigger against the shorter history (`rewind_clears_the_deferred_compaction_latch`), while `turns_taken`/`context_tokens` are per-run figures the next `accept_input` resets and the driver's opening estimate re-feeds — so a rewind below the last compaction floor is simply a shorter history and the threshold re-arms naturally on the next run (`rewind_history_to_re_arms_the_compaction_threshold_naturally`); the system prompt lives on the session config, outside the machine, and survives a rewind to zero on the wire (`rewind_history_to_keeps_the_system_prompt`); run records are not derived from machine history and stay as recorded. A rewind that removes anything also invalidates the usage anchor and any prepared turn, exactly as a prune adoption does. The wire evidence — the next provider request carries the pruned history, byte-shaped, on the captured `StreamRequest` (`rewind_history_to_prunes_the_next_provider_request`: `[user first, assistant first, user third]`, the superseded pair gone), and a rewound-to-zero loop runs again with the machine accepting input (`rewind_to_zero_empties_history_and_the_machine_runs_again`). The scanner is the family's existing one: `last_coherent_len` is now the unbounded face of a shared `coherent_cut(messages, limit)`.

- **fs tool path-key alias tolerance** — the fs family accepts the path-key spellings the recorded model drift actually sends, while the advertised schemas stay canonical. Session-history mining (469 sessions, 517 failed calls — 231 of them, 45%, exactly this class) shows `Read` and `Write` are *inverse* confusions: models send `file_path` to `Read` (26 failures) and `path` to `Write` (68 of its 69); `MultiEdit`'s per-edit `file_path` requirement is undiscoverable from its error (136 failures, 9 of which carried a top-level `file_path` that the strict parse silently ignored). **`Read`** gains `#[serde(alias = "file_path")]` on `path`, with a pre-parse canonicalization (`canonicalize_path_input`) that drops the alias spelling when the canonical key rides — a bare serde alias would leave the both-present winner to document order, so canonical-wins is enforced, not hoped for. **A shared fs helper** (`resolve_path_key`) gives Write/Edit/FileViewer canonical precedence — `file_path` first, `path` as fallback, never last-one-in — and `MultiEdit`'s `build_operations` accepts the per-edit `path` spelling plus a top-level `file_path` serving as the batch default for edits that lack one. **Errors teach now**: a still-missing path names the expected key, the accepted spellings, and the keys the input actually carried (`Missing file_path for Write (got top-level keys: path; accepted: file_path, or path)` — `received_keys` renders `no keys` for the empty object), `MultiEdit`'s miss names the per-edit location, and the shell's missing-`command` rejection gained the same shape (`Missing command (got top-level keys: no keys; expected key 'command')`). Pinned fail-first — all seven corpus-shape reds captured before the fixes (`read_accepts_file_path_where_path_was_expected`, `write_accepts_path_where_file_path_was_expected`, `edit_accepts_path_where_file_path_was_expected`, `multi_edit_accepts_per_edit_path`, `multi_edit_accepts_a_top_level_file_path_default`, `multi_edit_missing_everything_names_the_location_and_keys`, `an_empty_call_names_the_expected_key_and_the_absence`) — beside the three canonical-precedence guards (`read`/`write`/`edit` both-present resolve to the canonical key) and the updated shell error-string pin. Schemas advertise canonical names only — the fs goldens and schema pins green untouched. Alongside, four tightenings: a **present-but-mistyped canonical key refuses instead of silently serving the alias** — `{"file_path": 123, "path": "a"}` now errors `file_path must be a string, not number`, mirroring Read's strictness across Write/Edit/MultiEdit (per-edit chain included) and closing the family's teach-vs-serve split (`a_mistyped_canonical_key_refuses_instead_of_serving_the_alias` + Edit/MultiEdit siblings); the **received-keys echo is bounded** at 512 bytes with a char-boundary cut and truncation marker, per the rule that everything the model reads is bounded (`the_received_keys_echo_is_bounded`); the shell's teaching error **reuses** the shared `received_keys` helper instead of its inline twin; and the middle of MultiEdit's precedence chain — a per-edit `path` beating the top-level default — is pinned (`a_per_edit_path_beats_the_top_level_default`). MultiEdit's **batch default is `file_path`-only** (`resolve_default_file_path` — the schema never advertised a top-level `path`, and a stray one serves as no default: `a_top_level_path_is_not_the_batch_default`); a **mistyped per-edit spelling refuses before the default is consulted** (`per_edit_path`'s absent-vs-mistyped split — an edit mistyping its own target can never fall through to editing the default's file: `a_mistyped_per_edit_path_refuses_before_the_default`); a **mistyped top-level default blames the top level**, never masquerading as a per-edit miss (`a_mistyped_top_level_default_blames_the_top_level`); the **alias leg gained the typed teaching** the canonical already had (`resolve_path_key_typed` — `path must be a string, not number`, never telling the model to resend what it sent: `a_mistyped_path_alias_reports_its_type_not_a_miss`); and the shell's teaching-error test module gates on `unix` like its siblings (the repo's Linux-only cross-gates had hidden the compile break from non-Unix contributors). The advertised MultiEdit schema **admits the root-default shape** — the nested `file_path` optional, a top-level optional `file_path` as the documented default, no `anyOf` (the grammar-neutral rule), the semantic no-target check still the runtime's teaching error (`the_schema_admits_the_root_default_shape`, plus a strict JSON-Schema validator pin under `schema_validation`: `the_advertised_schema_accepts_the_documented_shapes`), the golden regenerated deliberately; and a family gate now validates every fs tool's advertised schema against its documented shapes (`the_documented_shapes_are_schema_legal_across_the_family`), turning this finding's class into a mechanical check. Riding the same commit, the steering slot **closes when the `run()` future is dropped** — a `SteeringCloseGuard` created beside `open_run` closes on drop (abort, timeout, `select!`), composing idempotently with `finalize`'s explicit close (`an_aborted_run_task_closes_the_steering_slot`).

- **Async tool selection — the `AsyncToolSelector` companion trait, `EmbeddingSelector` (`vector_index` feature), and `RewritingSelector`, wired beside the sync selector at the same per-turn seam** — for registries where keyword recall is structurally insufficient (MCP-mounted surfaces, fifty-plus tools). The companion trait mirrors [`ToolSelector`](crate::tool::ToolSelector) with `select` as a future: object-safe, consulted exactly once per turn at the request-build step ahead of the payload estimate (`turn_tool_schemas` awaits it), so the estimate and the wire share one selection; the engine takes a sync selector or an async one, never both — configuring either clears the other. The determinism contract is deliberately relaxed and documented for the async family: the value is the judgment a call buys, and the once-per-turn discipline is what keeps the estimate explainable. **`EmbeddingSelector`** embeds each tool's name + description once per registry generation (cached against the ordered name list — a runtime registry mutation re-embeds on the next turn) and the query fresh per turn, advertising the top-k nearest by cosine (`with_top_k`, default 8); any embed failure fails open to the full surface with a warning — selection never strands the model; cost is one embedding call per turn plus one per registry generation. **`RewritingSelector`** makes one small non-streaming call per turn that sees the tool names and one-line summaries — never the schemas, which would spend the tokens selection saves — plus the run prompt and the recent conversation, and answers with this turn's tool list; a transport error, timeout (`with_timeout`, default 10 s), budget refusal, or output naming no known tool fails open to the full surface; `with_budget(BudgetLimits)` rides the existing budget machinery — the selector owns a gate, feeds it every rewrite call's reported usage, measures wall-clock spend from its first selection, and refuses further calls on a hard crossing (tokens or duration); each conversation excerpt is capped at 400 bytes so the prompt grows with the tool list, never with the history's bodies; answers naming some tools advertise exactly those, in registry order. Pinned by the selector suite (`an_async_double_selects_through_the_companion_trait`; `semantic_proximity_selects_the_nearest_tool`, `a_query_embed_failure_fails_open`, `the_corpus_embeds_once_per_registry_generation` on a table-driven embedding double; `the_rewrite_prompt_carries_names_never_schemas`, `an_answer_naming_no_known_tool_fails_open`, `an_exhausted_budget_refuses_the_call` on the scripted test client) and the engine suite (`an_async_selector_is_consulted_once_per_turn` — one consultation per turn, both requests carrying the subset; `the_estimate_counts_the_async_selection_not_the_registry` — the machine figure derives from the async-chosen subset's own rendered cost; `an_async_fail_open_serves_the_full_surface_on_the_wire`; `configuring_one_selector_clears_the_other` both directions). Hardened edges pinned alongside: the engine races the async selection against the cancel signal (`a_cancelled_run_does_not_wait_for_the_async_selection` — a never-resolving selector under `cancel()` settles the run `Cancelled`); an unrankable query — every cosine non-finite, a zero-vector query embedding — fails open to the full surface instead of an empty selection (`a_zero_vector_query_fails_open_to_the_full_surface`), with the intentional-empty-versus-inability-to-rank contract stated on the trait; and the corpus cache keys on the embedded texts, so a same-name description overwrite re-embeds (`an_overwritten_description_re_embeds_the_corpus`). Neutralization-proven: the embedding fail-open replaced with a silent empty selection turns its pin red; deleting the budget pre-check turns the refusal pin red. Also pinned: `a_duration_budget_refuses_once_the_clock_runs` (a duration limit refuses the second call — the gate sees real elapsed time), `the_rewrite_prompt_bounds_each_conversation_excerpt` (a hundred-kilobyte tool result rides as a 400-byte head), `a_timed_out_rewrite_fails_open` (a never-answering double under a 5 `ms` timeout), and the engine-level `a_rewriting_selector_costs_exactly_one_call_per_turn` — the once-per-turn discipline pinned where the money is spent.

- **Keyword-selector recall hardening — `KeywordSelector::with_synonyms` and `KeywordSelector::with_core_tools`** — the shipped keyword selector narrows nothing when the prompt's vocabulary misses the tool descriptions ("check the weather" against "Search the web for pages"), and a partial wrong match can evict a tool every turn needs. Two deterministic layers close the gaps below the model-call line. **Synonym expansion at tokenize time:** a curated, bidirectional, query-side table (`check ↔ verify/inspect/search`, `find ↔ search/locate`, `open/show/view ↔ read`, `fix/change/modify ↔ edit`, `create/make ↔ write`, `start/launch ↔ run`, plus phrase pairs `look up → search`, `look at → read`, `look for → search`, `figure out → plan` — phrase matching is word-boundary-aware so "look upon" never fires "look up") expands the query's tokens before matching; `with_synonyms` adds host pairs that match exactly like the defaults. **The hybrid core set:** `with_core_tools` names tools included in every non-empty selection — the union of keyword matches and the core set, in registry order — so a strong wrong-side match cannot evict them; the union composes with fail-open rather than replacing it, so a total keyword miss still serves the full surface (core included) and a vocabulary miss never strands the tool the prompt actually needs. Alongside, the description recall audit touched the two builtins with the widest vocabulary gaps: `Grep` now says "find where something is defined, used, or mentioned" and `Read` says "open, view, or inspect a file" (`the_description_carries_the_lookup_vocabulary`, `the_description_carries_the_open_view_vocabulary`); the golden corpus was regenerated deliberately with `UPDATE_GOLDENS=1` and shows zero drift — the wire corpus does not carry these descriptions, verified rather than assumed. Pinned fail-first (the synonym pins red on an empty-table scaffold at their fail-open assertions) by `a_synonym_only_match_selects_the_tool`, `check_the_weather_selects_the_search_tool_through_synonyms`, `look_up_selects_via_the_phrase_table`, `host_synonyms_extend_the_default_table`, `unknown_synonym_words_change_nothing`, `core_tools_ride_on_every_prompt_including_misses`, `a_partial_wrong_match_cannot_evict_a_core_tool`, and `expansion_keeps_selection_deterministic`, and `the_widest_synonym_pair_does_not_pull_clearly_unrelated_tools` as the precision guard every future pair addition must argue against (`show → list` was dropped for failing it); every pre-existing selector pin and the engine surface pins stay green (no behavior change for a plain `KeywordSelector` on their fixtures). One refinement: the synonym table is applied query-side only — the tool-side lever for vocabulary gaps is description wording (the audit), not expansion, keeping the false-positive surface one-directional. Construction note (pre-release): `KeywordSelector` is a fielded `Default` type constructed as `KeywordSelector::default()`, not a unit value; host pair sides are lowercased on store, and each side must be a single tokenizer-surviving word (`every_default_pair_side_survives_tokenization` and `every_default_phrase_target_survives_tokenization` guard both tables mechanically).

- **MCP `structuredContent` validation against `outputSchema` (`mcp` feature) — the `StructuredContentPolicy` and `McpToolProvider::with_structured_policy`, client-side validation, and server-side emission** — both lanes of the spec's structured contract now flow. Client side: a tool whose server declared a compilable `outputSchema` has its `structuredContent` results validated on every call — a valid payload rides as a pretty-printed JSON part beside the server's text lane, with the whole output marked `DisplayHint::Json` for presentation layers; an invalid one becomes a soft tool error naming the violated schema keyword with the raw payload preserved (bounded at 2 `KiB`, char-boundary cut) so the model sees what came back and can adapt while the loop continues — the default `Error` policy; `with_structured_policy(StructuredContentPolicy::Lenient)` downgrades to warn-and-passthrough for hosts that trust their servers, updating already-discovered tools and later discoveries alike (the `with_call_timeout` discipline). A declared-but-unused schema serves the text lane alone; an uncompilable `outputSchema` is omitted at discovery with a warning (the tool stays listed, unvalidated — the standing `$ref`-refusal rules); a schema-less tool's `structuredContent` keeps today's JSON-stringified note byte-for-byte. Server side: a host tool whose result carries the `DisplayHint::Json` hint has its parseable-object payload emitted as `structuredContent` beside the text lane (`loopctl.mcp.structured` outcome counter on the client bridge, `loopctl.mcp.structured.emitted` on emission; a hint that lies — prose or non-object — keeps the text lane with a warning). Pinned by `valid_structured_content_becomes_a_json_payload`, `invalid_structured_content_is_a_soft_error_naming_the_keyword`, `lenient_policy_passes_an_invalid_payload_through`, `schema_without_structured_content_is_text_only`, `non_compilable_output_schema_omits_validation` (all on a scripted in-process server; the validation, emission, and discovery-omission arms are neutralization-proven — validation skipped, emission disabled, and the compile check deleted each turn their pin red), beside `server_emits_structured_content_for_json_payloads` and `a_lying_json_hint_keeps_the_text_lane_only` on the adapter, and the pre-existing text-path pins (`echo_round_trip_returns_text`, the legacy `bridge_result` structured unit tests) owning the byte-identical schema-less lane. One refinement: a non-object `outputSchema` stays carried verbatim (the landed verbatim-carriage pin owns that contract) — only uncompilable schemas are omitted. Four edges hardened: the invalid-verdict output now keeps the server's own text lane beside the violation (`invalid_content_keeps_the_server_text_lane_beside_the_verdict`); both the violation text and the payload appendix pass the same 2 `KiB` char-boundary cut — the violation embeds the failing instance, so the anti-flood bound now holds for root-level misses too (`an_oversized_violation_stays_under_the_bound`); server-side emission parses the payload's *last* text part rather than the joined text, so a prose-plus-JSON multipart round-trips its structured lane instead of silently losing it (`server_emits_structured_content_from_a_multipart_payload`); and `matches` compares the structured policy alongside `call_timeout` — a `with_structured_policy` change reaches already-registered tools through the next sync instead of leaving stale clones (`a_policy_change_reaches_already_registered_tools`).

- **The runtime-mutable tool registry — `ToolRegistry::remove`, `ToolRegistry::version`, `BareLoop::tool_registry`, the `on_tool_surface_changed` observer event, and MCP `sync_into`** — a loop's tool set is no longer frozen at construction: the registry's tool set lives behind an interior `RwLock` with a monotonic `version` counter, and the shared `Arc<ToolRegistry>` the engine already holds is the mutation handle (`BareLoop::tool_registry()` hands it out). A registration or removal is *advertisement*-immediate — the engine reads the registry when it builds the next turn's request, so a tool registered mid-run rides the very next request and never a partially-visible mid-turn state — and *execution*-draining: dispatch holds an owned `Arc<dyn Tool>`, so an in-flight call completes against the handle it fetched even after the name is removed (`an_in_flight_call_drains_after_its_own_removal`; the post-removal call itself resolves through the typed unknown-tool path, `a_removed_tool_fails_typed_and_leaves_the_surface`; the next-request advertisement is pinned by `a_tool_registered_mid_run_is_advertised_on_the_next_turn`). Every mutation bumps `version`, which the engine watches twice: the cached full-registry overhead reservation re-measures on a version move, so the overflow-salvage reserve can never over- or under-reserve against a stale schema set (`the_overhead_reservation_remeasures_on_registry_mutation`, neutralization-red with the version guard frozen), and each turn preparation reads the surface and its version as one registry-atomic pair (`versioned_surface` — mutations bump the counter inside the write lock, so a reader can never pair a new surface with an old count or the reverse) to report `on_tool_surface_changed` with the partitioned, name-sorted diff — added/removed names and the schema-changed names of in-place overwrites (`the_surface_change_event_reports_the_diff`, `an_in_place_overwrite_reports_the_changed_name`, `surface_event_lists_are_sorted_by_name`; neutralization-red with the dispatch deleted; `ToolSurfaceContext` joins the observer contexts, and the module-doc inventory census covers it). On the MCP side, `McpToolProvider::sync_into` re-lists a server and mirrors the result into a live registry as a diff: added names register, dropped names remove, an identical survivor is not touched at all (not even re-registered — handle identity survives), and a survivor whose advertised contract moved — schema, description, or the policy-bearing read-only/destructive annotations (`McpTool::matches`, the full observable contract) — refreshes in place; removals reconcile against the provider's registration baseline, not its discovery snapshot, so the documented refresh-then-sync sequence still sheds a tool the server dropped between setup and the first sync (`a_refresh_between_setup_and_sync_still_sheds_dropped_tools`), and the baseline keeps the registered *tool*, not just its name, so a server-side change a refresh already pulled into the snapshot still re-registers through the sync instead of the snapshot comparing against itself (`a_refresh_after_a_server_side_change_still_refreshes_the_handle`, neutralization-red with the comparison reverted to the snapshot). Both reconciliation arms are target-aware through the new `ToolRegistry::registration_matches` identity read (schema equality under the state lock): a dropped name is removed only while the target still holds this provider's registration — a foreign tool that overwrote the shared name is left alone with a warning (`a_dropped_name_never_removes_another_provider_s_registration`, neutralization-red with the ownership check deleted) — and a survivor counts `untouched` only when the target currently holds the matching registration, anything else re-registers and reports as `added`, so syncing one provider into several registries (`register_into(&first)`, then `sync_into(&second)`) populates each target instead of silently leaving it empty (`a_sync_into_a_second_registry_registers_its_survivors`, neutralization-red with the target check deleted); the survivor update path follows the same three-way rule (ours updated, foreign-held skipped with a warning and never overwritten, missing registered), so a changed tool on one provider's server can no longer silently take ownership of a name another provider installed in the shared registry (`a_changed_survivor_never_overwrites_a_foreign_registration`, neutralization-red with the skip branch deleted); `McpSyncReport` carries the diff (`sync_into_diffs_the_live_registry` drives a real in-process server through both survivor branches, `a_read_only_hint_flip_refreshes_the_surviving_handle` pins the metadata-only flip). `register_into` now takes `&ToolRegistry` (existing `&mut` call sites compile unchanged), `McpServerAdapter` serves its registry live per request (`registry()` exposes the served handle), and `ToolSchema` gains `PartialEq` for the survivor comparison. **Compatibility notes:** `register` relaxed `&mut self` to `&self` (existing call sites compile unchanged); `get` now returns an owned `Option<Arc<dyn Tool>>` instead of a borrow and `all_tools`/`concurrent_safe_tools` return `Vec<Arc<dyn Tool>>` — the dominant use patterns (`registry.get(name)?.call(...)` and friends) compile unchanged through `Arc`'s `Deref`, while callers that matched on `Some(&tool)` or held the borrow across a mutation should take the owned handle; if any of these read as breaking for a downstream, the release slot is the maintainer's call. Push subscription to `notifications/tools/list_changed` (a handler-bearing rmcp client) is deliberately deferred: this module's clients are fixed to the pure `()` handler, and the pull primitive is the seam that wiring will drive. Registry unit pins cover version monotonicity, idempotent no-op removals, shared-handle visibility, and the drain contract (`version_is_monotonic_across_mutations`, `removing_an_unknown_name_is_an_idempotent_no_op`, `a_registration_through_a_shared_handle_reaches_every_reader`, `a_fetched_handle_outlives_removal_and_still_calls`).

- **Compaction-immune attachments — the `Attachment` trait, `AttachmentStore`, the `FileAttachment`/`MemoryAttachment`/`TodoAttachment` built-ins, and `BareLoop::set_attachment`/`with_attachment`/`detach_attachment`/`attachment_ids`** — data the agent pins rides every outbound request as per-turn transient context (the same path context contributors and memory transients ride) and never enters the machine-owned history at all, so no compactor — truncating, summarizing, or chained — can even see it: zero loss by construction, not by a preservation pass running everywhere (the v0.5.0 exit criterion *"attachments survive compaction with zero data loss"*, re-anchored 2026-10-13 from the pre-0.4.0 extract/reinject design the machine-owned history refactor obsoleted). The rendered parts ride in one consolidated `User` message ordered most-durable-kind first (memory → file → the live todo list, pin order within a kind), re-rendered fresh from the store each turn so an updated attachment — a todo item checked off — shows its latest state on the next request; an oversized pin's outcome is provider-dependent and pinned both ways — a permissive backend serves the deferred turns, a rejecting one dies through the salvage retry (`an_attachment_larger_than_the_window_dies_on_a_rejecting_provider`); a deterministic `render` keeps the request prefix byte-stable across turns, the property provider-side prompt caching depends on. Token cost flows through the existing payload accounting: an attachment that eats the window defers the turn to compaction exactly like a contributor. `FileAttachment` bounds its inline contents at 8 `KiB` by default (char-boundary cut, byte-count footer; `truncate = false` renders verbatim at the caller's risk); an empty store builds no message, so an attachments-free loop's requests stay byte-identical. Pinned fail-first (all four engine pins red on the pre-wiring tree at the attachment-absent assertions, neutralization-red with the wiring deleted) by `attachments_ride_every_request_ahead_of_the_history` (the leading user message carries every rendered part ahead of the prompt; the machine history never contains them), `attachments_survive_a_compaction_pass_byte_equal` (a shrinking pass drops the conversational history wholesale; the post-compaction request still carries the pinned part byte-equal), `an_updated_attachment_renders_its_latest_state_on_the_next_request`, and `an_attachment_that_eats_the_window_defers_to_compaction_like_a_contributor`, beside the unit suite in `src/attachment.rs` (render forms per kind, the 8 `KiB` truncation boundary, id conventions, determinism, store replace-in-place ordering, kind precedence).

- **The dynamic tool surface — `ToolSelector`, `SelectionContext`, `StaticSelector`, `KeywordSelector` (+ `BareLoop::set_tool_selector`/`with_tool_selector`)** — the registered surface and the advertised surface come apart: a selector narrows what the model sees each turn, because frontier models handle a wide tool list while small ones pick the wrong tool, conflate similar tools, or call tools that do not exist — and every schema spends window tokens on every request. The engine consults the configured selector exactly once per turn and threads the chosen schemas into both the outbound request and the window estimate — the figure counts what the wire carries (the advertised subset's own rendered cost), never the whole registry — while the compaction reservation keeps the full-set figure, since a post-compaction turn may advertise the entire surface again; a `response_format` turn composes as before and counts no schema component at all. The default is no selector: every registered tool is advertised, byte-identical to a selector-unaware loop (goldens cover the unchanged shape). `KeywordSelector` tokenizes the prompt and each tool's name + description into lowercase alphanumeric words (glue words like "the"/"for" are stopwords — a prompt's "the" must not advertise every tool whose description says "the") and advertises the overlap, failing open to the full surface on a miss — zero advertised tools would strand a model that must act. An empty selection advertises nothing (the request omits `tools`), selectors own their miss policy, and `ToolConstraint::Strict` composes automatically by constraining to whatever is advertised. The embedding-based selector is deliberately deferred: `EmbeddingProvider::embed` is async and the request-build seam is sync — an async companion trait composes additively when a consumer exists. The seam hardened further: the per-turn context is now prepared once ahead of the call (`prepare_turn` — transients collected first, then selection over the very conversation the request carries, transients leading history, so an attachment or memory that introduces a tool is visible to the selection that needs it) and the selection query is the run's opening prompt on every turn, never a continuation turn's tool-result text; every estimate of the upcoming payload — the run's opening figure, the post-dispatch re-feed, the pre-request feed — counts that same prepared surface, so a wide registry can no longer manufacture a compaction against schemas the wire will not carry (the overflow-salvage reservation alone keeps the full-set figure, reserving for a salvaged turn's fail-open worst case); and a change in the advertised surface between requests invalidates the usage anchor with the wire prefix it was built on, so a narrowed surface lets the figure fall below the prior report instead of flooring at it. Pinned fail-first by `selection_reads_the_run_prompt_on_every_turn`, `selection_sees_the_transients_that_ride_the_request`, `the_run_start_estimate_counts_the_upcoming_surface_not_the_registry`, `the_post_dispatch_estimate_counts_the_next_surface_not_the_registry`, and `a_narrowed_surface_invalidates_the_usage_anchor_so_the_figure_can_fall`. Pinned by (neutralization-red with the selector never consulted) `a_keyword_selector_advertises_the_matching_subset_on_the_wire`, `the_window_estimate_counts_the_advertised_subset_not_the_registry` (the machine's figure derives from the subset's own rendered cost, wire-parity style), `a_keyword_miss_fails_open_to_the_full_surface`, `no_selector_configured_advertises_every_registered_tool`, and `selection_composes_with_response_format_suppression`, beside the unit suite in `src/tool/selector.rs` (tokenizer shape, glue-word drop, match/fail-open/determinism, object safety, static = all).

- **The fs tools' diff previews carry unified-diff `@@` headers with real line numbers** — each change region in a write/edit/multi-edit result now opens with `@@ -old_start,old_count +new_start,new_count @@` computed from the file's true 1-based positions, so a reader — model or display — can address the change where it sits in the file instead of reconstructing positions from context lines alone. Elided context folds into the next region's header; the golden fs corpus is regenerated deliberately for the new shape (each golden gained exactly its header lines). Pinned by the diff-rendering suite in `tool/builtin/fs/diff.rs` (36 pins green).

### Fixed

- **The edit tools' tolerance now follows the rule claimed for it — an unread target gets no whitespace guess** — *Edit results stop echoing files* above states that the whitespace-normalized rescue "applies only to files actually read here", and the gate did not say that: it tested the `resumed` flag alone, and a target carrying **no baseline at all** — never read, never observed by the tool — answered that test with "no", so the case with the least to go on was the one still allowed the guess. `FileSession::read_here` (a baseline exists *and* is a live observation) is now the one question both tools ask, and it decides only the tolerance: an exact anchor match still edits any target, because it verifies the bytes it overwrites, and the unchanged-check before each rename is untouched. Two refusals that described themselves as defects now say what they are — the declined rescue carries the rule it applied instead of a classification insisting the caller "refused a match it should have applied", and a `MultiEdit` anchor refusal rides `conflict::unverified_anchor_note`, the rule it actually applied, rather than `Write`'s "not writing until it has been read here", which the batch stopped telling the truth about the moment it began writing resumed targets. That note reads "has not been read in this session", true of a resume-armed file and a never-read one alike with one recovery for both, and a change with nothing to align on is now reported as "no line unique to either side to align on" — the two sides may share every line, only none of them *unique*, which is what the module's own `Unalignable` has always meant. Docs followed the code that shipped: the edit tools' pipelines no longer list a resume-hold they stopped applying, `conflict::resumed_baseline_message` claims `Write` alone, and `diff.rs` no longer describes the retired Before/After preview as a live fallback (or links a constant it deleted). Pinned by `an_unread_target_takes_no_whitespace_guess`, `an_unread_batch_target_takes_no_whitespace_guess`, `a_declined_rescue_says_why_rather_than_claiming_a_bug`, `a_batch_anchor_refusal_states_the_anchor_rule` and `an_unalignable_receipt_says_no_line_is_unique_to_both_sides` — each red on the unfixed tree first — beside `a_window_holds_a_line_both_sides_share`, which pins the rule `ChangeWindow::entries` now states: a region's matched middle may hold a line both sides share, and it is the context *between* regions the entries never carry.

- **The overflow-salvage matcher understands the self-hosted engine's snake_case phrasings** — a local engine answering an oversized prompt with `context_length_exceeded` / `prepared prompt exceeds Engine max_context 262144` matched none of the hosted providers' spaced phrases (`"context length"`, `"max tokens"`, …), so the arm that force-compacts under the refused payload and retries the turn never armed: the run hard-failed with the raw 400 instead of compacting. The underscore forms cannot collide with the `"context deadline exceeded"` / `"context canceled"` transient idioms the strict set exists to exclude, so they join verbatim. Pinned in `the_local_engine_s_oversized_phrases_arm_the_salvage` (fail-first against the old set) beside the existing end-to-end salvage pin.

- **A threshold pass whose configured compactor errored now defers like a stall instead of failing the run** — with a strictly selected compactor (no substitution chain beneath it), a transient model failure at the threshold would end the run even though the payload still fit the window.
