[`BareLoop`]'s compact handler catches [`LoopError::CompactionFailed`] whose `used` figure sits under the window when the trigger was `ThresholdExceeded`, marks the turn deferred, and surfaces [`on_compaction_failed`](crate::observer::LoopObserver::on_compaction_failed) with the stage's own cause — the next turn retries the same compactor. Emergency-line passes still fail the run honestly. A follow-up fixed the stored figure's scale: the deferral now feeds the machine through the driver's provider-scale conversion like every other driver→machine feed — under a settled non-identity calibration the raw manager-scale figure stored without conversion reads wrong by the calibration ratio — while the fit guard keeps comparing the error's own raw pair, like with like. Pinned by `a_deferred_threshold_pass_stores_the_provider_scale_figure` (the arm's store extracted into `defer_failed_threshold_pass`, observed at the seam the machine's between-runs terminal guard hides: a 4x-billed backend's stored figure is 4x the identity arm's, never the raw count — neutralization-red at `1000 ≠ 4000` with the conversion deleted).
- **`TokenSplitter::split_within_budget` falls back to the deepest pair-safe cut, not to preserve-almost-everything** — when no pair-safe split's tail fit the budget, the old fallback returned the plain `split()` shape (preserve all but the head), so a history with sparse boundaries — long tool-pair walls broken only by run seams — compacted to a no-change no-op every pass and drifted into the emergency stall. The budget is a goal: a tail over budget still shrinks the pass, and only a history with no pair-safe cut at all keeps the orphan-slide default. Pinned in-crate by `a_budget_no_tail_fits_takes_the_deepest_pair_safe_cut` (a fixture where the deepest boundary's orphan slide cuts deeper than any pair-safe-at-itself boundary — the count-based cut `split()` would return; mutant-red with the count-cut fallback restored), beside the re-cut `the_budget_split_keeps_the_count_floor_when_the_budget_cannot_fit_it` (a history with no pair-safe cut at all is what still degrades to the count-based cut), and through strict host-side selection pins (`a_qa_selection_compacts…`, `a_structured_selection…`) that the old chain masked.

- **The durable-pull exemption is budget-bounded — no retention class can make compaction impossible** — the structural killer behind every "could not shrink" marathon: `durable_pull_indices` exempted every [`Durable`](Retention::Durable) receipt in the to-drop region from eviction, verbatim, unconditionally, so a session whose Edit receipts alone (~59k tokens in the live incident) exceeded the landing target could never compact — every stage no-changed, the run limped on deferrals, and the window closed. The new `durable_pull_indices_within_budget` walks the dropped region's durable pairs newest-first and carries them while the budget allows, skipping a pair too costly to fit; a receipt the landing cannot hold is information the summary records, not a reason the run dies. Wired into every consumer: the truncating terminal's landing (the estimate in `choose_split`/`tail_and_pulled_tokens` judges the pull budget against the floor tail so an early split cannot become acceptable by silently dropping receipts a later split would keep; `assemble_kept` re-measures through the budgeted assembly), and both summarizers' `budgeted_split` loops (structured and qa). The semantics shift is deliberate: `Durable` now means "preferred verbatim, budget-bounded", never "un-evictable" — a pinned receipt that would kill the run rides the summary instead. Pinned at two levels: `an_edit_marathon_lands_under_the_durable_pull_budget` (terminal: a 60-exchange Edit marathon with receipts dwarfing the target lands, newest receipts verbatim) and `an_edit_marathon_lands_under_the_durable_pull_budget` in the structured summarizer (the user-facing chain: same marathon through the scripted summarizer lands under target on one call, newest receipts ride); `both_stamps_land_and_the_stamp_decides_what_rides_verbatim` pins the pair semantics — both `Requery` and `Durable` histories land, the stamp decides the landing's shape (requery sheds the payload to the floor; durable carries its verbatim budget of newest receipts). The old `reattach_dropped_results` (unbounded) is removed; `assemble_pulled` shares the assembly tail between the budgeted variants. The pairing hole in the budgeted walk is closed the same way: mate resolution went through a first-match id scan, so a reused call id paired a dropped durable receipt with an earlier exchange's call — the wrong pair rode the budget and the strict reconstruction then dropped the receipt outright — while the unbounded variant was occurrence-aware all along; the budgeted walk now resolves mates through the same `ToolPairing` scan (one pass, no per-result rescan), pinned by `a_reused_call_id_pairs_the_budgeted_pull_with_its_own_exchange` (the earlier spelling's call pulled in place of the receipt's own).

- **Sequenced-read-only shell receipts stamp `Requery`, not `Durable`** — the classification trap behind the session that could never compact: every workhorse compound of the shape `cd /repo && grep -n needle file` (the exact spelling agent prompts teach) fell through the single-command read-only check — compounds are disqualified there by design — and stamped [`Durable`](Retention::Durable), pinning its receipt verbatim for the history's lifetime. A shell-heavy session's accumulated output then exceeded every landing target wholesale, each pass no-changed, and the run limped on deferred compaction until the window closed. `retention_for` now classifies sequenced and piped commands segment-wise: splitting on the separator characters, `cd` admitted as a read-only prefix (the working directory is not file state), every non-blank segment must pass the same prefix allowlist with its operator and mutating-substring guards, and any mutating segment, redirection, or control separator keeps the whole receipt `Durable`; a failed read-only compound stays unclassed exactly as before. Pinned fail-first by `sequenced_read_only_compounds_stamp_requery` (six read-only compound shapes, red under the old single-command gate) beside `any_mutating_segment_keeps_the_receipt_durable` (redirection, build, branch-delete, sed-or, and semicolon shapes stay durable) and `a_failed_read_only_compound_stays_unclassed`. Concurrency classification is untouched: compounds still dispatch serially — evictability and parallelism are different safety questions with different answers. Two gaps are closed with fail-first pins: the compound gate normalized before splitting, so a newline — a statement separator the shell honors — collapsed two statements into one allowlisted segment and `pwd\ntouch marker` stamped `Requery`; the raw command now refuses outright on any control separator (`a_newline_sequenced_mutating_command_keeps_the_receipt_durable`). And the `cd` admission carried none of the segment guards, so `cd $(touch m) && pwd` stamped `Requery` while the substitution executed inside the `cd`; a `cd` segment bearing a shell operator now never qualifies (`a_cd_segment_with_an_operator_keeps_the_receipt_durable`).

- **The fs family's pre-reads of an existing target are type-gated, size-capped, and bounded** — every site that inspected old content before writing (`WriteTool`, `EditTool`, `MultiEditTool`, and both staleness checks) opened the target and read it to EOF with no gate: a writer-less FIFO at the write path parked the dispatch indefinitely (the open blocks; tokio parks its blocking thread), and a device or huge file ballooned memory through the unbounded read — `FileViewer` had refused non-regular targets softly since its own landing while the writing half of the family never got the guard. All five reads (`write`'s pre-read, `edit`'s and `multi_edit`'s loaders, and `conflict`'s byte-exact and hash checks) route through one shared guarded reader (`tool::builtin::fs::existing`): a metadata gate classifies absence, kind, and size **before** the open — so a FIFO is never opened and cannot park — the open itself is non-blocking (unix `O_NONBLOCK`) so a special file swapped into the gate-to-open window cannot park it either and the opened handle is re-checked for what actually answered, and the read itself takes at most the cap plus one byte, so a target that grew past the cap between its gate and its read is classified from what the take saw. The cap is the read tool's own whole-content figure (`DEFAULT_MAX_SIZE_BYTES`, 10 MiB) — one number governs what the family reads and what it writes over. Per-arm outcomes: `Write` **degrades** past the cap and onto special files it can atomically replace — the overwrite lands with a `Changed: … (modified, previous content not read)` header and no diff body (the new `OldContent::Unreadable`), the write's purpose being the write — but **refuses a directory outright** ("… is a directory; refusing to write"), where an atomic replace can never land; `Edit`/`MultiEdit` refuse past the cap and onto non-regular targets naming the guard (their old content feeds `old_text` matching and cannot be skipped); the staleness checks treat non-regular, directory, and over-cap targets as `Changed` — an expected-content or hash baseline was itself recorded under the same cap, so an over-cap or special target cannot be the file a baseline describes. Two legacy pins were re-cut to the classified contract (a directory write target now refuses as a directory rather than as an accidental read fault; the conflict check classifies a directory as `Changed` rather than an I/O fault, with a genuine stat fault still pinned via a path under a regular file). Pinned fail-first by `a_fifo_write_target_is_replaced_without_hanging`, `a_fifo_edit_target_refuses_without_hanging`, `a_fifo_multiedit_target_refuses_without_hanging`, and `a_non_regular_baselined_target_reports_changed_without_hanging` (each red on the pre-change tree as a process-level hang — the parked open survives even a future-level timeout) beside `a_swapped_in_fifo_cannot_park_the_gated_open` (the gate-to-open window's own pin, taken at the extracted non-blocking open: process-hang red on the plain open, green resolving-and-classifying under the flag), `an_oversized_target_writes_with_the_unreadable_header_and_no_diff`, `an_oversized_edit_target_refuses_naming_the_guard`, and `an_oversized_target_reports_changed_without_verifying` (each red on the pre-change tree's unbounded behavior), beside the both-ways boundary pin `an_at_cap_target_still_diffs_normally` (exactly at the cap takes the ordinary diff path).
- **The structured summarizer survives blank and over-budget retries instead of failing the pass** — the live failure behind a deferred threshold pass: a reasoning-heavy backend spent the summarize call's whole output thinking and handed back content-free text (`Blank` → the stage failed, the chain fell through, the pass stalled), or returned a summary past the summary budget twice (`OverBudget` → same fall-through). A blank response now earns exactly one rescue retry with the tighter prompt before the pass fails, and a second over-budget response is trimmed to the budget and committed rather than discarded — the trim bounds the template-rendered form (`trim_summary_to_budget`: render, measure, cut on line boundaries, mark `[trimmed]`, converge in three passes) because the section headers spend budget the raw text does not carry, and the chain's landing check still guards the final figure. Pinned by `a_blank_response_retries_once_then_fails_the_pass` (two blanks still fail with the cause named; exactly two calls) and `an_over_budget_retry_trims_to_the_budget_and_lands` (the trimmed summary lands the pass, fits the budget rendered, carries the trim marker, and costs no third call) — both red under the old immediate-fail arms. A budget below the smallest form the template renders — its own header plus the `[trimmed]` marker — now fails the pass instead of committing the trimmed-over summary, the committed form re-measured with the same render composition the trim loop bounds; pinned by `a_trim_that_cannot_fit_the_budget_fails_the_pass`. A trim whose single cut removes a whole one-line response leaves only the `[trimmed]` marker, which fits any workable budget — so both of the trim's committed returns now require substantive retained content (`retains_substantive_content`), a marker-only trim failing the pass through the same `OverBudget` seam; pinned by `a_trim_that_retains_no_content_fails_the_pass`. A cut that strips every entry beneath a heading leaves only heading lines, which a marker/whitespace rule accepts because headings are text — headings name sections but preserve none of what they held, so `retains_substantive_content` now requires at least one non-empty, non-heading line beside the marker; pinned by `a_trim_that_retains_only_a_heading_fails_the_pass`.

## [0.4.0] - 2026-10-10

### Added

- **Session-scoped reasoning retention — prior-turn thinking rides the conversation verbatim until a compaction pass ages it out** — the turn-boundary strip that dropped every prior turn's [`MessagePart::Thinking`] at each new run and each new model response is removed. The strip traded window space for a provider-side prefix-cache miss proportional to the stripped reasoning on every later request: because the request prefix changed at each boundary, KV/prompt caching never hit, and on caching backends each call re-prefilled the whole conversation — slower and more expensive on every turn of a reasoning-heavy session. Keeping the history verbatim holds the request prefix byte-stable across turns so provider-side prefix caching keeps hitting; the reasoning occupies window space for its lifetime, which tightens the compaction cadence on reasoning-heavy models (the mainstream hosted-agent tools make the same trade), and a compaction pass is what ages reasoning out. The newest assistant message's thinking always rode its own tool loop and still does — the interleaved-thinking replay contract providers require is untouched. Counting follows the wire: the history rides verbatim, so the raw counter figure is the wire shape by construction and no strip-mirroring filter exists. Pinned by `session_retention_keeps_prior_turn_thinking_at_the_boundary`, `session_retention_keeps_a_resumed_history_s_reasoning`, `session_retention_keeps_a_reasoning_only_assistant_message` (a reasoning-only assistant message survives whole — dropping it would rewrite the prefix the behavior exists to keep stable), and `live_turn_thinking_survives_its_own_tool_loop` (the within-turn replay contract); on the wire by the golden `session_scoped_reasoning_request_is_golden` (the final request carries both prior turns' thinking blocks); in the estimate by `session_retention_counts_prior_thinking_in_the_context_estimate` and `session_retention_trigger_counts_the_kept_thinking` (a seeded over-threshold thinking message fires the pass). The counted-versus-sent contract is pinned by `the_wire_payload_is_exactly_what_the_figure_counts`: a request-capturing client proves each request's message set, system prompt, and tool schemas are byte-identical to the hand-derived expectation (any transform between the counted history and the wire fails the set assertion), and the end-state figure is re-derived with the public counter over the captured payload plus the final response and independently re-derived overhead (an overhead component that stops being measured fails the figure assertion).

- **Context-estimate honesty — usage-fed estimate calibration, suppression-aware overhead, and `RunEndContext.calibration_ratio_bp`** — the engine's context-size estimate describes the payload the provider receives. Counting needs no wire-shaping correction: the conversation history rides the wire verbatim (see the session-scoped reasoning retention entry), so the raw counter figure is the wire shape by construction — an internal wire-shaped counter written against the since-removed turn-boundary strip never shipped with it. Estimates are usage-calibrated: when a provider reports real `input_tokens`, the prediction error feeds a rolling integer-thousandths ratio (the first report seeds it, later reports halve the distance; clamped to `[250, 4000]` so an absurd report cannot peg it) applied at every driver→machine feed so the machine's stored figure stays in one scale, at identity — byte-identical figures — until usable usage arrives; a zero-bearing report is no usage at all, so a backend reporting `input_tokens: 0` throughout never moves it. The ratio surfaces per update as a `loopctl.estimate.calibration` counter-style metric event and at run end through `RunEndContext.calibration_ratio_bp` (`None` = no ground truth ever arrived — the honest reading, not a disguised identity; an additive `with_calibration_ratio` builder serves synthetic events). And a turn running under a `response_format` no longer reserves the tool schemas its request suppresses (the OpenAI/Gemini wire bodies drop `tools` outright; Anthropic swaps in one synthesized forced tool, which rides uncounted — a bounded, small under-reserve stated in the docs), while compaction's reservation keeps the full sum for the post-compaction turn. Compaction triggers shift only on backends that report usage — toward the true payload, which is the point — and stay byte-identical on usage-less ones. The scale findings are fixed with the feature: the prediction the ratio samples against is the raw counter figure (dividing by a prediction that already carried the current ratio converged on the multiple's square root — a 4x-billed backend settled at 2x, half its true size), and the installed context manager's window is re-synced into the counter's raw units whenever the ratio moves (the driver-to-manager seam converts the provider window by 1000/ratio), so the compactor's target and the machine's trigger line live in one scale — without the conversion, a pass landing exactly on its target left the calibrated figure over the line and the run died on the no-progress guard; the pre-compaction and compaction-failed observer events carry the resolved provider window so their fields pair one scale throughout. Pinned by `the_first_usage_report_seeds_the_clamped_ratio`, `later_reports_smooth_toward_the_sample`, `zero_or_absent_usage_leaves_the_ratio_at_identity`, and `absurd_usage_reports_cannot_peg_the_ratio_outside_its_bounds` (neutralization-proven: the clamp deleted turned exactly the bounds pin red at `4000000000 ≠ 4000`; the seed rule deleted turned exactly the seed pin red at `1500 ≠ 2000`; the wire-shape filter and the `calibrated` application each neutralized turned exactly their pins red), `usage_reports_converge_the_calibration_ratio` (the second report samples against the ratio-corrected prediction, expectation derived from the engine's own counter over the exact request set), `a_backend_reporting_no_usage_leaves_estimates_uncalibrated`, `run_end_carries_the_calibration_ratio`, and `response_format_turns_do_not_reserve_suppressed_tool_schemas`. The scale fixes carry their own pins: `a_calibrated_backend_still_compacts_to_health` (a `BillingClient` reporting 4x the heuristic against a target-landing compactor — fail-first red `Err(CompactionStalled { used: 2048 })` on the unconverted tree, green after) and `a_settled_ratio_rescales_the_manager_s_target_units` (a 4x-settled ratio rescales the 2 000-token provider window into 500 raw units; neutralization-red at `2000` when the conversion is removed). The window syncs are window-only — a ratio-driven, probe, or model-switch re-sync preserves a host-installed manager's own `threshold` instead of resetting it to the session config's value (the install-time sync in `set_context_manager` remains session-authoritative as documented); pinned by `a_ratio_resync_preserves_a_host_installed_manager_s_threshold` (neutralization-red at `80 ≠ 50` with the reset restored). A model switch with a context window now routes through the same converting sync instead of writing the provider window verbatim — under a settled non-identity ratio the verbatim write left the manager's target and the trigger line in different scales, the stall shape the conversion exists to prevent; pinned by `a_switch_on_a_calibrated_loop_lands_the_manager_in_raw_units` (fail-first red at `2000 ≠ 500` on the verbatim write) and at the engine seam by `a_calibrated_backend_still_compacts_to_health_after_a_model_switch` (a 4x-billed run whose window arrives by model switch compacts to health; neutralization-red `Err(CompactionStalled { used: 2048 })` on the verbatim write — the target lands above the trigger line and the run dies on the no-progress guard).

- **`MockApiClient::without_usage()`** — the testing mock gains an additive builder that serves its scripted responses with no usage payload, the shape of a backend that reports no usage, for fixtures asserting identity behavior on the engine's estimate side; the fixed default report stays. The threshold-driven compaction suites (`compaction_noop`, `compaction_telemetry`, `demotion_sink`, `search_memories_tool`, and the unwired-subsystems hook modules) adopted the usage-free fixture so their pins keep testing threshold arithmetic rather than the calibration's clamp floor — their staged usage reports were fixed token counts unrelated to the scripted payloads, and against grown histories they crashed the ratio to its floor before the thresholds could trip.

- **Usage-anchored context accounting + no-LLM tool-output reclamation + overflow turn salvage** — three additions that let real numbers carry the window decision, reclaim tool-output bytes without a summarizer, and stop dying on a rejection the engine can survive. **Anchoring:** when the provider reports usable `input_tokens`, the context figure becomes `reported_input + calibrated(estimate of the appends since that report)` — the real number carries the figure, the error surface shrinks from the whole history to the tail; the anchor pairs the report with the calibration's own raw-prediction stash of the request it describes, arms only on non-zero reports (an all-zero backend report shape never anchors), and invalidates wherever the wire changes wholesale (a compaction pass, any model change — a different tokenizer breaks the pairing), and its reported age counts on the session-wide turn basis — an anchor surviving into later runs that report no usage reads as aging, not fresh; with no anchor the figure is exactly the calibrated estimate, so usage-less backends are byte-identical. The calibration ratio keeps its narrowed role: the figure's mechanism whenever no fresh anchor exists. Hosts see which figure drove the last trigger through `RunEndContext.context_figure: Option<ContextFigureInfo>` (`source`: `Anchored`/`Estimated`, plus the anchor's age in completed turns; additive `with_context_figure` builder). **Pruning:** `PruneConfig` (opt-in via `set_prune`, default absent = off; the prune line must sit strictly below the compaction threshold — validated with `InvalidInput`, never clamped) arms a standalone pass at the pre-request site before the compaction deferral: tool results beyond a protected tail token budget (default `2 000`) are cleared — untagged first, `Requery` only when the untagged pool cannot satisfy the minimum-free gate (a fraction of the window, default 10%, evaluated in the counter's raw units — the calibration's window conversion — so a settled ratio cannot distort the gate's verdict), `Durable` never — their content replaced by the `[cleared: prior tool output]` marker, the originals handed to the `DemotionSink` under the new `CompactReason::Prune` — with their identity intact (call id, tool name, error flag, retention class) and in a deterministic oldest-first batch — the machine adopting the rewritten conversation through the new `LoopMachine::adopt_pruned` (history/pending split preserved, no state transition). A prune that frees enough can avert the compaction it would otherwise defer to. **Salvage:** a provider rejection whose text reports an oversized prompt gets the turn's one compact-and-retry — the failed turn fires its turn-end event, the machine is forced onto the emergency compaction path (or, when the conversation has nothing to shrink, the figure resets and the turn re-enters directly), and the same turn is re-requested against the compacted history; the forced pass lands at a target strictly under the payload the provider refused — half the refused estimate, or the configured target when smaller — because the configured target can sit above what a provider whose real limit is below the declared window accepts, leaving a target-observing compactor nothing to shed and the pass declining into a no-progress stall; the budget is per run (turn numbers restart, and the spent retry is cleared at run start); a repeat rejection on a spent turn is a genuine misfit, dying `ContextExceeded` instead of looping. Whatever still kills the run, the overflow family's run-end carries `unfinished_input` (additive `with_unfinished_input` builder) so a host can offer one-click resubmission. Pinned by `the_context_figure_anchors_on_a_usage_report` and `appended_exchanges_grow_the_anchored_figure_by_their_estimate` (the delta is the appends' calibrated estimate and nothing else), `an_anchored_figure_counts_the_transients_that_re_ride_the_next_request` (a contributor-bearing loop's anchored figure carries the transient estimate that re-rides each request), `a_compaction_pass_invalidates_the_anchor_until_the_next_report`, `the_next_report_after_a_pass_re_arms_the_anchor`, `a_cross_run_anchor_ages_without_new_reports` (the session-wide age basis), `a_model_change_invalidates_the_anchor`, `a_zero_usage_report_never_anchors`, `run_end_reports_the_figure_source_and_anchor_age`; `pruning_clears_untagged_results_beyond_the_protected_tail` (marker, verbatim tail, sink handoff under `Prune`), `cleared_content_rides_the_demotion_sink` (identity and oldest-first order), `a_prune_pass_clears_a_report_armed_anchor`, `durable_results_survive_pruning_and_requery_clears_last`, `a_prune_below_the_minimum_free_gate_is_a_no_op`, `the_minimum_free_gate_reads_the_counter_s_scale`, `pruning_is_off_by_default` (plus the threshold and minimum-free validation), `a_prune_that_frees_enough_avoids_the_compaction`; `an_oversized_prompt_rejection_compacts_and_retries_the_same_turn` (`HardWindowClient` — a provider whose real limit the estimate cleared), `a_second_overflow_rejection_ends_the_run` (with the usage floored at the window and the terminal attempt's turn events paired), `a_follow_up_run_s_salvage_is_not_denied_by_the_prior_run_s_spent_retry`, `an_overflow_run_end_carries_the_unfinished_input`, `the_salvage_pass_lands_under_the_refused_payload_with_the_default_compactor` (the forced target under the refused payload — red pre-fix at the no-progress stall), `a_cancel_during_a_hanging_prune_sink_ends_the_run_typed`. Neutralization-proven: anchor arming disabled turned exactly the anchor pins red (`Estimated ≠ Anchored`); the marker replacement removed turned exactly the clear pins red; the retry arm disabled turned exactly the salvage pins red; the compaction invalidation removed turned exactly the invalidation pin red. Two legacy pins of the old reject-and-discard contract (`an_anthropic_prompt_too_long_rejection_discards`, `a_provider_context_rejection_discards_instead_of_salvaging`) are re-cut to the salvage contract (`..._recovers_via_retry`) — their scripted one-rejection-then-recovery fixtures now complete through the retry, which is the feature working.
- **`loopctl-client` — the family's shared daemon client, the seam (fifth companion crate, `crates/client`, `default = []` with no optional dependencies)** — the protocol code lives in one crate so no family binary smears it across its internals: the CLI's daemon-facing verbs, a host TUI's attach/stream/approval surfaces, and future third-party clients all consume `DaemonClient` through one object-safe `LoopctlClient` interface, and in-process consumers implement the same interface — a binary picks a mode without its call sites changing shape. The wire dialect is HTTP/1.1 REST + SSE over a per-user unix socket with `/v1` versioned paths and JSON bodies, carried by a deliberately minimal in-crate transport (one socket, no TLS/HTTP/2/redirects; connection-per-request, `Connection: close` — the curl-per-command shape) that implements all three response framings a compliant peer may use: declared `Content-Length`, chunked transfer coding (decoded incrementally through a cancel-safe chunk state machine — every phase transition commits only after its bytes are consumed, so the event stream's polled, dropped, and recreated reads resume exactly where the last completed step left off; chunked precedence is header-order-independent per RFC 9112 §6.3), and close-delimiting — every read bounded against unbounded peers (a 64 KiB head cap, a 4 MiB line cap, a 10 MiB one-shot body cap, conflicting duplicate `Content-Length` headers refused, and the reader's buffer compacted as frames are consumed, so a long-lived subscription's resident memory tracks the unread window, not the stream's lifetime). Construction performs the versioned handshake (`GET /v1/hello` → `{family, protocol, floor}`) and refuses a family member outside the negotiated floor in **either** direction: a foreign family, a server below the client's floor, and a server whose own floor is above the client's version all reject naming both sides — an old or too-new peer is never silently absorbed. The typed verb layer covers the flagship surface thinly (loops `ls`/`get`, runs `start`/`get`/`stop`, gates `ls`/`approve`/`deny` with reasons, schedules `ls`/`run-now`) over one generic request path with typed error surfacing (`{status, code, message}` envelopes); the observation surface is `subscribe(loop, since)` — an SSE stream whose `id:` is the resume cursor and whose `data:` is one trajectory-JSONL event (the pinned interchange types, reused from `loopctl::memory::trajectory` so the wire and the ledger cannot drift), with multi-`data:` joins, comment and `retry:` tolerance, and HTTP `410` mapped to the typed `CursorExpired` that states the relist contract. In-process consumers adapt their own event sources through `EventStream::from_pin_box` — the subscription surface is mode-unified in full, not only the verb signatures. An absent socket is the typed `SocketAbsent` — the in-process degrade signal — or, under an attached host-supplied spawn action, a bounded visible retry window ending in a typed `ConnectTimeout`; `connect_default()` resolves `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/loopctl/loopctl.sock` and honors `LOOPCTL_NO_AUTOSPAWN=1`. Pinned by the mock-daemon contract suite (`crates/client/tests/client.rs`, 31 tests against a local scripted UDS server — raw scripted responses so the framings are pinned, recorded requests so the paths and bodies are): the three framing decodes, `connect_performs_the_versioned_handshake`, `handshake_floor_refuses_mismatched_family_member`, `an_older_server_refuses_within_the_floor`, `a_newer_server_refuses_by_its_own_floor`, `error_responses_surface_status_code_and_message`, `verbs_hit_their_versioned_paths_with_json_bodies` (all eleven paths and methods, and every body-carrying verb's payload), `the_event_stream_yields_events_with_monotone_cursors`, `a_multi_data_frame_joins_into_one_event`, `a_malformed_event_payload_surfaces_as_a_typed_error`, `a_stale_cursor_returns_typed_gone_for_relisting`, `an_absent_socket_is_a_typed_error_not_a_panic`, `autospawn_spawns_then_connects_within_the_bounded_window`, `an_unspawnable_daemon_gives_up_after_the_bounded_window`, `no_autospawn_env_disables_spawning`, `default_socket_path_follows_xdg_runtime_dir` (EnvGuard-held), and `one_interface_serves_both_modes` (the in-repo form of the family's share-one-implementation claim — a second, in-process impl behind `Box<dyn LoopctlClient>` beside the daemon), the error-path pins (`a_hello_miss_answers_not_a_daemon`, both verb-404 envelope arms, `a_failing_spawn_action_surfaces_spawn_failed`, `an_unset_runtime_dir_is_a_typed_error`, `an_error_without_the_envelope_surfaces_the_lossy_body_text`, `a_data_frame_without_an_id_is_refused`, `a_non_numeric_id_cursor_is_refused`), the stream-feed pins (`a_chunked_sse_feed_with_split_writes_delivers_events` — a chunked event stream fed piece by piece mid-syntax, fail-first red on the cancel-unsafe decoder with `a chunk was not CRLF-terminated`, neutralization-proven by reintroducing the eager phase transition; `chunked_precedence_is_header_order_independent`; `construction_still_works_through_the_constructors` and `optional_fields_deserialize_leniently_from_older_daemons` — the five wire DTOs are `#[non_exhaustive]` with `#[must_use]` constructors, `Option` fields lenient by serde's own rule, the one non-optional defaulted field (`suspended`) declared explicitly and pinned), the in-process stream adapter pin (`an_in_process_subscription_delivers_through_the_shared_event_type`), the monotone-cursor second read, and the transport pins (`the_reader_reclaims_consumed_bytes_on_a_long_stream`, `a_body_at_the_total_cap_decodes_and_one_past_refuses`, and the duplicate-`Content-Length` pair). Neutralization-proven six ways: the family check, both floor checks, the chunked framing, the 410 mapping, and the SSE id→cursor each suspended → exactly their pins red, restored → green. The crate is the protocol's client-side executable spec: a conforming daemon must satisfy these pinned wire contracts. Unix-only by design — the transport is the unix socket, stated in the crate docs and enforced by a compile-time guard the Windows cross-check asserts.

- **Turn-end history publishing — `TurnEndContext.history: Arc<Vec<Message>>`** — hosts can read the machine's conversation at every turn boundary while `run(&mut self)` still holds the loop (`BareLoop::machine()` is a borrow the run owns, and run resolution is too late for a hard-killed process). A successful turn end carries exactly `LoopMachine::full_history()` — the committed history plus the run's turns so far, ending with the turn's complete exchange, a coherent set a host may snapshot or seed a resume from; a failed turn end carries the committed history only — an interrupted exchange, the run's own input included, never rides. All four firing sites populate it (the tool-free and tool-phase successes, the dispatch-failure arm, and the cancelled/stream-error/loop-detection arm through the shared failure helper); the cost is one O(history) clone per turn end, `Arc`-shared so multiple observers receive the same snapshot without a copy each — precisely the copy a persisting host would make anyway. The seam's incident: a cancelled compaction run lost its completed pass *host-side* — loopctl's in-memory compaction survives the same-turn cancel (`compaction_result` rewrites the history the moment the pass lands, before any turn completes), but no host-facing surface could observe that until run resolution, so a hard kill after the cancel flushed the pre-compaction conversation instead. Pinned by `a_completed_pass_survives_cancellation_of_the_same_turn` (the in-memory durability characterization — green today, red the day the compaction commit moves to turn/run completion; neutralization-proven on wrong-set adoption, and any mutant that skips adoption of an over-window history wedges the run in a re-compact loop rather than passing silently), `on_turn_end_carries_the_committed_history_plus_the_completed_exchange`, `a_failed_turn_end_carries_only_committed_history` (both neutralization-red on an empty/over-filled field), and `a_dispatch_failed_turn_end_carries_only_committed_history` (the fourth firing site — the dispatch-failure arm — pinned the same way: neutralization-red under both an over-filled and an empty snapshot), and `turn_end_history_reflects_a_mid_run_compaction` (the first event after a mid-run pass carries exactly the compacted set plus the post-compact exchange).

- **Prompt-cache breakpoints on the Anthropic client — `AnthropicClientBuilder::prompt_caching(bool)`, off by default** — multi-turn loops re-send the whole conversation every request, and the provider's prompt cache exists exactly to keep that re-send cheap, but the wire never asked for it. When enabled, every request from both wire paths (streaming and non-streaming share one body-builder exit, so the two can never disagree) carries `cache_control` on up to four breakpoints: the last tool definition, the system prompt (rewritten from its plain-string form into one marked text block; an empty system stays a string — marking nothing buys no cache), and the last two eligible content blocks of the final message — thinking and redacted-thinking blocks are skipped (the provider rejects `cache_control` on them, so a signed-thinking tail marks the text blocks around it instead) — the placement that keeps the previous request's prefix cached while rolling the frontier forward one turn at a time. A final message whose content is a plain string is wrapped into a one-block array so it can carry the marker. Off by default: requests stay byte-identical to a client that never asked to cache, and endpoints that reject the extra field simply leave it off. Pinned by `prompt_cache_breakpoints_mark_tools_system_and_tail_blocks` (all four placements, plus the non-tail message keeping its string shape), `prompt_cache_breakpoints_wrap_string_tail_and_skip_empty_system`, and `prompt_cache_breakpoints_skip_thinking_blocks` (a signed-thinking tail carries no marker on its thinking block).

- **Cached-token accounting — `Usage::cached_input_tokens` (with `with_cached_input`), parsed by every shipped provider** — a prompt cache only pays if the host can see the hit rate, so the usage type grows the cached share of the input count (a subset, never additional) and each provider fills it from its native report: Anthropic `usage.cache_read_input_tokens` — whose raw `input_tokens` **excludes** cache traffic, so every Anthropic-shaped conversion (non-streaming reader, the streaming `message_start`→`message_delta` latch, and Bedrock's Anthropic-shape responses) normalizes the input to raw input + cached reads + cache-creation spend, keeping the cached share the documented subset of the input; a delta without cache fields keeps the latched value — OpenAI-compatible `usage.prompt_tokens_details.cached_tokens` (object and field both defaulted, and the cached count parsed leniently — a non-numeric placeholder from an OpenAI-compatible server reads as zero instead of failing the whole usage object), and Bedrock Converse `cacheReadInputTokens`. Constructors are unchanged — `Usage::new` leaves the cached share at zero and `with_cached_input` attaches it after — so existing call sites and serialized usages (the field is `#[serde(default)]`) are unaffected. Pinned by `build_response_reports_cached_input_tokens` (the normalized input total: 1500 raw + 1200 read + 300 creation reports 3000 in, 1200 cached), `emitter_merges_cached_input_tokens_across_start_and_delta`, `emitter_keeps_latched_cached_tokens_when_delta_omits_them` (both re-pinned to the normalized totals), and the OpenAI triple `parse_final_chunk_with_cached_tokens_detail` / `parse_final_chunk_without_cached_tokens_detail_defaults_to_zero` / `a_non_numeric_cached_count_defaults_to_zero_without_dropping_usage`.

- **The external vector-store backends — 📦 `loopctl-vector`, the fourth companion crate, with two opt-in features (`qdrant`, `pgvector`; `default = []`)** — semantic memory past one process: `QdrantIndex` (the dedicated vector DB, self-hosted or cloud — memory shared across sessions and hosts) and `PgVectorIndex` (one `vector(n)` table plus an HNSW index in the Postgres a team already runs — zero new infrastructure). Both implement `VectorIndex` behind their own feature, so `VectorMemoryStore::new(embedder, Box::new(index))` swaps tiers with one constructor call and nothing else changes — same hybrid retrieval, same `LoopMemory` contract, same golden-set bar (the ≥ 80% precision gate runs against every backend). Each backend auto-provisions its target at construction (collection / `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` + `vector_cosine_ops` HNSW — idempotent under concurrent construction; pgvector additionally self-loads the `vector` extension, tolerating the concurrent-create catalog race), validates blank names and zero dimensions up front, and — because its table name is interpolated into DDL that cannot be parameterized — pgvector accepts only strict-identifier table names; an existing target's vector shape is validated before adoption (pgvector reads the `embedding` column's catalog dimension and requires the `id` column to be a not-null `uuid` under a unique, immediate, non-partial index over exactly `id` — the key shape its `ON CONFLICT (id)` upsert arbitrates; qdrant checks the collection's unnamed single-vector configuration, dimension, cosine distance, default float32 datatype, and absence of a multivector configuration), so a dimension-mismatched reuse — and a foreign table, a text- or partially- or nullable-keyed id, a named-vector layout, a quantized (`float16`/`uint8`/`turbo4`) datatype, a `MaxSim` multivector comparator, or a non-cosine metric likewise — rejects at connect instead of handing back a handle that fails, or silently mis-scores, on use. Both external tiers reject non-finite and zero-norm vectors at `add` and such queries at `search` with a named `LoopError::Memory` (cosine over a degenerate vector is undefined, and the pgvector index scan silently drops zero-norm rows — `HashingEmbedder` over empty text produces exactly that — while qdrant returns them; the in-process tiers keep their documented `0.0`-similarity fallback, the external tiers fail loudly instead of diverging silently), and a `k` of `0` returns an empty result on every backend (pinned in the shared contract suite; the server-side `limit` validators reject `0`). Failures map through one bounded helper to `LoopError::Memory("backend: …")` so no backend error type leaks; every search emits the same `loopctl.vector.index.search` counter the in-process indexes emit, plus a once-per-target provision event and a `loopctl.vector.provision.errors` counter. pgvector searches run inside one transaction that sizes `hnsw.ef_search` to `k` through a parameterized `set_config` (transaction-local, so pooled connections never leak the setting), which keeps a `k` above pgvector's 40 candidate-list default from silently capping results at 40 on index-scan plans, and a `k` above the setting's server ceiling of 1000 rejects with the ceiling named rather than returning short. The trait's sync `len` (an in-process shape) is answered by construction-seeded client-side bookkeeping (`RemoteCount`): the async constructors fetch the true server count once, `len` is then a lock read, exact while this process is the only writer since construction — clones of a handle share the one ledger (`Arc`), so every handle of a construction answers the same count — and cross-process writes are invisible until a fresh construction, by design and documented. Construction is `QdrantIndex::builder(url, collection, dim).connect()` (or `from_env` over `QDRANT_URL`/`QDRANT_COLLECTION`/`QDRANT_API_KEY`) and `PgVectorIndexBuilder::new(url, table, dim)` (`PG_VECTOR_URL`/`PG_VECTOR_TABLE`). A collection deleted mid-life does not wedge a qdrant handle: the next operation that fails naming a missing collection re-provisions once (a fresh, empty collection whose provision event is the visible trace of the loss) and retries itself. The crate is an excluded side-workspace with its own dependency graph and lockfile — the lockfile is untracked like the root's, so CI resolves it fresh — (the root workspace's own jobs never resolve the graph; `vector-check` compiles the trees within the side-workspace), gated by two new `make` targets — `vector-check` (fmt + clippy + check + docs + the crate's unit and doctests, across the no-feature, each single-feature, and the all-feature configurations, riding `make ci`) and `vector-e2e` (the live suites behind `LOOPCTL_VECTOR_E2E=1`) — and two new CI jobs: the service-less `vector-check` and the services-backed `vector-e2e` (`qdrant/qdrant` + `pgvector/pgvector` containers, gated on service readiness — `pg_isready` against the `postgres` database for Postgres; a runner-side `/readyz` poll for the Qdrant image, which ships no shell tooling). The shared contract suite (the crate's own `contract` module behind its `testing` feature, runnable against any `VectorIndex` implementation) runs six tests per backend — upsert-replaces, cosine ordering + k, remove/len, dim-mismatch, provisioning idempotence — beside the per-backend golden-set recall test, plus the pgvector `<=>` mapping pin (`pgvector_cosine_distance_maps_to_similarity_order`, neutralization-red under the inverted mapping), the connect-time shape-rejection pins (fail-first red on the unvalidated tree: pgvector `a_dimension_mismatched_target_rejects_at_connect` + `a_foreign_table_without_a_vector_embedding_column_rejects_at_connect` + the key-shape family `a_text_keyed_table_rejects_at_connect` / `a_partially_indexed_id_rejects_at_connect` / `a_nullable_id_rejects_at_connect` with the adoption counterpart `a_uuid_primary_keyed_table_is_adopted`, qdrant `a_dimension_mismatched_target_rejects_at_connect` + `a_named_vector_collection_rejects_at_connect` + `a_non_cosine_collection_rejects_at_connect` + `a_float16_datatype_collection_rejects_at_connect` + `a_multivector_collection_rejects_at_connect`), the degenerate-vector rejections (`a_zero_norm_vector_rejects_at_add` / `a_zero_norm_query_rejects_at_search` on both backends, fail-first red on the accepting tree; the unit-level `shared_validation_rejects_unusable_vectors`), the shared-ledger pin (`cloned_handles_share_one_count_ledger`, fail-first red on the deep-copying tree), the missing-collection recovery pin (`a_deleted_collection_reprovisions_on_the_next_operation`, fail-first red on the wedging tree), and the contract suite's `zero_k_returns_empty` (fail-first red on the qdrant error), and the pgvector `ef_search` pins (`a_k_above_the_default_candidate_list_returns_every_row` — k=60 over 8 000 analyzed rows returns 60, red at 40 on the unfixed tree — and `a_k_above_the_ef_search_ceiling_rejects`, where k=1001 rejects naming the 1000 ceiling and k=1000 returns full results, red at 40 on the unfixed tree), and the strict-identifier and env-profile unit pins; a `#[ignore]`d `qdrant_cloud_smoke` covers the managed tier. The choosing-a-store table (linear / hnsw / qdrant / pgvector, one line each) lives in the crate docs. `loopctl` itself is untouched beyond the workspace `exclude` entry — the hnsw precedent; hosts depend on the companion directly. Publishes after loopctl, like the other companions.

- **The grant store + tool descriptor digests (`tool::grant`: core types + the sync object-safe `GrantStore` seam; file impl behind a new dep-free `file_grants` feature; `default = []` intact)** — the persisted half of interactive approval, the TCC ledger: `save` upserts a `Grant` whose identity is the content-stable 16-hex digest of its `{tool, verdict, scope}` triple (re-granting a rule — including re-approving after a descriptor change — replaces its own entry instead of accumulating duplicates), `revoke` removes and returns the grant for the audit record, `clear` is the reset verb, and `load` treats a missing ledger as empty. The narrowness ladder is owned vocabulary with mechanical matching: `GrantScope::ThisTool` covers every call to the named tool, `ThisCall { args }` covers exactly the argument sets canonically equal to the stored one — key order never decides coverage — via `Grant::covers`; suggesting the narrowest rule for an approval and deny-over-allow precedence stay consumer policy (the approver's job, the host's ask UX), while `GrantVerdict` persists denies exactly like allows. The rug-pull defense rides the new defaulted `Tool::descriptor_digest()`: 16-hex FNV-1a-64 over canonical JSON of `{name, description, schema}`, the `GateDecision::args_digest` family — the recursive key-sorted canonicalizer moved from `permission.rs` to `tool` as a shared helper, `args_digest` byte-identical — so first-use approval can pin what the user actually saw and a later mismatch is the re-ask signal, stable across hosts and `serde_json` map backends under the same cross-backend guarantee the `digest-canonical` gate pins. `FileGrantStore` binds one project per directory (`<root>/projects/<16-hex project-path digest>/grants.json`, the reference layout the CLI documents as `~/.local/state/<app>/grants/`): the project identity resolves through a best-effort most-canonical ladder (canonicalize, else canonicalize-the-parent-and-join, else lexically absolute), so a project bound before its directory exists keeps its ledger after creation in the common cases; the file is created owner-only `0600` like the ledgers and replaced atomically on every write, staged under a per-process name beside the ledger whose owner-only mode is reset on open (a crashed write cannot leave a wide staged file behind) — the concurrency contract is single-writer, one process per project; concurrent processes over one project are unsupported (the last replace silently wins); `save`/`revoke` return the affected grant so hosts mint the gate-decision audit record. Pinned by `grants_are_project_scoped_and_revocable` (a sibling project sees nothing, revoke removes-and-returns, the persisted deny round-trips), `descriptor_hash_changes_when_schema_changes` (schema, name, and description each move the digest; identical descriptors stable across instances), `grant_scope_matching_is_canonical_not_key_order`, `a_regrant_of_the_same_rule_upserts_idempotently`, `a_created_grant_file_is_owner_only` (red under a mode-removed mutant), and `grant_store_survives_reload`.

- **The budget gate (`budget`, core-additive — no feature, no manifest-type change)** — spend accumulation with soft and hard lines, the "budgets are gates" decision made real: one engine-side `BudgetGate` consulted before each turn's model request. `BareLoop::with_budget_gate` installs it; the run begins it, the pre-request point (`do_turn`) consults it, and the response/turn-end paths feed it — provider-reported tokens (input+output from usage records, never estimates), completed turns (counted at turn end, so an in-flight turn never counts against itself), and wall-clock (the elapsed reading of the caller's clock seam passed as a `Duration`, so the gate itself never reads a clock and a replayed run crosses the same lines at the same logical instants). Per dimension, the **soft** line — a fraction of the hard limit, default 80%, movable via a validating `with_soft_fraction` (out-of-range is a loud `InvalidInput`, never clamped) — emits `on_budget_warn` (new `LoopObserver` callback + `BudgetWarnContext`, forwarded by the `EventHub` as `LoopEvent::BudgetWarn` with the `budget_warn` name) exactly once per run per dimension, however many checks pass between the lines; the **hard** line refuses the *next* request before it is sent, failing the turn with the new non-recoverable `LoopError::BudgetExhausted { dimension, spent, limit }` (the `MaxTurnsExceeded` precedent with the numbers for diagnostics) and minting a `GateDecision` deny record (rule id `budget.hard`, rule source middleware, tool `model_request`, reason naming the dimension and numbers) onto the same gate-decision trail every other gate writes, so audit and replay see budget enforcement like any gate — one engine, no parallel budget knob. A spend rocketing past both lines in one response still records its warn alongside the refusal. `BudgetLimits` is the gate's own input shape (`turns`/`tokens`/`duration`, `None` disables a line); the manifest's advisory `Budgets` stanza maps into it behind the `manifest` feature, with `cost_usd` deliberately mapping to nothing (no price table is consulted, so cost has no line — the gate refuses to guess a cost) and an unparseable `duration` string disabling the wall-clock line with a warn rather than guessing. The duration grammar is `parse_duration`'s own: number-then-unit pairs over `s`/`m`/`h` (`90s`, `5m`, `4h`, `1h30m`, `2h45m10s`), loud `InvalidInput`s on garbage. The soft-line arithmetic is exact integer math on the fraction's four-decimal rational form (`soft_line`/`rationalize` — no float casts, no rounding above the product, and a `u128` intermediate that cannot saturate below it, so the warn can never trail the refusal; the unit pins swept limits 1..100_000 × fractions 0.1..1.0 and pin fraction `1.0` to the hard limit at limits past the `u64` product bound). Pinned by the unit suite (grammar valid/sequence/garbage, fraction validation, soft-exactly-once, hard-numbers-plus-latched-warn, turns, wall-clock via the elapsed argument, `begin` reset for the next run, disabled lines inert, `BudgetExhausted` non-recoverable + Display, the soft≤hard sweep, and the two manifest-mapping pins under `--all-features`) plus two engine pins over a scripted `MockApiClient` run — `soft_threshold_emits_budget_warn_once` (six 75-token responses against a 400-token budget: the 375-spend check warns once with the (375, 400) numbers, the 450-spend check refuses, and the `budget.hard` deny is on the trail) and `hard_threshold_blocks_the_next_request_not_the_current_one` (two 75-token responses against 100: both complete — the crossing response's text is in the conversation and exactly two provider calls were made — and the third request is refused before it is sent, with the record's reason carrying "150 of 100"). `with_budget_gate` takes the gate by value — a shared handle let one loop's `begin()` wipe or top up another's counters mid-run, admitting an over-budget request or refusing the wrong run — so accounting is local to each loop by construction; the interference was reproduced red at the gate level before the fix, and locality is pinned by `two_loops_keep_independent_budget_accounting` (a second loop nested inside the first's tool dispatch, spending under its own gate, never shortens the first loop's served requests). A hard crossing no longer swallows the other dimensions' first soft crossings observed by the same check — every enabled dimension latches in the same check, the tokens → turns → wall-clock priority picks only the refusal — pinned by `a_hard_crossing_still_emits_the_other_dimensions_soft_warn` (red on the early-return swallow); and `with_soft_fraction` rejects fractions below the `0.0001` rationalization floor with the same loud `InvalidInput` instead of silently disabling every soft line, pinned by `a_fraction_below_the_rationalization_floor_is_rejected_loudly` (red on the accepted `1e-5`).

- **The shell tool family (`tool::builtin::shell`, new `shell_tools` feature: `builtin_tools` + `libc` + `tokio/process` + `tokio/io-util`; off by default, no preset registers it)** — the `Shell` tool and the `Jobs` tool over a pluggable `ShellBackend` seam. The model-facing name is `Shell`, not `bash`, so gate policies stay OS-portable; the schema is neutral (`command` required alone — no `anyOf`, grammar-server-safe — plus `timeout_s` default 120 clamp 600 zero-rejected, and `background`). The Unix backend (`UnixShellBackend`, `#[cfg(unix)]`) ports the original mechanics: `bash -c` in its own process group with `kill_on_drop`, an RAII `ChildGuard` whose drop SIGKILLs the whole group on timeout (sub-shells, pipelines, and grandchildren die with the deadline — and a reaped child disarms the guard so a detached helper survives), both streams read concurrently under a one-million-byte per-stream cap with drain-past-cap so a chatty child cannot block or exhaust memory, the shared `...[output truncated]` marker, and the `[exit {code}, {duration_ms}ms]` metadata line with nonzero exits rendered as error-text. The seam is the high-level object-safe `run(command, cwd, timeout) -> ShellOutcome` — the backend owns spawn, capture, and kill mechanics (job objects on Windows), the tool owns parsing, schema, shaping, and jobs; platforms without a backend answer an honest `Execution` refusal while the schema stays present for gate rules. Background jobs split as the original did: `background: true` records a running entry in a host-created shared `JobStore` (no process global — hosts hand one `Arc` to both tools, so every consumer gets an isolated slate) and detaches a completion task bounded by the job's timeout; the `Jobs` tool (`operation` ∈ `jobs`/`job_status`/`cleanup_jobs`, single required field) lists, polls, and clears. Stored payloads are middle-cut under the render cap with header room reserved; renderings bound commands at 256 bytes; terminal jobs evict past twenty. Dynamic concurrency is the allowlist — boundary-aware read-only prefixes (`cargo check` yes, `cargo checkout` no) with compound/redirection/control-character disqualifiers, the mutating-substring denylist (`find -delete`, `git diff --output`, `git branch -D`, …), and expansion disqualifiers (any `$` or `{`, because the shell expands `${IFS}`, `$'…'`, and `{a,b}` into words the checked text never shows) driving `is_safe_for_concurrent_execution`. Pinned by the shipped suites (62 tests: the allowlist boundary matrix, `read_bounded` growth/caps/drain/lossy-expansion, dual-stream capture, timeout killing the direct child and pipelines, truncation markers, detached-helper survival, the job store's listing/poll/cleanup/eviction/payload-cap/summary bounds, both schema shapes) including the named pins — `timeout_kills_the_process_group_unix` (a backgrounded marker-writing subshell dies with the group: only a process-group kill reaches it), `background_jobs_report_on_poll` (running in the listing, then the terminal payload with the captured output and exit metadata), and `schema_name_is_shell_not_bash` (the wire name is `Shell`, never `bash`) — plus `no_default_registry_enables_shell_tools` (the preset builders never construct the family) and the input-typing pins `a_wrong_typed_timeout_is_rejected_not_defaulted` / `a_wrong_typed_background_flag_is_rejected_not_defaulted` / `an_absent_timeout_still_takes_the_default` — a present-but-wrong-typed `timeout_s` (string, negative, float, null, boolean) or `background` is an `InvalidInput` naming the field, never a silent default, matching the shared search-parse rule the family was held to. Further pinned: `an_unavailable_backend_refuses_a_background_spawn_synchronously` (a `ShellBackend::available` probe — default `true`, `false` on the placeholder backend — is consulted before a background spawn is acknowledged, so a backend-less platform refuses synchronously with the same `Execution` text as a foreground run and records no job). Also pinned: `a_wrong_typed_command_is_rejected_as_typed_not_missing` with `a_missing_command_still_says_missing` and the `operation`/`job_id` siblings — required-field prompts split missing from wrong-typed, so a model that sent the field is told the expected type, not that it is missing. The one sanctioned `tokio::spawn` is the background completion task: its escaping lifetime is the feature, bounded by the timeout and the terminal-job cap. Also pinned: `terminal_eviction_keeps_the_newest_bound_and_never_a_running_job` (the eviction bound claimed here, verified through two neutralized eviction defects — no eviction at all, and running jobs counting against the cap), and the `cfg(not(unix))` placeholder backend is now compiled by a `windows-check` gate (`cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --features shell_tools,fs_tools,search_tools`, riding `make ci` and CI beside `darwin-clippy`) — demonstrated necessary: an undefined symbol inside the `#[cfg(not(unix))]` block passes every Linux gate green. Test targets stay out of the gate because dev-dependencies pull C-coded TLS crates (`ring`) that cannot cross-compile to msvc; the placeholder's refusal behavior is instead pinned on Unix through the fake-unavailable backend behind `an_unavailable_backend_refuses_a_background_spawn_synchronously`. The operator list carried `$(` but not bare `$` or `{`, so the expansion spellings `find . ${IFS}-delete`, `find . $'-delete'`, and `find . {-delete,}` passed `is_read_only_command` and a deleting `find` could dispatch in parallel — the list now refuses any `$` or `{` outright (subsuming `$(`), deliberately costing a benign `echo $HOME` its parallelism, and the tool's doc names the registering host's authority (the working directory is a starting point, not a sandbox), which closes the security question as a documented trust boundary rather than a defect. Pinned fail-first by `concurrency_check_shell_expansion_spellings_unsafe` (the three spellings plus unbraced `$IFS`, a quoted variant, `cat $(echo hi)`, `ls ${PWD}`, and the conservative `echo $HOME` direction — watched red on the unfixed classifier) with the three spellings joined into `concurrency_check_find_mutating_unsafe` and `concurrency_check_allowlist_hits` standing green as the no-over-block guard.

- **The search tool family (`tool::builtin::search`, new `search_tools` feature: `builtin_tools` + `ignore` + `lru` + `regex`; `default = []` intact)** — `GlobTool`, `GrepTool`, `CodeSearchTool`, and `TreeTool`, all four generic over a new `SearchSource` seam (a supertrait of `ContentSource` adding `walk_files`/`walk_entries`/`read_capped`/`likely_binary`/`file_too_large` over a neutral `SourceEntry`), with `FsSearchSource` as the shipped filesystem implementation — the seam exists so a remote tree source (a `GitHub` tree at a pinned revision) can serve the same tools later without touching them, and `Arc`-shared sources serve the seam directly so several tools can share one instrumented source. The walker is the ignore-crate machinery the family was ported with: `.gitignore`/`.git/info/exclude`/global gitignore inside git work trees (the `ignore` crate's `require_git` default — pinned as such), custom ignore files `.loopctlignore` **and** `.dchignore` applied regardless of git (a recorded superset over the original, which knew only `.dchignore`), and an always-exclude override list (`target/`, `node_modules/`, `.git/`, `__pycache__/`, `.venv/`, `dist/`, `build/`, `.cache/`, …) that prunes even in non-git trees, with directory *nodes* pruned in `walk_entries`, not just their files. Content search runs through a shared `SearchJob` engine — binary files skipped (extension list plus NUL/control-ratio sniff that keeps UTF-8 text readable), reads bounded by a 1 `MiB` per-file ceiling, per-file and global match caps, include/exclude filename globs — with compiled patterns served from a process-global bounded LRU cache (case-insensitivity folded into the key idempotently, so repeated calls never recompile). `Grep` returns `{file, line, content}` rows as pretty JSON; `CodeSearch` returns the token-efficient shape (`file` headers with `line: text` rows, or `file:line` pointers with `±context_lines` snippets when opted in, context clamped to 5); `Glob` sorts and pretty-prints relative paths; `Tree` renders the Unicode box-drawing listing with an `N directories, M files` summary. Oversized results spill to a uniquely named file under the `ToolContext` temp directory with a ~10 `KiB` character-boundary preview and a pointer to the Read tool, degrading to inline truncation when the spill cannot be written — the caller always gets a usable result. Search roots resolve lexically against the context cwd (`.`/`..` collapsed, no filesystem probing, no tilde expansion) and URL-shaped roots are `InvalidInput`s that redirect to a filesystem path before any walk happens. Port decisions recorded: the ported runner's context/containment policy collapses to plain context-cwd resolution (the walker is read-only and the fs-session machinery is `fs_tools`-gated), the `tempfile`-backed spill becomes a hand-rolled process-id + monotonic-counter filename (no new dependency under the feature), `tokio::fs` metadata checks become plain `std::fs` (the `tokio/fs` feature stays `fs_tools`-only), and the `FileViewer` spill pointer re-aims at loopctl's `ReadTool`. Pinned by the ported unit suites (walker: gitignore-in-git-repo, gitignore-not-applied-without-git, both custom ignore names, always-exclude pruning with dir nodes, depth semantics; content engine: global/per-file caps, binary and oversized skips, unreadable-file silence, relative 1-indexed matches; per-tool modules: matchers, parse rules, render shapes, spill behavior) plus `tests/search_tools.rs` over the real filesystem — the named pins `glob_respects_gitignore_like_dch_v1` and `grep_include_exclude_and_context_lines_match_dch_contract`, the family flag/name/schema coherence check, tree gitignore listing, binary skipping, and the spill-under-context-temp-dir scenario — and the cache's own `regex_cache_dedupes_compiled_patterns` pin. Contract pins hardened against adversarial probes: `filter_entries_slash_pattern_matches_relative_paths` (the v1 contract — any `/`-containing pattern matches the path relative to the root, and the walker's `*` crosses `/`, while an `srcextra/` prefix collision stays excluded), `call_rejects_a_wrong_typed_path` for Glob and Tree (a non-string `path` is the same `InvalidInput` correction prompt the shared content parse gives, proven to leave the source untouched), and `glob_spills_oversized_listings_like_the_content_tools` / `tree_spills_oversized_listings_like_the_content_tools` (the spill promise now wired into all four tools, with the spilled bodies re-parsed). PR-round pins: `walk_files_prunes_always_exclude_dirs_at_any_depth` (the always-exclude overrides are `!**/{dir}/**`, so a nested `pkg/node_modules/` no longer rides the root anchoring into `walk_files` results), `arc_shared_sources_keep_the_size_probe` (the `Arc` delegation forwards `ContentSource::size`, keeping the read tool's refuse-before-read guard alive through a shared source), `call_renders_plain_text_without_a_json_hint` with `call_tags_the_json_array_with_the_json_hint` (only Grep's JSON array carries the `Json` display hint; CodeSearch's grouped text is plain), `spill_files_refuse_a_pre_created_path` and `spill_files_are_owner_only` (spill files are created `create_new`-exclusively with mode `0o600` on Unix, degrading to inline truncation on any refusal), and `unicode_folding_and_classes_survive_the_minimal_feature_set` — backed by the new `search_tools-minimal` `make ci` gate, an external consumer probe (the `redaction-minimal` shape) that caught the real defect in-crate tests structurally cannot see: with `search_tools` alone, `regex` ran without `unicode-case`/`unicode-perl` (dev-dependency feature unification hides this inside the crate's own test builds — the probe watched `(?i)` refuse to compile a non-ASCII pattern), so the feature now carries `regex/unicode-case` + `regex/unicode-perl` in exact parity with the crate's `redaction` configuration. `regex/perf` stays off: the crate's regex dep is deliberately minimal (`default-features = false`), `redaction` sets the same two-feature precedent, and perf is speed-only, never semantics. Also pinned: `chunk_split_respects_the_budget_and_round_trips` and `chunk_split_keeps_a_fitting_body_whole` (the pure line-boundary splitter) plus `oversized_spills_chunk_under_the_read_ceiling_and_name_every_file` — a body over the Read tool's own 10 `MiB` refuse-before-read ceiling spills as several files each at or under that ceiling (the budget is `DEFAULT_MAX_SIZE_BYTES` imported, not a second constant, so the two ceilings cannot drift), every path named in a `written to N files:` listing, the chunks reassembling to the exact body — the spill pointer can never again name a file the named tool would refuse to open; a mid-spill failure now also removes the chunks it already wrote (`remove_orphans`, warned best-effort) before degrading to inline truncation, and the `search_tools-minimal` probe manifest writes the real `rust-version` key like its sibling targets. Findings 1–7 re-verified as genuinely fixed with their pins standing (the Unicode parity + minimal gate, the `Arc` size delegation, the plain-text CodeSearch hint, `create_new`+`0o600` spill creation, the `!**/{dir}/**` nested prune, the hyphenated probe `rust-version`, the Read-sized chunk split); the chunk splitter now budget-checks the final unterminated suffix at the last line boundary, pinned by `a_final_suffix_with_no_trailing_newline_cannot_escape_the_budget` (the `"aaaaa\nbbbbbbbb"` under a 10-byte budget rode one 14-byte chunk before the fix) and the end-to-end `oversized_unterminated_spills_chunk_under_the_read_ceiling` (an over-10-`MiB` body in `CodeSearch`'s no-trailing-newline shape still lands as files the Read tool will open, reassembling exactly); and the retained cleanup concern's mechanical half closed by ownership-before-write — the loop is now `spill_chunks` with an injected write step, each file joins the cleanup list at creation so a failed write removes every file including the partially written current one (`a_failed_chunk_write_removes_every_file_including_the_current_one`, fail-first through the behavior-preserving seam refactor). The remaining retained concerns are documented trust boundaries recorded for follow-up scope, not silently dropped: `FsSearchSource` names the authority the registering host chooses (module doc and type doc), and the output module doc names the spill lifecycle — who owns cleanup, and that no pointer is returned unless every chunk reached disk. A single matching line can be far larger than anything Read can return — the output cap bounds the joined numbered view and always cuts at complete lines, so a physical line past the cap could never be emitted at any offset, and the spill chunker's newline-only splitting left such lines whole (a grep row holding a near-`MiB` match, for instance). Spilled bodies now wrap physical lines past the new `MAX_SPILL_LINE_BYTES` (a quarter of Read's `DEFAULT_MAX_BYTES`, now `pub(crate)` so the two cannot drift) at character boundaries, continuation pieces carrying a `↪` marker, and the spill pointer discloses the convention whenever wrapping happened — pinned fail-first by `overlong_lines_wrap_at_character_boundaries_for_read_retrieval` (red on a 12,001-byte physical line surviving whole) and end-to-end by `grep_spilled_overlong_lines_stay_retrievable_through_the_read_tool`, which pages the actual `ReadTool` through the spilled file and recovers the whole 210-`KiB` matched payload from the numbered, marker-stripped lines; the one existing spill fixture whose rows crossed the wrap width was resized with intent unchanged (still spills, still re-parses as JSON). The dead `same-file` dependency is dropped from the feature, the README row, and the docs (its justification died with the collapse of the ported containment policy). The wrong-typed-input corrections this family ships are contract, not accident — a present-but-wrong-typed `pattern` (Glob/Grep/CodeSearch) or Tree `max_depth`/`include_files`/`pattern` answers an `InvalidInput` naming the field and its expected type instead of masquerading as missing (`parse_rejects_a_wrong_typed_pattern_as_typed`, `call_rejects_wrong_typed_max_depth_include_files_and_pattern`, `absent_optional_fields_still_take_their_defaults`); Grep's `Json` display hint applies only to the inline body — a spilled or degraded body is pointer prose and carries none; and `call_max_results_above_cap_is_lowered` discriminates for real (1,100 matches across eleven files, exactly the 1,000-row ceiling asserted from the spilled body). Also pinned: `the_json_hint_sits_exactly_on_the_spill_boundary` with `the_verbatim_flag_marks_the_delivered_inline_arm_and_only_it` (`truncate_or_spill` now returns the output together with whether the body came back verbatim, so Grep's hint keys on the spill decision itself — one predicate, no drift), and `an_absurd_max_depth_clamps_never_silently_defaults` (a `max_depth` above the clamp ceiling — including one a narrower `usize` cannot represent, as on a 32-bit host — meets the ceiling, never a silent default).

- **The metric stream gains its per-tool-call counter and origin-labeled retrieves; the uncut span-style events migrate to counter style** — `loopctl.tools.calls` settles one event per dispatched call on both dispatch paths (the direct registry path and the middleware pipeline share one emission exit, so each call is counted exactly once), carrying `tool`, `outcome=ok|error`, and a seam-sourced `duration_ms` — wall-clock never enters the stream, keeping replay byte-stable. `loopctl.memory.retrieve.results` labels every retrieve by origin — `trigger=turn` from the engine's passive per-turn injection (success and failure both counted), `trigger=tool` from `SearchMemoriesTool` — so passive injection volume and the model choosing to look are distinguishable in one stream, the input to any derived passive-vs-active ratio. The still-uncut span-style events migrate to the counter style in the same pass (`vector.index.search` → `loopctl.vector.index.search` in both the linear and HNSW indexes, `vector.embed` → `loopctl.vector.embed`, `embed.batch` → `loopctl.embed.batch`), and the store-internal events rename into a `memory.store.*` family (`memory.store.retrieve`, `memory.store.consolidate`) so a consumer can never miscount a `VectorMemoryStore`'s internal timing event as an origin-labeled retrieve. The Think tool's self-reported `loopctl.think.calls` is **deprecated**, not removed — it shipped in v0.3.1, so retiring it now would strip released metric-stream behavior without a deprecation cycle; the engine counter already counts every Think call (redundant double counting), the tool's docs carry the deprecation notice, and removal is scheduled for a `0.Y.0` window. Pinned by `tests/tool_call_metrics.rs` (`every_call_and_retrieve_settles_one_labeled_event`, a dedicated capture binary: one event per call on both paths, the outcome split, both trigger labels with exact per-origin retrieve counts, the store-internal `loopctl.memory.store.retrieve` distinguishability scenario over `VectorMemoryStore`, the deprecated `loopctl.think.calls` coexistence with the engine counter, delta-counted scenarios) and the updated capture binaries (the `loopctl-hnsw` search-metrics binary and `tests/embeddings_metrics.rs`).

- **`SearchMemoriesTool` — the model-callable memory window (`memory::search_tool`, no new feature flag: plain `tool` + `memory`)** — the active half of retrieval. The engine's per-turn injection stays passive and bounded (`memory_top_k`, the engine's own query heuristics); this read-only, concurrency-safe `Tool` lets the *model* query any `LoopMemory` store mid-turn (`search_memories`) when it decides the context is missing something — a small model deep in a task recalls the fact demoted three compactions ago instead of hallucinating past the gap. Schema written for small-model tool selection (a `query` plus an optional `limit`); the limit clamps to the schema's own `maximum` of ten — missing defaults to five, zero clamps *up* to one (a deliberate deviation from the crate-wide zero-disables sentinel: a search for zero results is a useless call, and the model deserves the nearest sensible behavior), and the schema description reports the configured default rather than a stale literal — both builder knobs clamp into the advertised bounds (`with_default_limit` into 1..=10, `with_max_result_chars` below 1 up to 1), so no configuration can contradict what the model is told or reduce an entry to nothing — a missing or blank `query` and a present-but-non-integer `limit` are `InvalidInput`s naming the field and its expected type. Output is model-facing text: one `[N] (category)` line per entry, each truncated on a character boundary to a configurable per-entry budget (default 1,000 chars, `with_max_result_chars`); retrieval is ranked, not matched — the store's nearest entries render whether or not they lexically match, and when the store returns nothing the honest `No stored memories match "…"` line renders instead, never an empty string (empty reads as failure to small models); every rendered body carries the same provenance framing as the engine's passive injection (a reference-only header), with `provider-derived` (untrusted, extractor-mined) entries excluded unless the host opts in through `include_provider_derived()`, in which case they render under the stronger untrusted header; a failing store surfaces as a soft `ToolError::Execution` carrying the typed `LoopError` text. The tool opts out of trajectory recording through a new provided `Tool` seam — `records_trajectory()`, default `true` — because a search tool that recorded would store its own answers and the next search would return them; the engine honors it for every tool, resolving through the executed tool's name when middleware redirects a call — so a redirect cannot smuggle an opted-out tool's output into the store. `with_name` renames `name()` and the schema's `tool` together so the registry key and the model's call can never diverge; the standard middleware contracts compose unchanged (memoize caches identical queries, the tool-health breaker degrades repeated retrieve failures, output-limiting stacks on the per-entry budget). With `MemoryDemotionSink` (`compact::demote`) and `VectorMemoryStore` (`memory::vector_memory`) this closes the context-quality story: what compaction evicts is demoted into memory, and the model pulls it back on demand. Pinned by `tests/search_memories_tool.rs` (`search_returns_stored_entry`, `empty_result_is_an_honest_line`, `limit_clamps_to_the_schema_maximum` (schema maximum and clamp read one constant), `a_missing_query_is_rejected_as_invalid_input`, `retrieval_error_is_soft`, `read_only_and_concurrency_safe_are_true`, `name_is_configurable`, `long_entries_truncate_at_the_configured_budget` (multibyte, boundary-safe), `memoize_composition_is_free_on_repeat` (engine-driven: two identical model calls, one underlying retrieve), and the headline `demoted_content_is_recallable_through_the_tool` — a real `BareLoop` run where `TruncatingCompactor` evictions demote into a `VectorMemoryStore` and the tool recalls the demoted fact with its `(trajectory)` category named); the composition and boundary pins (`output_limit_caps_the_joined_body_with_its_own_marker`, `memoize_follows_the_registered_name_not_the_default` (both directions of the renamed-registration cell), `a_zero_memory_top_k_still_serves_the_model_issued_search` (passive off, active on), `redaction_scrubs_secrets_from_memory_content` (bearer-shaped memory content renders as the redaction placeholder), `an_entry_exactly_at_the_budget_renders_whole` (the inclusive boundary), `an_all_untrusted_result_renders_one_section`, and `the_passive_memory_key_after_a_search_is_the_tool_result` (the engine's turn-input contract made visible)); plus a runnable registration-and-call doctest.
