Fix-round pins: `the_default_limit_is_bounded_by_the_schema_maximum`, `a_wrong_typed_limit_is_rejected_as_invalid_input`, `a_wrong_typed_query_is_rejected_as_invalid_input`, `an_empty_query_is_rejected_as_invalid_input`, `a_zero_result_budget_clamps_to_one_character`, the provenance pair `provider_derived_entries_are_excluded_by_default` / `provider_derived_entries_render_under_the_untrusted_framing_when_included`, `a_populated_store_serves_its_ranked_entries`, `rendered_category_labels_match_the_serde_wire_names`, `the_limit_description_tracks_the_configured_default`, `read_only_claim_matches_the_store_side_effects`, `a_search_does_not_become_the_next_search_result`, `memoize_serves_the_cached_answer_across_store_writes_until_ttl`, and `repeated_retrieve_failures_open_the_breaker`.

- **Real embedders for semantic memory — `OllamaEmbedder` (local, native `/api/embed`) and `OpenAiEmbedder` (`/v1/embeddings`) in `provider::embeddings`, behind the existing `ollama`/`openai` flags plus `vector_index` (no new features; `default = []` untouched)** — the two `EmbeddingProvider` backends a real consumer needs: one frontier-grade and one local. `OllamaEmbedder` speaks Ollama's *native* embedding endpoint (`{OLLAMA_BASE_URL}/api/embed`, a chat-profile `/v1` suffix is normalized away so both profiles share the variable; the legacy singular-input `/api/embeddings` is deliberately unused — no batching, deprecated upstream), defaults to `nomic-embed-text` (768 dims), needs no key, and appends an "is `ollama serve` running?" hint to transport failures; `from_env` requires `OLLAMA_EMBEDDING_MODEL` and deliberately refuses `OLLAMA_MODEL` (the chat model) so the two can never be silently conflated; `OLLAMA_EMBEDDING_DIM` and `OPENAI_EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS` optionally pin the geometry from the environment, with unparseable values failing config validation. `OpenAiEmbedder` defaults to `text-embedding-3-small` (1536 dims), reads `OPENAI_API_KEY` (required) / `OPENAI_BASE_URL` / `OPENAI_EMBEDDING_MODEL`, exposes the 3-series' Matryoshka truncation (`with_dimensions`, validated `256..=3072` — the series-wide bounds; each model's own maximum still applies server-side as its loud 400, and `text-embedding-3-large` pairs at its full 3072 — rides the request only when set), and redacts the key in `Debug` while sending it as a sensitive bearer header. Both: validated builders embedding the family's shared HTTP config (timeouts, pool, injectable client, trailing-slash base URLs trimmed at build so endpoints keep a single slash), `embed_texts` batching at 256 inputs or an estimated 250,000-token sum per request — whichever closes first — over the shared `post_json_checked` error-classification path with dimension-derived bounded response reads — the budget is `dim × 256 inputs × ~16 B/float × 2` clamped to 1–64 MiB, so a legitimate batch cannot brush the ceiling at any geometry (a 4096-dim Ollama model's full batch, ~12 MB, sits inside its ~33.5 MB budget) while hostile streams stay capped, refused up front on an over-declared content-length and mid-stream on a headerless chunked body, per-batch `loopctl.embed.batch` metric events carrying the usage pair when reported, an optional `normalized()` builder (L2 in place, scaled by the max-abs component so huge-but-finite answers normalize exactly instead of overflowing to a silent zero vector; zero vectors pass through; component finiteness is enforced on every answer, normalized or not — whether the parse itself refuses an out-of-range exponent depends on serde_json's feature set (the `float_roundtrip` parser does; the default one casts to `f32` infinity), so the explicit check is the configuration-independent gate), and **dimension honesty**: a server answer whose length differs from the configured `dim()` fails as a config-validation error naming both numbers after a `WARN` metric event — the index-corruption near-miss — because embedding models are not interchangeable across a store's lifetime. OpenAI responses reassemble by their `data[].index` field (never array position; row-count and permutation both verified), and every input is guarded against the 8191-token per-input ceiling before any wire traffic (chars/4 estimate, failure names the input index). The trait impls and re-exports sit in `memory::vector` under the matching flags, so `vector_index`-only builds stay provider-free. Pinned by the unit suite (env profiles via `EnvGuard`, builder validation, `Debug` redaction, the chars-not-bytes guard, normalization, unknown-field-tolerant parsing) and `tests/embeddings_wire.rs` (`openai_wire_reassembles_by_index_even_when_data_arrives_shuffled`, `openai_batch_splits_over_the_request_cap_and_preserves_order` (256+44), `dimension_mismatch_fails_loudly_naming_both_numbers`, `an_oversized_input_is_rejected_before_the_wire` (zero HTTP hits), `an_empty_input_returns_empty_without_touching_the_wire`, `the_normalized_builder_l2_normalizes_the_response`, `the_dimensions_parameter_rides_the_request_when_set`, `dimensions_stay_absent_from_the_request_when_unset`, `a_three_large_geometry_round_trips_at_full_length`, `normalization_survives_huge_but_finite_components`, `an_overflowing_exponent_fails_the_embed_at_the_parse`, the reassembly-refusal pair `openai_reassembly_refuses_duplicate_indices` and `openai_reassembly_refuses_a_short_or_out_of_range_data_set`, the count and bounded-body pins `ollama_count_mismatch_fails_naming_both_numbers`, `an_over_budget_content_length_is_refused_before_reading`, and `a_chunked_stream_past_the_budget_is_refused_mid_stream`, the Ollama wire round-trip and hint pins, the hint-gating, non-finite, and trailing-slash pins (`status_classified_failures_carry_no_serve_hint`, `ollama_non_finite_components_fail_even_without_normalization`, `non_finite_components_fail_even_without_normalization`, `a_trailing_slash_base_url_forms_a_single_slash_endpoint`, `ollama_trailing_slash_base_url_forms_a_single_slash_endpoint`), and the token-sum split units (`openai_batches_close_on_the_estimated_token_sum`, `openai_batches_keep_the_input_count_cap_and_ride_a_lone_oversized_input`)); the metric claims pinned by `tests/embeddings_metrics.rs` (`every_batch_settles_one_event_and_a_mismatch_warns`, the capture binary); live entries in `tests/provider_e2e.rs` (`*_embedder_round_trips_live`, plus the precision pair below).

- **`StructuredSummarizer` — the sectioned LLM compactor (`compact::structured_summarizer`'s `StructuredSummarizer`, `StructuredSummaryConfig`, `SummarySection`, `SummaryTemplate`, `StructuredSummary`, and the free `render`)** — the QA summarizer's three-step pass optimizes *retention*; this one optimizes *shape*: one LLM call (plus at most one retry when the first response's shape defeats the parser) produces a summary whose headings are contractually stable — key facts, decisions, pending tasks, open questions, each a `- ` bullet list — so a consumer (telemetry, the attachment layer, a UI) can locate "pending tasks" without parsing prose, and a model re-entering a marathon session scans a skeleton instead of a wall of narrative. `SummaryTemplate { sections, header }` drives both sides of the contract from one field: the system prompt enumerates exactly the configured sections with their exact `### ` heading strings, one-line purposes, the forbid-prose instruction, and the budget line; the renderer emits the header then each non-empty section in template order (empty sections are omitted, never rendered as bare headings; a fully-empty parsed summary renders the one-line stub, because an empty dropped context is a valid outcome). Two presets — `default_template` (all four sections, canonical order) and `action_only` (decisions + pending tasks) — with `SummarySection` `#[non_exhaustive]` (downstream matches need only a `_` wildcard arm; the in-crate accessors — `title`, `slug`, `json_key`, `items` — centralize the section's data); duplicates in `sections` render literally. The response is parsed back tolerantly — a JSON object keyed by the sections' `snake_case` names (via `extract_json_substring`, so a provider that answers in JSON mode works without changes) or a heading scan accepting any `#` depth, any casing, trailing colons and bold markers, `- `/`* `/numbered bullets, and fenced whole-document answers — with each section capped at 50 items so a runaway model cannot balloon one heading; a response with no recognizable heading triggers exactly one retry with a sharper exact-headings instruction, and a second malformed response degrades to its raw text as the single entry of the template's first rendered section (never an error — structured compaction degrades to free-form rather than failing the pass). `parse_output = false` skips parsing outright and carries the raw text the same way. Failure parity with the QA summarizer is baked in from the start: a blank or whitespace-only response fails the pass through the typed failure path (an empty summary must never demote a slice with nothing replacing it); a response whose heuristic token estimate exceeds the pass budget fails the pass naming the figure, strictly `>` so an exactly-at-budget summary passes, checked on the raw response text before any parse; any `ApiError` fails through `CompactionOutcome::failed` with the **original messages intact** — the `FallbackCompactor` seam. The split delegates to `TokenSplitter` (the same pair-safe boundary the other compactors draw); a leading `Role::System` message survives at the output head and never rides the transcript or `evicted` (via the `summarizable`/`leading_system` helpers now hoisted to the `compact` module root, shared with `qa_summarizer` alongside `budget_permille`, so the u128-verified per-mille conversion and both contract rules exist exactly once); the budget is reserve-then-cap — `min(max_summary_tokens, target × budget_pct)` held as an exact per-mille integer, fraction clamped `[0.05, 0.95]` with NaN/inf rejected to the 0.20 default, `max_summary_tokens` 6 000, `min_messages` 8, `preserve_recent` 6, `transcript_max_chars` 24 000 bounding the *input* prompt for small local models; `tokens_after` is counted with the context's own counter, and the dropped slice rides `evicted` for the demotion sink. The compactor is stateless — no prior accumulates, no mutex, trivially cancellation-safe; each pass summarizes the live transcript through the template, the deliberate simplicity for the fallback chain's reliable middle tier. Telemetry per the design: one `loopctl.compaction.structured.sections` event per configured section per **successful** pass (`state=populated|empty` — a climbing empty rate is the earliest visible sign the template has quietly degraded, which is why absent sections report empty rather than staying silent; a failed pass emits none, its visibility riding the failure warning instead) and a pass-complete debug line (budget, populated count, retry flag); no duration (the engine's clock-seamed `CompactTelemetry` already reports the pass) and no token-spend family (the QA summarizer's spend events cover the family). Always compiled, no feature flag, no new dependency, `default = []` untouched. Pinned by the module suite (41 tests against the in-module `RecordingClient` `ApiClient` double — the `testing`-gated `MockApiClient` deliberately avoided so the suite runs under every feature combination): `a_short_conversation_returns_no_change_with_zero_calls`, `the_split_delegates_to_the_token_splitter`, `the_budget_takes_the_smaller_of_the_cap_and_the_share` (the three budget cases, both pct clamps, NaN rejection, and the computed-fraction `0.1 + 0.2` per-mille case proving the shared `budget_permille` — the QA summarizer's saturation pin generalized to both consumers), `the_prompt_names_exactly_the_configured_sections_and_headings` (the default template names all four, states the enforced budget figure, and carries each section's purpose line; `action_only` requests its two and never the others), `render_produces_the_header_and_each_section_in_template_order`, `empty_sections_are_omitted_from_the_render`, `a_fully_empty_summary_renders_the_stub_line`, `the_action_only_template_renders_only_its_two_sections` (the template filters even populated sections), `the_parser_tolerates_heading_case_markers_bullets_and_fences`, `the_parser_also_accepts_the_json_object_shape`, `parse_then_render_round_trips_stably` (the canonical response reproduces byte-for-byte under the header and reparses equal), `the_parser_caps_each_section_at_fifty_items`, `the_summary_message_is_assistant_role_and_precedes_the_preserved_tail`, `a_leading_system_message_survives_and_never_rides_the_transcript` (absent from the prompt, still at the head, never evicted), `an_api_error_fails_the_outcome_and_keeps_the_original_messages`, `a_blank_response_fails_the_pass` (empty and whitespace-only shapes; one call each — no retry for content problems; neutralization-proven: deleting the blank guard turns exactly this pin red), `an_over_budget_summary_fails_the_pass` (both boundary sides self-calibrated against `CompactionOutcome::estimate_tokens` over the same scripted text; neutralization-proven: deleting the budget check turns exactly this pin red), `a_malformed_first_response_retries_once_then_succeeds` (two calls; the retry prompt carries the sharper instruction), `two_malformed_responses_degrade_to_raw_text_and_succeed`, `parse_output_false_carries_the_raw_text`, `evicted_carries_the_dropped_slice_for_the_sink` (order, content, and disjointness), `the_outcome_counts_tokens_with_the_context_counter` (a `ConstantPerMessageCounter` whose figure the static heuristic cannot produce), `a_transcript_budget_bounds_the_prompt`, and `the_summarizer_compacts_through_the_manager` (`compact_manual` end-to-end); the sections counter is pinned in its own binary — `tests/structured_summarizer_metrics.rs`'s `the_sections_counter_reports_populated_and_empty` (a one-section response yields four events: `facts` populated, `decisions`/`todos`/`questions` empty, each carrying its slug; neutralization-proven — renaming the metric turns exactly this pin red with zero events). An emptied `sections` list no longer silently discards the dropped context — `with_template` rejects a section-less template with a warning and the default template takes its place, and the render itself falls back to the canonical section order for a content-bearing summary under a hand-mutated empty template, so neither the config seam nor a direct `render` call can collapse a populated summary to a bare header — pinned fail-first by `a_cleared_sections_template_falls_back_to_the_default_and_never_discards` (red on the unfixed tree: the cleared template stored verbatim, `0 ≠ 4` sections) and `render_falls_back_to_the_canonical_sections_when_the_template_has_none` (red: the bare-header string itself in the assertion output); a JSON answer whose known-key arrays hold only non-string entries (numbers, nested objects) no longer parses to the nothing-salient stub — an empty array stays a recognized deliberate empty, but an array carrying unrepresentable content leaves the object unrecognized so the retry/raw-text path keeps that content — pinned fail-first by `a_numeric_only_json_response_degrades_to_raw_text` (red: one call instead of two, the content vanished behind the stub) with `an_all_empty_json_object_is_a_deliberate_empty_parse` holding the deliberate arm; the prompt pin gained the budget-figure and purpose-line assertions and `a_duplicate_section_renders_twice_by_design` pins the duplicates-render-literally claim; the retry-position interleavings are pinned by `a_blank_retry_fails_the_pass` and `an_over_budget_retry_fails_the_pass` (both failing with originals intact and the cause named, with `summarize`'s doc stating blank and over-budget responses fail regardless of which call delivers them); the stale QA-summarizer fold clause is deleted from `summary_budget`'s doc, the `#[non_exhaustive]` backtick typo is fixed, and the telemetry docs now match the emission (the sections counter emits per successful pass; a failed pass's visibility rides the failure warning). Content outside the template's sections can no longer vanish behind a bare header — `raw_text_summary` parks the degraded text in the template's **first rendered section** instead of always `key_facts`, and a parse that collected entries but fills no rendered section now counts as unparsed and takes the retry/degrade path (a fully-empty parse stays the deliberate stub outcome) — pinned fail-first by `parse_output_false_parks_the_raw_text_in_the_first_rendered_section` (red: the bare-header string under `action_only` with `parse_output = false`) and `a_parse_that_fills_no_rendered_section_degrades_to_raw_text` (red: one call instead of two — a key-facts-only response was accepted under `action_only` and its content discarded); and bullets under an unrecognized heading are no longer attributed to the previous section — an unknown heading line now ends the current section, so a rejected alternative can no longer surface as a settled decision — pinned fail-first by `bullets_under_an_unknown_heading_are_not_attributed_to_the_previous_section` (red: `decisions == ["use X", "use Y"]`). A rendered-artifact-vs-ceiling hardening proposal was declined with reason: the budget check deliberately runs on the raw response at the single chokepoint (the larger artifact), the only render-side growth a duplicated template section is a deliberate configuration, and the manager's whole-conversation fit check remains the backstop. A content-bearing response with a recognized heading but a non-bullet body no longer commits the nothing-salient stub over an evicted slice — the heading scan tracks the non-blank lines it cannot interpret (prose under a heading, an unrecognized heading, a preamble), and a parse that recognized a heading while collecting no entry over such lines counts as unparsed, taking the existing retry → degrade-to-raw-text path that preserves the content; the JSON empty-array shape stays the one deliberate empty, and a headings-only response (no interpretable lines at all) stays the deliberate empty too — only the presence of uninterpretable content distinguishes "nothing here" from "prose the scan could not read". The zero-`#` heading tolerance (a bare `Key facts:` line names a section) is stated in the parser docs as deliberate. Pinned fail-first (red on the unfixed tree: one call instead of two, the stub rendered, `Some(empty)` where `None` was owed) by `heading_plus_prose_responses_retry_then_degrade_to_raw_text_never_the_stub` (the exact scenario: two calls, the prose riding the summary message, never the stub), `a_heading_prose_retry_recovers_when_the_second_answer_is_sectioned` (the retry carries the sharper exact-headings instruction), and `an_unrecognized_heading_line_is_not_an_empty_statement` (the unknown-heading and bare-colon-over-prose shapes), guarded both ways by `headings_with_no_content_stay_the_deliberate_empty`, `a_bare_colon_heading_line_still_names_a_section`, and `a_populated_section_still_parses_over_a_discarded_preamble`; `estimate_text_tokens`'s doc now names the heuristic basis and the context counter's independence (the module's own `ConstantPerMessageCounter` pin proves the two figures can differ). A pre-compact hook's guidance now reaches the structured pass — `summarize` takes the pass's `CompactionContext` and appends `instructions` and every `additional_context` fragment to the base user prompt after the transcript, under the same labels the QA summarizer uses (`Additional instructions from the host:` / `Additional context to weave in:`), so the chain's two LLM tiers honor host guidance identically and the retry — which resends the same base prompt — carries the blocks too — pinned fail-first by `hook_instructions_and_additional_context_reach_the_prompt` (red on the unfixed tree: the first call's prompt carried only the transcript, the host blocks absent; green: both call positions asserted through a malformed-then-canonical script that forces the retry, the host block placed after `</conversation>`, and the absent direction — a context carrying no hook fields appends no host block).
- **`FallbackCompactor` — the never-fail compactor chain (`compact::fallback`'s `FallbackCompactor`, `FallbackConfig`, `TerminalCapture`, `ChainStage`, `StageOutcome`, `ChainReport`, and `ChainBuilder`)** — compaction becomes unconditionally successful: the chain holds an ordered list of inner compactors and runs them in sequence until one returns `success: true`, so with a non-failing terminal the chain as a whole never fails to compact and an out-of-tokens condition stops surfacing as a failed pass — `ContextManager::ensure_context_fits` returns `Ok(Compacted)` instead of `ContextOverflow`. The default chain (`default_chain(client)`) is quality-descending: `QaSummarizer` → `StructuredSummarizer` → `TerminalCapture(TruncatingCompactor)` — best-effort summarization first (both LLM stages on the caller's client, default configs, the dedicated-cheaper-client seam documented in both compactors), then the deterministic truncate that cannot fail; quality degrades gracefully, blind truncation only as the last resort. `ChainBuilder` composes custom chains (`stage(name, compactor)` for any `Arc<dyn ContextCompactor>`, `terminal(&truncator)` for the never-fail finale, `config`, `build`), warning — never erroring — when the last stage's name does not heuristically name a terminal (a `dyn` compactor cannot be inspected). **`TerminalCapture` enforces a structural floor** on the terminal truncator: it reconstructs the wrapped `TruncatingCompactor` with `preserve_recent = max(its own, terminal_min_recent)` at wrap time (default 4, `FallbackConfig` clamps ≥ 1), so the active turn and its neighbors survive every stage including the last-resort truncate — the guarantee lives in the compactor that actually runs, no per-call rewriting, no post-hoc stitching that could violate the truncator's tool-pair boundary. **The pass**: every stage receives the same `target_tokens` and a cloned `CompactionContext` whose `tokens_before` is recomputed with the context's own counter (over the original input); every stage runs against a clone of the original input — a failed stage's returned messages are discarded rather than handed onward, so a custom stage that fails with a partial or emptied list cannot corrupt the history its successors compact; each failure is logged at `warn` with the stage's error and recorded in the `ChainReport` (`StageOutcome` per stage that ran — name, success, the stage's own error and token figure, and monotonic wall-clock duration as report diagnostics, never a metric emission or a decision input); the winning stage's outcome rides out verbatim, its `evicted` handoff included. Past the loop — reachable only in a chain with no non-failing terminal — the chain fails honestly: `CompactionOutcome::failed` with the `"all compactor stages failed"` error, originals intact, nothing evicted (an empty builder chain has the same shape). **Provenance without contract changes**: `last_report()` returns the last completed run's report (a plain `std::sync::Mutex` + `recover_guard` behind `&self`, written once per exit, never held across an await, reflecting the last completed run), whose `winning_stage` index names the stage that carried the pass — the seam a host surfaces as `"FallbackCompactor(TruncatingCompactor)"` without the outcome or trait growing a name slot. Telemetry: the `loopctl.compaction.passes` counter (one event per **successful** pass at the winning stage, `stage` label = the stage's name — the honest label for custom chains; the default chain's names are its three tiers) and the `loopctl.compaction.degradations` counter (one event per failed stage the winner declined through, `from`/`to` naming the decline); no duration is emitted (the engine's clock-seamed `CompactTelemetry` owns the pass as a whole). Always compiled, no feature flag, no new dependency, `default = []` untouched. Pinned by the module suite (17 tests against two in-module doubles — a `ScriptedCompactor` `ContextCompactor` double serving scripted fail/shrink results while recording every call's target, context, and message texts, and an always-erroring `ApiClient` double; the `testing`-gated `MockApiClient` deliberately avoided so the suite runs under every feature combination): `the_default_chain_holds_three_stages_in_quality_order`, `a_first_stage_success_short_circuits_the_chain` (stage two's call count stays zero), `a_failed_first_stage_falls_through_to_the_second` (the failure's error recorded in the report), `two_llm_failures_fall_through_to_the_terminal_truncate` (the default chain over an unreachable client: three stages ran, the truncate's outcome carries the pass, its `evicted` non-empty, the newest message surviving), `a_terminal_terminated_chain_never_fails_to_compact` (the headline invariant over varied input shapes), `an_all_stages_failed_chain_fails_honestly`, `terminal_capture_raises_a_low_preserve_recent_to_the_floor` (a `preserve_recent = 1` truncator under floor 4 keeps the leading message plus four; the inner `min_messages` carries over), `the_default_chain_terminal_preserves_the_configured_floor` (default floor and a configured floor of six, each surviving a failing LLM stage), `each_stage_receives_the_running_tokens_before_and_a_stable_context` (`reason`/`window`/`turn` identical, running `tokens_before` recomputed with the context counter), `every_stage_receives_the_same_target_tokens`, `a_failed_stage_hands_the_original_messages_to_the_next`, `a_failed_stage_s_emptied_output_never_reaches_the_next_stage` and `the_all_failed_outcome_carries_the_original_not_a_stage_s_emptied_list` (fail-first, both red on the unfixed chain: the next stage received the emptied list and the all-failed outcome carried it), `last_report_records_every_stage_that_ran` (count, order, winning index), `an_empty_chain_builds_and_fails_honestly` (plus `terminal()` appending the capture stage), `a_custom_chain_stops_at_its_first_success` (the terminal after a succeeding custom stage never runs), and `the_chain_compacts_through_the_manager_without_overflow` (`ensure_context_fits` on an over-window conversation returns `Ok(Compacted)` — the acceptance's exact claim); the two counters are pinned in their own binary — `tests/fallback_compactor_metrics.rs`'s `passes_and_degradations_counters_report_the_chain_s_shape` (a fail-fail-truncate pass emits one `passes` event at the truncate stage and two `degradations` events naming each decline with `to=TruncatingCompactor`; fail-first: pushing the winner's entry before the degradation loop — emitting a self-degradation `from=TruncatingCompactor to=TruncatingCompactor` — fails the pin; neutralization-proven — renaming the metric string turns exactly this pin red with the wrong event count). The post-build `with_config` — behaviorally inert on a built chain, because the terminal floor is captured inside the `TerminalCapture` at `terminal()` time and `compact` never read the stored config — is removed before first release together with the dead `config` field; the floor is set through `ChainBuilder::config` before `terminal()`, exactly the path `the_default_chain_terminal_preserves_the_configured_floor` pins. The entry never advertised the method and no caller existed, so nothing else changes. The chain no longer trusts a failed stage's returned messages — `CompactionOutcome::failed` only "typically" carries the original input, and the pass-through handed a misbehaving stage's partial or emptied list to every later stage (terminal truncate included) and built the all-failed outcome from it, with the dropped messages surfacing as unreported `evicted` — so `compact` now clones the original input for each stage, counts each stage's `tokens_before` over the original, discards failed stages' returned lists, and fails on the original; pinned fail-first with the `Scripted::FailEmpty` double arm (a stage that fails returning an emptied list) by `a_failed_stage_s_emptied_output_never_reaches_the_next_stage` (red: the next stage observed the emptied list) and `the_all_failed_outcome_carries_the_original_not_a_stage_s_emptied_list` (red: the failed outcome carried the emptied list as the conversation). The chain's winning test is `success && reduced`: a stage that succeeds without reducing the token figure is recorded as declined and the chain continues, and when no stage reduces, the last unchanged outcome rides out so the caller's no-action path decides instead of a manufactured all-stages-failed error. Pinned by `an_unchanged_success_does_not_win_the_chain`, `a_chain_whose_stages_never_reduce_returns_the_last_unchanged_outcome`, and `a_length_reducing_token_growing_stage_does_not_win_the_chain`.


- **`QaSummarizer` — the three-step LLM compactor (`compact::qa_summarizer`'s `QaSummarizer`, `QaSummarizerConfig`, `PriorSummary`, `CompactionSummary`)** — truncation's information loss gets a real alternative: a compactor that *preserves the facts a future turn will need* instead of dropping the oldest messages. Each pass runs up to three non-streaming `create_message` calls on a caller-supplied `SharedApiClient` (a dedicated cheaper client is the documented production shape — the constructor takes either): **summarize** the about-to-be-dropped portion into a dense running summary; **question gaps** — ask the model what it would need from the dropped context that the summary does not carry, so the questions, not guesses, surface what is missing; **answer** — pull the answers out of the full pre-compaction transcript before it is discarded and fold them under a `## Facts recovered` section, `N. UNKNOWN` answers dropped so no hallucinated fact enters the summary. A sufficient-summary `[]` answer skips step 3 entirely (two calls, the step-1 text verbatim). Prompt craft follows the strongest observed in the wild (opencode's compaction): a summarization-agent system prompt that never continues the conversation, never answers questions inside it, never mentions compaction, and preserves exact paths/identifiers/commands/error strings; the accumulate-mode `<prior-summary>` merge instructions carry-forward-unmentioned-facts / excerpt-wins-on-conflict; the transcript rides the public `render_evicted` renderer (role lines, tool one-liners, per-part truncation) at a configurable character budget, and the previous pass's summary message — recognized by exact text equality — is filtered out of the transcript so accumulate mode never presents the same summary twice (it still rides `evicted` with the rest of the dropped slice; a leading system-role history message is the one message pulled back, unabsorbed). The split delegates to `TokenSplitter` — the boundary is pair-safe through the same occurrence-aware pairing `TruncatingCompactor` enforces, and deliberately differs twice: it additionally snaps to an assistant-to-user turn transition, and only a leading system-role message is pulled back rather than any first message; the token budget is reserve-then-cap — `min(max_summary_tokens, target × summary_budget_pct)` — with the fraction clamped to `[0.05, 0.95]` (NaN/inf rejected to the 0.25 default) and held as an exact per-mille integer through the module's shared decimal-rational parser, so the budget math never runs through a float cast; a new `transcript_max_chars` knob bounds the summarizer's *input* prompt for small local models. Pre-compact hook contributions are consumed as designed: `CompactionContext::instructions` rides as host instructions and every `additional_context` fragment as context to weave in. The running `PriorSummary` (text + 1-indexed pass) accumulates across passes behind a recovered plain mutex — read once at entry, written once at exit, never held across an await, so a cancelled pass (the trait's drop-safe contract) leaves the last committed value; `prior_summary()`/`reset()` expose and clear it, and the summary message carries no pass header so the continuing model never reads compaction metadata. Any `ApiError` fails the pass through `CompactionOutcome::failed` with the **original messages intact** and the step named, so a caller chaining compaction strategies can retry the pass with its input in hand. The summarizer's own token spend is deliberately outside the run's usage and reported per pass through `loopctl.compaction.summarizer.tokens` metric events (direction in/out, sticky `estimated` flag when any call lacked reported usage); no duration is measured inside the compactor — the engine's clock-seamed `CompactTelemetry::duration` already reports the whole pass. Always compiled (compaction carries no feature flag), no new dependency, `default = []` untouched. Pinned by the module suite (26 tests against an in-module `RecordingClient` `ApiClient` double — chosen over the `testing`-gated `MockApiClient` so the suite runs under every feature combination): `a_short_conversation_returns_no_change_with_zero_calls`, `the_split_delegates_to_the_token_splitter`, `the_budget_takes_the_smaller_of_the_cap_and_the_share` (the three pinned budget cases plus both clamp edges and the NaN rejection), `the_three_steps_fire_in_order_and_feed_each_other`, `no_questions_skips_the_answer_step`, `the_summary_message_is_assistant_role_and_precedes_the_preserved_tail`, `a_leading_system_message_survives_at_the_head`, `a_second_pass_accumulates_on_the_first_summary`, `accumulate_off_produces_a_fresh_prompt_each_pass` (fail-first: merging the prior into the prompt regardless of the flag fails the pin — the flag now gates the merge), `an_api_error_fails_the_outcome_and_keeps_the_original_messages`, `question_parsing_tolerates_fences_lists_and_garbage` (fenced JSON, both numbered and both bulleted marker spellings, `[]`, prose-to-empty, and the eight-question cap), `unknown_answers_are_dropped_and_known_answers_fold_in`, `reset_clears_the_prior_summary`, `the_outcome_counts_tokens_with_the_context_counter` (self-calibrated against the same counter instance), `evicted_carries_the_dropped_slice_for_the_sink` (order, content, and the disjointness the demotion-sink contract demands), `the_prior_summary_message_is_not_resummarized_as_conversation` (pass 2's prompt carries the prior exactly once — in `<prior-summary>`, not `<conversation>`), `hook_instructions_and_additional_context_reach_the_prompt`, and `the_summarizer_compacts_through_the_manager` (the `ContextManager::compact_manual` end-to-end seam). The per-mille derivation now computes its thousandfold scaling in a `u128` intermediate — a computed fraction such as `0.1 + 0.2`, whose 17-digit decimal rendering overflowed the `u64` numerator into saturation, previously budgeted at 184‰ while the getter reported 30% — pinned fail-first by `a_computed_fraction_budgets_at_its_full_per_mille` (red `left: 184, right: 300`); step 3 now renders the same accumulate-filtered transcript step 1 saw, so the answer prompt no longer presents the prior summary as answerable conversation — pinned fail-first by `step_three_never_resummarizes_the_prior_summary` (red: the prior text present in the pass-2 answer prompt's `<conversation>`); the three unpinned promises gained pins — `summarizer_metric_events_carry_direction_and_the_sticky_estimate_flag` (`tests/qa_summarizer_metrics.rs`, a process-global tracing capture in its own test binary, the one lane that observes a crate-internal emission deterministically under parallel sibling tests: a fully-reported pass sums its reported usage with `estimated=false` while one estimated call latches the sticky flag; neutralization-proven — deleting the emission turns exactly this pin red with zero events), `a_transcript_budget_bounds_the_step_one_prompt` (the step-1 prompt carries the saturation marker and drops the past-budget tail; neutralization-proven — rendering without the budget turns it red), and `the_fold_cap_sheds_the_latest_answers_first` (a budget admitting two of three bullets folds exactly those two, and a budget admitting none returns the summary whole; neutralization-proven — folding without the cap check turns it red with the third bullet present); the token-count pin runs against a constant-per-message counter whose figure the static heuristic can never produce (the static-estimate mutant that kept the old pin green turns the rewritten one red, `81 ≠ 49`); the split-provenance docs state the shared pairing and the two deliberate differences instead of claiming matched semantics; `prior_summary` documents that it reflects the last completed pass, not the last adopted history; and the module's 29 doubled `///` separator lines are deleted to the canon's single blank separator. An empty or whitespace-only step-1 response now fails the pass through the typed failure path instead of committing — a thinking-only or content-filtered response previously assembled an empty summary message, demoted the whole dropped slice with nothing replacing it in the feed, and stored an empty prior that silently disabled the next pass's prior-summary transcript filtering — pinned fail-first by `an_empty_summary_fails_the_pass_and_keeps_the_original_messages` (red on the unfixed tree: the pass reported success and committed the empty summary; neutralization-proven — removing the guard turns exactly this pin red) covering both the empty and whitespace-only shapes; and a leading system-role message no longer rides the summarizer transcript — `compact` computes the summarized slice once (`summarizable`: the dropped slice minus the pulled-back system message) before any LLM call and threads it through both prompts and the eviction list, so standing instructions are restated by neither the step-1 nor the step-3 prompt while surviving verbatim at the output head, and a slice that would summarize only the system message returns no-change without spending a call (defensive: the splitter's assistant-to-user boundary rule cannot produce that slice today — the check subsumes the reachable dropped-slice-empty case) — pinned fail-first by `a_leading_system_message_never_rides_the_summarizer_transcript` (red on the unfixed tree: `System: standing instructions` rendered inside the step-1 `<conversation>`) plus the pure slicing contract `the_summarizable_slice_excludes_only_a_leading_system_message`; neutralization-proven — removing the skip turns the transcript pin, the pre-existing `a_leading_system_message_survives_at_the_head` pin (whose evicted count catches the system message being both kept and demoted), and the slicing pin red together. The summary budget is now **enforced**, not merely instructed — a step-1 response whose heuristic token estimate exceeds the pass budget fails the pass through the same typed path (strictly `>`: a summary estimating exactly at budget passes), because a model that ignores the "keep it under roughly {budget} tokens" instruction previously shipped an oversized summary straight through — `fold_answers`' cap bounds only the folded section, the base summary being its never-shed part — silently breaking the reserve-then-cap guarantee that the preserved tail keeps its share of the window; the four "hard ceiling" doc claims and the "neither starve … nor balloon" sentence are now enforced facts, with the `summary_budget` doc naming the estimate basis, the strict boundary, and the separately-bounded fold section so the next round cannot re-ask — pinned fail-first by `an_over_budget_summary_fails_the_pass` (red on the unfixed tree: the over-budget pass reported success and shipped; green: failure with the error naming the `{budget}-token budget` figure, originals intact, one LLM call, nothing evicted, no prior committed; neutralization-proven — removing the check turns exactly this pin red), both boundary sides self-calibrated against `CompactionOutcome::estimate_tokens` over the same scripted text (exactly-at passes and rides the output verbatim, one-under fails).

- **External-approver `ask` channel — the `ask` module (`PendingAsk`, `AskResolution`, the `AskResolver` trait with `SyncAskResolver` and `ApprovalChannel`/`Approver`), `LoopManagers::with_ask_resolver`/`with_ask_timeout`, the engine's parked hook-ask arm, and two additive verdict/source variants (`GateVerdict::AskExpired`, `GateRuleSource::Engine`)** — hooks could `ask`, but the engine's only move was an immediate soft error: an unattended run had no way to *park* a pending decision and resume on an async answer. With a resolver installed on the managers, a hook's `Ask` now parks the run at the pre-execution boundary — the safe point where no tool has run and machine state is coherent — while the engine mints the serialized `PendingAsk` (id from the id seam; `session_id`/`run_id`/`turn`/`call_id` binding; the call's arguments reduced to the gate records' `args_digest`, never stored as content; the hook's prompt) and races the resolver against the cancel signal and, when `with_ask_timeout` is set, the deadline. An approval hands its record to the dispatch result, which it joins after the dispatch returns — the `with_gate` precedence lattice keeps the deciding record, so a downstream pipeline denial displaces the approval and exactly one record is emitted per gated dispatch from the engine's one emission site, whichever gate minted the record; the approved call itself runs through the unchanged pipeline/registry path; a refusal or expiry returns the soft denial the model reacts to; every ending leaves exactly one emitted record: the denial-family endings emit immediately at the park (`AskDenied` — rule `"ask"`, source `AskResolver` — for the approver's refusal, `AskExpired` (source `Engine`) for a deadline the approver outlasted, `AskUnresolved` for the headless default, `Cancelled` for a park the cancel signal cut short), while the approval (`AskAllowed`, the same rule and source) rides the dispatch result and is emitted after the dispatch returns — or earlier, when the call stops before dispatching (a pre-detection hard stop, the tool-health breaker's refusal) or a cancellation cuts the dispatch mid-execution, so a consulted-and-approved gate never vanishes from the audit trail. The expiry verdict is a widening of the just-shipped `GateVerdict` (serde `ask_expired`, deny-family in `with_gate` precedence), and `GateRuleSource::Engine` names the engine-policy outcomes — **both are additive variants in a pre-1.0 minor: exhaustive downstream matches on either enum need a new arm; migration is adding `GateVerdict::AskExpired` and `GateRuleSource::Engine` arms (a wildcard arm needs nothing).** Bindings are single-use by construction: the engine awaits per call, and `ApprovalChannel` drops the completion slot when the wait ends, so a late or duplicate external answer is inert — `Approver::resolve` returns `false` and nothing is queued. An approver that is dropped after delivering an ask denies it — `Approver`'s `Drop` drains every still-awaiting completion slot to `Deny`, so no run parks forever on an answerer that went away (the delivered-then-dropped case the send-error fail-closed path cannot see; pinned by `dropping_the_approver_after_delivery_denies_the_parked_ask`). No resolver installed: byte-identical headless behavior (the immediate soft error carrying the hook's message, pinned verbatim against the conversation) plus the one additive `AskUnresolved` record — `ConfirmationHandler`, `Interactivity`, and `PermissionMiddleware`'s own resolver path are untouched. The channel reference implementation fails closed (an approver that is gone denies — the headless posture), and `SyncAskResolver` adapts an answer-on-sight closure (the old sync answerer) to the trait. `PendingAsk` and `AskResolution` are serde types, so a host can move approvals across a process boundary; the spool format and queue verbs stay on the host side. No new dependency, no feature flag; the engine consumption rides `hooks`, the types compile everywhere. Pinned by `tests/external_approver.rs` — `parked_run_resumes_on_external_approval` (the pending is observed before the answer is sent, then the approval unblocks the run, the tool executes exactly once, and one `AskAllowed` record lands with resolver provenance, the call's digest, the `FixedClock`-stamped `ts`, and `tool_pre → gate → tool_post` order), `an_approver_refusal_denies_softly_and_records_it` (the approver refuses: the run still completes, the call never executes, one `AskDenied` record with `AskResolver` provenance and the `denied by approver` reason), `a_cancelled_park_ends_the_run_typed_cancelled_and_records_it` (a cancel fired while parked ends the run `Err(Cancelled)`, emits one `Cancelled` record with `Engine` provenance, and leaves the call's `on_tool_pre` unpaired — no post fires on cancellation paths, per the scoped `notify_tool_post` contract), `a_downstream_pipeline_denial_displaces_the_park_approval_record` (a hook ask approved into a pipeline whose permission middleware denies: exactly one record lands — the pipeline's `Deny`, `Middleware`-sourced; no `AskAllowed` is emitted for a call that did not proceed. Neutralization-proven: making `with_gate` attach unconditionally turns exactly this pin red with `AskAllowed` against the asserted `Deny`, restored green), `ask_timeout_defaults_to_deny_and_audits_the_expiry` (a never-answering resolver under a 50 ms deadline: the run still completes, the call never executes, one `AskExpired` record with `Engine` provenance), `approval_is_bound_to_the_args_digest` (an approved call, then the same tool with changed arguments: the approver is consulted twice with distinct digests — an approval never carries across calls), and `a_hook_ask_without_a_resolver_denies_headlessly_and_records_it` (the denial text in the conversation is the hook's message byte-for-byte, plus one `AskUnresolved` record), `an_approved_ask_records_its_decision_when_dispatch_is_cancelled`, `an_approved_ask_records_its_decision_when_the_breaker_refuses` (under `tool_health`), and `an_approved_ask_records_its_decision_when_pre_detection_refuses` (one pin per exit: the approval is recorded when the call stops before dispatching and when a cancellation cuts the dispatch mid-execution — the cancelled-dispatch pin's tool counts its own start, so the pin proves the cancel landed with the dispatch in flight rather than before it began; neutralization-proven for the dispatch-cancel arm: removing exactly that emission turned its pin red with zero records, restored green) — plus the unit pins `sync_resolver_answers_from_the_closure`, `approval_channel_resolutions_are_single_use`, `approval_channel_fails_closed_when_no_approver_listens`, `a_late_answer_completes_nothing`, `a_delivered_denial_reaches_the_parked_ask`, and `concurrent_parks_both_deliver` (two concurrently parked asks both deliver — parallel dispatch parks one ask per concurrently running call).

- **Gate decisions as data — `tool::permission`'s `GateDecision`/`GateVerdict`/`GateRuleSource`, the `on_gate_decision` observer event, `ToolDispatchResult::gate`, `PermissionMiddleware::evaluate` + `with_named_check`, the hub's `GateDecision` event, and the ledger's `gate.decision` kind** — hooks decided allow/block/ask, but nothing recorded *why*: asks, plan mode, audit, and cassette gate-replay all need the decision itself as serializable data. Every decision the permission middleware makes now mints one record — `{tool, args_digest, verdict, rule_id, rule_source, matched_pattern, reason, ts}` — riding the dispatch result out to the engine (`ToolDispatchResult.gate`, `with_gate`; the struct is already `#[non_exhaustive]`, so the field is additive), where the engine emits it through the new defaulted `LoopObserver::on_gate_decision` as `GateDecisionContext { turn, call_id, decision }` — the `call_id` pairs the record with the dispatch's `tool.call`/`tool.result` ledger lines and its pre/post observer events, and dispatches no gate consulted produce no record, as the callback documents. The arguments are never stored: `args_digest` is FNV-1a 64 over the input's canonical JSON — every object's keys sorted, recursively, by the digest's own canonicalization, so the rendering is a function of the value alone whichever `serde_json` map backend the host's graph resolves to (feature unification lets any downstream crate switch the backend to insertion-ordered `preserve_order`, which would otherwise split digests across hosts) — rendered as 16 hex characters, the demotion sink's fixed-algorithm hash promoted to shared internal use, so digests never churn across Rust releases; key order never moves it, any argument change produces a different digest except under a collision of the 64-bit hash, and for a rewritten call the digest is of the *original* input, because it identifies the call the model made. `GateVerdict` covers all nine paths — `Allow`, `AllowModified`, `Deny`, `Ask` (the pre-resolution answer), `AskAllowed`, `AskDenied`, `AskUnresolved` (headless: no resolver configured), `AskExpired` (the ask-deadline denial, added with the external-approver channel above), and `Cancelled` (the cancel-raced prompt, distinct from a refusal) — with exactly one record per gated dispatch: an ask that resolves emits the resolution, not the step. Provenance rides as `rule_id` + `rule_source`: `"middleware"` for an unnamed check, the id a `with_named_check(rule_id, f)` rule carries verbatim (the identifier manifests reference), `"context"` for a per-call claim, `"ask"` with source `AskResolver` when the user's answer decided, and `Engine` for the engine-policy outcomes the parked-ask family mints (expired, cancelled, headless — the external-approver entry above). `ts` is stamped by whoever holds a clock — the engine, from the managers' clock seam at emission — so a record read straight off a dispatch result before emission (and every dry-run record) carries `0`; no hidden `SystemTime::now()` bypasses the determinism seam. The evaluate-without-execute API, `PermissionMiddleware::evaluate(tool_name, input)`, resolves a synthetic dispatch (the engine's own default `Allow` claim, fresh cancel signal, default context) through the middleware's real rule stack — so `plan`/`can` verbs and dry-runs use the engine's policy, not a parallel one — and an `Ask` answers `Ask` with the prompt in `reason`, because a dry-run never consults the resolver. The stream surfaces follow: the event hub forwards the callback as `LoopEvent::GateDecision`, and the per-event ledger's reserved `gate.decision` label begins emitting — turn-scoped, `data` = the decision's fields plus `call_id` (`usage` stays the one reserved label; the Unknown-fallback pin's example line moved to it, a deliberate amendment). No new dependency, no feature flag; `default = []` untouched. Pinned by `tests/gate_decisions.rs` — `every_hook_decision_emits_a_record_with_rule_provenance` (an engine run gating four dispatches — deny, allow, ask→resolver approves, ask→resolver refuses — lands exactly one record each with tool, digest, verdict, rule id/source, engine-stamped `ts` from a `FixedClock`, and the `call_id` pairing; neutralization-proven — deleting the engine's emission call turns exactly this pin red with zero records, restored green), `evaluate_matches_enforcement_for_the_same_call` (evaluate and a real dispatch agree for allow/deny/modify; an ask evaluates to `Ask` while a counting resolver spy stays at zero until exactly the real dispatch consults it), `a_result_without_a_gate_carries_none`, `args_digests_are_canonical_and_stable` (key order does not move the digest, different args do, and a known fixture pins the literal hex `f1ed61cd8ca5e26b`, so an algorithm or canonical-rendering swap breaks the pin instead of silently changing every persisted digest), `a_named_check_carries_its_rule_id`, `an_unanswered_ask_records_the_headless_denial` (`AskUnresolved`), and `a_cancelled_prompt_records_the_cancellation` (`Cancelled`) — plus the hub mapping pin `every_callback_maps_to_its_event_kind_in_call_order` extended to the new kind, the ledger's `gate_decisions_land_in_the_ledger` (label, turn, `data` shape, `call_id`, digest self-calibrated against the same public machinery), and the amended `an_unknown_future_kind_still_parses_with_its_envelope_intact` now exercising `usage`. The digest's canonicalization is enforced by a new `make digest-canonical` CI gate — a scratch downstream crate built with `serde_json`'s `preserve_order` feature (the graph shape in-repo tests cannot see, the `redaction-minimal` pattern) asserting the fixture digest survives the insertion-ordered backend (neutralization-proven: reverting the digest to raw serialization turns the gate red with `8b6cb9110c0422ab` against the canonical `f1ed61cd8ca5e26b`, restored green); the events ledger's interchange table (the documented schema contract for `events.jsonl` readers) gained its `gate.decision` row with the `call_id` ↔ `tool_call_id` join stated, and two fail-closed census pins keep the docs from drifting again — `the_kinds_table_lists_every_emitted_kind_and_nothing_else` (every `TrajectoryEventKind` serde rename must have a table row and conversely) and `the_module_doc_inventory_lists_every_context_struct` (every re-exported observer context must appear in the module doc's inventory; `GateDecisionContext`, `ModelSwitchedContext`, and `PreCompactionContext` were missing there and are now listed); the documented emission ordering is pinned by the engine test's ordered `tool_pre`/`gate`/`tool_post` log per dispatch (neutralization-proven — moving the emission after `on_tool_post` turns exactly the order assertion red) with the hub fixture realigned to the engine's order; and the ledger's ordered pins now drive all eleven kinds with the gate line joining its `tool.call`/`tool.result` rows by call id (`event_records_land_in_ledger_in_order`, `the_observer_never_emits_the_unknown_kind`). A memoize cache hit no longer replays the first dispatch's gate record — the hit arm re-stamps the call id and clears the gate, so a dispatch no gate consulted emits no decision (the composition demonstrated: memoize outside, permission inside; the inverse is clean by construction) — pinned by `a_memoized_hit_replays_no_gate_record` (fail-first: removing the clear turned exactly the pin red with the replayed `allow-reads` record in the assertion output, restored green); stacked permission middlewares can no longer lie — `with_gate` attachment is verdict-aware: a deny-family record displaces any other, `AllowModified` displaces a plain pass-through, and equal precedence keeps the first writer, so the record that decided the outcome survives and an outer allow cannot erase an inner deny the engine acted on (a deny arm builds a fresh `gate: None` result, so a short-circuiting outer deny is unaffected) — pinned by `an_outer_allow_cannot_erase_an_inner_gate_s_deny` (fail-first: reverting to unconditional overwrite turned exactly the pin red with verdict `allow` against the asserted `deny`, restored green); the ledger entry's Unknown-fallback pin description now names `usage`; the `rule_source` and `matched_pattern` field docs carry content instead of a bare cross-reference and future-narration; and the digest-uniqueness phrasing states the 64-bit-collision bound wherever it appears (field doc, fn doc, and here). The first-writer-wins shape also let an inner pass-through mask an outer Modify gate's rewrite, the surviving `Allow` record contradicting the variant's own doc and breaking the call-digest join. Attachment is now verdict-aware through a precedence lattice — deny-family (the dispatch did not execute) over `AllowModified` over pass-through, equal tiers keeping the first writer — so the rewrite's record survives with the digest of the model's original arguments; two modifying gates cannot both be expressed (one record carries one rewrite, the first attached survives — stated on the builder doc). Pinned by `an_inner_pass_through_cannot_mask_an_outer_rewrite` (fail-first: on the first-writer-wins tree the pin failed with verdict `allow` against the asserted `allow_modified`, the inner `authorize-reads` record masking the outer rewrite — the round's own empirical finding reproduced in-tree) and the one-way-lattice guard `a_plain_allow_does_not_displace_an_inner_rewrite` (a plain pass-through never displaces upward; the two mutants each named by their kill — the pre-fix first-writer-wins tree turns exactly the mask pin red, and the unconditional-overwrite mutant turns exactly the guard and the deny pin red — restored green after each).

- **Model context-window probe — `ApiClient::model_context_window` with llama.cpp `/props` + Ollama `/api/show` discovery, the resolved compaction denominator, and `TurnEndContext.context_window`** — the compaction denominator was a host *declaration* (`SessionConfig.context_window`, defaulted to 200 000), and every threshold — the 80 % trigger, the 95 % emergency line — was a fraction of that guess: a host that never set it ran on the default even when the model took 8 K or 32 K (the emergency line firing *after* the provider had already refused), a host pointing one config at different models kept the first model's number, and a `--parallel` local server whose per-request truth is `n_ctx / total_slots` was indistinguishable from one where the declaration is exact (the incident: a ~140 K-token run 500'd "Context size has been exceeded" against a llama.cpp proxy whose real per-slot window — 65 536 — was machine-readable in `GET /props` all along). The engine now asks: the new defaulted trait capability returns the provider's disclosed window when one exists (fail-soft — a timeout, HTTP error, or unparseable body is `None`, never a turn error; the hosted APIs expose no metadata endpoint and keep the default `None`), and the shipped OpenAI-compatible client implements it against the two families that do disclose — llama.cpp's `/props` (`default_generation_settings.n_ctx`, **divided by `total_slots`**, because a `--parallel` deployment splits the KV budget across slots and the per-slot allocation is what any single request must fit) and Ollama's `/api/show` for the current model (a POST carrying the model name; the `context_length` key matched by suffix, since real Ollama keys it by architecture — `llama.context_length`; both deviations from a naive GET-and-literal-lookup verified against the real APIs). The probes address the server root — the base URL with one trailing version segment (`/v1`, `/v1beta`) stripped — reuse the client's pooled HTTP layer under their own short total deadline (a request-level bound of 5 seconds covering connect through body completion, so a wedged metadata endpoint — a llama.cpp proxy that accepts and never answers — costs run start seconds instead of the 120-second conversational read timeout, fail-soft `None`), read their bodies through the crate's bounded-body control (the same 10 MiB ceiling every completion path enforces, so a hostile endpoint cannot buffer an unbounded document before JSON validation), and build their `Authorization` header through the one shared sensitive-header construction the completions path uses (`bearer_header`, `set_sensitive(true)` — the probes were the one credential-bearing request class outside the redaction discipline); they cache per model epoch: the answer is asked at most once per model, `set_model` clears the cache (an Ollama show answer is per-model; a hot-swapped model re-probes rather than serving the previous model's window), and a probe that started under a swapped model returns to its caller without poisoning the cache. At each run start the driver resolves the denominator once — probe over declaration (the disclosure is what the server will actually accept; the declaration is a guess), with a declared `0` — the documented policy-disable sentinel — kept as-is, since a deliberate opt-out outranks any disclosure — then freezes it for the run's whole life, so the trigger and the emergency line cannot disagree mid-run; a model-switch apply invalidates the resolution so the next run re-resolves for the new model. The installed `ContextManager` is re-synced only when a probe actually disclosed — the same clone-and-sync a model-switch window change performs, unifying machine trigger and compaction target on the disclosed number — while a probe-less resolution leaves a host-installed manager exactly as built: the declaration governs the machine's trigger and never rewrites the manager's own targeting, **and** reverts a manager an earlier probe had synced back to the declared window (a `switch_model` without `with_context_window` resets the client's probe cache, and the next run's probe-less resolution left the machine triggering on the declared 200 000 while the manager still targeted the stale disclosed 4 096 — the trigger/target split the sync exists to prevent; a `manager_synced_from_probe` flag arms on the probe sync and the next probe-less resolution consumes it). The run-start resolution is itself raced against the cancel signal in the same biased `select!` the compaction pass uses, so a cancel during the probe ends the run typed `Cancelled` through the ordinary finalize path — salvage, `on_run_end`, signal re-arm — instead of waiting the probe out. `TurnEndContext.context_window: Option<u64>` (marker-covered additive) reports the denominator on all six turn-end sites — `Some(resolved)`, `None` exactly under the disabled policy, so a utilization display divides `context_tokens` by the limit the engine actually enforced; the `EventHub` and per-event ledger forward it by value with no change of their own. Telemetry: `loopctl.context.window` (gauge: `source=probe|declared`, `window`) at each resolution where the value changed, and `loopctl.context.probe.failures` (`kind=timeout|http|parse`) per failed probe attempt. Pinned (the engine pins fail-first by neutralization — `machine_policy` reverted to read the declared window turns exactly the probe-preference pin red, restored green) by `context_manager_prefers_the_probe_over_the_declaration` (a ~5 200-token payload against a probed 4 096-token window compacts; against the declared 200 000 it never would), `context_manager_falls_back_to_the_declaration`, `a_disabled_window_stays_disabled_despite_the_probe`, `turn_end_carries_the_resolved_window`, `turn_end_reports_no_window_under_the_disabled_policy`, and `probe_never_surfaces_as_a_turn_error` (a probe gated on a notify the test holds: the run waits inside it without failing, completes normally on release, and the declared window stands); the client pins by `openai_client_probes_llama_cpp_props` (262 144 over 4 slots → 65 536, from a `/v1`-segmented base URL), `probe_treats_a_single_slot_as_the_total`, `openai_client_probes_ollama_show` (the architecture-prefixed key, the POST body carrying the model name asserted), `probe_failure_is_none_not_error` (garbage body + 404 → `None`), `probe_is_cached_per_client` (two consultations, one HTTP hit), and `a_model_swap_reprobes_the_window` (each model's show endpoint hit exactly once, the second window is the new model's); the manager-preservation contract is pinned both ways by `a_probe_less_run_leaves_a_divergent_installed_manager_untouched` (fail-first — the pre-fix tree rewrote a 50 000/60 custom manager to the declared 200 000/80, `left: 200000, right: 50000`) and `a_probing_run_still_syncs_a_divergent_installed_manager` (a disclosed 4 096 still re-syncs it — the gate kept the unification); the revert by `a_probe_less_run_restores_a_manager_an_earlier_probe_synced` (fail-first — the pre-fix tree left the manager at `4096` against the asserted `200000`) with its no-op arm pinned by `a_model_switched_window_survives_a_probeless_run`, the deadline by `a_wedged_metadata_server_yields_none_within_the_probe_deadline` (a local server that accepts and never answers yields `None` inside the 5-second deadline against the client's explicit 120-second read timeout — red on the pre-fix tree, which blew the outer bound waiting the read timeout out), the bounded read by `an_oversized_metadata_document_yields_none` (a valid `/props` document padded past the 10 MiB ceiling is refused, not parsed — red on the pre-fix tree, which read and disclosed it), the sensitive header by `bearer_headers_are_marked_sensitive`, and the cancel race by `a_cancel_during_the_run_start_probe_ends_the_run_typed` (red on the pre-fix tree — the run waited the hanging probe past a 2-second outer bound; green, it returns `Err(Cancelled)` with the prompt salvaged into history); and the live bundle is inspectable through the new `BareLoop::managers()` accessor, the read seam of the existing `session()`/`machine()`/`tools()` family.

- **Context-size visibility — `LoopMachine::context_tokens()` / `BareLoop::context_tokens()` and `TurnEndContext.context_tokens`** — the engine has always known its running context-size estimate: the driver feeds it into the machine at every growth and shrink point (run start, after tool results land, before transient-bearing model calls, with every model response, and the compacted size after each compaction pass), and it is the exact figure the compaction threshold and the 95% emergency line evaluate — but the number was write-only: `set_context_tokens` existed with no getter, no observer event carried the estimate, and the exact figure was not host-reproducible (the request-overhead rider is internal), so a consumer could not see how full the context was except through the provider's after-the-fact `input_tokens` (absent when a provider reports no usage). The estimate is now exposed verbatim — one source of truth, no re-estimation: a getter beside the setter, with the terminal freeze as part of the contract (the setter no-ops on a terminal machine; the getter reports the figure the run ended with), plus a `BareLoop` passthrough for at-rest reads, and `TurnEndContext.context_tokens` — filled at all six turn-end sites — the tool-turn and terminal successes, the dispatch/turn/loop-detection error arms, and cancel — with the machine's figure at the moment of the event — so live monitors see it every turn during a run, where `run()`'s `&mut self` blocks polling; the `EventHub` carries the context by value, so subscribers receive the field with no hub change. The turn-end figure is the engine's forward-looking estimate, present even when the provider reports no usage — distinct from the provider-reported `input_tokens` beside it, and combinable with `SessionConfig::context_window` for a utilization view. Pinned fail-first (compile-red on the missing surface, then green) by `a_fresh_machine_reports_zero_context_tokens`, `the_context_tokens_getter_returns_the_last_fed_estimate` (neutralization-proven — deleting the terminal guard turns exactly this pin red, `999 ≠ 500`), `compaction_shrinks_the_exposed_context_size` (neutralization-proven — deleting the compaction assignment turns exactly this pin red, `90 ≠ 40`), `the_context_size_accessor_exposes_the_engine_estimate_after_a_run` (the passthrough equals the machine getter and is non-zero after a two-turn run), `turn_end_carries_the_engine_s_context_estimate` (one figure per turn, non-decreasing across the tool turn, and the final figure equals the at-rest accessor — the live and at-rest lanes report one number), `the_hub_forwards_the_turn_end_context_size`, and the non-success lanes by `a_failing_turn_s_turn_end_carries_the_engine_s_context_estimate` (an empty-queue API failure: the error-path turn-end carries the non-zero run-start estimate, equal to the frozen at-rest figure; neutralization-proven — zeroing the error site's fill turns exactly this pin red) and `a_cancelled_turn_s_turn_end_carries_the_engine_s_context_estimate` (a deterministic mid-stream cancellation through the blocking-client harness: the cancelled arm's turn-end carries the same non-zero figure, equal to the at-rest accessor; neutralization-proven — zeroing the cancelled site's fill turns exactly this pin red). The tool-turn success event fires after this turn's tool results are recorded and the estimate refreshed — moved from the dispatch helper's `Ok` arm, where it predated the results feed and the run's final tool turn (the max-turns edge) ended with a last event whose figure excluded the results it had just produced — so the live lane's figure includes the results on every turn, pinned by `the_last_turn_end_figure_survives_the_max_turns_edge` (a one-turn budget: the run's single turn-end carries the post-results figure, equal to the at-rest accessor — fail-first red `59 ≠ 66` under the old placement, green after the move). The event's `duration` is captured when dispatch completes — ahead of that recording and refresh — so the O(history) context recount never inflates the reported tool-phase timing, restoring the `TurnEnd` duration contract the failure path already kept (both same-phase events now measure the same span). Pinned by `the_success_turn_end_duration_excludes_the_context_recount` (fail-first red `150ms` under the prior placement — an injected slow token counter's full recount rode inside the reported duration; green after the capture moved, with a consulted-counter guard so the pin cannot pass vacuously).
- **`on_attempt_reset` — the observer signal for retried stream attempts** — fired exactly once before the first event of each retried attempt (never before the first), carrying `AttemptResetContext { turn, attempt }`: the engine already discarded the failed attempt's accumulator on `AttemptReset`, and observers that buffer per-attempt deltas — text or thinking — now get the same cue to discard theirs, instead of concatenating the retry's deltas onto the dead attempt's prefix — the gap a live per-attempt delta buffer could not detect on its own. The delta callbacks' retry caveats name it as the live-display reset point, and `EventHub` re-emits it as `LoopEvent::AttemptReset`. Pinned by `a_retry_fires_attempt_reset_before_the_retry_s_deltas` (one reset, after the dead attempt's thinking delta and before the retry's), `a_double_retry_numbers_attempts_consecutively` (a second reset carries `attempt: 3` — numbering is consecutive across the retry ladder), `a_clean_stream_fires_no_attempt_reset`, `host_dispatches_on_attempt_reset_to_all_observers` / `host_dispatches_on_attempt_reset_with_no_observers` (which asserts the host was actually empty), and the hub lifecycle pin's new `attempt_reset` entry.
- **Thinking-effort control — `structured::ThinkingEffort` and `RequestOptions::effort`, with `thinking_effort(...)` defaults on the three built-in client builders** — one request-level dial (`Low`/`Medium`/`High`/`Max`; an unset field is off — or inherits a client default when one is set) mapped by each provider onto its native wire parameter: OpenAI `reasoning_effort` (`Max` clamps to `"high"`, the parameter's ceiling), Anthropic `thinking: {type: "enabled", budget_tokens}` on the ladder 2048/8192/16384/32768 with a headroom rule (`max_tokens` rises to `budget + 1` when below it, so the body never asks for a budget the same request forbids) and an up-front rejection of the one combination the Messages API refuses — an effective effort (request or client default) alongside a `response_format`, whose forced tool choice cannot coexist with extended thinking, and Gemini `generationConfig.thinkingConfig.thinkingBudget` on the ladder 1024/8192/16384/24576 — beside `includeThoughts`, emitted alone when thought summaries are off. Precedence everywhere is the per-request value over the client default (`options.effort.or(client)`); an unset per-request value inherits the client default, and turning effort off is a client-level concern — build the client without a default (there is no per-request off). A client that cannot forward an effort level — any implementation keeping the trait-default `*_with_options` methods — rejects one with a config error instead of silently serving an unthunked request. Default inertness is pinned twice: `the_effort_key_appears_only_where_a_scenario_set_it` (no `effort` key in any golden except the scenario that sets it — the field renders only when set) and the byte-identical regeneration of every pre-existing golden. Wire reach is pinned per provider by `a_high_effort_reaches_the_openai_wire` / `a_high_effort_reaches_the_anthropic_wire` / `a_high_effort_reaches_the_gemini_wire` (new goldens `openai_wire_effort`, `anthropic_wire_effort`, `gemini_wire_effort`), on the plain non-streaming path by `a_client_effort_default_reaches_the_non_streaming_wire` (the OpenAI `create_message` body carries the default too), composition with the model override by `effort_rides_the_model_override_seam` (golden `effort_injected`: `effort` and `model` ride together through `turn_request_options`), the Anthropic headroom rule by `a_high_effort_enlarges_max_tokens_for_headroom` (a 1024-token request under `High` sends `max_tokens: 16385`; a 32768-token request is untouched), the ladder's outer rungs by `the_ladder_s_outer_rungs_reach_the_openai_wire` / `the_ladder_s_outer_rungs_reach_the_anthropic_wire` / `the_ladder_s_outer_rungs_reach_the_gemini_wire` (`Low` and `Max` reach each wire — the OpenAI `Max` clamp to `"high"`, the 2048/32768 budgets with the 32769 headroom edge, and the 1024/24576 budgets), the forced-tool-choice rejection by `effort_with_response_format_is_rejected_loudly` on both transports plus `a_client_effort_default_under_a_response_format_is_rejected_loudly` / `a_client_effort_default_under_a_response_format_is_rejected_loudly_on_stream` (a client default is caught by the same gate on both transports — each of the two gate copies carries its own pin), the fail-loud default gate by the effort direction of `default_with_options_rejects_every_unsupported_field`, the inheritance rule by `a_per_request_unset_inherits_the_client_default`, and the client-default layering by `a_client_effort_default_is_overridden_per_request` on all three providers (default alone reaches the wire, the per-request value wins, a request without one keeps the default).

- **Reasoning persistence and replay — `MessagePart::Thinking` history, `replay_reasoning(...)` on the OpenAI and Anthropic client builders, and thinking-aware compaction** — streamed reasoning now survives the turn: the stream accumulator gathers thinking deltas into a `MessagePart::Thinking` part on the assistant message instead of discarding them, compaction's character rendering counts thinking text like text (both token counters share the rendering, pinned by `thinking_text_counts_like_text_in_both_counters`, neutralization-proven — zeroing the thinking half-arm turns exactly this pin red), eviction-to-memory demotion renders a demoted trace as a ` thought: …` line and a redacted trace as ` thought: [redacted]` — never a dangling bare marker (pinned by `render_names_a_thinking_part_as_a_thought_line` and `render_marks_a_redacted_thinking_part_without_dangling_the_marker`, the latter fail-first red on the dangling form), and the Bedrock converter skips the part outright. Replay is opt-in per client and off by default — requests stay byte-identical to a client that never saw reasoning. When enabled, the OpenAI client joins each history thinking part, in part order, into the assistant message's `reasoning_content` field — the field `GLM` round-trips natively; `DeepSeek` documents the input lane only as a Beta prefix-completion feature (`prefix: true` on the last assistant message), so the field reaches it best-effort, not as a guaranteed round-trip; the non-streaming reader captures `message.reasoning_content` into a `MessagePart::Thinking` part ahead of the visible text, so the round trip's capture half exists on `create_message` too, not only on the streaming transport (fail-first pinned by `build_response_captures_reasoning_content_as_a_thinking_part`) — and the Anthropic client renders each part as its own capped `32 768`-character `<thinking>` text block ahead of the turn's other content, plain text that every Messages-compatible endpoint accepts, including gateways that reject unsigned native thinking blocks — the cap counted in characters and pinned at its boundary by `replay_reasoning_tail_keeps_exactly_the_last_32_768_chars` (at-cap whole, one-over drops exactly the head, and a multi-byte trace loses whole characters, never halves). Pinned per provider by `replayed_reasoning_rides_the_wire_only_when_enabled` (the block/field reaches the wire when on, nothing does when off) and `every_history_thinking_part_rides_the_replay_in_order` (fail-first red: the converters originally kept only the last of several thinking parts — a message with interleaved reasoning dropped all but the final trace on replay; both providers now carry every part, in order). The tool-result replay payload — the `[prior assistant reasoning]` user message the OpenAI client appends when a history message mixes tool results and reasoning — carries the marker line plus the trace with no source indentation (twelve spaces of the literal's layout previously rode the wire; `the_tool_result_reasoning_payload_carries_no_source_indentation`, fail-first red on the indented payload). The replay-tail helper is hoisted to the shared provider module — the `openai`-only feature combination compiles again (the OpenAI renderer called the anthropic-gated helper, breaking `cargo check --features openai`; found while re-running the probe pins under that combination — `replay_reasoning_tail_keeps_exactly_the_last_32_768_chars` moved with the helper), and the reasoning golden mock's exhausted script fails the call with a typed `ApiError` naming the fixture bug instead of panicking while holding the script lock (`an_exhausted_reasoning_script_fails_the_call_instead_of_panicking`, fail-first red on the panic).

- **Signed thinking blocks survive for Anthropic continuations — `MessagePart::Thinking` and `DeltaPart::Thinking` gain `signature` and `redacted` fields, and the Anthropic client returns them natively under an effective effort** — extended thinking signs each reasoning block, and the Messages API rejects a continuation whose assistant tool-use message lacks the original `thinking`/`redacted_thinking` block (`400: Expected thinking or redacted_thinking, but found tool_use`); the response path dropped both, so effort plus a tool loop could not complete. The emitter now carries `signature_delta` and the `redacted_thinking` payload as thinking-lane deltas (each with empty `text`, preserving the render-a-placeholder signal), the accumulator latches them onto the flushed part — a true latch that a later text-bearing thinking delta cannot clear (`a_text_fragment_after_the_signature_keeps_the_latched_signature`, fail-first red against the last-write-wins write it replaced) — and the non-streaming reader captures the same fields from complete blocks, and request building returns a signed or redacted part verbatim — `{"type":"thinking","thinking":…,"signature":…}` / `{"type":"redacted_thinking","data":…}` ahead of `tool_use` — whenever an effective thinking-effort level is set, independent of the replay flag; the plain `<thinking>` text-block replay still governs when thinking is not enabled. Serialization is compatible both ways: the new fields default when absent and serialize only when carried.
