Pinned by `signature_delta_rides_the_thinking_lane_as_its_own_delta` and `redacted_thinking_data_rides_the_thinking_lane` (superseding the inverted `emitter_signature_delta_is_ignored`), `signature_and_redacted_payload_survive_into_the_built_message`, `build_response_captures_thinking_blocks_with_their_signatures` (superseding the inverted skip pin), and the wire pins `signed_thinking_blocks_ride_continuations_when_effort_is_enabled` / `redacted_thinking_blocks_ride_continuations_verbatim` / `signed_blocks_stay_text_replay_when_thinking_is_disabled` (the replay arm is neutralization-proven — deleting it turns exactly the first two red), and the unsigned-history exposure by `an_unsigned_thinking_part_leaves_tool_use_alone_under_effort`: an unsigned part under an effective effort sends `tool_use` alone on the wire — the Messages API `400` the `thinking_effort` builder doc tells pre-upgrade, cross-provider, and hand-built histories to avoid — with the skip arm neutralization-proven (letting unsigned parts ride natively turns exactly this pin red). Histories recorded before this change carry unsigned parts and still lean on the plain-text replay.


- **The filesystem tool family — `tool::builtin::fs`'s `WriteTool`, `EditTool`, `MultiEditTool`, `FileViewerTool`, and `FileSource`, behind the additive `fs_tools` feature** — the file-writing tool family as library capability, with a stable model-facing surface: names (`"Write"`, `"Edit"`, `"MultiEdit"`, `"FileViewer"`), schemas, and output strings (`Created:`/`Changed:` diff previews — an overwritten file whose previous bytes are not UTF-8 gets a `Changed:` header that says so instead of a creation report, and an existing target that cannot be opened or read refuses the write rather than being replaced — conflict/staleness/resumed messages, FileViewer headers and `[Navigate:]` hints), staleness semantics (hash and byte-exact detect-on-write checks, the identity re-check at rename, newest-observation-wins baselines, and resume markers holding the first write of **any** writing tool — Write, Edit, and MultiEdit alike — for a live read), `MAX_EDITS = 50`, page-size defaults and caps, containment policies, `dry_run`, and `skip_linter`. One `FileSession` wires the family: the working directory every path resolves against, the `ResolvePolicy` (`Contained` default with the lexical-plus-symlink containment walk — post-open verification on every platform: through the descriptor on Linux; off Linux on unix by the opened handle's identity — `fstat` on the handle against `fstatat(AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW)` of the entry its spelling's landing names through that landing's pinned parent directory, itself walked no-follow from the anchor, compared dev/ino through `TargetIdentity` — and a verdict-time current-location gate asks the kernel where the opened handle lives right now (`fcntl(F_GETPATH)` on Apple platforms) and refuses unless that location is under the pinned root, so a directory relocated out from under the anchor between the walk and the verdict cannot make an outside handle pass; on unix platforms with no way to read an open file's current location the gate fails closed and contained reads are refused there outright (a deliberate tightening — the supported matrix is Linux and macOS), so a path pointed outside at open and restored inside before the check is refused (pinned by `portable_verify_refuses_a_read_opened_outside_and_restored_inside` and `portable_verify_refuses_a_depth_two_read_opened_outside_and_restored_inside`, with `portable_verify_accepts_a_read_through_an_inside_symlink` and `portable_verify_accepts_a_depth_two_read_whose_entry_unchanged_under_the_anchor` pinning the preserved legit reads through the pinned-parent walk, `a_multi_component_name_is_refused_by_the_entry_stat` pinning the entry stat's single-name contract, and `an_inside_handle_passes_the_current_location_gate`, `an_outside_handle_is_refused_by_the_current_location_gate`, and `a_relocated_handle_is_refused_by_the_current_location_gate` pinning the current-location gate itself, with `a_handle_on_a_platform_without_a_location_lookup_fails_closed` pinning the other-unix fail-closed arm; the residual is the hardlink case — an outside file already hardlinked inside shares the opened identity — plus the post-check relocation window, the check-then-act residual every such pair carries, both outside the path-swap threat model); name-based against the pinned root on platforms without descriptor operations, their documented residual — so replacing the workspace spelling mid-session cannot redirect contained reads or writes outside the session's root — `Unrestricted` for operator opt-out), the pinned workspace anchor, and the shared baseline map — installed on each `ToolContext` via `attach` and shared with `FileSource`, the filesystem `ContentSource` the `read` tool reads through, so every read arms the staleness guard the next write against the same file consults (the resume re-arm `record_resumed_read` re-arms restored sessions with marked baselines). Syntax validation is a host policy, not a library one: `ContentValidator` is an object-safe optional gate whose diagnostics block the write with the family's shared refusal text shape (a host plugging equivalent diagnostics gets the same blocking behavior), and `skip_linter` bypasses it for one call. Contained writes persist exclusively through the descriptor-pinned walk (`openat` + `O_NOFOLLOW`, `mkdirat`, `renameat`, identity re-check by `fstatat`) on unix — Linux and macOS alike, a workspace the operator spells through an alias included — and are refused outright on platforms without descriptor-relative operations: there is deliberately no pathname-based fallback, so no persistence path re-resolves a validated pathname; `MultiEdit`'s batch contract is intact (validate-all-then-write, overlap/duplicate/alias refusals — hard-link aliases keyed by unix dev/ino via std, mid-batch external changes reporting the files already written). `FileViewer` refuses non-regular targets softly (a directory, a FIFO, or a device can never hang the viewer on an open or a stream that never ends) and its windows come from single-pass streaming scans with a 16 KiB per-line display cap — a huge file costs its window, not its size, and a single-line giant costs at most one capped line — while IO stays O(file) because pagination needs the line total; diff output flushes trailing context only when it closes a change region, so distant unchanged tail lines no longer render as if contiguous with the last hunk. `fs_tools = ["builtin_tools", "dep:libc", "dep:tempfile", "tokio/fs", "tokio/io-util"]`, `default = []` untouched. The output surface is pinned by `tool_behavior_matches_the_golden_corpus` — a 20-scenario corpus (writes, edits, multiedits, viewer pages, and the four schemas) replayed byte-for-byte, workspaces normalized, with the corpus's lint findings reproduced by a table validator so gate ordering is part of the pin; sequences are pinned by `conflict_stress_suite_passes` (repeated writes, external changes and reverts, recovery loops, spelling aliases, batch-refreshed baselines, view-only reads arming nothing); the memory bound by `streaming_read_memory_is_bounded_by_window` (a 1 GiB sparse fixture serves a 100-line window under a 256 MiB VmHWM growth budget, proven by neutralization — a whole-file mutant grew 1029 MiB and went red; the streaming pass restored and green) and its single-line sibling `a_single_giant_line_is_capped_not_loaded` (a one-line 256 MiB fixture serves its capped window under the same budget).

- **Line-aware read tooling — `tool::builtin::read`'s `ContentSource` and `ReadTool`** — reading content through any source a host cares to define, with a contract that makes truncation impossible to miss. `ContentSource` is an object-safe trait (boxed futures, the `LoopMemory` house pattern) that decides what an address *means* — a filesystem path, a blob at a pinned revision, a document id — and returns either decoded `SourceContent::Text` or raw `SourceContent::Bytes`; `ReadTool<S>` owns windowing and kind over it. Resolution rules: explicit `offset` (1-indexed) and `limit` win over `line_range`; `line_range` accepts `'1-100'`, `'50:'`, `':100'`, or a single line; zero `offset`/`limit`, malformed or inverted ranges, and negative values are `InvalidInput`, never silently defaulted; limits clamp to the line ceiling; an `offset` beyond the content yields a one-line "beyond content length" message; empty content reports itself as empty. Every output line is numbered `cat -n` style, right-aligned to the widest shown line number. Markers: a view starting past line 1 opens with `[Lines before offset N omitted — use offset=1 to read from the start]`; a line-ceiling cut ends with `[FILE TRUNCATED: Showing lines X-Y of T. Use offset=N to see the remaining R lines.]`; a byte-ceiling cut (long-line content) is cut back to complete lines and closes with a footer naming the returned range, the next offset, and the remedy — or, when no complete line fits, points at the first line that does, or past the window when none does; a partial view that reaches the end closes with the plain `[Showing lines X-Y of T]`; whole content under both ceilings carries no markers at all. Defaults — 400 lines, 32 768 bytes of joined output, a 400-line window for offset-only reads — overridable via `with_max_lines` / `with_max_bytes` / `with_default_offset_limit` (values below 1 clamp to 1); a `size` probe on the source (default: unknown) drives a refuse-before-read guard at a 10 MiB default (`with_max_size_bytes`), and the refusal states plainly that the limit applies to whole content, not the requested window. Extension-detected images (png/jpg/jpeg/gif/webp) return native multipart output — a standard-base64 `ImageSource` part plus a one-line summary naming address, size, and MIME, capped by encoded size (`5 MiB` default, `with_max_image_bytes`) so an oversized image is refused in the tool output instead of failing a later provider request; other binary content is refused with a soft error naming the address and byte count. The tool reports `is_read_only` and `is_concurrency_safe`; source errors surface as their own `ToolError` verbatim — no wrapping, no retry. Pinned by six named pins — `explicit_offset_and_limit_win_over_line_range`, `the_full_file_is_returned_when_no_range_is_given`, `a_zero_limit_or_offset_is_rejected_as_invalid_input`, `a_cut_output_names_the_next_offset_and_the_total_range`, `an_uncut_output_carries_no_truncation_marker`, `a_source_error_surfaces_as_execution_unchanged` — plus the edge matrix (line-range formats, malformed ranges, negatives, ceiling clamp, byte-cap and no-complete-line markers, beyond-length and empty-content messages, numbering alignment, builder overrides and zero clamps, the image payload vector and ceiling, binary refusal, the size guard, the mid-content no-fit header, and the flags) against one fake source, with the default ceilings pinned by `the_default_ceilings_are_the_documented_ones` (the exact `400`-line cut and offset-window markers — a loosened `contains`-style check that could not tell the ceiling from `500` was tightened back to the marker text).

- **The v1 `loop.yaml` manifest model — `manifest::ManifestDocument`, `ResolvedManifest`, and the `manifest` feature** — a loop's declarative center becomes a library-owned model so the CLI, cassettes, and docs share one resolver instead of each growing a parser: strict serde types for every section (`version`, `metadata`, `agent`, `models` with named entries and a `fallbacks` chain, `tools`, `mcp` servers as argv arrays, `permissions` mode plus deny/allow rules, `budgets`, `context`, `memory`, `schedule`, `triggers`, `cassettes`, `sandbox`, `profiles`), all `deny_unknown_fields` + `#[non_exhaustive]` + schemars-exportable, behind one additive feature (`manifest = ["dep:serde_yaml_ng", "dep:schemars"]`; the archived `serde_yaml` is not a runtime dep). `ManifestDocument::parse` runs a strict typed pass whose unknown-field failures carry the dotted field path, the offending key's own line:column, and a Levenshtein did-you-mean suggestion (extracted from `serde`'s stable message shape, with a lossless fallback to the raw message); the version gate hard-errors at parse time on anything other than `1`, and multi-document YAML is refused. `resolve(profile, &dyn EnvResolver)` applies the named profile as a tag-aware deep-merge over the raw tree (scalars replace, maps merge recursively, lists replace unless `!append`; overlays touching `version` or nesting `profiles` are rejected — the strict parse catches the textual case with a span, a raw-tree guard covers the programmatic one), re-parses the merged tree as defense in depth, validates the written document before expansion (version gate, builtin-XOR-mcp tool entries, finite numbers in every `f64` field — `budgets.cost_usd`, `context.compaction.trigger`; a deterministic prefix scan — `sk-`, `AKIA`, `eyJ`, `ghp_`/`github_pat_`, `xoxb-`, no entropy heuristic, so `pin: sha256:…` never trips — rejects literal key-shaped strings in any token of any value, `token_key` included: after separators like `Bearer sk-…` and inside reference defaults like `${VAR:-sk-…}`), and validates the effective document after expansion (model/tool/MCP-server cross-references resolve against expanded names; rule strings carry the `tool:pattern` shape against expanded text) — so a defaulted `${SERVER:-github}` reference validates the server it names, and a rule that expands shapeless is rejected instead of reaching the gate engine. Profiles carry their own all-optional overlay types (`ModelsOverlay`/`ModelEntryOverlay`, `McpServerOverlay`), so a profile retunes one field of one model or server without re-declaring the entry; required fields are enforced on the merged document, where they belong. Interpolation is compose-style (`${VAR}`, `${VAR:-default}`, `${VAR:?message}`, `${secret:NAME}`, `$${` escape) over the JSON projection — one walk covers every present and future string field — with two exemptions by design: `token_key` values (env-var names, never values) and the unapplied `profiles` section (declarations resolve when their profile is chosen, not before — an unused overlay's `${…}` can neither fail another profile's resolve nor move its hash). The result is two views with one pin: the runtime manifest (every effective reference expanded, secret-consuming strings expanded for process wiring, never to be logged) and the pinning projection, whose FNV-1a 64 `config_hash()` over the sorted-keys canonical JSON is the identity pin every run and cassette records — never a function of an unselected profile's environment or of a secret's value, whatever field the reference lives in: unapplied profiles are absent from the pin, and any string whose written form carries a `${secret:…}` reference (plus MCP server `env` maps wholesale) is pinned as written. The resolver is injectable (`EnvResolver`, default `EnvVars` = process env, `EnvMap` for deterministic tests), so keyring/file resolution chains are the consumer's addition. `manifest_json_schema()` exports the draft-2020-12 JSON Schema from the same derives that parse the document (`additionalProperties: false` on every object type), so the schema cannot drift from the parser. Pinned by `unknown_field_error_carries_span_and_suggestion`, `profiles_merge_scalars_maps_lists_with_documented_semantics`, `missing_required_env_var_fails_validation_with_variable_name`, `literal_api_key_in_manifest_fails_validation`, `schema_export_round_trips_every_type` (strictness, draft URL, and a to_value/from_value round trip), `unused_profile_references_neither_fail_resolution_nor_move_the_hash`, `mcp_env_pins_as_written_and_never_leaks_resolved_secrets`, `secret_references_outside_mcp_env_pin_as_written`, `non_finite_budget_values_fail_validation`, and `non_finite_compaction_triggers_fail_validation` (`tests/manifest_model.rs`; the defect pins were written red first and the schema strictness was neutralization-checked), `defaulted_references_in_names_and_rules_validate_on_the_expanded_document`, `rules_without_a_shape_after_expansion_fail_validation`, and `a_profile_retunes_one_field_of_a_model_entry`, plus the in-module unit suites for the grammar, merge edges, version gate, cross-refs, hash determinism, and the secret-scan token boundaries.
- **Compaction is observable start-to-end — `on_pre_compaction`, a widened `on_compaction`, and a live `CompactTelemetry`** — observers could only hear that the context shrank (three token fields), never that a pass was starting, why it ran, or how well it compacted; the rich `CompactTelemetry` existed but was dead code on the runtime path (constructed in one unit test, never by the engine). `LoopObserver` gains `on_pre_compaction(&PreCompactionContext)` — notification-only, fired after the manager-exists check and before the pre-compact hook veto, so vetoed, cancelled, and no-action passes show a start without a completion (the span shape observability backends expect); hooks remain the flow-control path and are untouched, with the distinction stated on both sides. The pass-completed `on_compaction` widens once, additively under the existing `#[non_exhaustive]` marker: `reason` (the `CompactReason`, matching the pre event), `evicted_messages` (the demotion sink's delivery size — pairing the window's loss with memory's intake on the same run), and `telemetry: CompactTelemetry`, now built by the engine on every compacting pass. The telemetry itself gains the full design: pre/post role-scoped token distribution (`user_tokens` + `assistant_tokens` sums to the whole-slice estimate; tool results count under their actual `Role::User`), post-side role message counts, `density` (tokens per message, `0.0` for empty), `compression_ratio` (`post == 0` → `INFINITY`; a pass that grew the conversation reports an honest `< 1.0`), `headroom_pct` (`1 − post / context_window` clamped to `0.0..=1.0`, computed against the manager's own window; a window of `0` reports the `0.0` sentinel — no window to measure against, not a full window), and `compactor_name` (host-supplied; the engine passes `None` — the concrete type behind `Arc<dyn ContextCompactor>` is unrecoverable without `Any` bounds). Compaction duration is now measured un-feature-gated (the hooks arm reuses the same start). Pinned by `both_events_fire_once_per_compacting_pass`, `the_pre_event_carries_the_pre_state`, `the_post_event_carries_reason_and_evicted_count`, `a_cancelled_pass_fires_pre_without_post`, `a_no_action_pass_fires_pre_without_post`, `a_vetoed_pass_fires_pre_without_post`, and `the_chain_orders_pre_demote_post_and_hook` (the new `tests/compaction_telemetry.rs`), the observer dispatch pins `on_pre_compaction_default_is_noop` and `host_dispatches_on_pre_compaction_in_registration_order`, the computation suite (`compression_ratio_math`, `headroom_clamps_to_the_unit_interval`, `role_token_distribution_sums_to_the_estimate`, `density_is_tokens_per_message`, `compactor_name_threads_through`), the duration pin `the_reported_duration_covers_the_compactors_call_time`, and the two doc examples.

- **Eviction→memory demotion — `DemotionSink`, `MemoryDemotionSink`, `render_evicted`, and `CompactionOutcome::evicted`** — compaction stops destroying information: every message a pass removes from the feed is handed to a pluggable sink once per completed pass, before the compacted history replaces the old one, so eviction becomes a handoff instead of a discard (a retried pass may redeliver the same slice — sinks must tolerate redelivery; the engine delivers at most once per pass that classifies `Compacted`). `CompactionOutcome.evicted` carries the removed messages (message-granular, conversation order — every input message with no surviving output representative, computed as the complement over the truncator's origins map, which folds the dropped slice, orphan-emptied messages, and below-`min_messages` sanitize-reduced passes into one rule: removed ⇒ demoted); the type is already `#[non_exhaustive]`, so the field is additive — the three outcome constructors keep their signatures and producers chain `with_evicted`. `DemotionSink` implementations must tolerate redelivery (keying on content identity keeps duplicates detectable) and be drop-safe (the engine awaits `demote` inside the cancel-raced compaction future — a cancel mid-write drops it, and since the machine never adopts the compacted history, nothing leaves the feed undelivered); a rejected delivery logs one warning and never fails the compaction that already succeeded. `MemoryDemotionSink` renders the slice through the public `render_evicted` (role lines, `calls: name(input)` and `result[name, ok|err]` summaries, `[image]` markers, char-boundary-safe saturation with a `…[evicted {n} more messages]` marker that appends past the 8 000-char default body budget via `with_max_chars`) and stores one `MemoryCategory::Trajectory` entry per delivery, tagged `demoted`, `demoted-hash:<FNV-1a>` (a fixed-algorithm hash, stable across Rust releases, so persisted tags never churn), and `session:<uuid>` — duplicate deliveries are detectable by the shared hash tag and fold under consolidation's near-duplicate merging when the same store also backs the engine's memory (`set_memory`) and a run completes successfully; consolidation runs nowhere else. The sink lives on the manager bundle beside memory (`LoopManagers::with_demotion_sink`/`set_demotion_sink`/`demotion_sink`, `BareLoop::with_demotion_sink`), survives manager resets, and defaults to `NoopDemotionSink` — behavior byte-identical to before demotion existed. Hosts driving `compact_manual`/`compact_with_reason` directly receive `evicted` and own the demotion. Pinned by the `src/compact/demote.rs` unit suite (renderer shape, saturation boundary, hash determinism, trajectory storage, duplicate detectability, noop, explicit and zero render budgets, and the duplicate-fold under consolidation), the truncator's evicted pins (population + exactly-one-of-output-or-evicted, clean no-change paths, orphan-filtered and below-minimum-reduced demotions, first-message/pulled survival, and the partition rule as a proptest over generated conversations), the manager's rogue-claim classification pins (an unverified `evicted` claim rides through a token-shrinking pass and is discarded by a no-shrink one — the field is the compactor's claim, never diffed), and `tests/demotion_sink.rs` (`demoted_content_is_retrievable` — the compact-then-recall headline over `InMemoryStore`; `sink_failure_does_not_fail_compaction`; `noop_default_is_byte_identical`; `a_cancel_during_a_hanging_sink_ends_the_run_typed`; `with_demotion_sink_wires_the_manager` — constructed through the `with_demotion_sink` builder; `delivery_precedes_the_compaction_observer` and, under `hooks`, `delivery_precedes_the_post_compact_hook` — one ordered log; `memory_and_sink_share_one_store_across_turns` — both halves over one store with recall on, no trigger spiral; `default_budget_recall_does_not_spiral_the_compaction_trigger` — the adversarial default-budget sizing driven, surviving because the recalled transient never feeds the history estimate; `an_emergency_pass_delivers_with_its_reason`).

- **Cassette record/replay harness for provider wire contracts (test infrastructure, `httpmock` + `serde_yaml` dev-deps)** — a recorded exchange is ground truth produced by the real server; replay matches the request **body bytes** exactly, plus method, path, and the recorded query pairs and headers, so a request built differently than the real server accepted misses every mock and fails as a 404 (parameters or headers the client *adds* beyond what was recorded are invisible to the match — value changes are caught, additions are not). That miss is the outbound-drift guard: self-authored mocks pin our own mental model of the wire, which is how the Bedrock `"system": null` bugs shipped. Recording is a deliberate human act (`LOOPCTL_CASSETTE=record` + `LOOPCTL_E2E=1` + real credentials for non-local providers — the harness panics otherwise) driven from `tests/provider_e2e.rs` through a forwarding proxy; everything is scrubbed before touching disk (credentials never recorded — header allowlist at record time, sensitive query values rewritten to a fixed `cassette-key`; provider-generated ids `msg_`/`call_`/`cmpl-`/`chatcmpl-`/`resp_` become deterministic placeholders renamed consistently across bodies, paths, and pair values, so an id echoed from a response into a later request keeps matching), and a fail-closed meta-test (`committed_cassettes_carry_no_secrets`) scans the corpus on every run — an empty corpus fails it too. Binary bodies (`body_base64`) are refused at replay rather than silently losing the body matcher. Initial corpus: eleven cassettes across seven providers, each replayed hermetically in CI — local-Ollama `openai-compat` (`minimal_text`, `tool_call_lifecycle`), real-OpenAI `openai` (`minimal_text`; `tool_call_lifecycle` with the fragmented tool-argument stream Ollama emits as one delta), `anthropic` (`claude-sonnet-4-5`: `minimal_text`, `tool_call_lifecycle` with `input_json_delta` fragments), `gemini` (`gemini-3.5-flash`: `minimal_text`, `tool_call_lifecycle` with `functionCall` parts), and `deepseek`/`grok`/`zai` minimal-text recordings — pinning all three tool-calling dialects and six providers' response shapes. Error-shaped recordings are not wired — a mid-stream-error cassette needs only a scenario and driver, while a 429-then-200 retry pair also needs sequenced serving (first-match replay cannot tell two byte-identical retry requests apart, so it would serve the 429 to every attempt). The harness itself is pinned by `replay_fails_on_body_drift` (proven red by neutralization — body matching dropped, the drifted request was served 200), `replay_consumes_every_interaction`, `scrubber_placeholders_ids`, `scrubber_handles_multi_byte_text_around_generated_ids`, `renames_handle_prefix_overlapping_ids`, `renames_require_a_leading_token_boundary`, `scrub_drops_noise_response_headers_and_rewrites_sensitive_queries`, `binary_cassette_bodies_are_refused_at_replay`, `cassette_safety_flags_a_planted_key`, `record_mode_requires_credentials`, `record_mode_requires_provider_credentials`, and `unconfigured_environment_defaults_to_replay`. Cassettes and review records are excluded from the publish tarball before the corpus can approach the crate-size limit. The recording doctrine ships with the harness: record first, derive the assertion, review the derivation; never weaken a cassette to make it pass — provider drift re-enters only through a deliberate re-record, at minimum once per minor release; and every unit test of provider-facing behavior must say why it isn't a cassette test. One production fix rode along (see Fixed): the `gemini`-only feature combination did not compile.

- **`#[derive(Tool)]` failure-mode matrix at full breadth** — fifteen new trybuild cases join the existing set (28 compile-fail + 3 pass): the `allow_extra`/`deny_unknown_fields` conflict, lifetime-bearing inputs, a handler attribute that is not an identifier, a handler with the right name but a non-`async fn run` shape, non-string attribute values, tuple and unit structs, container-level `#[serde(from)]`/`rename_all_fields` rejections, values on flag keys, two pass pins (private fields in a `pub` struct; `deny_unknown_fields` alone), and the empty-string rejections. The generic-struct rejection now names lifetime parameters in its span. Name and description overrides must be non-empty — `#[tool(name = "")]` and `#[tool(description = "")]` fail at the attribute's span, and a doc comment with no text now counts as absent (so the missing-description guard cannot be bypassed by an empty `///`); an empty name or description never reaches a provider that would reject it at request time. `crates/derive/README.md` documents the supported/rejected table the matrix pins, 1:1 — nothing is warned; every mode the derive cannot mirror exactly, or cannot accept, is a hard error.
- **`MockApiClient` captures streaming requests** (feature `testing`) — `captured_stream_requests()` clones out every request the mock served on the streaming transport, oldest-first, recorded at call time (before the returned stream is polled), mirroring `captured_requests()`'s point-in-time contract — so a test keys behavior off streaming request content (system prompt, conversation shape) instead of matching on the request's `Debug` rendering. The rejected-before-send rule holds on both transports: an options-carrying call rejected at the guard is counted by `with_options_calls` — which now tallies `stream_messages_with_options` alongside `create_message_with_options` — but never captured. A call failed by a scripted `with_errors` entry is still captured on both transports; the `with_errors` doc no longer claims such calls are *counted* — plain calls have no counter, only the options-carrying twins do. `captured_requests()` (non-streaming) keeps its name, shape, and behavior. Pinned by `a_streaming_request_is_captured_with_its_exact_shape`, `a_streaming_request_is_captured_before_the_stream_is_polled`, `a_scripted_streaming_failure_is_still_captured`, `a_streaming_request_rejected_before_send_is_counted_not_captured`, `a_rejected_streaming_call_between_accepted_ones_leaves_the_list_contiguous`, `captures_preserve_call_order_across_both_transports`, and `concurrent_calls_on_both_transports_keep_their_captures_intact`.
- **Transport-fallback surfacing (`Turn::transport_fallback`, `Run::transport_fallback_count()`, `on_transport_fallback`)** — when streaming exhausts its retry ceiling and the handler's last-chance non-streaming request serves the turn, that degradation is now caller-visible instead of indistinguishable from a healthy streamed turn: the run record flags the turn, `Run::transport_fallback_count()` totals them, and a new defaulted `LoopObserver::on_transport_fallback` hook (with `TransportFallbackContext { turn, stop_reason }`) fires live for each one, before its `on_turn_end`. The fallback itself is a degradation report, not a failure (unrelated policies can still abort the turn afterwards, leaving no flagged record), and the `fallback_to_non_streaming = false` path is unchanged (the exhausted ladder still fails the turn outright). Distinct from `on_fallback`, which reports a *model* fallback. Runs serialized before the flag existed deserialize with it unset. Pinned by `a_fallback_served_turn_is_flagged_on_the_run_record`, `a_healthy_streamed_turn_is_never_flagged`, `disabling_the_fallback_still_fails_the_turn_outright`, `runs_serialized_before_the_flag_still_deserialize`, `the_observer_hook_fires_for_fallback_turns_only` (which also pins hook-before-success-bookkeeping ordering and the stop-reason payload against the record), `a_tool_calling_fallback_run_counts_every_fallback_turn` (multi-turn and tool-carrying totals), and the non-streaming-mode assertions in `non_streaming_turn_returns_assembled_message`.
- **Per-turn stop reason (`Turn::stop_reason`, `TurnEndContext::stop_reason`)** — a run's turn record and the `on_turn_end` observer event now say why each turn's *model* stopped, so a max-tokens truncation (`StreamStopReason::MaxTokens`) is distinguishable from a clean final answer (`EndTurn`) without parsing the text — the distinction a caller could not recover before (`Run::stop_reason` covers only terminal run *errors*, so a truncation passed as success). The value is the provider's stop field verbatim: `Turn` records it from the turn's model call, and each turn's single turn-end event carries it — the LLM-phase event for a tool-free turn, and for a tool-carrying turn the tool-phase event, which forwards the recorded turn's reason (matching the token-pair provenance); turns that failed before the model finished hold the `EndTurn` default with `success`/`error` telling that story. `StreamStopReason` gains `Default` (`EndTurn`), matching the wire layer's absent-field precedent, so runs serialized before the field existed deserialize with `stop_reason == EndTurn`. Pinned by `a_max_tokens_stop_surfaces_on_the_turn_record`, `a_normal_end_turn_is_distinguishable_from_max_tokens`, `the_tool_phase_forwards_the_recorded_turns_stop_reason`, `each_turn_fires_exactly_one_turn_end_event`, `runs_serialized_before_the_field_still_deserialize`, and the cancel-path assertion in `run_cancel_during_streaming_returns_fast`.

- **Cassette replay asserts contract headers are present (test infrastructure)** — closing a hole the cassette doctrine itself recorded: replay matched only the recorded header allowlist, so a client refactor that stopped sending `anthropic-version`, `x-api-key`, `x-goog-api-key`, or `authorization` passed every hermetic test (cassettes, goldens, error-path contracts alike) and failed only against the real provider. Every replay mock now requires the provider's contract headers to be *present* — presence only, never the value, since credentials are never recorded — for all seven corpus dialects (`anthropic`/`zai`: `x-api-key` + `anthropic-version`; `gemini`: `x-goog-api-key`; `openai`/`deepseek`/`grok`/`openai-compat`: `authorization`). Proven fail-first: the pin's missing-header direction served 200 before the guard (`left: 200, right: 404` captured), misses after; all eleven replay suites pass unchanged, confirming every corpus client already sends its contract headers. Pinned by `replay_requires_the_provider_contract_headers`. Additions of new headers beyond the recording remain invisible, as before — value changes and dropouts are caught.

- **Rate-limit cassette machinery (test infrastructure)** — a 429 cassette can now land without harness changes: three `rate_limit_error` scenarios (anthropic, gemini, openai) carry a dedicated attempt driver that runs the minimal request once against the real provider — no bursts — and `CassetteSession::finish_rate_limited()` writes the cassette only when every recorded response is actually a 429, dropping the exchange otherwise so the scenario's name never lies about what the file holds (a first attempt against all three providers met no 429 and correctly wrote nothing). The regular record drivers exclude the rate-limit scenarios; the replay tests ship `#[ignore]`d and activate when a cassette lands, asserting the replayed exchange surfaces as the structured `ApiError::RateLimit` with the recorded `Retry-After` parsed (the expectation is computed from the cassette, never hardcoded). The 429-then-200 retry-pair limitation stands until sequenced serving exists.

- **Redirection extraction sees both wrapper levels, and the verifier checks the real parent under the default cwd** — a redirection outside a shell wrapper (`sh -c 'echo hi' > out.txt`) was dropped when the payload replaced the outer tokens, so the cached read of the outer target went stale; both levels are now one write set. And the write-verifier's path check resolved the default `cwd: "."` against the filesystem root, checking the wrong directory in both directions (accepting writes whose real parent was missing, rejecting valid writes under relative cwds); a relative cwd is now anchored to the process working directory lexically before resolution — and the path extractor anchors the same way, so reads, writes, and the verifier share one path identity (the extractor previously root-anchored relative cwds, including its context-free seam's implicit `.`). Pinned by the outer/both-levels redirection directions, `an_outer_wrapped_redirection_evicts_the_cached_read` (the eviction end-to-end), and `the_default_cwd_checks_the_real_parent` including the fail-first `src/x` direction that root anchoring fails.

- **`RunConfig::with_reset_managers` builder + property-shaped pins** — the `reset_managers` knob was `#[non_exhaustive]`-sealed: settable by no consumer outside the crate (the root cause of its never-exercised state), now a documented builder pinned by `reset_managers_returns_a_tripped_breaker_to_the_primary` (a tripped breaker re-routes to the primary under `with_reset_managers(true)`). Two contracts previously pinned only as scenarios gain property coverage: the tool-health oracle/gate agreement (`oracle_and_gate_agree_over_random_interleavings`, random probe/outcome interleavings asserting the pure oracle names the mutating gate's verdict) and memoize invalidation (`no_stale_entry_survives_a_completed_write_over_random_interleavings`, a shadow-model oracle over random read/write interleavings).

- **Real verifier + path extractor builtins — `CommandVerifier` and `WritePathExtractor`, plus the two seams they need** — the small-model profile's verify-on-write and memoization pillars stop being no-op scaffolds with zero host code. Two additive trait seams make them possible without a single public-API break: [`Verifier::verify_call`] (the model's input in hand; defaults to the existing `verify`, so project-state verifiers are unaffected) and [`PathExtractor::paths_with_cwd`] (the call's working directory; defaults to the existing `paths`, and the memoize middleware now passes the cwd so a read of `f.txt` under `/a` and a write spelling `../f.txt` under `/a/b` normalize to one key (`/a/f.txt`) and evict). **`CommandVerifier`** judges statically — nothing executes: a quote-aware tokenizer whose failures are soft verdicts naming the problem (`unparseable command: unterminated double quote`); a wrapped-shell allowlist (`sh`/`bash` default, diagnostic names the offender and the allowed set); deny patterns matched against the whitespace-normalized command **at token boundaries**, so `rm -rf /` and the compact fork bomb deny while `rm -rf /home/user/build` passes (extendable via `with_deny_globs`/`with_allowed_shells`); and edit/write path checks — the target's parent must exist and not be read-only, resolved against the cwd. **`WritePathExtractor`** probes the `path`-style fields (`path`/`file_path`/`filename`/`filepath`) and shell output redirections — the `>`, `>>`, `1>`, `1>>`, `2>`, `2>>`, `&>`, and `>&` operators, target inline (`>out.txt`, `2>err.log`) or as the next token, through one level of `<shell> -c` wrapping (descriptor duplications like `2>&1`/`>&2` and quoted redirects yield nothing) — normalized lexically — deliberately not `canonicalize`d, which would fail exactly for not-yet-written targets. Diagnostics are one-liners written for a small-model reader. Wiring: `ConstrainedProfile::pipeline_builder_with_builtin_verification()` swaps both no-ops for the builtins and extends both the verified list **and the memoize write list** with shell-class tool names, so a shell redirection to a cached path evicts it exactly like an edit-tool write — **explicit this release**: the default `pipeline_builder()` keeps the no-ops, and the 0.4.x flip to builtins-by-default is deliberately a separate change. Pinned by the `tests/builtin_verifier.rs` suite — the deny class with actionable diagnostics (wrapped and bare), benign writes passing including the token-boundary proof, unparseable-soft-not-silent, extraction across field names and `..` segments, redirection extraction (`2>&1` excluded), `write_invalidates_cached_read` (the end-to-end eviction through differing path spellings), and `profile_wiring_is_explicit_this_release` (the default passes what the verified variant denies — the flip will show as a seen diff). Proven red by neutralization: dropping the boundary matcher and the cwd seam failed three pins, restored green.

- **Golden request-bytes harness — the refactoring gate for request construction (test infrastructure)** — the exact outbound request the engine builds for a fixed fixture is frozen in committed canonical-JSON goldens under `tests/golden/`, so a refactor that changes system folding, tool-schema emission, memory injection, option routing, or a provider's wire body fails a golden diff instead of relying on a reviewer to notice the drift. Engine scenarios run deterministic fixtures through `BareLoop` against a recording client that captures the exact request-and-options pair of every outbound call on both transports — the pair the engine hands the provider: `minimal_chat`, `system_tools` (system prompt plus three registered schemas, pinned in registration order), `multi_turn_tool_results` (the second request carries the correlated tool result), `fallback_routed` (a scripted permanent auth failure trips the breaker; the golden shows the model override moving primary → fallback), `constrained_decode` (the `response_format` rides the options; tools are still advertised engine-side — suppression is a provider-layer behavior), and `memory_injected` (retrieved entries prepend the "Relevant memory…" transient message). Provider wire scenarios drive the real OpenAI/Anthropic/Gemini clients against a local server and capture the body each put on the wire from the recording (`openai_wire`, `anthropic_wire`, `gemini_wire` — one shared tool-round-trip conversation rendered in all three dialects). Canonicalization sorts object keys (struct field order is not a contract) while pinning array order. Regeneration is a deliberate, reviewed act — `UPDATE_GOLDENS=1 cargo test --all-features --test golden_requests`, then the diff is reviewed like code — and without the variable any difference, a missing golden, or a corrupt golden fails loudly, naming the scenario. Pinned by `golden_diff_fails_loudly` (drift, missing, and corrupt golden directions) and `update_goldens_is_explicit` (compare mode never writes; only the exact variable value regenerates).

- **The event hub — `observer::EventHub`, `LoopEvent`, and `ObservedEvent`** — the opt-in observer-to-broadcast adapter: a built-in `LoopObserver` that forwards every one of the twenty lifecycle callbacks onto a `tokio::sync::broadcast` channel as an owned `LoopEvent` (one `#[non_exhaustive]`-enum variant per callback, named as the callback without its `on_` prefix and carrying the cloned context) stamped with a hub-assigned `run_id` and a per-run `seq` into `ObservedEvent { run_id, seq, event }` — the run id steps up by one at each `on_run_start` (first run `1`), so the runs flowing through one hub are distinguishable, and the `seq` counter resets at the same moment, so each run numbers from 1. One hub serves at most one active run: do not share it across concurrently running engines — the counters are shared and no callback carries a run-scoped signal to attribute interleaved events by, so overlapping runs stamp overlapping, non-monotonic sequences (the misuse is at least visible: the run id restamps mid-flight with no intervening `RunEnd`); and a hub hands every subscriber the full content of every callback — user text, model output, tool inputs, and tool results (a result's text rides the following turn's start query; its `ToolPost` event carries hash metadata only) — so keep it within one trust scope. Notify-only semantics are preserved exactly: sends are synchronous best-effort whose errors are discarded, so a receiverless hub or a full ring is invisible to the engine, and a slow consumer is lagged by the channel — the consumer's own `RecvError::Lagged(n)` is the documented lag report (the hub cannot observe receiver state and logs nothing). Always-compiled core with no feature flag: `tokio/sync` is already an unconditional dependency and the house rule gates integrations, not engine adapters; `default = []` untouched. Register `Arc<EventHub>` through the engine's observer path and hand each consumer its own `subscribe()` receiver. Pinned by `event_hub_forwards_every_observer_callback` (a scripted engine run forwards exactly the callbacks it fires — 15 events for a tool turn plus a final answer, `text_delta`/`stream_success` before each `response` — in order, run id 1 throughout, seq 1..=n, a second run carrying run id 2 and restarting its sequence at 1), `slow_subscriber_is_lagged_not_blocking` (a never-draining capacity-2 consumer never blocks the run, then learns its dropped count from `Lagged(n)` and still receives the newest events), `every_callback_maps_to_its_event_kind_in_call_order` (all twenty mappings, in-crate with constructed contexts, seq 1..=20), `each_run_on_a_shared_hub_carries_its_own_run_id_and_sequence` (two sequential runs through one hub: run ids 1 then 2, each run's sequence restarting at 1), `a_mid_flight_run_start_restamps_subsequent_events_with_the_newer_run_id` (the visible signature of the unsupported concurrent share — a second `RunStart` before the first run's `RunEnd` restamps subsequent events with the newer id), `a_tool_results_text_rides_the_next_turn_start_not_the_tool_post_event` (the `tool_post` event carries the result hash and never the output text, while the following turn's start query carries that text — pinning both channels the trust-scope docs name), and `a_zero_capacity_hub_is_floored_to_one` pinning the capacity floor (`0` is floored to `1`, construction never panics).

- **Per-event ledger records — `memory::trajectory`'s `EventLedgerObserver`, `TrajectoryEvent`, and `TrajectoryEventKind`** — the per-event stream beside the per-run ledger: register `EventLedgerObserver` next to `TrajectoryObserver` and every mapped lifecycle callback lands as one JSONL line in a sibling `<dir>/events.jsonl`, through the same bounded `LedgerWriter` (drop-oldest queue, worker-thread writes, truncate-back repair — a slow sink never blocks a run). The envelope `{seq, ts, run_id, session_id, turn, kind, data}` numbers each run's events from `1` under a per-sink monotonic `run_id` (the event hub's discipline; cross-ledger correlation rides `session_id`, since the per-run ledger's `run_id` is its own minted uuid), with `turn` `null` for run-scoped kinds and the `turn.started` query truncated at the observer's capture limit (`with_capture_limit` configures it; the default, 2,000 characters, is the per-run ledger's). Ten v1 kinds with dotted labels (`run.started`/`run.ended`, `turn.started`/`turn.ended`, `compaction`, `fallback`, `model.switched`, `detection` — loop and convergence folded under one kind, discriminated by `data.detector` — `tool.call`, `tool.result`) — eleven with the `gate.decision` kind that joins this same release; deltas, `response`, stream events, `tool_call_received`, `pre_compaction`, `attempt_reset` (the streamed-attempt reset cue), and `transport.fallback` (the streaming-to-non-streaming transport retry — mechanics, distinct from the mapped model `fallback`) are excluded, and `usage` is the one reserved label this version does not emit — an unknown `kind` from a newer release deserializes to the fallback `Unknown` (the line's envelope and `data` survive for an older reader; the observer never emits it). `tool.result` carries metadata only (result hash, error flag, duration — never output text, which rides the following turn's query, the engine's documented channel); a run start before the previous run's end warns once and restamps id-and-sequence like the hub; callbacks outside an open run — before the first start and after a run's end, stray or duplicate — are ignored like the per-run observer; and the per-run `trajectory.jsonl` is untouched — `LedgerWriter` gained an internal file-name parameter and its per-run bytes are unchanged. Reader tolerance stays a consumer contract: an unparseable trailing line is end-of-record; no loader ships. Pinned by `event_records_land_in_ledger_in_order` (every mapped kind in call order, seq `1..=n` per run, the run id stepping across two runs, the session id on every line, turn nullness, and every kind's `data` object exactly), `per_run_record_stays_backward_compatible` (a legacy-format line still parses into `TrajectoryRecord`, the per-run file's bytes are exactly one serialized record, and the per-run observer never writes the events file), `events_outside_a_run_are_ignored` (nothing written before any run start, with a had-the-chance second run proving the observer records normally), `callbacks_after_a_run_end_are_ignored` (a stray turn start and a duplicate run end after the run's `run.ended` write nothing — the closed run's stream is sealed — and the next run records normally), `a_configured_capture_limit_bounds_the_events_ledger_query` (a ten-character limit truncates the `turn.started` query to ten characters), `a_run_start_before_the_previous_run_ends_warns_and_restamps` (exactly one warn, the id steps, the sequence restarts, already-written lines kept), `separate_ledger_files_do_not_share_lines` (one directory, two writers, and neither file's lines parse as the other's schema), `an_unknown_future_kind_still_parses_with_its_envelope_intact` (a `"kind":"usage"` line parses into `Unknown` with its `seq` and `data` intact — an older reader keeps a newer ledger's lines; the example rode `gate.decision` until that kind became real in this same release), and `the_observer_never_emits_the_unknown_kind` (a full run of every mapped kind produces no `Unknown` line — the fallback is deserialization-only).

- **Determinism seams — the `determinism` module (`Clock`/`SystemClock`/`FixedClock`, `IdGen`/`UuidIdGen`/`SeededIdGen`, `Rng`/`ThreadLocalRng`/`SeededRng`), installed via `LoopManagers::with_clock`/`with_id_gen` and `StreamHandler::with_rng`** — the three implicit nondeterminism sources in a run (monotonic time, uuid-v4 identity, the retry-jitter draw) become injectable, unblocking byte-stable cassette replay: a host installs a `FixedClock` plus a `SeededIdGen` and two same-seed engines produce identical captured request bytes, equal session ids, and equal per-position run ids — while default construction keeps the exact live reads (`Instant::now`, `SystemTime::now`, `Uuid::new_v4`, `fastrand::f64`), so behavior with untouched managers is unchanged and every golden stays byte-identical (none regenerated). Always compiled, no feature flag, no new dependency (`uuid` and `fastrand` are already core). Each seam sits at its consumer: clock + id-gen on `LoopManagers` (the session id is minted inside the `BareLoop` constructors, so a post-construction builder would be too late; the seams persist across `reset_all`, like the memory backend), rng on `StreamHandler` (the retry-jitter draw is the only engine-adjacent rng read, and the handler owns it). The engine now sources every id and duration through the seams: private `new_session`/`new_run` build the records in-crate from `managers.id_gen()`/`managers.clock()` (`Session::new`/`Run::new` keep minting v4 ids for external callers, documented as the unpinned path), plus session start, run end, both turn-phase starts, the turn/loop/detection duration sites, per-call tool durations on both dispatch paths (the middleware-pipeline result is re-stamped through the clock in `dispatch_via_pipeline`, so the default-installed pipeline's `on_tool_post`, post-tool-hook, and health-registry durations are seam-sourced too, spanning the whole dispatch exactly as the direct-registry path reports), and the compaction duration — the last through the new additive `ContextManager::build_telemetry_with_duration`, the pinned twin of `build_telemetry` taking a caller-measured duration so the engine reports one measured through its clock. Retry jitter gains `StreamRetryConfig::jittered_base_delay_with(attempt, &dyn Rng)`; `jittered_base_delay` delegates through `ThreadLocalRng` with its exact prior behavior, and the zero-jitter short-circuit stays ahead of any rng consultation (no randomness, no allocation). `SeededIdGen` emits v4-shaped ids (version/variant bits set), so pinned sequences stay compatible with everything that handles today's ids; `FixedClock` captures a real `Instant` for its monotonic half (the type is opaque — no synthetic instants exist) and elapses zero; `elapsed_since` is saturating, so a pinned clock reports zero durations and a stepping clock exact multiples. Non-seam reads remain, deliberately unrouted (memory stores' `SystemTime` scoring, the trajectory observer's run-id mint and `started_at`, provider rate-limiter instants, `Retry-After` compares, the fallback breaker's recovery-timeout instant `fallback_switched_at` — which gates when a tripped breaker retries the primary, control-flow timing rather than a reported artifact — and in-flight `Run::duration()` polling) — the host-built components among them are what the public module is the adoption surface for; the breaker's read steers control flow (which model serves a turn) rather than any reported artifact. Pinned by `seeded_id_gen_replays_the_same_sequence_from_a_seed`, `seeded_rng_replays_the_same_draws_from_a_seed`, `fixed_clock_freezes_both_halves_and_elapses_zero`, and `system_clock_advances_and_elapses_forward` (ordering-only assertions — no timing margins); `pinned_rng_makes_jitter_deterministic`, `zero_jitter_short_circuits_before_consulting_the_rng` (a counting rng records zero consultations and the delay equals `base_delay` exactly), and `with_rng_replaces_the_handler_s_rng` (`Arc::ptr_eq`); `seams_install_via_builders_and_survive_reset_all`; and at the engine level `default_seams_match_current_behavior` (the equivalence pin — plain managers vs explicit default seams produce identical request bytes, both sessions' ids v4-shaped and distinct, proving the defaults are the live reads, not stubs), `pinned_seams_produce_identical_request_bytes_across_runs` (the replay pin — same-seed engines byte-identical, ids equal across engines and advancing within one), `engine_session_and_run_ids_come_from_the_id_gen_seam`, `engine_durations_come_from_the_clock_seam` (a stepping clock advancing exactly `7 ms` per read makes the run's duration a positive multiple of `7 ms`), `pipeline_tool_durations_come_from_the_clock_seam` (fail-first red under the unfixed default construction — a tool turn through the default-installed pipeline under a `FixedClock` reported real wall-clock microseconds on `on_tool_post` — green once the pipeline result is re-stamped through the clock), and `compaction_telemetry_durations_come_from_the_clock_seam` (neutralization-proven — reverting the `run_compaction` wiring to the raw `build_telemetry` turns exactly this pin red, a real sub-millisecond span against the `7 ms` step) — the other wiring pins neutralization-proven: reverting the session-id and run-start sites to the raw `Uuid::new_v4()`/`Instant::now()` reads turns exactly the id-seam, duration, and replay pins red (real `20 ms` elapsed against the `7 ms` step), restored green.

- **`VectorMemoryStore` — the semantic `LoopMemory` backend (`memory::vector_memory`'s `VectorMemoryStore` and `VectorMemoryConfig`, behind the new `vector_memory` feature = `vector_index`)** — the first semantic memory backend, and a drop-in `impl LoopMemory`: no trait change, no new required method — a v0.1.0 consumer's `InMemoryStore` and a new consumer's `VectorMemoryStore` are interchangeable behind `Arc<dyn LoopMemory>` (pinned side by side in `vector_store_satisfies_the_loop_memory_contract_like_in_memory_store`). The store embeds every entry through an `EmbeddingProvider` into a `VectorIndex` and retrieves by blending three channels — semantic cosine (clamped to `0.0..=1.0`), lexical word overlap (the flat stores' lowercase-substring semantics, so match parity holds across backends), and a token-level tag bonus — with `VectorMemoryConfig` weights clamped and normalized to sum 1 at construction (non-finite weights become zero — a poisoned channel goes silent, and an all-poisoned config restores the defaults), `overfetch` widening the semantic pre-filter so the re-rank matters, `prune_threshold` pruning below the floor with `validated` entries exempt, and a strict `dedup_threshold` cosine above which near-duplicates merge (`1.0` disables). `store` awaits its embedding before taking any lock at all (a store↔consolidation pass lock then serializes just the index-and-map writes against consolidation passes so the snapshot-bound removals are sound, overlapping stores interleave their embeds instead of queueing behind each other's embedder latency; `retrieve` never takes it, so a slow or remote embedder still never blocks readers); `retrieve` over-fetches, re-ranks, sorts descending with an id tiebreak, and stamps `access_count`/`last_accessed` on returned entries only — including the `limit` nearest entries an empty or wholly-unrelated query still returns, the module-documented divergence from `InMemoryStore`'s never-stamp baseline; `consolidate` snapshots under a short read lock, decides prunes and merges over the store's own copy of each embedding (each survivor's near-duplicate candidates come from one bounded index search — `dedup_candidates` wide, default 16, strictly above `dedup_threshold`, so planning costs one search per survivor instead of an all-pairs scan and never re-embeds through a possibly-remote provider; the merge set is therefore top-k-bounded, wider duplicate clusters and transitive chains merging across repeated passes), awaits the index removals before taking the write locks, folds victims' topic tags into the deterministic survivor (higher sanitized relevance, then access count, then recency, then id) — never the `provider-derived` provenance tag, which belongs to the entry that earned it — and an absorber is never itself a victim within a pass, so every fold's survivor is live at apply time and transitive chains complete across passes, each folded tag reaching the chain's final survivor — and reports accurate `ConsolidationStats` including `bytes_saved`. The ≥ 80% semantic-precision acceptance gate runs over `golden_set()` — the shared fifty-entry/twenty-query labelled fixture (gated `testing`), published on the store module so the in-crate precision gate and the `loopctl-hnsw` companion's drop-in gate measure the same set. Pinned by `golden_set_queries_hit_a_relevant_memory_eighty_percent_of_the_time`, `lexical_exactness_promotes_an_entry_and_the_weight_knob_flips_it`, `a_tag_hit_promotes_an_entry_until_the_tag_weight_is_zeroed`, `retrieval_increments_access_count_on_returned_entries_only`, `consolidate_prunes_low_relevance_and_evicts_its_vector` (through a `SharedIndex` test handle, because `retrieve` deliberately filters candidates missing from the payload map and would otherwise mask a stale index vector), `consolidate_never_prunes_a_validated_entry`, `consolidate_merges_near_duplicates_and_folds_their_tags`, `an_absorbed_victims_tags_survive_a_transitive_merge`, `a_dedup_window_too_narrow_for_the_cluster_leaves_a_duplicate_behind`, `the_default_dedup_window_merges_a_ten_entry_cluster_completely`, `merge_planning_is_deterministic_across_identically_built_stores`, `a_non_finite_weight_zeroes_its_channel_and_all_non_finite_restores_the_defaults`, `a_second_store_embeds_while_the_first_is_still_parked_in_its_embed`, `concurrent_store_and_retrieve_complete_without_deadlock` (no data lock is held across the embed await; the store↔consolidation pass lock serializes inserts against passes, and `retrieve` never takes it), `an_embedder_failure_propagates_as_a_loop_error`, `a_store_cannot_complete_while_a_consolidation_pass_is_mid_sweep` (a `GatedIndex` parks the pass mid-sweep and a racing store cannot complete until it drains — the replacement survives retrieved and counted), `a_retrieve_stamp_between_snapshot_and_apply_races_the_victim_into_survival` (the raced branch's pin: a `GatedIndex` parks the pass mid-sweep, a mid-pass `retrieve` stamps the merge victim, and the pass keeps it, re-links its vector, and suppresses its fold), `the_golden_fixture_spans_four_of_the_six_categories` (the fixture's documented four-category composition, characterization-pinned), and the engine-level pin `a_large_memory_injection_stays_a_transient_turn_message` (a large injection reaches the outbound request and never persists into machine history — the v0.3.0-anchors transient-accounting pin).

- **`loopctl-hnsw` — the HNSW `VectorIndex` companion (new companion crate, `crates/hnsw`)** — approximate-nearest-neighbour search for `VectorMemoryStore` at scale: a pure-Rust, incremental HNSW (Malkov & Yashunin) over `u32` slots — true add-per-call linking (the batch-only shape of the originally recommended `instant-distance` cannot satisfy the trait's upsert contract), tombstoned `remove` (dead slots stay traversable so the graph never loses connectivity, never come back in results, and are over-fetched past via `k + tombstones`, with the effective `ef` capped at the total slot count), compaction that clears tombstones into a fresh graph replayed in slot order with the seed reset — automatic past the new `HnswParams::max_tombstone_ratio` (default 1.0, sanitized to finite > 0; `add` and `remove` run the replay under their own write lock once tombstones exceed live × ratio, so debt never grows unbounded behind a boxed index) or on demand through the inherent `rebuild()`, with a public `tombstone_count()` accessor for observing the debt, and fixed-seed determinism (`fastrand` level draws, id tie-breaks): same insert sequence, identical search results at build time and across rebuilds (the pinned, result-level contract). Distances are loopctl's own `cosine_similarity`, so scores and orderings are directly comparable with `LinearVectorIndex`; dimension mismatches reject with `LoopError::Memory` exactly like the reference index; one `loopctl.vector.index.search` metric event per search. The crate ships in the `loopctl-sqlite` shape — a standalone companion depending on `loopctl` one-way (`default-features = false`, `vector_index`): add it as a direct dependency and wire `HnswIndex` into `VectorMemoryStore::new` with one `Box::new`; no feature on `loopctl` itself is required (an optional `dep:loopctl-hnsw` edge would form a package cycle, which cargo rejects). Pinned by `hnsw_recall_at_ten_tracks_brute_force_on_random_unit_vectors` (recall@10 ≥ 90% over 1 000 seeded random unit vectors, 100 queries — the quality gate), `same_seed_and_insertion_sequence_build_identical_indexes`, `removed_ids_are_tombstoned_until_rebuild_compacts`, `an_upsert_replaces_the_old_row_without_duplicating`, `added_ids_round_trip_through_search`, `dimension_mismatch_is_rejected_like_the_linear_index`, `search_on_an_empty_index_returns_no_matches`, and `golden_set_precision_survives_the_hnsw_backend` (the shared golden set over the HNSW backend), plus `tombstones_cannot_crowd_live_results_out_of_the_top_k`, `equal_scores_tiebreak_by_id_like_the_linear_index`, and `every_successful_search_emits_one_metric_event` (its own capture binary), with the `slot_key` seam pair covering `add`'s capacity pre-check, and the auto-compaction pins `removals_past_the_ratio_compact_tombstones_without_a_rebuild`, `upserts_past_the_ratio_compact_tombstones_without_a_rebuild`, `sub_ratio_tombstones_stay_until_an_explicit_rebuild`, `a_configured_ratio_below_one_compacts_sooner`, plus the in-crate `a_degenerate_tombstone_ratio_sanitizes_to_the_default`.

- **Compaction-pass failure surfacing (`LoopObserver::on_compaction_failed`, `CompactionFailedContext`, `LoopEvent::CompactionFailed`, `LoopError::CompactionFailed`/`CompactionStalled`)** — a compaction pass that was attempted and died is now caller-visible on both channels, where the real cause was previously a `tracing::warn` hosts without a subscriber never saw and a misdescription ("Context window exceeded") for what was actually a summarizer transport failure. The engine's compaction error arm fires the new defaulted observer callback (reason, turn, pre-pass size, window, and the compactor's own error text — `None` when the pass *succeeded* but its result still did not fit, the two sub-cases a display layer must distinguish) before returning, the event hub forwards it as a new `LoopEvent` variant beside the success `Compaction` event, and the run failure itself is the new `LoopError::CompactionFailed { used, limit, cause }` carrying the compactor's verbatim error (recoverable — a transient compactor error salvages the failed run's coherent prefix, so the retry the variant invites continues the conversation; the measured cannot-fit outcomes `ContextExceeded` and `CompactionStalled` keep the full-discard arm). The machine's no-progress guard gets its own `LoopError::CompactionStalled { used }` — "compaction made no progress at N tokens", claiming no token limit at all — killing the nonsense "used N of N tokens" wording that misread a stall as a budget; not recoverable, on the measured evidence that retrying the same compactor changes nothing. The hooks run-end reason classifies the whole family as `ContextOverflow` — a died pass, an unfit pass, and a stall are context conditions to a host reading `on_run_end`, not generic errors. Pinned by `a_failed_compaction_pass_fires_on_compaction_failed` (cause-carrying variant, one event with the compactor's text, no success `on_compaction`), `a_pass_that_succeeds_but_does_not_fit_keeps_the_window_error` (the unfit pass keeps `ContextExceeded` with the window as the limit and reports its event with `error: None`), `the_no_progress_guard_names_the_stall_not_a_bogus_limit` (variant, size, and wording free of the "of N tokens" self-limit), and `run_end_reason_compaction_failures_are_context_overflow` (fail-first: both variants classified as plain `Error` before the fix), and the salvage routing by `a_transient_compactor_failure_salvages_the_prompt_for_the_retry` (fail-first on the pre-fix discard arm) — the first three verified red under the pre-fix mapping and dispatch.

- **Compaction amnesia: retention classes, the pin seam, and the rolling compactor (`tool::Retention` on `ToolOutput` → `ToolDispatchResult` → `MessagePart::ToolResult`; `ContextManager::with_pin`/`set_pin`/`remove_pin`/`pins` + `CompactionContext.pinned`; 🧩 `compact::rolling`'s `RollingCompactor` + `RollingConfig`)** — three additive workstreams answering the live-session finding that passes complete mechanically and still cost the run its working set. **Retention classes** are tool-authored advisory metadata: `Requery` (file reads, search hits — the shipped read/search builtins stamp it) withholds the content from every compaction transcript render (`result[name, ok]: [re-derivable — content omitted]`) so the summarizer physically cannot restate what it never saw — the paired `calls:` line is the pointer and both LLM compactors' prompts state the convention — while `Durable` (write-family receipts — the shipped side-effect tools stamp it; the `Shell` tool classifies by command shape, not exit status: read-only-shaped commands (the concurrent-dispatch allowlist with its operator and mutating-flag guards) stamp `Requery` on success so a large query's stdout stays evictable and stay unclassed on failure, every other command's receipt stamps `Durable` whatever the exit code, and `MultiEdit`'s partial-apply stops stamp `Durable` because earlier files already landed) is exempt from eviction: a shared helper pulls the durable result *and its paired call* verbatim into the kept side of the truncating terminal and both LLM compactors, excluded from `evicted`, never summarized. The demotion sink's render keeps full content (memory entries stay recallable); the class never reaches any wire serializer and changes no token counting. **The pin seam** lets a host exempt content from eviction entirely: a registry on the manager (`with_pin` at build, `set_pin` at runtime over the shared `Arc` — re-statement replaces in place, ids order the set) whose entries ride `CompactionContext.pinned` into every pass, are carried verbatim at the output head by all shipped compactors (counted in their landing, so the chain's target arithmetic includes them), and are re-checked by a manager backstop that prepends anything a custom compactor dropped — the contract holds for every compactor, not just the cooperative ones. The install guard rejects a pin set counting above the compaction target with `LoopError::InvalidInput` (the budget-gate validating-builder precedent): a host pinning everything gets the error at install, never a silent no-op pass — and a target that computes to zero disables the guard, the same no-target meaning the truncating terminal gives a zero target. **`RollingCompactor`** ages turn groups (pair-safe role-transition boundaries) instead of big-bang passes: each aged group gets its own bounded `create_message` — input one turn under `per_call_max_chars` (48 000 default), never the whole history — producing micro-summaries that accumulate across passes and coalesce past a count cap (12 default) via one bounded merge of the oldest fitting run; the big-bang ledger message rides verbatim — adopted by exact text when the compactor carries one (`restore_ledger` is the host's persistence seam, `ledger_summary` the read half) or by the shared summary-header prefix when a fresh pass finds a big-bang summary at the head (the head scan is pin-aware — it skips the leading system message and the contiguous pinned run before inspecting the next message, so a pinned session's own output shape `[system, pin, ledger, …]` keeps the ledger in view); carried micro-summaries are recognized from the history (a declined pass's state never re-injects summaries the winning stage removed — the history decides what is carried forward); a summarizer error fails the pass so the chain degrades honestly; composed as a chain-leading stage (core, un-gated, out of `default_chain`), burst growth lands over the target and declines to the big-bang tiers under this release's landing verification. Pinned by `a_requery_result_renders_as_the_omission_marker_in_the_transcript_only` (+ the demotion-side preservation assert), `a_durable_result_renders_in_full`, `a_durable_result_and_its_call_survive_the_truncating_terminal_verbatim`, `a_durable_result_and_its_call_survive_a_qa_pass_verbatim`, `retention_rides_the_dispatch_path_into_the_history` and `an_engine_run_keeps_durable_content_across_the_boundary` (the dispatch path and a full engine run), the per-builtin `stamped` family (`a_read_output_is_stamped_requery`, `a_glob/grep/code_search/tree_output_is_stamped_requery`, `a_write/an_edit/a_multi_edit_receipt_is_stamped_durable`, `a_shell_receipt_is_stamped_durable`), `pinned_content_survives_a_pass_byte_for_byte` and its qa/structured twins, `a_pin_set_larger_than_the_target_is_refused_at_install`, `the_manager_backstops_a_compactor_that_drops_the_pins`, and the rolling suite (`every_rolling_call_sees_one_turn_of_input`, `aged_turns_roll_into_micro_summaries_while_young_groups_ride_verbatim`, `micro_summaries_coalesce_when_the_count_cap_is_exceeded`, `the_ledger_rides_a_rolling_pass_and_a_big_bang_pass_identically`, `a_burst_the_aging_cannot_absorb_declines_to_the_chain_terminal`, `rolling_declines_cleanly_into_the_chain`, `growth_faster_than_aging_still_lands_under_the_window`) , `retention_rides_the_dispatch_path_without_a_pipeline` (the direct registry arm — reached through a machine past `Start`, which skips the default wiring — forwards the class like the pipeline arm), `a_rolling_pass_adopts_a_big_bang_ledger_at_the_head`, `a_declined_pass_does_not_resurrect_vanished_micro_summaries`, `a_read_only_command_s_output_is_stamped_requery` / `a_mutating_command_s_failed_receipt_is_stamped_durable` / `a_read_only_command_s_failed_output_is_not_classified` / `retention_for_classifies_by_command_shape_not_exit_status`, `an_external_change_mid_batch_reports_the_applied_files` (extended with the partial-apply `Durable` assert) and `a_conflict_before_any_write_stays_evictable`, `the_durable_pull_cost_is_folded_into_the_tail_budget`, and `the_terminal_holds_the_landing_to_one_exact_count`, plus `the_stage_target_is_the_full_target_while_the_fit_check_keeps_the_reserve` (the reserve side-switch, neutralization-red on the re-netted stage target) — all proven red by neutralization or fail-first; the same families also pin: `a_pinned_session_carries_an_adopted_ledger_across_a_pass` (pin-aware head scan, neutralization-red), the zero-target arm inside `a_pin_set_larger_than_the_target_is_refused_at_install` (neutralization-red), and the portable temp-dir `cat` fixture behind `a_read_only_command_s_output_is_stamped_requery` (withholding, durable pull, pin carriage, backstop, size guard, the turn-group boundary, the shell exit-status classification, the budget iteration, the exact re-measure, the rolling adoption and stale-state paths, and the direct-dispatch arm each disabled or absent for one run).

### Changed

- **Breaking:** **`DeltaPart::Thinking` gains `signature` and `redacted` fields** — exhaustive construction and destructuring of the variant add the two fields (`..` patterns compile unchanged); `MessagePart::Thinking` grows the same pair, but that variant is new in this release. Serde is compatible in both directions: the fields default when absent and serialize only when carried, so recorded streams and trajectories keep parsing and unchanged events keep their exact bytes.

- **Breaking:** **`RequestOptions` is `#[non_exhaustive]`** — the marker rides the new `effort` field: a later option lands additive instead of forcing another breaking release. In-crate literals compile unchanged; external struct literals move to `RequestOptions::new()` / `default()`, already the documented construction path, and pattern-matching needs a `..` wildcard in out-of-crate destructuring patterns (an exhaustive `let` binding fails with E0638 without one), while construction and field access are unrestricted. No behavior change — the struct is a plain data carrier whose fields all default to prior behavior.

- **The companion crates moved under `crates/`** — a repo-layout change only, no code or API movement: `derive`, `sqlite`, `hnsw`, `client`, and `vector` now live at `crates/*`. The first four remain workspace members; `vector` — as before — stays workspace-excluded, built by manifest path. Path dependencies deepened with the move: `..` → `../..` inside each crate, the root manifest's `loopctl-derive` path re-pointed, and the derive hygiene fixture's `../../..` → `../../../..`; the workspace member list and the Makefile's manifest-path targets (`derive-consumer`, `vector-check`, `vector-e2e`) followed. No public surface, feature, or version changed.

- **Window decisions move to real numbers, and a refused request gets a retry instead of a dead run** (see the `Added` entry for the full mechanism) — two visible changes to existing behavior. On backends that report usable `input_tokens`, the figure the compaction trigger evaluates is now anchored to the provider's own number (`reported_input + calibrated tail`) instead of the raw heuristic — triggers move toward the true payload, so a run whose heuristic under-counted compacts before the provider refuses rather than after; usage-less backends are byte-identical. And a provider rejection whose text reports an oversized prompt (`maximum context length`, `prompt is too long`, …) no longer ends the run discard-first: the turn gets one emergency-compaction-and-retry per run — the forced pass lands strictly under the refused payload — and only a repeat rejection on the same turn ends the run, as `ContextExceeded` with the reported usage floored just above the window so the pair never reads as fitting.

- **A tool turn's turn-end duration now covers the whole serving attempt** — the tool-phase `TurnEndContext.duration_ms` (and the trajectory recorder's `duration_ms` behind it) is measured from the turn's model-call entry through dispatch completion, where it previously covered the dispatch phase alone; a machine resumed mid-phase falls back to the dispatch-entry instant. The post-dispatch context recount stays outside the measured span (pinned by `the_success_turn_end_duration_excludes_the_context_recount` with a recount-shaped slow counter). Pinned fail-first by `a_tool_turn_s_duration_covers_the_model_phase`.

- **Breaking:** **`ContextManager::compact_with_reason` takes a `CompactSizing` parameter** — the trailing `reserved_tokens: u64` argument became `sizing: CompactSizing { reserved_tokens, target_override }`, the parameter-struct shape that keeps the signature under the crate's argument ceiling while adding the landing-target override the overflow-salvage arm needs (a target strictly under the payload a provider refused). Migration: pass `CompactSizing::reserving(n)` where you passed `n`, or a struct literal with `target_override` to force one pass's landing target. Pinned by `the_salvage_pass_lands_under_the_refused_payload_with_the_default_compactor` (the default truncating compactor over a provider whose real limit sits below the declared window — red pre-fix at the no-progress stall).

- **The default post-compaction target tightens from 30% to 15% of the trigger threshold** (`ContextManager`'s `compact_target_pct` default) — a pass now aims to land at half the headroom it used to, doubling the runway between a landing and the next trigger crossing on the default configuration: compaction pays off half as often on long-running sessions while each pass still clears the same threshold line. Migration: a host that wants the old cadency back sets `.with_compact_target_pct(30)` on its `ContextManager`. The default's own pins moved with it (`test_compact_target_tokens_default` now expects 24 000 of a 200 000-token window, `test_compact_target_default_is_threshold` expects 15); the mechanics pins that happened to ride the default were pinned to explicit fractions (`test_ensure_compacts_when_over_threshold`, `the_chain_compacts_through_the_manager_without_overflow`, `compaction_telemetry_durations_come_from_the_clock_seam`) and the policy fixtures whose toy windows could not satisfy the tighter target at their fill sizes were re-sized, not re-configured — `run_start_compacts_at_the_threshold` (window 1 400→2 400, fills 1 500→3 300, second-run prompt 600→300 chars; geometry probe-verified: run 1 ends at 1 880 under the 1 920 line, run 2 crosses at 1 960 and lands at 239), `default_loop_compacts_at_the_threshold` and `small_model_profile_compacts_at_the_threshold` (window 1 200→3 200, script 12→32 turns), the two demotion-recall fixtures (window 8 000→16 000, script 4→9 big turns), and the probe pin (disclosed window 4 096→8 192 with an 80-message bulky history) — each preserving the pin's subject with the tighter default in force.
- **Breaking (behavior, not API): over-target compaction landings decline instead of passing silently** — the `ContextCompactor` contract's "the target is a hint" sentence is retired: the shipped compactors hold their landings to `target_tokens`, and a chain stage that lands above it falls through to the next tier. The visible changes: a `TruncatingCompactor` install no longer keeps exactly `preserve_recent` messages regardless of size (the count is a floor and the target sizes the tail above it), and a floor that cannot fit the target no-ops (reaching the caller as `CompactionStalled` through the machine's no-progress guard) instead of shipping an over-target landing the next pass immediately re-triggers on. Migration: read exact post-truncation message counts as floors; tests and hosts that passed generously sized targets to the truncating terminal should pass targets that demand shedding, and a host that genuinely wants the count-pinned shed can pass a target of zero — the documented degenerate path where the count floor governs.
