- **Breaking (behavior, not API): default construction wires the small-model profile — `FrontierProfile` is the opt-out** — `BareLoop::new` (and every public constructor over a fresh machine) now installs the builtin-verified pipeline (output cap → `CommandVerifier` verify-on-write → `WritePathExtractor`-invalidated memoization), the goal reminder re-injecting the original request past turn five, and — only when the client's new defaulted `ApiClient::supports_tool_constraints()` probe declares support (`true` for the shipped clients that forward constraints, `openai`/`anthropic`/`gemini`; `false` for Bedrock, which does not encode the constraint on the wire; the testing mock ties it to `with_tool_constraint_support`) — the strict tool-call constraint. A pipeline the managers bundle already carries survives default construction — the default fills only the vacant seam, so `LoopManagers::new().with_pipeline(..)` composes with the flip instead of being clobbered by it. `SessionConfig`/`RunConfig` defaults are untouched (no 32k window, no 100-turn cap); the flip is wiring. Migration: `BareLoop::new(..).with_profile(&FrontierProfile)?` (or `FrontierProfile::apply(&mut loop_)`) restores the bare loop's dispatch path and request shape — both spellings delegate to the new `Profile` trait and are pinned to produce identical request streams with no verify, cache, or reminder artifacts; the opt-out also gains `BareLoop::clear_contributors()` as its contributor seam. Explicit `ConstrainedProfile::apply` owns the same seam — contributors registered before the apply are cleared, so exactly one reminder rides each reminder turn; hosts that keep their own contributors register them after the apply — and the apply leaves an existing middleware pipeline untouched (installing its builtin-verified stack only on a loop with none), so verification and memoization survive it. A resumed machine past `Start` is never re-wired; the loop's wiring comes from the host, exactly as before the change — while a machine **at** `Start`, fresh or a checkpoint returned to it, is wired like a fresh loop. The frontier opt-out followed by a constrained apply installs the verified stack: the constrained apply treats a middleware-free pipeline as vacant, so the last-stated profile wins. Pinned fail-first by `default_construction_wires_the_small_model_profile` (deny-class write fails softly, repeat grep serves from cache, oversized output caps — all absent pre-flip), `default_construction_reinjects_the_goal_from_turn_five`, `supports_tool_constraints_defaults_false`, `frontier_profile_restores_the_bare_loop` (identical request streams from both spellings), `opt_out_composes_with_explicit_middleware`, `with_profile_chains_and_propagates`, and the deliberately amended `default_construction_wires_the_builtin_verifier` (was `profile_wiring_is_explicit_this_release`, whose own comment promised this seen diff).

- **Breaking:** **`ContextManager::build_telemetry` is an instance method** — it now takes `&self` and reads the manager's own `context_window` (plus a `compactor_name: Option<&str>` parameter) to compute the new `headroom_pct`, so two managers with different windows report different headroom over the same messages. Migration: callers of the former associated function bind a `ContextManager` receiver and add the name argument — pass `None` when the compactor does not self-identify. Pinned by `build_telemetry_reads_the_instance_window`.

- **Breaking:** **The public surface is `#[non_exhaustive]` everywhere a scheduled release widens it** — twenty-two types gain the marker (`Usage`, `ToolSchema`, `Message`, `MessagePart` (and its `ToolResult` variant), `MemoryEntry`, `CompactionOutcome`, `CompactTelemetry`, `CompactionContext`, `CompactReason`, `PreCompactStats`, `PostCompactStats`, `EnsureContextResult`, `RunEndContext`, `TurnEndContext`, `ToolPreContext`, `CompactedContext`, `TransportFallbackContext`, `StructuredError`, `Turn`, `Run`, `Session`, `ToolCall`), so every future field or variant addition these releases need is additive and ships in a patch release instead of forcing another breaking window. The rule, stated for users: public data-carrying types are `#[non_exhaustive]` by default; matching and reading are unrestricted (matches need a `_` wildcard arm); constructing them externally was never a supported pattern — the crate provides constructors. Four constructors join the surface (`ToolSchema::new(tool, description, input_schema)`, `RunEndContext::new(success, error, total_turns, duration_ms)`, `CompactionContext::new(tokens_before, reason, context_window, turn, counter)` for hook-less compactor fixtures, and `CompactionOutcome::failed(messages, tokens_after, error)` completing the outcome trio), and `#[derive(Tool)]` now emits `ToolSchema::new` in its generated `schema()` — **the derive and the crate must upgrade together** (an older derive emits struct literals that no longer compile; a newer derive against an older crate calls a constructor that does not exist yet). Serde is untouched — pre-hardening JSON deserializes identically, pinned (`old_serialized_data_deserializes`); in-repo fallout was 52 compile errors, every one a struct literal or missing wildcard in tests/examples, all migrated. Pinned by `tests/semver_hardening.rs` (`construction_still_possible_via_constructors`, `derive_tool_still_compiles`). Migration, one line per type: matches add a `_` arm; struct literals move to the constructor.

  | Type | Migration |
  |---|---|
  | `Usage` | `Usage::new(input_tokens, output_tokens)` — already existed |
  | `ToolSchema` | `ToolSchema::new(tool, description, input_schema)`; `#[derive(Tool)]` users change nothing — the generated code moved for you |
  | `Message` | `Message::new(role, parts)` / `Message::user(...)` — already existed |
  | `MessagePart` | matches add a `_` arm; construct via `MessagePart::text` / `tool_call` / `tool_result` (already existed); the `ToolResult` variant is itself `#[non_exhaustive]` — patterns keep using `..` |
  | `MemoryEntry` | `MemoryEntry::new(category, text)` for fresh entries (already existed); a store restoring a persisted row uses the new restore builders (`with_id`, `with_tags`, `with_created_at`, `with_relevance`, `with_access_count`, `with_last_accessed`, `with_last_decayed`) alongside the existing `with_tag`/`validated` |
  | `CompactionOutcome` | `CompactionOutcome::compacted(messages, tokens_before, tokens_after)` / `no_change(messages)` / the new `failed(messages, tokens_after, error)` |
  | `RunEndContext` | `RunEndContext::new(success, error, total_turns, duration_ms)` |
  | `TurnEndContext`, `ToolPreContext`, `CompactedContext`, `TransportFallbackContext` | engine-constructed structs, nothing to construct or match — destructuring patterns need `..` |
  | `StructuredError` | matches add a `_` arm; variant construction is unaffected |
  | `Turn`, `ToolCall` | engine-constructed and serde-carried; deserialization unchanged, nothing to migrate |
  | `Run`, `Session` | `Run::new(input, config)` and `Session::new(config)` remain the public constructors — unaffected by the marker |
  | `CompactTelemetry` | engine-constructed, nothing to migrate |
  | `CompactionContext` | `CompactionContext::new(tokens_before, reason, context_window, turn, counter)` — the hook-less fixture shape; the driver fills hook fields internally |
  | `CompactReason`, `EnsureContextResult` | matches add a `_` arm; variant construction unaffected |
  | `PreCompactStats`, `PostCompactStats` | engine-constructed, nothing to migrate |

- **`FallbackManager::set_original_model` accepts `impl Into<String>`** — widened from `String` so `&str` callers compile without `.to_string()` (every existing call site is unaffected; the name is trimmed and empties rejected either way, per the setter-validation fix below).

- **`ToolRegistry::all_schemas` yields schemas in registration order** (see the golden harness above) — the advertised tool order was map iteration: unspecified and different on every process, so identical registries built byte-different requests. Schemas now follow the registration sequence, and a re-registration keeps the first position (matching `all_tools`), making outbound requests reproducible byte-for-byte across processes. The emitted tool definitions are unchanged, but their array order now follows registration order — wire-observable in provider request bodies and MCP tool listings, and non-breaking. Pinned by `all_schemas_preserve_registration_order` and by the golden suite itself, which stays green across fresh processes.
- **Breaking:** **`JsonSchemaGrammar::from_schemas` returns `Result<Self, ApiError>` and fails loud at construction** (feature `grammar`) — the old signature serialized the compiled envelope with `unwrap_or_else(|_| "{}")`, so any serialization failure silently downgraded the grammar constraint to "match anything": constrained decoding that quietly becomes unconstrained decoding. The escape is gone. Two failure modes now return a config-validation error before any request can leave: a tool whose `input_schema` does not explicitly declare the string `type: "object"` — a declared non-object `type` in either the string form or JSON Schema's array form (the array form rejects even when it names only `"object"`: the tightening pass reads the string form, and an accepted array form would ride untightened), a schema with no `type` at all (fail-closed: an `enum`/`const`-only schema can emit non-object values, and tightening never injects the missing type; degenerate empty or non-string type arrays reject too), or a non-object schema document such as a boolean schema — and, defensively, an envelope serialization failure (near-unreachable today; the typed path exists so refactors can never reintroduce reachability silently). The reachable rejection is genuinely reachable, not theoretical: before the validation, a `type: "array"` schema rode the grammar as a property (captured as the pin's red). The module docs now draw the two-envelope seam honestly — whole-output guided decoding (this grammar; a server that honors it returns the entire completion as one JSON object keyed by tool name, which the **consumer** parses back into a tool call — loopctl does not do that parsing, and the OpenAI-compatible request still advertises tools natively alongside the grammar, so a server that ignores the guided field answers with an ordinary native tool call) vs API-native tool constraining (`tool_constraint: Strict`, the provider enforces the format) — with the provider support matrix (OpenAI-compatible servers with a guided-decoding field accept the grammar, and the field rides only when tools are registered — an empty tool list goes out unconstrained rather than forcing the completion into an empty tool set; Anthropic, Gemini, and Bedrock Converse fail fast with a typed error instead of degrading). One `debug!` per request that carries the grammar names the envelope and grammar size at the serving site, and the live smoke's "≥99% valid" prose is reworded to what runs — and its validity oracle strengthened and extracted into a unit-pinned helper (`the_smoke_oracle_accepts_one_valid_call_and_rejects_the_rest`): every one of the 4 prompts must yield exactly **one** valid tool call, either exactly-one native registered tool call with object arguments or a whole-output envelope keyed by a registered tool **with an object value** — any parseable JSON, two interleaved tool starts, and `{"tool": 42}` all fail — with any statistical claim deferred to a larger benchmark corpus. Migration: `JsonSchemaGrammar::from_schemas(&schemas)` becomes fallible — add `.expect(...)` in tests or propagate the config-validation error; well-formed object schemas behave identically. Pinned by `grammar_rejects_non_object_schemas_loudly` (string, boolean-schema, and all three array-form directions; proven red by neutralization) and `envelope_docs_match_the_wire` (the emitted grammar equals the documented whole-output envelope; proven red by dropping `additionalProperties`).

- **Breaking:** **`Turn` and `TurnEndContext` gain a public `stop_reason` field** (`StreamStopReason`, `#[serde(default)]` on `Turn`). Serialization is backwards-compatible. Migration: exhaustive struct literals add `stop_reason: StreamStopReason::EndTurn` (or the turn's real reason); struct-update (`..base`) and serde consumers are unaffected.
- **Breaking:** **`MockApiClient::with_options_calls` now also counts `stream_messages_with_options`** (feature `testing`) — the counter tallies options-carrying calls on both transports, mirroring the rejected-before-send rule; tests asserting the counter after streaming options calls read one higher. Migration: subtract the streaming calls or assert `captured_stream_requests().len()` instead.
- **Breaking:** **`Turn` gains a public `transport_fallback` field** (see Added). Serialization is backwards-compatible. Migration: exhaustive struct literals add `transport_fallback: false`; struct-update and serde consumers are unaffected. Related: the `struct-field-name-threshold` in `.clippy.toml` rises 8 → 9 — the flag pushed `Turn` to eight fields, activating the stutter check on the pre-existing `Turn::turn` field; the threshold's comment described a builder-suggestion gate the key does not implement.
- **Breaking:** **the builtin tool wire names are CamelCase — `ReadTool` registers as `Read`, `ThinkTool` as `Think`** — every builtin now spells its name the way the fs family always has (`Write`, `Edit`, `MultiEdit`, `FileViewer`), so one convention covers the whole `builtin_tools` surface: the name a schema advertises, a model calls, and a host matches by string. The rename also repairs a silent mismatch: the small-model preset's default `MEMOIZED_TOOLS` list already expected `"Read"`, so the lowercase builtin had been missing the profile's memoize set since it shipped — after the rename the default list and the builtin agree with no presets change. The model-facing error strings that name the tools follow the spelling (`Read input must match the schema: …`, `Think requires a string `thought` field`), as do the module docs and doctest examples. No alias or redirect ships: an old-name call now surfaces through the ordinary unknown-tool path. Migration: hosts, prompts, and name lists referencing `"read"`/`"think"` switch to `"Read"`/`"Think"`; nothing else moves — signatures, schemas, and feature gates are untouched. Pinned fail-first (assertion-red on the unfixed tree — `read ≠ Read`, `think ≠ Think`, and the name-convention predicate false on both) by the deliberately amended `schema_advertises_the_documented_fields` (its prior text pinned the lowercase spelling) and `schema_advertises_only_the_thought_field`, and the convention is guarded by the new `builtin_tool_names_are_camel_case`, which constructs every builtin — `ThinkTool`, `ReadTool`, and the four fs tools under `fs_tools` — and asserts the CamelCase predicate, so a future lowercase or kebab-case builtin cannot land green. The doc sweep the first pass scoped too narrowly is extended to the preset recipe and the fs submodule docs — `ConstrainedProfile`'s small-model recipe now says `Think`, and the `file_viewer`/`file_source` module and type docs say `Read` (the FileViewer contrast restated against `Read`'s real whole-file line ceiling, replacing a phantom `~200`-line quick-lookup cap) — pinned by `no_builtin_doc_names_a_tool_by_the_old_spelling`, a fail-closed sweep over every doc line of the builtin tree and `presets.rs` that allowlists exactly three `ContentSource` read-method references (the read-counter metric doc fragments) and nothing else (fail-first red naming all four stale sites); and the name-convention predicate is narrowed to the ASCII CamelCase the convention requires, so Unicode lookalikes (`Ｒead`, `Édit`) cannot pass it — pinned fail-first (red under the Unicode predicate it replaced) by `the_name_convention_predicate_rejects_non_ascii_lookalikes`.
- **The `Read` tool's default window is right-sized for small context slots — 400 lines (was 2 000), 32 768 bytes of joined output (was 400 000), and a 400-line default for offset-only reads (was 2 000)** — a default read is the unit of context spending, and the old ceilings let a single casual read carry on the order of a hundred thousand tokens — enough to overfill the crate's own pinned 32 768-token small-model window three times over — with nothing marking the cost until the provider rejected the request. The resized default keeps a typical read to a few thousand tokens and moves whole-document consumption onto explicit `offset`/`limit` paging, where every page's cost is visible and the cut marker names the returned range and the next offset. Nothing else in the windowing contract moves: a cut view still carries the same truncation markers, explicit `offset`/`limit` still win over the defaults, the byte ceiling remains the real bound, and the `10 MiB` outright-refusal threshold and `5 MiB` image ceiling are untouched. The `FileViewer` module doc's `Read` contrast is reworded number-agnostic and scoped to the default window — a hard digit there would re-stale on every future resize, and `Read` itself seeks arbitrary windows via `offset`/`limit` and `line_range`. One marker wording changed with it: the byte-capped footer no longer appends `the content has very long lines` — a cause the tool cannot verify and, at the new budget ratio (the byte budget shrank 12.2× against the line budget's 5×), a false one for an ordinary ~90-byte-per-line window whose 400 lines sum past the ceiling — it states the byte bound, the returned range, and the remedy, and the `DEFAULT_MAX_BYTES` docs now describe the ceiling as the real bound on the joined output rather than a long-line guard. Pinned fail-first by `an_ordinary_width_default_read_cuts_at_the_byte_ceiling_without_a_cause_claim` (an exact 400-line, 90-byte-per-line fixture cuts at lines 1-344 of 400 naming `offset=345` and the remaining 56 lines, with no cause claim — red against the clause) and `the_file_viewer_contrast_scopes_the_read_claim_to_the_default_window` (red against the unqualified doc), beside the existing long-line pins that hold the other side of the boundary. Pinned fail-first (red on the pre-resize tree — a 401-line file served whole with no marker, a 12 000-line file cut at 2 000, and the ceiling pin asserting 400 against the old default) by `default_window_is_right_sized` (both sides of the line boundary — exactly 400 lines serves whole with no marker, 401 cuts naming the continuation — plus the 400-line offset-only window and the 32 768-byte bound), `a_big_file_default_read_cuts_with_a_marker` (a ~100 KiB default read returns the first window and the marker naming the returned range and the next offset), and `explicit_limits_beat_the_new_defaults` (an explicit `offset`/`limit` window is identical before and after the resize); `the_default_ceilings_are_the_documented_ones` is deliberately amended to the new numbers. Migration: a per-call `limit` is clamped to the line ceiling, so restoring a larger window is a construction-time concern — build the tool with `with_max_lines(2_000)` / `with_max_bytes(400_000)` / `with_default_offset_limit(2_000)` for the previous window; per-call explicit windows at or under 400 lines are unaffected.

- **The LLM compactors demand a progress ledger, take host-configured summary instructions, and the chain's stage trail surfaces on the outcome and the compaction event** — a pass can succeed mechanically and still destroy the run: the summary is content preservation (facts, decisions, identifiers), never *progress* preservation, so the model re-entering on it cannot tell covered work from new work — the observed shape (a session under the qa chain) re-established settled facts and re-read already-reviewed files after every pass, re-inflating the context until the next pass wiped the strictly larger loss, until the run died unconverged. Both compactors now demand a ledger: `QaSummarizer`'s step-1 instruction opens with one — *a progress ledger: one line per goal or work item … done (with its conclusion or finding) or remaining, naming the files or areas already read, reviewed, or verified with what was found* — ahead of the dense running summary (ledger-first, so a budget-squeezed model sheds prose before state, and the "with its conclusion" phrasing pushes checkable, boolean-shaped claims over invented quantities), and the question step probes ledger completeness ("including any goal or work item the summary's ledger omits or leaves with an unclear done-or-remaining status") so a dropped ledger is caught as a gap; `StructuredSummarizer`'s `PendingTasks` purpose — the one source the system prompt and the docs both read — becomes the continuation's task ledger (per-item done/remaining with coverage of already-read/verified work; heading, slug, json key, template order, and the other three sections unchanged). Both configs gain `with_summary_instructions` / `summary_instructions()` — advisory host text riding the summarize prompt inside a delimited `<host-instructions>` block (data separated from directives the way `<conversation>` and `<prior-summary>` separate theirs), ahead of the per-pass hook channel, with empty values warned-and-rejected so a misconfiguration cannot silently render nothing. And the chain's provenance leaves its pull-API black hole: `CompactionOutcome` gains `stage: Option<String>` (+ `with_stage`), `FallbackCompactor` stamps a one-line trail — every declined stage with its reason, the winner as `Name (won)`, a no-reduction ride-out stating so, and a stage-less chain reporting `None` rather than an empty string (the documented "no provenance available" value) — and the engine copies it onto `CompactedContext.stage` for `on_compaction`, so any observer can see which stage carried each pass (and why the others declined) without a tracing subscriber or the `last_report()` pull; `ChainReport` keeps its full-record role. The QA pass also renders its dropped-context transcript once per pass, shared by the summarize and answer steps, so the answer prompt cannot drift from what was summarized and the render cost is paid once. Pinned fail-first by `summarize_prompt_opens_with_the_progress_ledger_requirement`, `question_prompt_probes_ledger_completeness`, `a_ledger_shaped_summary_rides_into_history_verbatim`, `pending_tasks_purpose_demands_done_and_remaining_per_item`, `host_configured_instructions_ride_the_summarize_prompt` / `host_configured_instructions_ride_the_structured_prompt` (both red under a neutralized tag wrap), `empty_summary_instructions_are_rejected_with_a_warning`, `chain_outcome_carries_the_winning_stage_trail`, `a_no_reduction_ride_out_names_every_stage_and_states_none_reduced`, `an_empty_chain_builds_and_fails_honestly` (extended: no stages ⇒ `stage: None`, red on the unconditional stamp), and the engine-level `on_compaction_carries_the_stage_trail` (neutralization-red with the engine copy removed).

### Fixed

- **A threshold-triggered compaction stall no longer kills the run when the payload still fits the window** — a compaction pass that made no progress used to trip the no-progress guard and end the run, even when the conversation was still servable. Now, when the trigger was the threshold (not the 95 % emergency line), the post-pass figure still sits under the window, and nothing was shaved, the pass defers: the threshold trigger stands down for the rest of the turn (a per-turn latch cleared at every turn and run boundary, so a later trigger retries), the turn is served uncompacted, and `on_compaction_failed` fires with a non-fatal notice naming the deferral so the host's status row explains why the context figure did not drop. Emergency-line stalls still end the run with the same typed error as before. Machine snapshots from before this change deserialize unchanged (the new latch field is `#[serde(default)]`, defaulting down — exactly the pre-change semantic). Pinned by `a_threshold_stall_that_fits_the_window_serves_the_turn` (which also asserts the deferral-notice event — one `on_compaction_failed` with the `ThresholdExceeded` reason, zero on the emergency-death sibling `an_emergency_stall_still_ends_the_run`) and the pre-change-snapshot round-trip `a_snapshot_without_the_deferral_latch_round_trips`.

- **The redaction entropy heuristic bounds tokens on all whitespace, not just spaces** — `scrub_high_entropy` split candidate tokens on `' '` only, so space-free multi-line tool output (a piped `ls` listing, CRLF logs) collapsed into one giant candidate whose ordinary mixed-case filenames cleared the 4.5-bit threshold: an approved `ls` came back as a single `[REDACTED:high_entropy]` with only the space-separated tail (` 0, 2ms]`) surviving. The heuristic now splits on `char::is_whitespace` with every separator preserved (`split_inclusive`), because a credential never spans a newline while ordinary listings often carry none; the marker format, the 32-char minimum, the 4.5-bit threshold, the token alphabet, and the echoed-placeholder pass-through are unchanged, and the curated patterns are untouched — a known secret inside a newline-separated listing (or sharing a line with ordinary text) still redacts. Pinned fail-first in `tests/redacting_middleware.rs`: `a_space_free_multiline_listing_passes_through_verbatim` (the exact `ls` repro, byte-identical after scrub), `known_secrets_inside_a_newline_listing_still_redact`, and `tabs_and_carriage_returns_bound_tokens_like_spaces` (CRLF listing verbatim; a tab-bounded high-entropy token still redacts with its tabs preserved) — all three failed against the pre-fix split, and the pre-existing single-line, boundary, and randomized idempotence pins stay green.
- **A mid-session model switch now re-syncs the installed context manager's window** — `switch_model(...).with_context_window(n).apply()` wrote the session config but left the manager on its install-time copy, so the machine triggered on the new window while the manager targeted and fit-checked the old one: switching to a smaller window dead-ended the next pass at the no-progress guard (`ContextExceeded` with identical before/after measurements), switching to a larger one over-evicted toward the stale small target. `apply` now rebuilds the manager (clone + `with_context_window`, the same sync `set_context_manager` performs at install) in the same operation. Pinned both directions in `src/engine/bare/tests.rs`: `a_switch_to_a_smaller_window_keeps_compacting_under_the_new_one` and `a_switch_to_a_larger_window_does_not_fail_history_between_the_windows`.
- **A programmatic model switch no longer double-fires `on_model_switched`** — `ModelSwitch::apply` fired the observer event but left the engine's routed-model baseline stale, so the next routed turn re-fired the same transition and observers double-counted programmatic switches. `apply` now records the switch as the routing baseline. Pinned by `a_programmatic_switch_fires_the_observer_once_across_apply_and_a_turn`.
- **Fallback recovery no longer locks out behind a dying primary** — `Recovering` (the half-open probe state) had exits only for rate-limit failures and successes, so a primary failing with auth errors, 404s, or timeouts kept the manager half-open forever: every run probed the dead primary, failed, and left a healthy configured fallback unserved — indefinitely, since manager state persists across runs by default. Transient failures during recovery now count toward the same `trip_threshold` that trips from `Primary` (mirroring the tool-health breaker): reaching it re-trips to the fallback, and a probe success resets the window. Pinned in unit (`transient_failures_during_recovery_retrip_after_the_threshold`, including the success-resets direction) and through the engine (`transient_probe_failure_retrips_to_the_fallback_model`: trip → fallback → cooldown → auth-failing probe re-trips → fallback serves again).
- **A tripped breaker with an empty fallback chain refuses to serve the known-bad primary** — `active_model()`'s empty-chain arm returned the primary the breaker had just declared bad (with `is_using_fallback() == true` and no `on_fallback` event), contradicting its own doc ("the caller should fail rather than retreat"). The arm now returns `None` and the engine's `FallbackExhausted` guard covers empty chains exactly like exhausted ones — a typed failure, never a request back to the bad primary. The old unit pin asserting the retreat is flipped to the decided fail-closed contract (`tripped_breaker_with_empty_chain_refuses_the_primary`), plus the engine pin `tripped_empty_chain_fails_the_turn_instead_of_serving_the_primary`.
- **Tool-health gate refusals now reach the loop detector** — a breaker-open refusal returned before `post_detection`, so a model hammering an unavailable tool produced N identical soft errors the detector never saw: the run ended at `MaxTurnsExceeded`, never `LoopDetected` — precisely the non-adapting model the detector exists to catch. Gate refusals are recorded as detection operations, matching the shield/permission precedent. Pinned by `gate_refusals_reach_the_loop_detector` (tripped breaker + always-recall model → `LoopDetected`, not max-turns).
- **The command deny set holds for separator-adjacent and flag-split spellings** — `rm -rf /; echo done`, `rm -rf /&&x`, and the split flag forms (`rm -r -f /`, `rm --recursive --force /`) previously passed while trivially-equivalent spaced forms denied: the boundary check accepted only spaces and the deny list carried only compact flags. Shell separators (`;`, `&`, `|`, `(`, `)`, `#`) are now token edges and the split forms ship in the default deny list. Pinned across seven evading spellings in `deny_globs_block_the_destructive_class`.
- **`FallbackManager` model-name setters reject empty and whitespace-only names** — the chain names flowed into per-request `model` overrides past `with_model`'s trim-empty guard, surfacing as wire-level API errors (`model: ""` on the OpenAI body, in the Gemini URL) instead of typed configuration errors. All six name-taking entry points (`for_model`, `set_original_model`, `set_fallback_model`, `add_fallback_model`, `insert_fallback_model`, `set_fallback_models`) trim and reject empties with `LoopError::Config`, matching `ModelSwitch::apply`'s discipline; the bulk setter validates its whole input before touching state, so a bad name leaves the existing chain untouched (all-or-nothing). Pinned by `model_name_setters_reject_empty_and_whitespace_names` and `chain_mutation_apis_validate_names_all_or_nothing` (including the trimmed-name-routes assertion).
- **Checkpoint resume is documented and pinned at the run boundary** — resuming a machine serialized mid-phase (`from_machine_with_managers` on an `AwaitingTools` checkpoint) silently dropped the pending tool calls when `run()` began. That is now the documented contract (the constructor states it; drive mid-phase tool phases in the run that started them) and `resuming_a_mid_tool_phase_checkpoint_drops_the_pending_work` pins the boundary precisely: pending visible pre-run, dropped and never dispatched post-run, committed history surviving.
- **Cancellation is observed while a compaction pass is in flight** — `handle_compact` awaited the compactor with no cancel race, so a cancel fired mid-pass went unobserved until the compactor returned: against a hung compactor the run hung with it, and even a slow one delayed cancellation by the full pass. The pass is now raced against the cancel signal with the same biased select the dispatch path uses — the run ends typed `LoopError::Cancelled`, the in-flight pass is dropped and its result never lands (the machine ends terminal-cancelled, not awaiting a compaction result), and the run's completed exchanges are salvaged into the conversation while the half-compacted history never lands (the run's own `Run` record still carries its turn entries and the `Cancelled` stop reason). Proven fail-first: the pin hung against the unfixed engine (a 10-second outer timeout elapsed with the run still inside the compactor); green after. Pinned by `a_cancel_during_an_in_flight_compact_ends_the_run_typed`.

- **`#[derive(Tool)]` no longer breaks under a renamed `loopctl` dependency** — the expansion hard-coded `loopctl::` paths, so a downstream manifest like `lc = { package = "loopctl" }` failed to compile the generated impl (`E0433`), the hygiene angle in-crate tests cannot see. Generated paths now resolve through the name the consumer's manifest binds (`loopctl-derive` gains the standard `proc-macro-crate` resolver; unrenamed builds emit byte-identical paths). Pinned by the consumer-hygiene fixture — a standalone downstream crate in `crates/derive/tests/consumer/` that consumes the derive under a renamed dependency with bare `use` paths and builds under `RUSTFLAGS="-D warnings"` — wired into `make ci` as the `derive-consumer` gate.

- **Compiling with only the `gemini` feature failed** — the `ProfileSeed` impl block in `src/provider.rs` missed the feature gate its struct carries (`any(openai, anthropic)`), so every provider-graph combination that compiles `provider.rs` without those two features (only `gemini` today) hit `E0425`. No released feature set CI exercises was affected; found while recording the Gemini cassette.

- **Cancel/failure salvages completed turns instead of wiping the run** — every non-success `run()` exit (cancellation at any point, provider failure after retries, max-turns, detection stops) funneled through `discard_pending`, so one cancel cost the entire run's conversation: a long agentic task held in a single `run()` resumed with total amnesia while a display fed by live observer events still showed the full history. `finalize` is now three-way: success commits the whole pending buffer; cancellation and ordinary failure call the new `LoopMachine::salvage_pending()`, which truncates pending at the last coherent boundary — every assistant tool-call message followed by results for all of its calls — commits that prefix, and returns the dropped incomplete tail (at most the in-flight turn: a cancel mid-model-call drops nothing, a cancel mid-dispatch drops only the unanswered assistant message, so no orphaned tool calls or partial assistant messages ever reach the next request); `ContextExceeded` keeps the full discard, because committing an unshrinkable over-window history would wedge every future run against the same overflow. The pinned invariant moves from "nothing from the abandoned run survives" to "no incoherent tail leaks" — `failed_run_after_noop_compaction_leaves_history_clean` is unchanged and now pins the deliberate `ContextExceeded` exception. Pinned fail-first by `a_cancelled_run_salvages_completed_turns_into_the_next_request` and `a_cancel_mid_dispatch_drops_only_the_unfinished_turn` (request capture: the next run's first provider request carries the salvaged conversation) and `a_mid_stream_cancel_salvages_the_prompt` (amended from `a_mid_stream_cancel_commits_nothing`); machine-unit pins `a_coherent_pending_salvages_everything`, `an_unanswered_trailing_tool_call_is_dropped`, `an_unanswered_first_assistant_turn_keeps_only_the_prompt`, `a_text_only_trailing_assistant_message_is_coherent`, `an_empty_pending_salvages_nothing`; deliberately amended pins `a_hard_failed_run_recovers_on_the_next_run`, `a_cancelled_run_keeps_its_prompt_in_history` (was `failed_run_leaves_history_clean`), `compaction_then_cancellation_leaves_history_compacted`, and `a_cancel_during_an_in_flight_compact_ends_the_run_typed`; `tests/golden/fallback_routed.json` regenerated deliberately (the fallback-routed request carries the salvaged prompt from the failed primary attempt ahead of the re-issued one — coalesced into one user turn by the wire-coalescing entry below).

- **Adjacent text-only user messages coalesce on the wire** — salvage commits a failed run's prompt ahead of the next run's input, and memory transients prepend a user-role section ahead of the prompt, so the outbound request could carry consecutive user turns; OpenAI accepts them and Anthropic merges them server-side, but Gemini documents multiturn requests as alternating roles, making every post-failure resume a wire rejection waiting to happen. `build_turn_request` now folds any run of adjacent `Role::User` messages whose parts are all non-empty text into a single user message (texts joined with `\n`, order preserved) — a request-build view only, leaving machine state, committed history, and serialized runs untouched. A boundary next to anything else never merges: tool-result messages ride `Role::User` and their pairing with the assistant's tool calls is load-bearing, so the all-text guard is what keeps a tool round-trip intact; image-bearing and empty-parts messages keep their boundaries too. `tests/golden/fallback_routed.json` and `tests/golden/memory_injected.json` regenerated deliberately (the salvaged-then-reissued prompt and the memory-section-plus-prompt pairs each became one merged user message; section framing, order, and every other request untouched). Pinned fail-first by `seeded_adjacent_user_history_coalesces_into_the_request`, by the `src/engine/bare/llm_turn.rs` unit suite (`adjacent_text_only_users_coalesce_newline_joined`, `a_run_of_three_users_coalesces_in_order`, `a_tool_result_bearing_user_blocks_coalescing`, `an_image_bearing_user_blocks_coalescing`, `non_user_roles_never_coalesce`, `empty_parts_messages_never_coalesce`), and by the deliberately amended memory-injection pins `heuristic_only_memory_injection_is_byte_identical` and `a_tagged_only_store_renders_only_the_stronger_section` (the section renders verbatim and the prompt coalesces into the same user turn).
- **The redaction entropy heuristic now measures token runs, not whitespace-bounded pieces** — the whitespace bounding fix stopped multi-line collapse, but the measured core of each piece still kept interior punctuation, so any space-free stretch of credential-alphabet characters redacted whole when long and dense enough: a toolchain `PATH=$HOME/…/bin:$PATH` assignment, `git status` repository paths, cargo's `target/debug/deps/…-<hash>` test-binary paths, `git+https://…#<rev>` dependency sources, JSON raw-string literals in source code, and space-free call chains all collapsed into `[REDACTED:high_entropy]` markers on ordinary tool output. Candidates are now the maximal runs of token characters inside each piece — every non-token character (dots, colons, brackets, quotes, `$`, `?`, `#`) bounds a candidate exactly as whitespace does, because a credential is a dense run of credential-alphabet characters, never a dotted, bracketed, or interpolated structure. One guard keeps structured-but-alphabetic runs honest: a run with two or more `/` separators and at least two word segments — four or more pure ASCII lowercase letters each; digits disqualify a segment, because uniform-random material mixes digits and case while directory names do not (`target/debug/deps/…`) — is a path, not a credential, while a random base64 run's slash-segments are mixed-case noise — so slash-bearing secrets (the contextless base64 key class this lane exists for) still redact. The marker format, the 32-char minimum, the 4.5-bit threshold, and the curated patterns are unchanged. Pinned fail-first in `tests/redacting_middleware.rs` — `a_shell_path_assignment_passes_through_verbatim`, `git_status_porcelain_paths_pass_through_verbatim`, `cargo_test_output_paths_pass_through_verbatim`, `a_git_dependency_source_line_passes_through_verbatim`, and `a_json_raw_string_literal_passes_through_verbatim` (all five red before, byte-identical after), the neutralization-proven path guard `cargo_test_output_paths_pass_through_verbatim` + counter-guard `a_slash_bearing_credential_run_still_redacts` (the path rule disabled turns exactly the cargo pin red while the slash-bearing credential stays red), the word-segment rule by `a_digit_bearing_segment_run_is_not_path_shaped` and `a_run_is_path_shaped_only_on_four_letter_word_segments` (fail-first red: a credential whose only word-shaped segments carry digits or fall under four letters passed unmeasured — the first pins the exact 40-character 4.82-bit counter-example, the second enumerates word-pair pass-through against single-word, digit-bearing, and three-letter segments, each direction asserted), the per-segment masking by `a_dense_segment_inside_a_path_shaped_run_masks_alone` (fail-first red: a dense ≥32-character secret riding at the end of a path-shaped run — `…/repos/tokens/<secret>` — passed whole, because the exemption covered the entire run; a path-shaped run is now measured per segment — each `/`-segment faces the same length and entropy gates on its own, so the separators and word segments survive while the dense segment masks, and the pinned paths keep passing because their segments stay under the gates, the cargo artifact's trailing segment being 30 characters — and a `<crate>-<16-hex>` artifact tail under a `deps`/`build`/`.cargo` parent is exempt however long the crate name, so a 15+-character name whose hash clears the threshold no longer redacts ordinary `cargo test` output hash-dependently, pinned fail-first red by `a_long_crate_name_test_binary_segment_passes_verbatim` with the foreign-parent counter-guard `an_artifact_shaped_segment_after_a_foreign_parent_still_masks`), plus the earlier pins — code expressions, markdown link cells, the `::` core, the unpunctuated run, the boundary pair, commit sha, the whitespace trio, and the curated lanes remain pinned.
- **The curated `api_key_kv` pattern no longer fires on variable declarations** — the key-value lane (`token`/`secret`/`api[_-]?key` + separator + a 16-or-more alphanumeric value, optionally quoted) matched `let token = "…"` and every peer declaration shape, swallowing the statement into `let [REDACTED:api_key_kv];` — reading any source that declares a secret-named variable destroyed the line, and an agent reviewing such code read the marker as a syntax error. The pattern now carries a declaration arm — a participating `decl` capture group (`let`/`const`/`var`/`static`/`final`/`fn`/`def`/`function`, an optional `mut`, an optional type annotation) — and `SecretPatternSet::scrub` is capture-aware: declaration-shaped matches pass through verbatim while credential dumps keep firing, including the quoted env-dump shapes (`export TOKEN="…"`, the pinned `SECRET="…"`) — `export` is deliberately not a declaration keyword, and host-added patterns without a `decl` group are unaffected (the pass-through is inert for them). A declaration's quoted value now masks in place through the pattern's own `value` capture whenever it is not placeholder-marked — `let token = "[REDACTED:api_key_kv]";` — syntax intact, value masked; a bare initializer that is structurally an identifier — strict `UpperCamel`, every maximal lowercase run spanning at least two letters, with a digit ending a run exactly like an uppercase does (`SecretStoreHandle012345`, `SecureHandle::new()`), or a uniformly lowercase letters-only word — and a quoted value carrying a placeholder marker (`placeholder`, `example`, `sample`, `dummy`, `changeme`, `your`, `xxxx`, `redacted`, compared case-insensitively) pass verbatim, so reading source that declares a benign secret-named variable still survives, while any other value — quoted or bare — masks: a lowercase letter followed anywhere by an uppercase, a camel look-alike whose humps never reach two letters, a lone lowercase letter that a digit terminates, and a lowercase token carrying any digit — hex and base36 secrets — are credentials, not code — the digit-termination rule closes the absorbed-lone-letter sub-band — the majority of a strict-camel pass rate that measured ~2.5% of random 24-character values, now ~0.9%, an undisclosed residual of the hump-only rule, and the digits-disqualify rule — a digit ends identifier-hood exactly as it disqualifies a path word segment — closes the bare lowercase-hex lane the entropy lane can never backstop, hex topping out at 4.0 bits against its 4.5 gate; the 24-27-character hump-bearing values (`dGhpc0lzTXlDcmVkaXQ7ab`) and a 44-character text-derived value that clears the entropy lane's length gate while sitting under its density mask through the same lane; the rule is structural, not statistical (the hump-anywhere shape it replaced passes ~85-90% of 24-character random base64), and its chance bands are disclosed and measured, not silent: a uniformly lowercase letters-only bare value passes by design as a word-led identifier — uniform-random material essentially never lands there, since hex and base36 carry digits and digits disqualify — and an uppercase-led token whose lowercase runs all span two or more letters — the chance strict-camel band — passes at a measured ~0.9% of random 24-character values, an order of magnitude lower at 44, with single-letter-hump-before-digits names (`Ed25519PrivateKey`) masking as the accepted cost. The pass-through as first shipped carried a leak band: every 16–31-character secret in a declaration passed both lanes — the KV arm passed it through while the entropy lane's length gate starts at 32 — and a 32+-character but below-threshold literal (hex) passed both lanes the same way; the in-place masking closes both. The declaration keywords cover Rust, `JavaScript`/`TypeScript`, Python, and `final`'s adjacent-name shapes — type-prefixed declarations (Java, C, Go) are not covered — and the annotation tolerance is 48 characters: past it no key-value arm fires, and the value leans on the entropy heuristic alone. Assignments without a declaration keyword (`token = "…"` mid-function) still fire. Pinned fail-first in `tests/redacting_middleware.rs`: `a_let_declared_token_literal_passes_verbatim` and `a_mut_declaration_passes_verbatim` (both red before — the plain arm fired on the declaration), the annotation guard `a_type_annotated_declaration_passes_verbatim` (a declaration whose type name itself looks like a value — red before), and `an_exported_quoted_kv_still_redacts` (green before and after — the env-dump counter-guard); the value masking by `a_dense_quoted_secret_in_a_declaration_masks_its_value` and `a_low_entropy_hex_secret_in_a_declaration_masks_its_value` (both fail-first red — the exact 16–31-character and below-threshold bands the pass-through leaked) with its counter-guard `a_placeholder_marked_quoted_value_passes_verbatim`, the bare-value shape rule by `a_bare_credential_shaped_declaration_value_masks_in_place` (fail-first red — the exact unquoted 24-character mixed-case initializer demonstrated passing both lanes) with its counter-guard `a_bare_type_initializer_passes_verbatim` (a strict-camel bare initializer is code, not a credential), the structural rewrite by `a_hump_bearing_bare_base64_initializer_masks_in_place` and `a_text_derived_base64_bare_initializer_masks_via_the_key_value_lane` (both fail-first red — the hump-bearing base64 fixtures demonstrated passing verbatim) with its counter-guard `a_uniformly_lowercase_bare_initializer_passes_verbatim`, the digits-disqualify closing of the lowercase lane by `a_digit_bearing_lowercase_bare_initializer_masks_in_place` (fail-first red — an externally reported exact 32-character lowercase hex initializer `a3f9c2e81b7d4f6a0c5e9b2d7f1a8c3e` passed verbatim through both lanes) with the same letters-only-word counter-guard still green (`defaultconfigurationvalue` carries no digit), and the digit-termination tightening of the strict-camel rule by `a_strict_camel_by_digit_resets_initializer_masks_in_place` (fail-first red — the digit-absorbed lone-letter band) with its accepted-cost pin `a_single_letter_hump_before_digits_masks_like_a_credential`, and the property suite now plants declaration shapes — strict-camel survivors, hump-bearing secrets, and letter-led digit-bearing hex secrets — so the class is property-covered; and the annotation tolerance by `the_declaration_arm_spans_exactly_48_characters_of_annotation` (neutralization-proven — shrinking the tolerance to 47 turns exactly this pin red) beside `an_annotation_past_the_tolerance_matches_no_kv_arm` (the documented cliff, green both sides); the declaration arm is neutralization-proven (removing `let` from the keyword list turns exactly the three declaration pins red, the export guard green), the compound-name extension is pinned by `a_compound_named_declaration_passes_verbatim` (`let entropy_token = …` — the plain arm matched `token` inside the identifier, red before the widening) with its counter-guard `an_env_style_compound_key_dump_still_redacts` (`OPENAI_API_KEY=…` — the underscore env convention — keeps firing because no declaration keyword precedes it), and the pre-existing KV pins (`api_key_kv_is_redacted`, the case-and-separator variants with their quoted `SECRET="…"`) stay green.
- **Compaction no longer wedges on agent tool-loop histories — the `TokenSplitter` slides an orphaning cut instead of refusing it** — in a history of pure tool exchanges every role-transition boundary puts a kept tool result's call on the dropped side, so the splitter's pair-safety test refused every candidate and the split came back empty. The splitter now carries the truncator's own pairing discipline: a boundary that would orphan a kept result slides backward through `ToolPairing::adjusted_split` to its fixed point, the kept side stays self-contained exactly as `TruncatingCompactor` guarantees, the dropped slice ends with a complete exchange, a boundary that needs no slide still wins immediately (chat histories keep today's cut), and zero remains the answer only for genuinely unsplittable tails. Pinned by `splitter_slides_an_orphaning_cut_back_to_the_straddling_call`, `splitter_still_prefers_a_boundary_that_needs_no_slide`, `splitter_slides_a_straddling_boundary_back_to_the_call`, `qa_summarizer_compacts_a_tool_loop_history`, and engine-level `a_tool_loop_run_crosses_the_compaction_boundary_instead_of_stalling`.


### Security

- **Ledger files are created owner-only on unix — `trajectory.jsonl` and `events.jsonl` both open with `0600` at creation** — both ledgers persist user-derived text (prompt, response, tool results riding the next turn's query), so restricting creation to the owner is free defense in depth. It is not a trust boundary: the mode applies only when the file is first created, an existing file's permissions remain the host's to manage, non-unix platforms keep platform defaults (file access stays the host's responsibility there), and the open stays best-effort — a failure is the ledger's existing single-warn drop path. Pinned by `a_created_ledger_file_is_owner_only` (both a `trajectory.jsonl` writer and a named sibling writer produce `0600` files on unix).

## [0.3.2] - 2026-09-17

### Added

- **Trajectory → memory extraction (`memory::extractor`)** — turn recorded runs into learned memories: recovery pairs (a failed call later retried successfully → `ErrorPattern`), proven tool chains from non-failed runs (→ `Strategy`), and same-turn repeated-call waste (→ `Insight`, tagged `optimization`). `extract` reads the newest complete record from a single-record file or an accumulating JSONL ledger (torn or corrupt lines skipped); `extract_into` writes each mined item straight into a `LoopMemory` store with `relevance = quality` — a mid-loop store failure already carrying the `Memory` variant is annotated with the partial count, and every other variant passes through untouched, so a recoverable `Api` failure behind a custom store stays retryable instead of flattening to the terminal `Memory` variant. `ExtractionObserver` runs the pass automatically at run end on a spawned task — best-effort, since the ledger writer queues asynchronously, so hosts that require every run's extraction call `extract_into` directly. Three strategies behind `ExtractionConfig`: `Heuristic` (default — deterministic, offline, zero tokens), `Llm` (a caller-supplied `ApiClient` reads a budget-capped summary; its JSON answer is parsed leniently so bracketed prose around the array does not reject it; answers over 64 KiB and individual lessons over 2,000 bytes are rejected outright so a runaway response cannot flood the store or stall the runtime, and every LLM-mined memory carries a `provider-derived` provenance tag — stored provider text is untrusted, and the tag lets hosts filter or frame it at retrieval), and `Hybrid` (the LLM refines heuristic candidates, falling back to them on provider failure). Mined quality never reaches the 0.9 auto-validate threshold — strategy chains and LLM self-assessments cap at 0.85, repetition insights at 0.8, recovery pairs sit at a flat 0.7 — a rule-based mining pass is not confirmation. Telemetry: exactly one `loopctl.memory.extract.attempts` event per pass (`ok|no_client|parse_error|api_error`) plus per-category `loopctl.memory.extracted`. Pinned by `heuristic_mines_recovery_from_error_then_success`, `heuristic_mines_strategy_from_successful_chains`, `heuristic_mines_repetition_as_optimization_insight`, `llm_strategy_parses_the_provider_array`, `hybrid_falls_back_to_heuristic_on_provider_failure`, `extract_into_writes_mined_memories_to_the_store`, `extraction_observer_never_panics_on_a_missing_ledger`, `extract_reads_the_newest_record_from_an_accumulating_ledger`, and `a_self_assessed_quality_never_reaches_the_validated_tier`; the ledger and runtime hardening is pinned by `a_torn_trailing_ledger_line_still_yields_the_complete_record`, `a_corrupt_line_does_not_abort_the_ledger_scan`, `repeated_recovery_pairs_dedupe_to_one_memory`, `extract_without_a_tokio_runtime_errors_instead_of_panicking`, and `an_overlong_ledger_line_is_skipped_without_buffering_it`.
- **Real memory consolidation (`memory::consolidate`)** — the store-agnostic primitives every `LoopMemory` backend composes: `quality_score` (relevance ×0.45 + log-scaled access ×0.30 + recency ×0.15 + validated ×0.10), category-weighted exponential `decay_relevance`, Jaccard `cluster_duplicates` with `merge_cluster`, and the one-call `consolidate_entries` pass (decay → merge → prune → stats, where merging clusters near-duplicates, promotes each cluster's highest-quality member into the canonical slot, then folds corroborations). Decay is idempotent over cadence: each pass decays only the time since the last via the `last_decayed` stamp, so hourly and daily passes produce the same relevance, and it measures from `last_accessed` when set (else `created_at`) so retrieved memories resist decay — the access fold never rewinds a fresher stamp. Merges corroborate — relevance rises capped at 1.0, tags union, access counts sum — and adopt the cluster's earliest `created_at` while keeping the canonical's decay baseline, so a merged survivor is never charged the duplicate's age. Garbage cannot destroy a store: config thresholds normalize per pass, out-of-range or non-finite relevance degrades to zero and is excluded from clustering — a corrupted entry can neither corroborate a survivor nor merge into an unprunable one — and `retrieve` applies the same guard when scoring. `InMemoryStore::consolidate()` runs the full pass (see Changed); `with_consolidation(config)` tunes it. Pinned by `quality_score_weights_relevance_access_recency_and_validation`, `decay_relevance_is_idempotent_across_consolidation_cadences`, `cluster_duplicates_threshold_and_category_partition`, `merge_cluster_unions_tags_sums_access_and_caps_relevance`, `consolidate_entries_prunes_merges_and_keeps_the_valuable`, `consolidate_entries_promotes_the_strongest_member_into_canonical`, `garbage_thresholds_degrade_to_conservative_behaviour`, and `an_out_of_range_relevance_does_not_poison_the_ranking`; the clock and window hardening is pinned by `decay_relevance_never_redecays_time_across_a_backwards_clock`, `a_merged_survivor_decays_only_the_canonicals_window`, and `a_zero_threshold_falls_back_instead_of_collapsing_categories`.
- **Persistent memory backends (`memory::FileMemoryStore` feature `file_memory`, plus the `loopctl-sqlite` companion crate)** — learned memory survives a process restart, the capability `InMemoryStore` deliberately lacked. `FileMemoryStore` (in-crate, zero new dependencies) mirrors the store in memory and appends one compact-JSON `MemoryEntry` per line to a JSONL file — `store` is a single O(1) append — a non-finite `relevance` is refused before anything is written — serialized it would be a `null` the loader treats as fatal mid-file corruption, bricking the next open — and the loader refuses a foreign line whose number overflows `f32` into infinity, a value the store's own writer could never have written, caught loudly at the open instead of riding the mirror into a bricking rewrite (pinned by `a_non_finite_relevance_is_refused_before_it_bricks_the_file` and `a_foreign_infinite_relevance_line_fails_the_open`), with the line and its terminator in one write so concurrent appenders (even through two not-yet-converged spellings of one file) can never weld a line, and a failed append never removes bytes — no truncation rollback is attempted, since restoring the pre-append length could delete a concurrent writer's line that landed in the window; the store refuses further appends through every handle that converges with it — a sibling's store joins the latched state before it can write, and a unification carries the poison into the surviving state (pinned by `a_latched_append_failure_refuses_a_sibling_spellings_store`) — until a fresh `open` repairs the tail — and `open` reloads the file (a torn final line is dropped with a warning and its tail repaired, so a later append cannot weld onto the fragment; a malformed earlier line fails the open — corruption is loud; a missing file is created without truncating, so concurrent opens cannot discard a writer's lines), and `consolidate`/`flush` rewrite the file atomically and durably (an exclusively-created unpredictable temp file — a planted symlink at a predictable temp name can never redirect the rewrite — renamed over the target, with the containing directory synced so the rename survives power loss), so a crashed rewrite never half-overwrites and a completed checkpoint is on disk; a durability-unconfirmed failure (the rename landed, the directory sync did not) still commits a consolidation to the mirror and the file — only fsync-durability is unconfirmed — and the rewrite's stale-temp sweep removes only UUID-shaped `<name>.<uuid>.tmp` siblings, never a user's `<name>.backup.tmp`. Handles on one path within a process share state — `open`/`new` attach to the same live store (paths are identified by their canonical location, so the two constructors agree through symlinked directories and through a final symlink, dangling ones included — handles registered while a symlinked parent was still dangling migrate to the resolved key on the next construction, and spellings that resolve to one file unify into a single shared store, preserving the file's append order and adopting entries only the file still holds (a dropped sibling's un-converged appends) and pairing each entry with the file occurrence whose content it equals — an occurrence left without an equal copy adopts its own durable content, and a merge keeps every stale copy rather than dropping one that merely shares an id — so divergent copies under one id keep both contents across a convergence rewrite (pinned by `divergent_copies_under_one_id_survive_a_convergence_flush`, `divergent_copies_under_one_id_survive_a_unification`, `merge_mirrors_keeps_a_stale_duplicate_for_its_own_occurrence`, `reorder_pairs_each_duplicate_occurrence_with_its_own_content`, and `a_lone_spellings_flush_keeps_a_files_earlier_duplicate_content`) — the unification also runs before a flush or consolidation, so a converged handle rewrites from the unified mirror, and any rewrite out of a store that was ever provisionally keyed re-syncs against the file first, so a sibling's durable append that preceded the re-sync is adopted rather than stranded on the replaced inode — and a consolidation re-syncs before its access-log fold, pairing each mirror copy with its equal durable occurrence while the two still carry identical metadata, where folding first would leave a durable and a stamped copy for the pass to double-count or persist twice (pinned by `a_provisional_consolidate_does_not_double_count_prior_accesses` and `a_provisional_consolidate_with_merging_disabled_keeps_one_copy`) — and every handle shares state once any construction, append, or rewrite observes the resolution — each of those converges the registration before touching the file, so an append through any spelling that converges queues on the shared lock and lands on the replaced file, never between another handle's re-sync read and its rename — spellings whose symlink chains outrun the key derivation's follow budget can key apart forever, and the single-write append remains the weld guard between them (pinned by `a_store_through_a_second_spelling_converges_before_it_appends` and `stores_racing_a_flush_never_lose_an_append`)), and concurrent handles cannot drop each other's entries in a rewrite or weld appends — while cross-process access remains unsupported by contract (no file locking); multi-process durability is the companion's job. `loopctl-sqlite` (separate crate, `rusqlite` bundled so no system SQLite is required) provides `SqliteMemoryStore` over WAL-mode SQLite: every `MemoryEntry` field round-trips (`SystemTime` at millisecond resolution, tags as JSON text, `relevance` on `REAL`), retrieval loads every entry and ranks it with the same scorer (identical results, ordering, and tie-breaking to the flat stores; the FTS5 index is maintained on every write but not consulted by retrieval), retrieval access stamps are durable rows in a shared `access_stamps` table (visible to every store instance on the file, surviving restarts; `consolidate` consumes them inside its transaction, so a stamp is either folded or survives for the next pass — never lost to a retrieve racing a consolidate), and several stores on one file coordinate through WAL. Both backends rank and consolidate through the newly shared `memory::score::score_entry` scorer and the existing `consolidate_entries` pass, extracted from `InMemoryStore::retrieve` behavior-identically — retrieval behaves identically across all three backends. A `testing`-feature fault injection (`memory::file::fail_next_rewrite_at` with `RewriteFaultStage`) makes the rewrite-failure pins deterministic on privileged runners, where directory permission bits cannot force failures. Pinned by `stored_entries_round_trip_through_retrieve`, `memory_survives_a_process_restart`, `a_missing_or_empty_file_opens_as_an_empty_store`, `consolidate_prunes_and_rewrites_the_file`, `a_failed_rewrite_leaves_the_original_file_intact`, `a_torn_final_line_is_dropped_on_open`, `mid_file_corruption_fails_the_open`, `concurrent_stores_never_interleave_lines`, `appends_through_two_spellings_of_one_file_never_weld`, `unconverged_spellings_concurrent_stores_never_weld_a_line`, `an_unrecoverable_append_failure_marks_the_store_unusable`, `a_flush_from_a_lone_provisional_spelling_adopts_the_file`, and `ranking_matches_the_in_memory_store` (`tests/file_memory.rs`, feature `file_memory`), plus the companion suite in `loopctl-sqlite` (restart-survival, FTS5 ranking, WAL multi-writer).

- **`MemoryEntry` implements `PartialEq`** — copies compare by content, which is what a file-store convergence uses to pair each mirror copy with the file occurrence it belongs to (and user code can compare learned memories directly).

- **`BareLoop::from_machine_with_managers`** — the resume constructor that keeps the host's `LoopManagers`: the machine arrives seeded (via `LoopMachine::from_history` or a deserialized checkpoint) and the caller's bundle — observer host, dispatch pipeline, context manager — survives into the resumed loop, where `from_machine` builds a fresh bundle (dropping every installed observer) and `new_with_managers` starts from an empty machine. A bundle that carries no `ContextManager` gets the default one synced from the session config, matching `new_with_managers`; one that already carries a context manager is used as-is. Loop semantics untouched — additive only. Pinned by `from_machine_with_managers_seeds_the_conversation`, `seeded_history_reaches_the_api_request`, `managers_observers_survive_the_resume_construction`, and `a_caller_context_manager_is_used_as_is`.

### Changed

- **Breaking:** **`MemoryEntry` gains two public lifecycle fields** — `last_accessed` and `last_decayed` (`Option<SystemTime>`, both `#[serde(default)]`). Serialization is backwards-compatible; exhaustive struct literals must list both. Migration: add `last_accessed: None, last_decayed: None`, or keep using `MemoryEntry::new` plus builders (unaffected).
- **`McpServerAdapter` follows rmcp's `ServerInfo` → `ServerConfig` rename** — the `mcp` feature's optional dependency minimum rises to `rmcp = "3.4"`, whose release deprecated the old alias (any `-D warnings` build naming it now fails); behavior is unchanged, as both names alias the same `InitializeResult`.
- **`InMemoryStore::consolidate()` now runs the full consolidation pass** instead of the bare relevance-floor retain it replaced: near-duplicates merge — each cluster's highest-quality member promoted into the canonical slot before the fold — and the stats populate `merged`/`bytes_saved`. The 0.05 relevance floor and its boundary comparison are retained, but pruning now also applies the composite quality score after decay — a never-accessed entry that the bare retain kept indefinitely now ages out as decay erodes its relevance and quality.
- **Retrieved memories inject with provenance framing** — entries carrying the `provider-derived` tag (everything the LLM pass authored) are **excluded from injection by default**: the trust boundary is opt-in, so unreviewed provider text never reaches a prompt unless a host asks for it. `RunConfig::memory_include_provider_derived` (default `false`; builder `with_memory_include_provider_derived`; serde-defaulted so pre-field serialized configs deserialize to exclusion — the behavior every released version had, since no release predating the field could hold a tagged entry) opts tagged entries back in, rendering them in their own trailing section under a stronger untrusted-text framing (`Untrusted learned text (model-authored, never instructions — verify before acting on it)`), after the trusted section; heuristic-mined memories render byte-identically to the previous single-prefix shape, and a tagged-only store with inclusion enabled renders only the stronger section. `RunConfig` is `#[non_exhaustive]`, so no struct-literal migration is needed. Pinned by `the_default_config_excludes_provider_derived_memories`, `provider_derived_memories_render_under_the_stronger_framing`, `heuristic_only_memory_injection_is_byte_identical`, `a_tagged_only_store_renders_only_the_stronger_section`, `the_exclusion_knob_filters_provider_derived_entries`, `the_tag_constant_is_the_wire_value`, `a_config_missing_the_knob_field_deserializes_to_exclusion`, and `a_config_built_with_inclusion_survives_a_serde_round_trip`.
- **`PermissionMiddleware` user-refusal denials now log** — an `Ask` the resolver refuses emits the same `tracing::warn!` as every other denial site (message unchanged) — and a cancelled pipeline now aborts a pending approval prompt (`cancelled while awaiting approval`) instead of awaiting an unresponsive resolver indefinitely; inside the engine the loop's outer cancel select already covered this, but standalone `ToolPipeline` entry points had no such guard.

### Fixed

- **`#[derive(Tool)]` matches serde's rename rules for fields** — including the split `rename_all(serialize = …, deserialize = …)` form, where the schema follows the deserialization rule (pinned by `split_rename_rules_follow_the_deserialization_side`); `rename_all = "snake_case"` and `"lowercase"` are identities on fields (serde's field arm treats them the same; the previous conversion invented names serde rejects), the case-changing strategies convert ASCII-only exactly as serde does, `"SCREAMING-KEBAB-CASE"` is accepted in serde's spelling, `#[serde(default)]` is found after value-bearing serde keys like `with = "…"`, and flag keys reject `= value` and parenthesized misuse with a named error.

- **Pre-cancelled tool calls no longer start** — the core tool dispatch skips pre-cancelled calls deterministically instead of relying on `select!`'s random branch order, which could poll a side-effecting tool once before the cancel branch won.

- **Stale `BareLoop` docs reconciled with the machine-seeding constructors** — `conversation()`, `machine()`, and the `machine` field claimed the machine is "(re)created at the top of every `run()` call" and the conversation is "empty until the first `run()`", false since the `accept_input` refactor and for both machine-taking constructors: the machine exists from construction, `run()` reuses it (committed history preserved, per-run state reset), and a seeded machine's history is visible — and rides the first outbound request — before any `run()`. The struct doc's `# Construction` index now lists all four constructors (the resume pair was missing), and the `managers` field doc no longer says "fresh on construction" for the caller-bundle constructors. Doc-only; the resume constructor's default-seeding direction (a context-manager-free bundle gets the session-synced `ContextManager`) is now pinned by `from_machine_with_managers_seeds_the_conversation`.

## [0.3.1] - 2026-09-05

### Added

- **Vector primitives (`memory::vector`, feature `vector_index`)** — the two traits every semantic-retrieval memory store is built from, plus dependency-free reference implementations: `EmbeddingProvider` (`dim()`, required `embed(&str)`, provided `embed_batch` that loops `embed` and preserves input order) turns text into an `Embedding`; `VectorIndex` is a nearest-neighbour store keyed by `Uuid` (upsert on `add`, cosine-scored top-`k` `search` sorted descending with an id tiebreak, idempotent `remove`, `len`/`is_empty`, dimension mismatches surface as `LoopError::Memory`). Both traits are object-safe async — boxed futures exactly like `LoopMemory` — so a store can hold `Box<dyn EmbeddingProvider>` / `Box<dyn VectorIndex>` and swap backends without touching its contract. Reference impls: `LinearVectorIndex` (brute-force O(n) cosine scan, `RwLock` interior mutability, usable as the correctness oracle for faster indexes) and `HashingEmbedder` (a deterministic hashing embedder whose retrieval quality is unsuitable for real use but which exercises the whole layer with no network and no API key; zero-token input yields the documented zero vector). Free `cosine_similarity` helper, an example (`vector-index-demo`), and an integration suite. **`default = []` unchanged** — the feature adds no dependencies.
- **Token-counter presets (`compact::RatioTokenCounter`)** — per-provider characters-per-token ratio estimators for hosts who know whose tokenizer dominates their workload: `RatioTokenCounter::openai()` (4.30 chars/token, 4 tokens per-message overhead), `anthropic()` (3.60, 5), `gemini()` (4.05, 4), and `local_default()` (3.80, 4, the conservative local-model starting point), plus `new(ratio, per_message)` for host-calibrated ratios — the honest long-term answer for local tokenizers (three-minute calibration recipe in the type docs: send one known text, read `input_tokens`, build the counter). A ratio that is NaN, infinite, or not positive is rejected with a warning and the 4.0 default takes its place (mirroring the shield's non-finite discipline), and construction emits the `loopctl.window.token_counter` event naming the governing estimate (`preset`, `ratio`, `per_message`). The presets share `HeuristicTokenCounter`'s message rendering via a common helper, so the two counters cannot drift in what they count — only in how they divide it. **Defaults are unchanged:** the engine still uses `HeuristicTokenCounter` unless a host installs a counter; presets are opt-in. Pinned by `preset_constants_are_the_documented_values`, `count_is_chars_over_ratio_plus_overhead`, `non_finite_ratio_falls_back_with_a_warning`, `renders_the_same_surface_as_the_heuristic`, `saturates_on_absurd_input`, and `construction_telemetry_names_the_governing_estimate`.
- **`request_structured_prompted` (prompted-JSON structured output)** — the schema-in-prompt fallback for providers without native `response_format` support (most local models behind Ollama/vLLM reject or ignore `response_format`, and loopctl since 0.3.0 rejects request options a client cannot forward rather than silently degrading them): `structured::request_structured_prompted<T>()` embeds `T`'s minified JSON Schema in the system prompt with strict-JSON instructions (`structured::prompted_system_prefix`, public for hosts composing their own flows; a caller-supplied system prompt is prepended, never replaced), sends a plain `create_message` — no `RequestOptions`, so there is nothing for a local server to reject — extracts with the lenient scanner (fences and prose-wrapped JSON accepted), and on a parse or schema failure makes exactly one corrective retry feeding the concrete error back to the model, bounded to 2,000 characters so the corrective message cannot add a second copy of a long failure to the retry, and replaying the failed answer as a text-only assistant turn — its tool-call input rendered inline (a replayed tool call without its result would be rejected by strict providers) and an empty reply as a non-empty `(empty reply)` turn (empty assistant content being rejected by content-validating providers); a second failure returns the existing `StructuredError::Deserialize` carrying the reason and the last output truncated to 2,000 characters (no new variant — `StructuredError` is not `#[non_exhaustive]` until 0.4.0 opens that window). Provider-agnostic by construction; on native-supporting providers prefer `request_structured` (the module docs gain the decision table). Telemetry: the `structured.extract` span (`mode=prompted`, per-attempt `ok`), the `loopctl.structured.prompted` counter (`outcome=first_try|after_retry|failed|api_error`), the attempts-count event, and per-attempt token counters. Pinned by `a_prose_wrapped_fenced_answer_parses_on_the_first_try`, `a_corrective_retry_recovers_a_malformed_first_answer`, `an_oversized_first_failure_rides_the_retry_bounded`, `a_tool_call_only_failure_still_reports_its_input`, `a_tool_call_failure_replays_as_a_text_only_answer`, `an_empty_first_answer_replays_as_a_non_empty_turn`, `a_second_failure_returns_the_reason_and_bounded_last_output`, `the_prompted_wire_carries_no_tools_and_no_options`, `a_caller_system_prompt_is_preceded_not_replaced`, `an_api_failure_fails_fast_without_a_retry`, `an_api_failure_on_the_retry_itself_surfaces_as_the_api_error`, `telemetry_names_and_outcomes_match_the_documented_contract`, and `prompted_prefix_carries_the_schema_and_rules`.
- **`MockApiClient` records the non-streaming wire** (feature `testing`) — `captured_requests()` clones out every served `create_message` request oldest-first, and `create_message_calls()` / `with_options_calls()` count the two wire paths in disjoint counters, so tests can assert on the exact request shape a caller produced and on which path it took. A request rejected before sending (unsupported options) increments `with_options_calls` but is never captured, since it never reached the wire. `with_errors(Vec<Option<String>>)` scripts failures by call position — each call consumes the front entry: a `Some` fails that one call (still counted and captured, like `with_error`), a `None` or an exhausted script serves normally — so a test can fail exactly one turn, for example only the corrective retry. The prompted-structured pins use this to prove the fallback never sends a tools payload or request options a local server could reject, and to drive the retry's own api-failure path.
- **`ThinkTool` (feature `builtin_tools`)** — a no-side-effect scratchpad the model reasons into before acting: `tool::builtin::ThinkTool` advertises `think(thought) → "ok"`, with the tool description written as an instruction template (restate the goal, list the options, check the plan against constraints, decide) so it teaches the procedure rather than just naming the tool. `is_read_only` and `is_concurrency_safe` are true by definition; a missing or non-string `thought` field is a `ToolError::InvalidInput` naming the field; the acknowledgement is constant — the thought is already in the conversation as the call's input, so echoing it back would double its token cost. First resident of `tool::builtin` (the home for future built-ins); registration is the only way the tool enters a session, and `default = []` is unchanged. Pinned by `call_returns_fixed_acknowledgement`, `wrong_typed_input_is_invalid_input`, `missing_thought_field_is_invalid_input`, `flags_are_true`, and `schema_advertises_only_the_thought_field`.
- **Configurable provider-profile builders (`*_builder()`)** — a middle tier between the zero-argument profile constructors and the bare client builders: `provider::deepseek_builder()`, `grok_builder()`, `moonshot_builder()`, `ollama_builder(model)`, and `azure_builder(resource)` (each behind that provider's existing feature, returning `OpenAiClientBuilder`), plus `zai_builder()` (returning `AnthropicClientBuilder`). Each returns the underlying client builder with the provider's facts pre-seeded — endpoint, credential environment variable (alias fallbacks preserved), and default model. Hosts driven by a configuration file apply their own precedence: an explicit `with_api_key`/`with_model`/`with_base_url`/`with_timeout` overrides the seeded environment, and a bad seeded fact — an invalid Azure resource name, a missing seeded key, or Azure's missing deployment model — fails at `build()`, in the order the constructors have always reported, naming the variable (`MOONSHOT_API_KEY`, `AZURE_OPENAI_MODEL`, …), so no provider fact (endpoint string, env-var name, default model) needs to be duplicated outside the crate. The zero-argument constructors are now thin wrappers over these builders and behave identically (their error messages included). `BedrockClientBuilder` also gains the shared HTTP configuration surface the other builders already delegate to — `with_timeout` (read-timeout semantics), `with_connect_timeout`, `with_http_client`, and the pool/TCP knobs — instead of a hardcoded default client. Pinned by `moonshot_builder_seeds_the_documented_facts`, `explicit_overrides_beat_the_seeded_environment`, `azure_builder_requires_a_model_naming_the_env_var`, `ollama_builder_seeds_local_defaults_and_cloud_env`, `bedrock_builder_accepts_the_http_knobs`, and the `moonshot_constructor_and_builder_agree` / `zai_constructor_and_builder_agree` tests.
